# 1761 Pochaiv Menaion — Vol. 9: May (Pochaiv Lavra, 1761) <a id="1761-pochaiv-menaion-vol-9-may-pochaiv-lavra-1761"></a>

## MONTH OF MAY
## May 1. Holy Prophet Jeremiah

*The month of May has 31 days.*

*On the 1st day: the Holy Prophet Jeremiah.*

*Stichera of the Prophet, Tone 8, Podoben: "Lord, Even if Before the Judgment Seat" (Гдⷭ҇и а́ще и на суди́щи):*

O Lord, before he was formed, Thou didst foreknow glorious Jeremiah, and before birth from the womb Thou didst sanctify him as a prophet, as truly foreknowing the freedom of his mind; by his prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls.

O Lord, even though holy Jeremiah was cast into the mire,[^1] yet noetically did he receive lovely fragrance, as though taking up his abode in the mansions of Paradise; by his prayers, as Benefactor, preserve us all.

O Lord, shining with Thy most radiant beams, the prophet was seen to be honorable and blessed, even by adversaries; for the foe is openly put to shame by virtues; and the things to come he foreknew as present risings.

*Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion, in the same Tone, Podoben: "The same Tone and Podoben" (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

Descend, O Bride of God, unto the sighing of my contrite heart; receive, O pure Virgin Mary, the lifting up of my hands, as thou art loving toward mankind, and cast me not away, O all-immaculate one; that I may sing and magnify thee who hast magnified our race.

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "The same" (то́йже):*

O beginningless Word of the Father, and my Son, enthroned together with the Spirit: how hast Thou stretched out Thy most pure hands upon the Wood? What is this Thy great poverty now, O most Good? Thus cried the all-immaculate one, standing before Thy Cross.

*Troparion, Tone 2:*

*Troparion of the Prophet, Tone 2:*

Celebrating the memory of Thy Prophet Jeremiah, O Lord, therefore we pray Thee: save our souls.

*Canon, Tone 8.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God.

*Troparia:*

Foreknown before thy creation, thou wast sanctified from the maternal womb, and wast anointed a prophet unto the nations, crying aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God.

Having beforehand received understanding of these things, and foreseeing the movements of thy mind, O Jeremiah manifest of God, He establishes thee as a guide unto the people.

Having cleansed the perceptive faculty of thy mind from carnal defilements, thou wast shown forth as a witness of truth, being exceedingly enriched by thy Creator.

*Theotokion:*

Having taken up His abode within thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, and having beyond nature given birth in the flesh unto the Word of the Father upon earth, thou hast appeared as Sovereign Lady of all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the establishment of them that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of them that are darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparia:*

Having sanctified Thy prophet before his birth, O Master, Thou didst send him as a guide unto them that were astray.

The grace of the Spirit taking up its abode upon thy lips, like a most radiant luminary, shone forth upon them that are in darkness.

Unable to endure the myrrh of thy fragrance, the vile assembly of the Israelites[^2] shut thee in a pit.

*Theotokion:*

Through thee, O all-pure one, the supersubstantial and beginningless Son of God was well-pleased to dwell together with mankind.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Having Risen from the Tomb" (Воскр҃се и҆з̾ гро́ба):*

God inclined His ear unto thee to hear His words, O God-proclaiming prophet; therefore, as present, thou didst make clear unto all things that were not yet. And with lamentations over thy people who had departed from the Lord, thou didst weep; and now being in the heavens, thou prayest that we may be saved.

*Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*

Into the deep of grievous despondency have I fallen; from the multitude of mine evil and impure deeds have I come into perplexity, and by despair am I now possessed: O Sovereign Lady, Birthgiver of God, save me thyself by thy compassion; for thou art the help and cleansing of sinners a saving cleansing.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hanging upon the Cross, the undefiled ewe-lamb cried aloud: O my Child, what is this strange and unexpected sight? How is the Life of all condemned unto death in the likeness of mortals? But rise on the third day from the dead, O Word, as Thou hast said, that rejoicing I may glorify Thee.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*

The most radiant illumination of the All-Holy and Divine Spirit, flashing upon thy heart, wrought enlightenment for them that are in darkness.

Having suffered in sympathy more than all the prophets, O all-blessed one accepted of God: vouchsafe mercy unto me who am in despair through many transgressions.

Having become a mediator by evident and divine illumination, by thy mediation calm the storm of my turbulent life.

*Theotokion:*

Entreat God Whom thou didst bear, O all-pure Virgin, by thy prayers to grant forgiveness of transgressions.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant Thy peace unto us, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*

As lightning Thou didst send Thy prophet, consecrated Jeremiah, unto them that are in darkness, announcing the brilliant things of Christ.

As a chosen arrow hidden in a quiver, O Jeremiah, by His foreknowledge thy Master showed thee forth in the times that were fitting.

Possessing pure spiritual ears, O glorious one, when the Spirit conversed with thee, thou wast vouchsafed to listen, O prophet.

*Theotokion:*

Great and awesome is the mystery of the Virgin: for heaven, having shown forth God unto mankind through her on earth, maternally nurtured Him as an Infant.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The abyss of sins and the storm of transgressions troubles me, and plunges me into the depth of violent despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me as unto Peter, O Pilot, and save me.

*Troparia:*

Thou didst draw forth enlightenment and grace richly, O God-bearer, having broadened the spiritual capacities of the soul; and filling them, thou hast communally granted radiance unto all who desire it, O prophet.

The sea of this world being tossed up, the turbulent surges of afflictions beholding, thou didst desire the outermost courts, and as a river pouring forth a fountain of tears, bitterly to lament the malice of thy people.

The death of the Deliverer thou didst mystically foretell, O God-proclaiming one: for like a lamb they lifted Christ up upon the Wood, the Prince of Life, the lawless Jewish council, the Benefactor of all creation.

*Theotokion:*

The Word Who was begotten bodiless of the Father before the ages, is born in the flesh in time from thee, O pure one; and in His shadow shall we all live, as Jeremiah prophesied of old.[^3]

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*

Having cleansed thy light-bearing heart by the Spirit, O great prophet and martyr, glorious Jeremiah, thou didst receive the gift of prophecy from on high, and didst cry aloud with a great voice among the nations: This is our God, and none other shall be compared unto Him, Who, having become incarnate, appeared upon the earth.[^4]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Troparia:*

Thou art now no longer in hopes, but beholding in deed the fulfillment of the things spoken by thee, O prophet, thou criest aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

A divine instrument thou wast shown to be, struck by the hand of the Comforter, uttering faithful voices, and singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

Writing down thy lamentations, O prophet, thou didst not darken divine gladness, which from infancy thou didst clearly enjoy, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*

To enter into the womb of the Virgin, as Thou wast well-pleased, desiring to save mankind, Thou didst clearly teach the prophets of the wonder: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Having become conquerors over the tyrant and the flame by Thy grace, cleaving exceedingly unto Thy commandments, the children cried aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparia:*

Into the heavens hast thou ascended rejoicing, O God-inspired Jeremiah, and with the bodiless hosts thou standest before the throne of the King of all, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto the ages.

Having become a preacher of piety, O all-lauded and consecrated one, thou didst rebuke the weakness of impiety, O Jeremiah: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto the ages.

Shining with the light of divine radiance, thou sendest flashes of brilliance unto them that love thee, having received them as a most luminous mirror, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*

My nature the Giver of life received from thee, and cleansed it from former defilements, O Mother of God, Whom we hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Truly we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, being saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparia:*

Commingled with the purest Light, the beam of Divinity, O blessed and glorious one, thou didst clearly acquire His manifestations upon earth.

Having reached the incorruptible Spring, and by the illuminations thereof, O God-inspired one, Thou hast manifestly received the end desired by thee.

With the light-bearing beam which thou now dost enjoy, vouchsafe, O blessed Jeremiah, to enlighten all that hymn thy memory.

*Theotokion:*

Knowing thee to be a haven of salvation, who believe in thy most pure birthgiving, we pray thee to be delivered from afflictions.

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek.  
*Verse:*  
The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet.  
*Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 186 [2 Cor 4:6–15].[^5] Alleluia, Tone 5: Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His name. Gospel according to Luke, pericope 14 [Luke 4:22–30].[^6] Communion Hymn: The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance.*

## May 2. St. Athanasius the Great

*On the same month, on the 2nd day: Our Father among the Saints Athanasius the Great, Patriarch of Alexandria.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*

Vouchsafed immortal life, when thou didst preach the consubstantial and co-eternal Son and Word, Christ the Giver of life, unto the beginningless Father and Begetter: then with thy fiery tongue, O glorious one, thou didst burn up the substance of heresies, having driven having driven Arius away from the Church of Christ. And with divine words thou didst enlighten, O wise one, the hearts of the pious to worship the Trinity in one essence.

Like a river of God, full of spiritual waters, thou wast shown forth, O most marvelous one, overflowing with divine teachings, and watering the entire plenitude of the Church, but drowning the council of heretics in the deep of thy divine words, O father, and in the depth of thy understandings. For thou, O most wise, didst preach Christ as consubstantial with the Begetting Father, O good, divine, and most wealthy Athanasius.

As a mystic teacher and divine hierarch, thou didst endure perils and persecutions patiently, and slander, O blessed one, and long-lasting banishments for the sake of the faith of Christ. Clearly didst thou preach Christ as consubstantial, co-eternal, equal in operation, and co-beginningless with the Primal and Co-beginningless Father. Therefore He Himself adorned thee manifestly with most radiant teachings, and showed thee forth most radiant unto the whole world.

*At the Aposticha, Tone 5, Podoben: "O Venerable Father" (Преподо́бне ѻ҆́тче):*

O venerable father, God-manifest Athanasius, from infancy thou wast known as a luminary of the Church, shining with most radiant visions, and enriched in every manner of virtues; with fire-breathed words thou didst burn up heresies, while guiding all the faithful by the most divine radiance of the Trinity, understood in one Godhead and possessing co-eternal glory: entreat Her, and pray unto Her evermore, to grant oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy unto the world.

O venerable father, God-speaking Athanasius, being aptly named after immortality,[^7] by its mediating path didst thou walk, being manifestly enriched with the splendor of life; having acquired a pure mind, O God-wise one, thou wast found a champion of the Word, and didst breathe according to the All-Holy Spirit, enlightened by Whom thou didst conquer thine enemies, protected by Whom thou wast shown to be invincible, and deified by Whom thou art a partaker thereof, O holy one: ask for all that there be granted oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy.

O venerable father, God-proclaimer Athanasius, having endured perils and frequent afflictions, thou hast now been vouchsafed the delights of Paradise, having entered within the divine mansions; thou hast drawn near unto the tree of incorruptible life as a victor, adorned with a righteous crown, O blessed one, and patterned with the diadem of the kingdom on high, and shining with beauty. Pray unto Christ, entreat Christ, O God-wise one, to grant oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy unto the Church.

*Glory... Tone 6:*

Let us all hymn Athanasius, the hierarch of Christ, as having abolished all the teachings of Arius, and clearly proclaiming in all the world the dominion of the Holy Trinity, one God in three persons undivided; Whom he also entreats on behalf of us who in faith celebrate his memory.

*Both now... of the feast.*

*At the Aposticha, of the Triodion. Glory of the Saint, Tone 3. By Germanus:*

Once again the gold-flowing Nile, who is named after immortality,[^8] having arrived in his yearly memory, by fair risings overflows with gold-streaming outpourings of immortality, bearing the fruit of incorruptible food by lofty divine speech; he teaches us to worship the undivided dominion of the Trinity, and pours forth divine teachings, watering the thoughts of the faithful, and prays for our souls.

*Both now... of the feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*

A pillar of Orthodoxy wast thou, confirming the Church with divine dogmas, O hieromartyr Athanasius; for having preached the Son as consubstantial with the Father, thou didst put Arius to shame, O venerable father: entreat Christ God to grant great mercy unto us.

*Canon of the feast, with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Saint, on 6, the acrostic of which is: "Athanasius is the praise of Orthodoxy." A composition of Theophanes. Tone 8.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The rod of Moses, working a wonder of old, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, drowned Pharaoh the chariot-driver, but saved fleeing Israel on foot, as they sang a song unto God.

*Troparia:*

Unto Athanasius I offer praise, and as praising virtue, unto God rather do I offer praise: from Whom is granted unto men the praiseworthy gift of virtue, of which he was a living image and seal.

Full of God-wisdom wast thou, and brighter than the sun by an angelic life, O blessed one; shining forth now, O venerable one, thou hast surpassed the laws of praises that come from us: yet accept, O father, even if it be not according to worthiness, this praise.

As is right, then, we weave a song in thy glorious memory but falling short of greater things, O Athanasius, worthy of thy songs, we pray that forgiveness be granted unto us, and ungrudging grace of the Spirit through thee.

In youth thou didst mortify the leaping of carnal passions, having in thy youth acquired the wisdom of elders, well-ordered, O blessed Athanasius: for by desire of wisdom thou didst set aright all thy senses.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst bear Him Who was incarnate for our sake, O all-immaculate one, Who before was bodiless, the beginningless Word of the Father, O virginal boast, receptacle of purity, and temple of cleansing, Sovereign Lady of the world, Bride of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou only Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*

Having cleansed both soul and body from every defilement, O Athanasius, thou wast shown forth as a temple worthy of God: therefore the plenitude of the Trinity rested within thee, O most rich sacred initiate.

Having found thy soul cleansed from passions, as the grace of the Comforter desired, It manifestly shows forth efficacious works within it, and appoints thee, O father, as a most radiant luminary unto the world.

A rule of the hierarchate wast thou shown forth unto hierarchs, and a depiction of active life: while thy word, O most wise one, is clearly an image of contemplation, and the definition of thy theology was shown forth as teaching.

*Theotokion:*

Higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim hast thou appeared, O Birthgiver of God: for thou alone didst receive the uncontainable God in thy womb, O undefiled one. Therefore all we the faithful bless thee in hymns, O pure one.

*After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the feast. Then the Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*

Having learned the words of wisdom, and overthrown the deceit of Arius, thou didst preach the Orthodox faith unto the world. For thou didst truly make clear that Christ, our Life, the Word of the Father, is enthroned together with Him. Therefore with one mind the Church glorifies the consubstantial Trinity in the unity of the Godhead, O God-bearer Athanasius: entreat Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto them that with love honor thy holy memory.

*Glory... Both now... of the feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God my rejoicing, not leaving the bosom of the Father, and visiting our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Into thee the life-bearing breath from on high breathed, which first came in God-befitting manner upon the Spirit of Christ in the upper room and filled the disciples, showing thee forth, O thrice-holy father, as an apostle preaching the Orthodox faith.

Being vehemently kindled with divine zeal, thou didst assist the Council, and before thou wast made a primate, in Nicaea thou didst preach the consubstantiality; therefore Christ appoints thee as chief shepherd and teacher of the Church.

Possessing lips as the rudders of the Church by divine providence, O Athanasius, as with an axe thou didst hew down all the soul-corrupting babblings of the heretics, uprooting them like thorns; and watering the rational seed, thou didst cultivate it in faith.

By thy fiery tongue the akin division of Arius and the confusion of Sabellius were burnt up, which from madness are evil and equally impious, and all the heretical blasphemies together with them, O Athanasius.

*Theotokion:*

A new Adam in place of the first wast Thou from the Virgin, a Deliverer and Savior of all in place of the foremother, and true immortal Life in place of death. Therefore knowing her who bare Thee to be the Birthgiver of God, worthily do we bless her, O pure one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light unwaning, and alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*

Having Christ speaking in thee, O father, through the sweet-sounding organ of thy tongue, thou didst put to shame the most insane heresy of idols,[^9] guiding those who had gone astray, and turning them unto the true God, O Athanasius.

All destructive heresies thou didst refute, O father, by words and writings, driving diverse deceit out of the world by wise demonstrations, and making plain incontrovertibly the Orthodox faith, O blessed one.

Truly as a great teacher, a teacher of many teachers, O Athanasius, the Church of Christ falsely does not proclaim thee, O father, but reckons thee with the Apostles, as making manifest the blameless word of faith.

*Theotokion:*

Having acquired maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one pure one, despise not our prayer for thy akin providence toward us; for unto the Master we Christians favorably offer thee as our only cleansing.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparia:*

Apollinarius having appeared as a sower of strange seed, depriving Christ of a rational soul and mind,[^10] was strongly refuted by thee, O all-blessed one, as foolish and corrupted in mind.

The diamond strength[^11] of thy God-inspired words, O blessed one accepted of God, like a sound of thunder clearly resounded in the ears of heretics, and blackened their faces.

Every master of eloquence, lovingly embracing in thy writings what was wisely composed according to the Spirit, and foreseeing the budding heresies that would arise, prophetically turned from them by thy guidance.

*Theotokion:*

The Only-begotten Son, invisible before the ages, in the last days became the first-born, visible in the flesh from thee, O Mother of God, the God of my salvation.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "In the Streams of Thy Blood" (Крове́й твои́хъ):*

Having planted the teachings of Orthodoxy, thou didst cut down the thorns of false glory, multiplying the seed of faith with the rain of the Spirit, O venerable one: therefore we hymn thee, O Athanasius.

*Ikos:*

Pouring forth torrents of thy words, divine teachings unto the world, thou didst water the souls of the faithful, O Athanasius; and thou didst drown the champions of heresy like other Egyptians. Having written for us the God-inscribed law of Orthodoxy, showing forth that the Trinity is to be honored in one Godhead, by Which preserve us from invisible enemies, O deliverer: therefore we hymn thee, O Athanasius.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The fire was put to shame of old by God's condescension in Babylon; therefore the children in the furnace, dancing with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparia:*

Having Orthodoxly preached the Word as equally honored and enthroned together with the Father, the Only-begotten Son, O father named after immortality, straightway thou teachest that the Spirit is enthroned together, consubstantial, and co-eternal.

As a sacredly-manifest teacher and preacher, proclaiming the Orthodox faith brighter than the light of the sun, He granted thee unto the ends of the earth, as a silencer of impiety *At the Praises, Sticheron 3:*[^12]
By the radiance of thy divine words, O venerable father, the whole Church is adorned with fair beauties, and worthily honors thy all-festal memory, O Athanasius, glory of priests, radiant and great preacher of piety, champion of the Trinity.

*Glory, in the same Tone. By Patriarch Germanus:*
A great and true church trumpet, the much-suffering Athanasius, summons the universe today to radiant praise; let all the faithful, running beforehand to the feast of the good shepherd, honor his sacred labors with hymns: that through his prayers we may receive from Christ God great mercy.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, of the Feast. Glory, Tone 2:*[^13]
Let us sacredly praise the great adornment of hierarchs, Athanasius, the invincible defender: for he, having cut down the ranks of heresies by the power of the All-Holy Spirit, raised trophies of victory for Orthodoxy throughout the whole universe, piously combining the Trinitarian mystery by the property of Persons, and again uniting Them without confusion into one by identity of essence, and Cherubically theologizing, he prays ever for our souls.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, the appointed Ode; and of the Saint, Ode 3. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*
*Epistle to the Hebrews, composite pericope 318 [Heb 7:26–8:2].*
*Alleluia, Tone 2:*
*Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, composite pericope 34 [Matt 10:1, 5–8].*
*Communion Hymn:*
*In everlasting memory [shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings. Alleluia].*

## May 3. Holy Martyrs Timothy and Maura

*And the Falling Asleep of our Venerable Father Theodosius, Hegumen of the Monastery of the Caves, the Founder of the Cenobitic Monastic Life in the Rus' Land.[^14] His service is written after this service.*

*Stichera of the Holy Martyrs, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant Among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
*Sticheron 1:*
The pair among the martyrs who suffered valiantly, and put to death the combatant, and loved Christ, and hated the world, the worthy citizens of the noetic Zion: Maura today and Timothy, having come together in faith, let us praise with one accord, all ye lovers of the martyrs.

*Sticheron 2:*
Bodily chastisements, the gouging out of thine eyes, the piercing of thine ears, the torment of the wheel, the hanging of a heavy stone didst thou endure, O Timothy, casting down the enemy, and being borne aloft to divine glory, O glorious one. Therefore having come together in faith, we suppliantly praise thee, the glory and boast of martyrs.

*Sticheron 3:*
With beam-like radiances didst thou darken the enemy, having purely suffered, O glorious Maura: for thou didst endure the severance of the extremities, the cutting off of thy fingers, the burning with candles, and the temporal crucifixion, and hast passed over unto the divine rest, where the choir of martyrs ever rejoices.

*Glory, both now, of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for possessing Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors and crushed the powerless audacities of the demons: through their supplications, save our souls.

*The Canon, whose acrostic is: I honor the sufferings of Timothy and Maura. A composition of Joseph.[^15] Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the deep on foot as if upon dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh engulfed, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!*

*Troparion:*
The sacred and honorable solemnity of the holy sufferers, O sacred people, let us sacredly honor, that through their *Troparion:* [^16]
parent [of ungodliness], raises banishments against thee.

*Troparion:*
Immeasurable were the tribulations which thou didst endure, O blessed one, contending for piety, and radiant likewise are thy crowns: for thou wast as adamant unto those who beat thee, and unto thyself as a magnet thou didst draw God.

*Troparion:*
Strange dogmas and things foreign to the Church of Christ didst thou banish, theologizing the Trinity in Hypostases and Unity in Godhead, unto Whom thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
In time didst thou, who alone knewest no wedlock, give birth in the flesh to the timeless Word Who was formerly bodiless, O Virgin. Unto Him do we sing, crying with one accord: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
O glorious adornment of hierarchs, Athanasius, pillar of light and confirmation of the Church, champion of the Trinity, golden trumpet of theology, certain lawgiver of the monastic life, thou feastest with us, perfecting priests, and bringing the faithful people unto Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Named after immortality wast thou truly, for by active discourse thou didst excel: surpassing those of moral word by the certainty of thy life, thou didst attain the summit in both. Therefore, having shone forth with changeless virtue, thou hast acquired immortal memory unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Most radiantly didst thou display the hierarchy, O god-receptive initiate of heavenly mysteries; by thy noble sufferings thou didst banish delusion through thy frequent banishments for the sake of the Trinity, Whom preaching thou criest: Bow down before the Father, bless the Son, hymn the Spirit unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
A receptacle of the Unwaning Light wast thou, O all-pure one: for He became light unto those in darkness and shadow, enlightening all things with the grace of the knowledge of God; Whom we the faithful unceasingly sing unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Heaven was struck with awe at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, the Mother of God.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shine forth in thy life like lightning, and all thy utterance poured forth like thunder, O father, upon those whom the sun surveys: for from heaven above thou truly hadst inspiration. Therefore the grave of oblivion has not consumed the heralds of thy memory, O Athanasius.

*Troparion:*
Now illuminated most clearly by the thrice-sunlike and tri-radiant Light, thou revealest a dawn-like radiance unto those who sing to thee, shedding it from above in sunlike manner as through a mirror: and do thou grant, O blessed one, whatever is asked beyond our merit, whether great or small.

*Troparion:*
As we hymn thee, we earnestly pray thee: pray for the Church, to grant victories by the Cross unto the king,[^17] and unto the Orthodox faith strength against the heresies of enemies, and unto us salvation, and peace unto the world, on thy commemoration, O Athanasius.

*Theotokion:*
O mediatress of all Christians, Mother of God, all-immaculate Virgin, as thou art merciful, deliver thy afflicted servants from tribulations and misfortunes, and heal those suffering from the wounds of cruel arrows and the sicknesses of wounds.

*Exapostilarion:*
*Podoben: "O Unchanging Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*
A golden-flowing river hast thou appeared, watering with the streams of thy teachings, O minister of the Trinity, adornment of hierarchs: for by thy teachings, O Athanasius, thou didst destroy the teaching of Arius.

*Glory, another Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Having Adorned the Sky with Stars" (Не́бо звѣзда́ми):*
Let us hymn in praise the glory of hierarchs, the nursling of Egypt, the adornment of immortality, Athanasius the Great, as the overthrower of Arius and defender of the faithful.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant Among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
*Sticheron 1:*
By thy fiery teachings, all brushwood-matter of heresies is consumed, O most glorious one, and the impious host of the disobedient is drowned in the depth of thy understandings, O venerable Athanasius; and by thy teachings the Church of the faithful is daily adorned, O blessed one, honoring thee with loud voice.

*Sticheron 2:*
From the Church of Christ thou didst drive away the noetic wolves, O all-wise one, with the rod of thy teachings, and didst fence her round about with the fortifications of words, and didst present her unto Christ immovable and unshakable; Whom implore to deliver from corruption and tribulations those who in faith celebrate thy all-honorable memory.

*Troparion:* [^18]
ye bound your souls unto Christ by the laying aside of the flesh, O ever-memorable ones.

*Theotokion:*
O all-good Lady, who didst give birth in the flesh to the all-good God, sweeten my heart that has been embittered by passions, that in faith and love I may magnify thee.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The angel made the furnace a bed of dew for the venerable children, whereas the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
The most glorious one, being burned with candles on every side, felt it not, bearing in her heart the fire of the love of Christ, and crying unto Him earnestly: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having cast away the wicked slumber from thine eyes, O Timothy, thou didst endure the blinding of thine eyes, beholding Christ with the eyes of the heart, and singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having despised earthly things, the divine martyrs inherited the dwelling on high; let them be honored with sacred hymns, singing unceasingly with us: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Virgin, who didst give birth to the all-hymned God, show those who hymn thee to be partakers of the Light, and deliver them from Gehenna and fire, and from every harm of the enemy, through thy prayers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Out of the flame didst Thou pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and with water didst Thou consume the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we supreme exalt unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Keeping the laws of the Almighty, ye did not bow down to the dominion of lawless men, nor did ye offer honor unto the deceitful demons, O valiant martyrs, heirs of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Being joined together in noble union, ye took the easy yoke of the Lord together upon your neck, O martyrs, and were numbered among the flocks of martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Having suffered upon the wood of the cross for many days, O all-lauded ones, ye depicted the honorable passion of Him Who willingly suffered for our sake: Whom we supreme exalt unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Truly a most violent death did ye endure, O holy ones, by the prolongation of pain: therefore ye have passed over unto the painless life, hymning Christ unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast adorned, O Virgin, having given birth to Christ the Lord, the most fair in beauty, Who enlightens the faithful with divine radiances: Whom we supreme exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*It is not possible for men to behold God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was made manifest unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we bless thee.*

*Troparion:*
Ye were counted worthy to behold the glory of Him Who gave Himself up out of compassion, O all-lauded ones: for ye kept His laws and became partakers of His sufferings. Therefore having come together, we piously honor you.

*Troparion:*
Ye made the measure of hours equal in number, O wise ones, nailed fast for nights and days unto Him Who drew near to the Cross out of compassion;[^19] and confessing Him Who reigns in piety over all, into His hands ye committed your spirits.

*Troparion:*
The host of angels, the blessed choir of the prophets, apostles, martyrs, and venerable ones gathered at your departure; and the Master of all crowned you, O martyrs, with crowns of victory, as He is supremely good.

*Troparion:*
For us who honor you with love and celebrate your honorable solemnity, O valiant sufferers of Christ, ask ye remission of sins, amendment of life, and deliverance from all grievous things, O wise ones.

*Theotokion:*
Enlighten me with thy light, O Virgin rejoiced by God among women, I pray thee, and deliver me from the eternal fire: that I may magnify thee, that I may glorify thee, that as is meet I may hymn thy majesty.

*Service of our Venerable Father Theodosius, Hegumen of the Monastery of the Caves, and Founder of the Cenobitic Monastic Life in the Rus' Land.*

*At Small Vespers, Stichera, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
*Sticheron 1:*
True and blameless, venerable and guileless, and holding aloof from every evil thing wast thou known to be, O blessed one, adorned with virtues in truth, and shining with piety: therefore we call thee blessed, and celebrating today thy holy and honorable falling asleep, we rejoice.

*Sticheron 2:*
The blameless and most honorable, exceedingly high pillar, and one unconquered by the craft of the enemy *Troparion:* [^20]
supplications we may be delivered from tribulations.

*Troparion:*
Endeavoring to receive crowns of sacred victory from Christ, the valiant martyrs, having manifestly chosen to die rather than to live, therefore obtained glory.

*Troparion:*
Fearing not bodily afflictions, nor being terrified of tortures, nor of the fire of the persecutors, O passion-bearing martyrs, ye manfully proclaimed Christ before the judgment seat.

*Theotokion:*
The transcendent Word, having taken essence from thy pure blood, deified us and delivered us from corruption, O pure one: therefore we the faithful ever glorify thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.*

*Troparion:*
Sacredly opening the books of God unto the people, and enlightening hearts, O warrior of Christ, Timothy, thou didst enter rejoicing into the honorable martyrdom.

*Troparion:*
Having kindled thy soul with divine longing, O martyr, thou didst draw near patiently unto the fire of tortures: therefore did the divine dew of Christ refresh thee, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thy mind being enlightened by looking steadfastly unto God, O martyr, thou didst not feel the touch of the cruel red-hot irons, O glorious Timothy.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is impassible by nature, Thy Son, O Virgin, took flesh from thy pure blood, and by His honorable Passion delivered me from passions, O pure one.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having endured manifold wounds, and received crowns from God, pray ye unto the Lord for us who celebrate your all-sacred memory, O exceedingly great Timothy and all-glorious Maura, that He may grant peace unto the city and the people: for He is the confirmation of the faithful.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Having honored God, they darkened delusion, the wise Timothy and the glorious Maura; and having endured every torture, O all-lauded ones, they shone brighter than the rays of the sun, and became fellow-ministers with the angels: whom in faith we glorify.

*Glory, both now, of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church sings in god-befitting manner, crying aloud out of a pure mind, feasting in the Lord.*

*Troparion:*
Having desired the great glory of good renown, ye were counted worthy thereof, having become children *Troparion:*
of the divine light, O great martyrs of Christ, equal in station unto the angels.

*Troparion:*
Joined unto the ranks of the saints, and having ears well-obedient unto the divine laws, O glorious one, thou didst manfully endure the boring through of thine ears, O Timothy.

*Troparion:*
Having lifted thee up upon the tree, the vain-minded weighted down thy head with a stone, O glorious Timothy; but Christ strengthened thee, adorned by the divine Passion.

*Troparion:*
Ye truly abhorred the unstable course of fleeting things, O blessed ones, and gave yourselves over unto sufferings: therefore have ye received the good things of eternity.

*Theotokion:*
O tabernacle of purity, sovereign dwelling-place of all, and fulfillment of all the prophets, O pure Virgin rejoiced by God, show mercy unto those who hymn thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate the souls of those who lovingly rise early unto Thee, I pray Thee, to know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest us out of the darkness of sin.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst lovingly obey the light-bearing words of thy betrothed, O Maura; and having cast away evil darkness, thou becamest light through martyrdom, O divinely-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having armed thyself with the Divine Spirit, O honorable Maura, thou didst go forth rejoicing unto combat with the deceitful one; and having overcome him, O martyr, thou didst receive crowns of victory.

*Troparion:*
Having confessed Thee as the One Lord Jesus, Who with Thy hand didst fashion man, O honorable one, she was shorn of the fingers of her hands, manfully enduring unjust torment.

*Theotokion:*
Calm the storm of my thoughts, the uprising of passions, and the tempest of tribulations, O Virgin rejoiced by God, and save me who hymn thy majesty.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Plenteous in Mercy!*

*Troparion:*
Spiritually winged with the hope of the Cross, O holy martyrs, ye safely traversed the abyss of tribulations by faith, and rested in the divine havens.

*Troparion:*
Having heated a cauldron, the impious cast thee into it as Thou didst confess Christ; but Thou remainedst unharmed, O Maura, being found cooled by the dew of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
By bonds, O martyrs, ye manfully and brightly loosed all entanglements of the enemy, and having loved unto the end Him Who crowned you *At Small Vespers, Stichera (continued):*
we all call blessed, and in faith we praise Theodosius the Great, who was a well-pleasing servant of Christ and of His all-pure Mother: earnestly entreating him to save our souls from tribulations.

*Sticheron 3:*
The monastic life hast thou made clear, shining forth from the Rus' land like the sun: for guarding thy body with abstinence, thou didst put to death the movements of passion by prayers and vigils. Therefore we pray thee, O all-blessed Theodosius: cease not to help us and to visit thy servants.

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^21]
Come, assembly of the faithful, merrily today! Come, all ye joyfully into the honorable church! Let us hymn the god-bearing fathers, and clap our hands at their most radiant memory, whom Christ our God has glorified! Therefore with the voice of rejoicing and a pure conscience, let us cry aloud, saying: Rejoice, earthly angel and heavenly man, O all-blessed Anthony, dwelling-place of the venerable! Rejoice, eastern gold-beaming star, O father Theodosius, beacon and shepherd of monastics! Rejoice, our boast, joy and helpers of the universe! Dwellers with the angels in the light of the Holy Trinity Who crowned you, earnestly pray to deliver us from enemies visible and invisible, and from every tribulation, who celebrate your all-festal memory with faith and love.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
*Sticheron 1:*
A radiant morning and day has thy feast appeared, enlightening the hearts of us who praise in faith thy honorable struggles, O father Theodosius.
*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*

*Sticheron 2:*
A day of salvation and a feast of gladness has appeared: let us gather together, O faithful, having cleansed our souls, to hymn the all-blessed Theodosius.
*Verse: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord, in His commandments he will have great delight.*

*Sticheron 3:*
Having found Anthony upon earth like the sun, shining with virtues, thou didst show thyself to be a most radiant star: remember unto the Lady those who hymn thee, O venerable Theodosius.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, thou who didst receive joy for the world, Christ the Giver of life, and who didst assuage, O Virgin, the grief of the first mother.

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Having been exalted in virtue, from youth having loved the monastic life, valiantly attaining unto thy desire, thou didst settle in the cave, and having adorned thy life with fasting and radiance, in prayers thou didst abide as one bodiless. Having shone forth in the Rus' land like a radiant luminary, O father Theodosius, entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, both now, of the Feast.*

*At Great Vespers, we sing: Blessed is the man.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 5:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "O Venerable Father" (Преподо́бне ѡ҆́тче):*
*Sticheron 1:*
O venerable father, god-bearing Theodosius, having greatly struggled in temporal life by hymns, fasting, and vigil, thou wast an example unto thy disciples; and now thou dost exult with the bodiless hosts, unceasingly glorifying Christ, the divine Word and Deliverer from God, Who bowed His head upon the Cross and delivered the human race from deception: Him entreat, unto Him pray, O venerable one, to grant peace and great mercy unto the universe.

*Sticheron 2:*
O venerable father, god-bearing Theodosius, truly wast thou counted worthy of the divine life, having found it by purity and fasting: for from them thou hast passed over unto true life, unceasingly glorifying with the bodiless hosts Christ, the King of all, Who ineffably took flesh from the Virgin, overthrew Hades, and raised the dead from the ages: Him entreat, unto Him pray, O venerable one, to grant peace and great mercy unto the universe.

*Sticheron 3:*
O venerable father, god-bearing Theodosius, having found as thou didst desire by thy pure soul: for the grace of the All-Holy Spirit took up His abode in thee as all-pure light, by Whose visitation being radiantly illumined, unceasingly glorifying Christ, the one God in two essences, Who suffered in the flesh upon the Cross and remained impassible in His Godhead: Him entreat, unto Him pray, O venerable one, to grant peace and great mercy unto the universe.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Come, let us all gather together at the divine commemoration of our father Theodosius: for having received from youth the calling from on high, he was named by the priest the God-given gift. Therefore was he a teacher of the right faith unto the Christ-loving princes, a firm defense unto the nobles, a compassionate father unto orphans, a fervent protection unto widows, consolation unto the sorrowful, a treasure unto the poor, a ladder leading the monastic choir up unto the heavenly height, and simply unto those who had recourse unto him he became a fountain of ever-flowing water [^22]
having become a fountain of ever-flowing water unto those who had recourse unto him, he unceasingly prays that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And three Readings.*

*The Reading is from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wis 5:15–6:3].*
The righteous live for evermore; their reward also is with the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive a kingdom of majesty, and a crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take to Him His jealousy for complete armor, and make the creation His weapon for vengeance upon the enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall take true judgment instead of a helmet. He shall take holiness for an invincible shield; He shall sharpen His severe wrath for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the foolish. Right-aiming shafts of lightnings shall go forth; and as from a well-drawn bow of clouds they shall fly unto the mark. And as from a stone-bow shall hailstones full of fury be cast out. The water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall drown them relentlessly. Yea, the spirit of power shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall scatter them. And iniquity shall lay waste the whole earth, and evildoing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O kings, and understand; learn, ye judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and that boast yourselves in the throngs of nations. For power is given you from the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High.

*The Reading is from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wis 3:1–9].*
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and there shall no torment touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die: and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction: but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly benefited: for God proved them, and found them worthy of Himself. As gold in the furnace has He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign in them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth: and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him: for grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and visitation in His elect.

*The Reading is from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wis 4:7–15].*
Though the righteous happen to die early, yet shall he be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor that is measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him: so that living among sinners he was translated. Yea, speedily was he taken away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of naughtiness does obscure things that are honest; and the wandering of concupiscence does undermine the simple mind. He, being made perfect in a short time, fulfilled a long time: for his soul pleased the Lord: therefore hastened He to take him away from among the wicked. This the people saw, and understood it not, neither laid they up this in their minds: that His grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and that He has respect unto His elect.

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 1:*
*Sticheron 1:*
Come today, ye lovers of the feasts, having gathered a choir let us piously celebrate the all-glorious and honorable memory of the great father, the universal luminary, and let us gloriously honor him, saying: Rejoice, O Theodosius, kinsman of the venerable and the righteous. Rejoice, thou who didst gather the most radiant host of honorable fathers; rejoice, faithful servant and steward of the monastery of the Mother of God,[^23] for whom thou didst erect an excellent temple unto the glory of God. Wherein singing, we pray thee: Entreat that we may be delivered from every temptation and danger, and from the assault of barbarians, and that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, Tone 2:*
*Sticheron 2:*
O disciple of the Savior, O god-wise father Theodosius, divinely moved harp, divinely speaking mouth, spiritual commander, good shepherd, boast of the venerable: thou didst seek God, and wast illumined by His god-beaming radiances; thou didst converse with God by unceasing purifications and illuminations; having illumined thy mind with fountains of tears, thou wast shown forth, O all-blessed one, as an all-radiant and fiery pillar: the boast of monastics, the glory of the fathers, and the servant of the Trinity: and now preserve us all by thy mediation.

*Sticheron 3:*
O venerable father Theodosius, thou didst show compassion unto those melting in the heat of thirst; thou didst recompense in a god-befitting manner, ruling by signs, as an excellent servant, imitating thy Master. Thou didst multiply the wine, and by thy command didst fill empty granaries *Sticheron 3 (continued):*
with flour; and the vessel lacking in honey thou didst fill to overflowing; having known unrevealed things, thou didst adorn the Church, showing forth martyr-like sufferings; thou didst grant a multitude of healings, thou didst love the poor; having had compassion on men by thy customs; and thou didst console thy belly with bread and water, in the flesh imitating the life of the bodiless ones. Therefore offering glory unto thee, we pray: Leave us not, preserving thy servants by thy prayers.

*Sticheron 4:*[^24]
Two most radiant luminaries have shone forth in Rus', Anthony and Theodosius; for the one, through his divine word, asked heavenly dew upon the earth, and brought down fire from heaven, and clearly proclaimed future things, having received prophetic grace; while the other asked rain from heaven, and rebuked the prince concerning brother-hatred and unjust lust for power; and he saw the Lord in person; and in the air like Elijah, in fiery light, he was seen over the customs-house, lifting up his hands and offering prayers unto God. And now exulting with the Angels, they pray for us, that with them we may be counted worthy of the true Light, and that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Having preserved that which is according to the image unharmed, and having ascetically set the ruling mind over the destructive passions, thou didst ascend unto that which is according to the likeness, as far as is possible; for having valiantly forced nature, thou didst hasten to subject the worse unto the better, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit. Therefore thou wast shown forth as the summit of monastics, a dweller in the wilderness, an instructor of those who run well, a most certain rule of virtue. And now in the heavens, the mirrors being done away, O our father Theodosius, thou purely beholdest the Holy Trinity, praying unceasingly for us who honor thee with faith and love.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
*Sticheron 1:*
O god-wise father Theodosius, from the womb thou didst draw thy mind near unto God; thou didst despise all things for the ruling King, offering unto Him worthy fruits from thy labors, O venerable one, crowned with virtuous crowns. Therefore thou didst find a wealth of good things, O thou who art worthy of wonder.

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*

*Sticheron 2:*
Thou art the instructor of monastics, and the most radiant star of the Rus' land, illuminating with wonders all this country *Sticheron 2 (continued):*
for God chose thee from youth, O father, as a shepherd of rational sheep. Therefore the choir of the venerable received thee; with them, we pray thee, entreat that remission of sins be granted unto thy flock.

*Verse: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord, in His commandments he will have great delight.*

*Sticheron 3:*[^25]
Rejoice, O all-hymned Virgin, who by a pillar of fire didst show forth the pasture where the divine flock was to be pastured; and Theodosius immediately erected that church, most exceedingly fair and all-marvelous unto thee, wherein we all celebrate thy falling asleep, O all-pure Theotokos, as we faithful stand around the shrine of thy favorite.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
We honor thee, O our father Theodosius, as the instructor of a multitude of monastics: for by thy path we have learned truly to walk rightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ and didst confound the power of the enemy, companion of the Angels, fellow-partaker with the venerable and the righteous: with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, of the Feast.*

*Troparion of the Saint, twice. Theotokion: O Virgin Birthgiver of God: once.*

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice. Glory, Troparion of the Saint, Tone 8 (written at Small Vespers). Both now, of the Feast.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Thou didst follow the calling of thy Lord: forsaking the world and all the fair things of the world, O all-blessed Theodosius, thou didst endure bodily afflictions, being earnestly strengthened in the cave, in a place grievous and narrow, and didst valiantly drive away the hosts of demons. Therefore we ever faithfully praise thy memory with hymns.

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
With streams of tears thou didst richly water thy soul, O wise father, and hast reaped the fruitful ear of virtues; and having become a shepherd unto the sheep, O venerable one, thou didst nourish them upon the grass of temperance. Therefore being released from life gloriously, thou wast united unto the choirs of the fathers, O Theodosius. Therefore we cry unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. (Twice)

*Glory, both now, of the Feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having been sanctified from the womb in truth, O Theodosius *Sessional Hymn (continued):*
all-blessed one, thou didst live upon earth like an Angel: for having put to death the flesh, and worthily emulating the life of Anthony in the cave, thou wast shown forth, O blessed one, as a favorite of the Trinity. Therefore unto the emulation of thy divine life thou didst gather choirs of monastics, O glorious one: O our god-bearing father, pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love. (Twice)

*Glory, both now, of the Feast.*

*The Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? Let every breath.*

*Gospel according to Luke, composite pericope 124 [Luke 6:17–23].*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Ascending upon the divinely flowing chariot to heaven, by virtues thou didst reach the utmost city through fasting. Going forth from earth unto the sublime beauties of Jerusalem, and worthily receiving honor for thy afflictions and labors, thou rejoicest with the heavenly ranks, O all-blessed one, having become an heir of eternal good things and a citizen of the Kingdom: but pray, O god-bearing Theodosius, unto the Savior of all, to give peace to the world and to save our souls.

*The Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos to 6; and two Canons of the Saint to 8. Tone 8.*

*First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The wonder-working rod of Moses, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, once drowned the chariot-driving Pharaoh, but saved the fugitive pedestrian Israel, who sang a song unto God.*

*Troparion:*
Having acquired a god-pleasing struggle, O father Theodosius, thou wast shown forth a beholder of God. Therefore pray unto God, Whom thou didst please, to drive away the darkness of my ignorance, and to inspire a blessed word to hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
From thy mother's womb thou didst cleave unto God, O venerable one, like the most wise Paul and the Prophet Jeremiah of old. Therefore divine grace by the lips of the priest called thee, O Theodosius, the servant of God.[^26]

*Troparion:*
Having given ear from youth unto the commandments of the Savior, O wise one, accounting this life as nothing, desiring to bear the Cross upon thy shoulders, thou didst hate all earthly things, looking unto heavenly things, O Theodosius.

*Theotokion:*
The Word of God, taking flesh from thy all-pure womb, O all-pure Virgin, illumined unto all the love of divine knowledge, and alone counted men worthy of the angelic choir and led them up unto the heavens.

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the deep on foot as if upon dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh engulfed, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the most radiant rays of God, O father Theodosius, illumine my mind by thy prayers, that I may radiantly hymn thy divine memory.

*Troparion:*
Unto God Who was given up didst thou offer thyself from youth, from Whom thou didst receive rich grace of wonders: therefore being an incorruptible treasure unto thy flock.

*Troparion:*[^27]
A new Abraham didst thou appear unto us, offering sacrifice unto God: for the Lamb that truly takes away the sins of the whole world, baking the prosphora loaves with thy pure hands.

*Theotokion:*
The Word of God, Who exists before the morning star, appeared unto us as man, being incarnate for our sake of the all-pure Virgin; and having come forth from her, He preserved her unharmed.

*Katavasia of the Feast.*

*Ode 3. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Supreme Fashioner of the heavenly circle and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Firm Foundation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*[^28]
Thy divine and blessed soul being enriched with incorruptible and spiritual gifts, O holy one, thou didst desire to see the Sepulchre of the Lord; but from on high thou wast restrained by the divine providence of God.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to become a perfect, true, and blameless sacrifice, O blessed one, unto the Lamb Who takes away the sins of the whole world by His all-pure Blood, thou didst offer the unbloody sacrifice unto Him.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst become an acquirer of monastic rules from the years of youth, O father, and a master over thy mind; fleeing the worldly abyss, thou didst receive instruction from the divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, thou who alone didst bear the Lord of all; rejoice, thou who didst proclaim joy unto men; rejoice, tabernacle and unquarried mountain: the confirmation of the faithful, O only all-hymned one.

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.*

*Troparion:*
Having given ear unto the heard voice, Theodosius ran into the church, desiring to take the Cross of the Lord upon his shoulders: having forsaken all attachment unto the world, he followed Him.

Having examined thee, thy mother who bore thee commanded thee to strip off thy garment; and she saw a fearful sight, thy loins streaming with blood from the iron belt.[^29]

*Troparion:*
Greatly desiring to see the Sepulchre of the Lord, O blessed Theodosius, but being restrained by the Master's providence, thou wast a fellow-converser with the venerable Anthony.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding didst thou conceive, O all-hymned Virgin: for alone from the ages hast thou given birth unto the Creator, God, and Maker of all, the Word of God.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. Then, Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4:*
Beyond human understanding wast thou shown forth, O venerable one, upon earth: standing like a holy divine garden in the midst of the house of the Lord, thou yieldest the fruit of thy afflictions unto thy disciples; for from thy lips thou didst cause the grace of the Spirit to grow, having become an example of struggles and a rule unto those who love the Lord. Whom do thou now cease not to entreat, that they may be saved who hymn thee.

*Glory, both now, of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!*

*Troparion:*
The grace of the Spirit grew within thee, O father, when she who bore thee saw thee, weeping and mourning bitterly: thou didst stand shedding streams of tears, and didst remain an immovable and unshakeable pillar.

*Troparion:*
Through fierce men the all-wicked enemy sought to tear thee away, O Theodosius, from the most fair nest desired by thee; but thou didst account him as nothing, driving away his ranks by thy prayers, and singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Sprinkling thyself with spiritual rain, and being watered from the living fountain, and by the tongue of thy instructor Anthony, thou didst sprout as a divine ear of grain, and dost nourish multitudes, having truly become an inexhaustible granary like Joseph's.

*Theotokion:*
Who is able worthily to tell the depth of thy wonders, O pure one? For through thee the whole world, offering praise unto thee, is shown mercy, and is delivered from grievous misfortunes, and from diverse evils, enemies, and temptations.

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church divinely sings, crying out from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.*

When thy mother learned, O father, that thou wast living with the blessed Anthony in the cave: she came and wept, but having consoled her, thou didst persuade her to flee the evils of the world.[^30]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast truly an innocent priest of God, O holy one, offering unbloody sacrifices, and singing with pure hymns unto the Lord, ever keeping festival.

*Troparion:*
Ask remission of sins for us, O servant and defender of God, we thy flock cry unto thee, and preserve those who hymn thee from the snares of the enemy.

*Theotokion:*
The Word of God, Who shone forth from the Father, sanctified thy all-pure womb: and for the sake of His abundant goodness delivered the human race from the ancient curse.

*Ode 5. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.*

*Troparion:*
Thy righteous deed shone forth through virtuous struggles, O father: for having made spacious the dwelling that was formerly humble, thou didst bring many worshippers unto God, Who grants them every virtue through thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
By the radiant beams of thy struggles, a multitude of demons was driven away from thy dwelling: for the grace of the Spirit of the Creator of all ministered unto thee, and showed thee forth glorious, O Theodosius.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst hymn the compassionate Savior in the midst of the Church. Therefore He sent His rich compassions unto thee: for He resolved the sorrow of want, pouring forth ever-flowing gifts unto thy flock.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast higher than the heavenly hosts, O thou full of God's grace, who didst conceive the Word Who made all things by a word, and didst bear Him Who was ineffably begotten of the Father before the ages.

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of those who lovingly wake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin.*

*Troparion:*
A pure vessel adorned with the flowers of virtues art thou, O father Theodosius: from which thou pourest forth a fountain of instruction unto the flock, sweeter than honey and the honeycomb.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst put to death the carnal nature by virtuous struggles, O wise father Theodosius. Therefore Christ showed thee forth as shepherd, where He formerly chose the pasture for the purest Virgin.

Thou wast an instructor unto monastics, and a shepherd unto the sheep being pastured on the pasture of the Mother of God: therefore God grants thee double joy, O Theodosius, through the prayers of the pure Mother of God.

*Theotokion:*
Finding thee a holy house, He Who dwells in the heavens willingly settled in thy womb. Therefore with thankful voices we ever hymn thy all-pure childbearing, as we stand in thy house.

*Ode 6. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my afflictions, for my soul is full of evils, and my life draws near unto Hades; and I pray as Jonah: Lead me up from corruption, O God!*

*Troparion:*
By prayers and vigil, and unceasing psalmody, thou didst make thy soul a divine paradise, having clearly sprouted the venerable Life-bearing Tree, the Savior and Lord.

*Troparion:*[^31]
Thou didst choose secret humility, imitating the Master's poverty, O blessed one: of thine own will walking the road, by the command of a lazy servant thou didst undertake to drive the wagon to the divine place, where being bowed down to, thou wast recognized by those who met thee.

*Troparion:*
Imitating Him Who washed the feet of His disciples, O god-wise one, thou didst choose to draw water and carry it upon thy shoulder, and carrying wood with thine own hands and chopping it, thou didst show thyself to monastics as the initiator of labors.

*Theotokion:*
The crafty enemy was slain through thee, O Theotokos, who formerly destroyed the whole creation by corruption; and we who were dead have ascended unto life through thee: for in truth thou didst give birth unto Life, the Lord of all.

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!*

*Troparion:*
Having erected the true church of the Mother of God, wherein thou didst gather a choir of a multitude of monastics who lovingly hymn thy honorable memory.

*Troparion:*
Unto those who flee unto thy protection with faith, thou pourest forth a fountain of healings unto all, and pourest out holiness, O venerable one, and illuminest hearts, driving away demonic deception.

*Troparion:*
Lift up thy hands unto Christ God, O venerable one, and preserve thy flock, O father, which thou hast radiantly gathered by the Spirit, through thy prayers, who hymn thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice O all-hymned one, who didst bear the all-hymned God of all: show those who hymn thee to be partakers of the Light, O pure one, and deliver them from the eternal fire.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Today let us honor the star of Rus', which shone forth from the East and came unto the West: for he enriched all this land with wonders and goodness, and all of us, by the creation and grace of the monastic rule, the blessed Theodosius.

*Ikos:*
Who is able to recount thy struggles, O father? Or who shall number the multitudes of thy labors and marvelous wonders? For while yet in the flesh, thou didst see the bodiless one conversing with thee in bodily form, and bringing thee a gift, gold sent by God.[^32] Beyond man wast thou shown forth, through humility and a gentle disposition, and filled with spiritual wisdom: therefore receiving the Spirit within thyself, thou wast seen in light, shining like the sun. And me who hymn thee, illumine by thy radiance, O venerable one of the monastic rule, divine Theodosius.

*Ode 7. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*The Children coming out of Judea once in Babylon trampled the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
Unto thy prayers, O blessed one, whosoever flees with faith shall never be put to shame: having received the grace of his petition, O father, he returns exulting, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a father and defender unto orphans, hope unto widows, and a resurrection unto all the sick, O father. Therefore we sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
An ornament unto monastics and a path of salvation unto men wast thou: for having enriched all with thy words of divine mysteries, thou wast granted to say: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Taking flesh from the virginal womb, O Christ, Thou hast appeared for our salvation. Therefore knowing Thy Mother to be the Theotokos, with thanksgiving we sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Angel made the furnace pour down dew upon the holy Children, but the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
The grace of God which overshadowed thee by the Spirit was poured out in thee, illumining thy heart, O father, which grace taught thee to sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The revelations of thy words have gone forth into all lands, revealing the dispensations of corruption unto those who ever sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The manifold grace of the Spirit rested upon thee, O father, and showed forth most glorious wonders unto those who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Fleeing beneath thy protection, O Sovereign Lady, we are delivered from cruel things; for having thee as our hope, all we faithful pray: Despise not thy servants, O Virgin Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 8. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Foreseeing things as a divine prophet, O all-blessed Theodosius, when thou wast revealed, by the Spirit thou didst command him who had brought the Gospel manuscript unto thee to carry it forth; and he cried unto thee, confessing with a pure mind the sacrifice unto the all-pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*[^33]
The pious prince who held this land, rushing unto thy dwelling as a hart unto the springs of water, that he might obtain imperishable food, desired indeed to sup with thee, O blessed one; but when the cellarer cried out in want, at thy command thy entire vessel was filled with mead.

*Troparion:*
The angelic powers hymned thy struggles, O Theodosius, and the choir of the venerable praised thy life, the radiant and adorned rejoicing of monastics. Therefore now also meeting him, they rejoiced, singing in concord: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, do I set forth as the guardian and unconquerable defender of my life; be a helper unto me from perils and afflictions, delivering thy unworthy servant who sings with love: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, purely supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable, and with water Thou didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous; for Thou doest all things, O Christ Heirmos (continued):*
*...by Thy sole willing: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages.*

*Troparion:*
The choir of thy disciples hymns Christ with their instructor: standing round about thy shrine, having received thy labors worthy of God, Whom we supremely exalt unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, thou didst follow Christ God Who was crucified, having mortified the body by many labors and sicknesses; thou didst acquire true faith and love, renouncing the world and loving Christ alone.

*Troparion:*
The shrine of thy relics pours forth health by divine power, O father Theodosius, and heals the passions of soul and body; therefore worthily we hymn thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Unto thee do we now proclaim the voice of Gabriel: Rejoice, O most blessed Sovereign Lady, full of God's grace, through whom we have been delivered from the ancient curse; for thou art the mediatress of the salvation of all.

*Ode 9. First Canon of the Saint. Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, being made man from a virginal womb. Therefore, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, we faithful magnify thee.*

*Troparion:*
Thy memory hath shone forth unto us today like the sun, O venerable one: kissing thy shrine, we rejoice, and standing round about it as a divine ark, with faith we sing of thy dormition with the ranks on high, O Theodosius.

*Troparion:*[^34]
Rejoice, O land of Rus', having received from the Lord an unstealable treasure, a great helper, the godly-blessed and fervent defender Theodosius; and rejoice thou also, O capital city of Rus', with the choir of ascetics!

*Troparion:*
A festive assembly is gathered today at thy memory to hymn the Lord, Who granted thee the good struggles which thou didst endure. Therefore pray unto Him, O Christ, that thy flock which thou hast acquired, O wise one, may be delivered from enemy stumbling-blocks through thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Save me, O only Deliverer, Who didst receive the fervent repentance of the harlot and the prodigal, and the sighing of the publican, O Savior; and be merciful, O Christ, unto me the prodigal, through the prayers of Thy pleasing servant, driving away the multitude of my sins.

*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Savior Who wast born, having preserved her who bore Thee without corruption after childbearing: when Thou shalt sit to judge my deeds, overlooking my sins and iniquities, being sinless and merciful, as the Lover of mankind.

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 9.*

*Second Canon of the Saint. Tone 6. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate appeared unto men: Whom magnifying with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.*

*Troparion:*
O father, worthy shepherd of shepherds: vouchsafe to illumine with virtues these children whom thou hast gathered by faith; and pray, O venerable one, that forgiveness of sins and correction of life may be granted unto us.

*Troparion:*
Having clearly lived with the holy fathers, thou wast a servant of the all-pure Virgin, O blessed one, and an exceptional shepherd of monastics, being truly a pillar of orthodoxy, the hope and confidence of the faithful who sing in the house of the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Having overthrown the snares of the enemy by the power of the Almighty, thou didst conquer them and put their legions to flight, granting health unto the sick; therefore rejoicing, we ever honor thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably conceived Him Who shone forth from the Father without a mother, thou didst bear Him, O Mother of God, all-hymned pure Virgin: pray, O most holy one, unto thy Son and our God for thy servants.

*Exapostilarion. Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жє́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Having received the light of the consubstantial Trinity in thy heart, O Theodosius, father of fathers: thou didst enlighten the pious, being strengthened in the cave with the great Anthony, one of the same kin with the angels; with them, remember those who hymn thee with faith. (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Being completely wounded with faith and the love of Christ, O blessed one: following Him, thou didst renounce the world, thy homeland, and thy lineage; running unto the venerable Anthony, from whom thou wast instructed in ineffable things which ear hath not heard, and which entered into thy heart; with him we sacredly hymn thee, honoring thee, O Theodosius. (Twice)

*Sticheron:*
