Having desired a spotless life, as well as patience, love, and humility, all-night standing, and abstinence, by which thou didst mortify the lusts of the flesh: therefore having received divine attainment, thou rejoicest together with the choirs of the venerable; forget not, O blessed one, to ask remission of sins for those who worthily hymn thee.

*Sticheron:*[^35]
When the author of evil, the enemy, brought robbers against thy flock, O venerable one, desiring to destroy those who were in it: yet seeing the church suspended high in the air they departed, fearing the grace given unto thee from God, O venerable one; they heard a voice in the church, the sound of ineffable angelic singing; rejoicing with them now, remember us who with faith hymn thy light-bearing and honorable memory.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O venerable father, the sound of thy right deeds hath gone forth into all the earth; therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors, thou didst destroy the legions of demons, and didst attain unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate; having boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for our souls.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Great Doxology, and the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Saint, Ode 3 and 6, from both canons.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.* [LXX Ps. 115:6]  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?* [LXX Ps. 115:3]  

*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 334.*[^36]  
*Alleluia, Tone 2:*
*Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.* [LXX Ps. 131:9]  
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 47.*  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.* [LXX Ps. 111:6]  

## May 4. Holy Venerable-Martyr Pelagia

*Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When by the divine nod thou wast catechized by divine words, O blessed one, like a glorious, fertile earth thou didst receive the spiritual seed into the furrows of thy heart; and thou didst sprout a hundredfold ear of grain, which delights the hearts of all who faithfully honor thee with grace, O martyr, truly Pelagia!

*Sticheron:*
Having sought the beauty of the only Lover, Christ, Thou didst pass by the beauties of the world; Thou didst hasten unto the ever-flowing fountains of holy baptism, and having been filled with them, Thou wast immediately kindled with a thirst for suffering; therefore having zealously entered into the exceedingly red-hot vessel, Thou wast vouchsafed a divine end.

*Sticheron:*[^37]
A chosen and pure bride, thou wast a bride unto Him Who was born of her that knew not man; having first despised the temporal bridegroom, O glorious one, having abhorred the earthly purple robes by the uprightness of thy mind: therefore thou hast now received eternal glory and the garment of incorruption, O martyr Pelagia!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Pelagia, O Jesus, cries with a great voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering, and am crucified and buried with Thy baptism, and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; but as an unblemished sacrifice, receive me who with love have sacrificed myself unto Thee. By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: I weave a true praise for Pelagia.*[^38]  
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the Red Sea deep with dry feet of old, Israel by the cross-shaped hands of Moses conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*

*Troparion:*
As I sail the deep of thy wonders, grant me now spiritual coolness, O all-praised martyr of Christ, Pelagia; and guide me unto the calm haven of thy praises.

*Troparion:*
Having from infancy foresightedly dedicated thyself unto thy Creator, O honorable one, thou didst cast away the love of corruptible things, O all-glorious Pelagia; and courageously by divine power thou didst trample down the passions.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to receive the bath of divine baptism, O glorious one, having found the hierarch of Christ, Pelagia, thou didst run unto him, and having partaken of it, O martyr, thou wast filled with divine light.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast holier than the holy, O pure Virgin, having borne upon thine arms Christ, the Holy of holies, Who by divine powers holds all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!*

*Troparion:*
Like good earth, having received the rational seeds, O all-wise martyr of Christ, thou didst richly cause them to sprout. (Twice)

*Troparion:*
Thou didst guide thyself, as chosen, unto the Sovereign Master and God, rejoicing, O glorious martyr of Christ, being strengthened by divine teachings.

*Theotokion:*
O pure one, thou hast raised up my fallen image, having manifestly given birth unto the Author of a resurrection beyond nature.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Having despised temporal things, and become a partaker of heavenly good things, receiving a crown because of suffering, O all-honorable Pelagia: as a gift thou didst offer streams of blood unto Christ the Master; pray unto Him to deliver us from afflictions who honor thy memory!

*Then, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*
Having kindled thy soul with the love of Christ, thou didst enter courageously into the exceedingly kindled cauldron, O godly-wise Pelagia, and becamest a sweet-smelling sacrifice unto thy Master. Therefore celebrating thy all-holy memory today, we hymn thee.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*[^39]
Clothed in a radiant purple robe by the most glorious bath of regeneration, thou didst strip off all the fair worldly purple robes, O all-honorable martyr Pelagia.

*Troparion:*
By the desire for heavenly beauties, O passion-bearer, having darkened the desire for earthly things: therefore thou wast winged, crying and saying unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Leaving the defiled faith, thou didst wisely run unto Christ, O all-glorious martyr Pelagia, having trampled down every deception of idols, O accepted of God!

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing wedlock thou didst give birth, O Virgin, and after childbearing thou wast shown to be virgin again; therefore with unceasing voices we cry unto thee with unwavering faith: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, O Lord, my light, hast come into the world, the holy Light, turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast able to destroy the fierce rage of the enemy, O all-praised Pelagia, being strengthened by the Master's cross and grace. (Twice)

*Troparion:*
Now thou rejoicest truly, having been united with thy Bridegroom, O most radiant one, by martyrdom, having the lamp of thy virginity.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord dwelt in thee, O Mother of God, edifying man who had fallen into corruption by the serpent's deceit.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.*

*Troparion:*
Having lawfully betrothed thyself unto Christ, thou didst finish the course of martyrdom; and calling upon thy Bridegroom, thou wast established in His calm haven. (Twice)

*Troparion:*
A most fair crown of righteousness was woven for thy head, O godly-wise Pelagia, crowned martyr: for thou didst keep the faith blamelessly even unto the end.

*Theotokion:*
The crooked serpent formerly poured out venom into the ears of Eve; but thou alone hast shaken this off, O Mother of God, having given birth unto Him Who destroyed it.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned more by love of piety than by flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*[^40]
In the manner of a furnace, in a hidden instrument, O martyr, kindled by divine love of Christ, enduring thou didst cry out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord! (Twice)

*Troparion:*
He Who clothes the heaven with clouds, O glorious martyr, enlightened thee as thou sangest, stripped of divine garments: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O sanctified divine dwelling of the Most High! For through thee joy was given, O Birthgiver of God, unto those who cry: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Daniel, stretching forth his hands, shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and the children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*
As a martyr, as truly unfeigned of Christ, O all-praised one, thou didst bless His power, godly-wisely teaching all, and drawing people unto piety who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! (Twice)

*Troparion:*
Having betrothed thyself unto Christ by faith, thou didst swallow up the entire assault of the tormentor, pouring forth a deep of wisdom, O all-honorable Pelagia, and didst adorn thyself with three crowns, O martyr, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
We faithful confess the Son without beginning from the Father, and the Holy Spirit, saying that from thee, O all-immaculate one, the Word took flesh, co-beginningless and only-begotten unto the Father and the divine Spirit; and we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A cornerstone was cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, which art thou, O Virgin: even Christ, Who hath united the disparate natures. Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God!*

*Troparion:*
A word cannot express thy praises, O Pelagia, for thou pourest forth a deep of wonders by the power and grace of thy Bridegroom: Whom pray now to save all who hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
Standing before the tribunal, O bride and crowned martyr Pelagia, thou wast vouchsafed to stand before the ultimate desire, and didst receive the food of eternal delight.

*Troparion:*
Entreat thy Bridegroom to grant peace unto the world, O godly-wise martyr Pelagia: to preserve the Church from misfortunes, and to enlighten with grace those who hymn thee!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be virgin and mother beyond nature, O Birthgiver of God, having conceived the Father's Word, and given birth unto Him incarnate: by Whom being saved now, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God!

## May 5. Holy and Glorious Martyr Irene

*Stichera, Tone 4:*[^41]
*Tone 4, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*
Radiantly clothed in a purple robe from thine own blood, O all-honorable one, thou didst ascend unto the King, the Creator of all, and the Lord, Whom thou didst desire, from Whom thou didst truly receive a crown of victory; with Whom thou reignest now, as a virgin and much-suffering martyr.

*Sticheron:*
The assault of burning fire thou didst endure, O all-praised one, and all tearings of the flesh, looking unto the rewards of that world, O all-honorable one: which the only Jesus prepared for those who love Him and fervently desire Him as the most fair Bridegroom of souls.

*Sticheron:*
Delighting in the love of Christ, thou didst hate the pagan gods and soulless carved idols, O glorious Irene; most manifestly thou didst set thyself forth as a pillar of the knowledge of God unto those who behold thee, having suffered, finished the course, and kept the faith blameless.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Irene cries with a great voice, O Jesus: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering, and am crucified and buried with Thy baptism, and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; but as an unblemished sacrifice, receive me who with love have sacrificed myself unto Thee. By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: Shield me by thy prayers, O martyr Irene.*[^42]  
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou didst lead the people out from Egyptian bondage, and didst cover Pharaoh's chariots and power.*

*Troparion:*
Being the crown of passion-bearers, O Christ, by her prayers grant peace unto the world, and unfading glory unto those who hymn Thee!

*Troparion:*
Clearly shining with virginity, adorned with the beauty of thy martyrdom, O renowned Irene, thou standest before thy Bridegroom.

*Troparion:*
Pray thy Bridegroom to be merciful unto the churches, O godly-wise Irene, and to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thee.

*Theotokion:*
The Word of God having become flesh, and being as we are, O holy Virgin, thou didst ineffably give birth unto Him, beyond word and understanding.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with power: therefore my heart hath been established in the Lord.*

*Troparion:*
Perceiving the myrrh that was poured forth, and having received by grace Him Who filled the universe with sweet fragrance, thou didst take wing unto His desire, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Thou hadst God as a helper in thy struggles, and as a defender, O all-wise one: therefore being crowned, thou didst conquer the tormentor.

*Troparion:*
A pillar of virtues unshaken, and a dwelling of piety didst thou become, and a receptacle of courage and chastity, O all-praised one.

*Theotokion:*
Death ran unto thee, O holy one, but was shattered against Him Who was born of thee, O all-immaculate one: therefore we name thee the beginning of immortality.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Adorned with the beauties of virginity, O virgin Irene, by martyrdom thou wast most fair, stained with thy blood that flowed forth, having cast down the deception of godlessness; therefore thou didst receive the honors of victory from the hand of thy Creator.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Thou Hast Risen" (Воскре́слъ є҆сѝ):*
Great peace accrued unto thee, O martyr, having been released from the flesh after many sicknesses; rejoicing, He received thee on high in the heavens, all-enlightened and radiant with suffering, and led thee rejoicing into the divine bridal chamber.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Preaching Thine appearance upon earth, O Christ God, the prophet cried with rejoicing at Thy coming: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*[^43]
He Who grants peace unto thee, seeing that for the sake of peace thou didst venerate Him, O thou named for peace, filled thee with manly understanding and strength, O martyr.

*Troparion:*
With strong zeal having surpassed womanly weakness, O all-blessed one, firmly thou didst rebuke those who serve deceit, O martyr.

*Troparion:*
Showing forth divine patience, thou didst cast down the audacities of the tormentors, crying unto thy Bridegroom: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
With unceasing hymnody and supplications, thou didst hymn the Good One, O godly-wise one, and having ascended unto Him, thou seest ineffable glory.

*Theotokion:*
Joyfully with glad voices, O all-pure one, we who are saved through thee cry out: Rejoice, O all-honorable Virgin! Rejoice, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Send down Thy enlightenment upon us, O Lord, and loose us from the gloom of transgressions, O Good One, granting Thy peace.*

*Troparion:*
With manly understanding, O glorious one, thou didst clearly confess Christ the God of all: and by a firm word, O passion-bearer, thou didst put the adversary to shame.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst desire to follow after Christ's Passion, and by likeness fervently didst follow after it, suffering ascetically for the Master, O most rich one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst lead legions of martyrs unto Christ, O glorious one, by Thy wisdom and theology: truly mystically instructing them by the word of faith.

*Theotokion:*
Having been taught the mystery of thy conception, and of thine ineffable and divine childbearing, O Virgin, now with all piety we hymn thee, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried out* in the sea monster, praying: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst right the fall of the foremother Eve, O passion-bearer, casting down the enemy's strength, and vanquishing his arrogance by divine grace.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired strength of understanding, O Irene, thou didst oppose the tormentors with nobility of soul, and with a crown of victory wast crowned by Christ God.

*Troparion:*
By the blood of suffering, O martyr, thou didst make natural beauty even brighter, O pure one, having firmly undertaken courageous struggles, O good virgin.

*Theotokion:*[^44]
The God-seer prefigured the mysteries of thy childbearing, seeing the bush burning and unconsumed, O pure Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The children of Abraham once in Babylon trampled down the flame of the furnace, crying out with songs: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
Crowned as a martyr, standing before the throne of thy Master, preserve those who cry with faith: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
In the heavenly mansions thou wast vouchsafed to dance, O martyr, crying, O Irene worthy of praise: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Desiring to attain the beauty of thy Lover, thou didst endure the pains of martyrdom, O Irene, crying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Following the words of thy lips, in faith we call thee blessed, O Mother of God, crying unto Him born of thee: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious children in the furnace: then prefigured, but now fulfilled, it stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst suffer valiantly, O blessed Irene, and cast down the tormentor, having invincible assistance, the power of thy Master working with thee; and thou didst cry: Praise the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Wounded with youthful love, thou didst follow after thy Bridegroom, bearing His sufferings and wounds upon thy body, O martyr, and crying: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Thy patience in torments, O crowned Irene, receiving derided those who trust in vanity and godlessness, O glorious one, and in faith sang: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
The Wisdom of God, the Fashioner of all things, having dwelt in thy womb, O Mother of God, built for Himself a temple, rational and ensouled, and saved the world that cries: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and highly exalt Him unto ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve by the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding of the fruit of thy womb didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.*

*Troparion:*
Dancing around thy Lover on high, O good virgin Irene, ask peace for the world, and remission of sins for those who celebrate thy light-bearing and sacred memory with faith.

*Troparion:*
Christ placed thee within the bridal chamber as a martyr and a virgin, where thou rejoicest with the virgins and with the armies of martyrs, O all-praised crowned Irene; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Having opened the heavenly gates, the only One Who reigns over all and the Builder of righteousness received thy all-immaculate soul, O Irene, named after divine peace; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Having unashamed boldness toward thy Son, O all-immaculate one, entreat Him to send down upon the world a peaceful order, and like-mindedness unto all the churches, that we all may magnify thee.

## May 6. Holy and Righteous Job the Long-Suffering[^45]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Righteous and guileless, true, blameless, and faithful, shunning every evil deed, O Job, thou wast known, O blessed one, adorned with virtues in truth, and shining with piety. Therefore we call thee blessed, and celebrating today thy holy and honorable festival, we rejoice.

*Sticheron:*
Adorned with the wealth of piety as with a costly purple robe, O blameless Job, clothed with mercy and righteousness as with a divine crown, over tormenting passions thou didst reign. Therefore thou hast passed over unto the rational kingdoms, O all-glorious one, to stand before the eternal King.

*Sticheron:*
Job, who became invincible in temptations through the sores of his flesh, an unshakable pillar, a rampart of courage, made known unto all, who by philosophical thought crushed the stings of the devil, divine and godly-wise, the confirmation of the pious, the Lord's blameless servant, let us praise!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Seeing the wealth of Job's virtues, the enemy of the righteous plotted to steal it: and tearing apart the pillar of the body, he stole not the treasure of the spirit; for he found the soul of the blameless one armed, but me he stripped and took captive. Anticipate me before the end, deliver me from the deceiver, O Savior, and save me!

*Canon, whose acrostic (without the Theotokia) is: Worthily to hymn the long-suffering Job.*[^46]  
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The choirs of Israel with dryshod feet passed through the Red Sea and the watery deep, and seeing the captains and enemies submerged therein, with gladness sang: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!*

*Troparion:*
Beholding with spiritual eyes the strong adamant adorned with the crown of patience, the Church of Christ keeps festival in joy, crying: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Neither the tongue of the exceedingly wise nor the mind of man is able to comprehend thy praises; for the uncreated Tongue named thee pious, true, and blameless. Therefore with one accord we cry: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*
The deceiver asked to conquer the righteous one, who was hedged about with spiritual wealth though destitute; and having violently subdued him by nakedness, he was confounded. For through patience Job cried: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!

*Theotokion:*
The choirs of the prophets hymn thee, O pure one, and the multitudes of the bodiless venerate thee; for beholding the Creator of all, Who ineffably came forth from thy womb, unceasingly they sing unto Him: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with power: therefore my heart hath been established in the Lord.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst remain unbending in mind, when thou of thy beloved children and stripped of wealth, singing: Blessed be the name of my Lord!

*Troparion:*
A furnace of virtue didst thou make of wealth and poverty; for inflamed by both, as gold seven times refined, thou wast shown to be most tested.

*Troparion:*
By the words of thy wife, as unto our first parent, the serpent, the author of malice, approached thee; but he was deceived, boasting immeasurably of his cunning.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O unwedded Mother, who didst contain God the Word in thy womb, and didst give birth unto Him incarnate, as at once God and man.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As the First-Fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
As true and righteous, godly and blameless, sanctified wast thou shown to be, O all-glorious one, true servant of God; thou didst enlighten the world in thy patience, most patient and most noble one. Therefore all of us, O godly-wise one, hymn thy memory.

*Then, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "The Beauty of Virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дв҃ства):*
Job the blameless and most honorable, the towering pillar, who by the Spirit was invincible against the wiles of the enemy, let us all call blessed, and faithfully praise as a great servant of Christ, who fervently entreats Him to save our souls from afflictions.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, that Thou wast born of a Virgin, to deliver from deception those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
A most sufficient pattern and an ensouled pillar of virtues was blameless Job shown to be unto us. Make us emulators of him as we cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
The suffering of the purulent sores of thy guileless soul heals, by thy piety and patience, the transgression of the sacred Eden, O most blessed Job.

*Troparion:*
Reasoning with holy thought, Job sang: If we have received good things from the Lord, shall we not bravely endure painful things? Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
O unwedded Bride of God, who in the flesh didst give birth unto the Savior, we hymn thee, O most glorified one, and unceasingly cry unto thee: Rejoice, O intercessor for thy servants!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst cause the light to shine, and didst enlighten the morning, and show forth the day: Glory to Thee, glory to Thee, O Jesus, Son of God!*

*Troparion:*
Comforting thee in thy severe afflictions, they added pains to thy wounds and wove for thee more abundant crowns, O glorious Job.

*Troparion:*
With youthful vigor in cruel trials thou wast shown to be invincible, and in wealth, O righteous one, thou wast not lifted up unto insolence, O blameless Job.

*Troparion:*
Keeping unharmed the treasury of the soul, thou didst valiantly endure the cruel storm of the enemy, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
Unceasingly creation offers the angels' voice unto thee, O Virgin: Rejoice, O pure Mother of Jesus the Son of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried out in the sea monster, praying: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!*

*Troparion:*
The hearing is sweetened, delighting in thy words, for thou wast shown to be a mouth filled with mysteries by the divine Spirit Who made and instructed thee.

*Troparion:*
Soothing the heart's pain of the sorrowing, from thine eyes thou didst pour forth tears of mercy, standing before orphans and widows, O glorious Job.

*Troparion:*
Thy life was pious and God-loving; therefore death was shown to be rest, and thou didst pass over unto unceasing gladness, O Job.

*Theotokion:*
We pray for thy servants, O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God; pray unto Him Who was incarnate of thee, for thee alone we know as our intercessor.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously ensnared: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
Not ignorant of the precepts of the Law, but bearing the fulfillment of the Law and the Prophets, O Job, through love thou didst please God, singing: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Not seeking treasures upon earth, but hope in the heavens, thou didst account gold as dust, and desired to sing unto God: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
The enemy of the righteous stripped thee of children and possessions, O Job; but the strength of thy soul ceased not to cry: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Triadikon:*
The Word and the Spirit from the Father, one three-hypostatic Nature, theologically praising in piety, O godly-minded ones, in faith let us cry: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Triadikon (continued):*
...blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Piously let us hymn as Mother of God unto ages her who ineffably received into her womb beyond word the immutable God, Who through mercy partakes of men.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who appeared in the image of an angel in the fiery furnace unto the hymn-singers, Christ God: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
The entire choir of those keeping festival on high rejoices with Job, who is hymned in marvels: Praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The Bestower of the contest redoubled crowns for thee on earth and in heaven, O godly-wise Job. Therefore thou criest: Bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The suppuration of thy flesh pours forth healings most richly for souls and bodies unto those who sing: Bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Sickness and health, wealth and poverty surged in thy life, O blessed one; for radiant wast thou shown in all, crying: Ye peoples, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Her who ineffably and without seed conceived, and gave birth unto the Joy of the universe, Christ God: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve by the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding of the fruit of thy womb didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.*

*Troparion:*
Job was worthily crowned with the brightness of patience; but thou weepest and lamentest, unable to bear the righteous one's crown: for having hoped in vain, thou wast put to shame, O Satan, the only enemy of the stumbling.

*Troparion:*[^47]
As far as it is attainable for man to see, having perceived Thy immortal glory, Job, seized with fear and reverent awe, cried out in great trembling: I am dust and ashes, but Thou art the Lord; therefore we all magnify Thee.

*Troparion:*
The books of life worthily received thee, and the heavenly treasuries, as ripe wheat, full of spiritual days, O Job, blessed servant of the Lord; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Triadikon:*
O Trinity in three Hypostases, one pure Unity, and one sovereign all-holy *Triadikon (continued):*
...Trinity, our God, Thou hast shown Thyself as Light and Life and incorruption unto the world: save from deception Thy servants who cry unto Thee, that in faith we may worship Thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be the receptacle of Wisdom beyond understanding, the best, O all-immaculate Maiden, an ensouled throne and gate; therefore the virgins, loving thee as Queen, O Virgin, were brought after thee, O young handmaidens of God.

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones. *Verse:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?  
*Epistle to the Galatians, pericope 213.*[^48]  
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*
Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, in His commandments he will greatly delight. *Verse:* Mighty upon the earth shall be his seed.  
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 43.*[^49]  
*Communion Hymn:* In everlasting memory...

## May 7. Apparition of the Sign of the Precious Cross in Jerusalem[^50]

*Commemoration of the sign of the Precious Cross which appeared in the heavens in the city of Jerusalem, stretched forth with stars from the holy Place of the Skull even unto the holy Mount of Olives, at the third hour of the day, during the reign of Emperor Constantius, son of Constantine the Great. And the memory of the Holy Martyr Acacius.*

*Stichera of the Cross, on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Today the divine multitude of the faithful rejoices, for the heavenly Cross is manifested unto the ends of the earth: it illumines the firmament with unapproachable light, enlightens the air, and adorns the face of the earth. The Church of Christ sings with divine hymns and ministers in veneration; the divine and most marvelous Cross protects her from on high. Strengthened by its power, let us draw near unto the Master, crying: Give peace unto the world, and enlighten our souls!

*Sticheron:*
As a most honorable ladder, the all-honorable and glorious Cross was seen today: it leads over from earth unto the heavens those who venerate it with unwavering faith. All the nations rejoice, fervently running unto baptism, and the Church as a bride is adorned with the divine image; for what the Jews sought to hide illumines the air from on high, and saves those who run unto it in faith.

*Sticheron:*
A bond of divine power, sent from on high, the Cross appeared unto us in Zion, shining exceedingly with grace, with measureless light; on the glorious Place of the Skull the heavenly Cross shone forth unto the ends of the earth, proclaiming unto all the salvation of the world through the Passion of our crucified God. Bowing down before it, let us sing unceasingly, and ask in faith: Grant peace unto the world, and save our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
The heavens declared Thy glory, O Lord, the awesome sign of Thy Cross, and all the earth bowed down in fear; and we, praising Thee, thankfully cry unto Thee: Being passionless God, Thou wast pleased voluntarily to suffer in the flesh for our sake; save the people whom Thou hast acquired with Thy blood!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
The image of Thy Cross hath now shone brighter than the sun, which Thou didst stretch forth from the holy mountain even unto the Place of the Skull, and in it didst make clear Thy strength, O Savior: thereby strengthening also our faithful king;[^51] whom do Thou also ever save in peace, by the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, O Christ God, and save us!

*The service of the Martyr Acacius is sung at Compline.*  
*Canon of Acacius, the composition of Theophanes. Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing on dry foot the Red Sea's deep of old, Israel, by Moses' outstretched hands in the form of a cross, vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the beauty of guilelessness, O glorious one, with boldness thou didst stand before the Source and Cause of good things; make Him now propitious unto us by thy supplications.

*Troparion:*
Our true intercessor today, Acacius, crying with a loud proclamation, summons us unto a feast; let us therefore run with diligence and gladness, O ye lovers of the martyrs!

*Troparion:*
Partaking of remission of sins by baptism, he received the sonship of God's grace; and unto a second baptism by the blood of martyrdom thou wast vouchsafed, O most blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Unable to bear seeing lawlessness exalted, O glorious Acacius, named after guilelessness,[^52] thou didst clearly proclaim Christ's divinity before the tribunal of the tormentors.

*Theotokion:*
In thee, O full of grace, all-pure one, the footsteps of God were manifestly seen, Who preserved thee a virgin, and after childbearing kept thee incorrupt who gavest Him birth.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the Father's hypostatic Wisdom, O Christ: for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*
Adorned with divine understanding, wisdom, and word, thou didst destroy the senseless weavings of the wise; therefore we honor thy glorious memory.

*Troparion:*
Shining with courageous and steadfast mind, thou didst overcome the perversities and armaments of the ungodly, and didst tear down the strongholds of deception.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the power of God Almighty, thou didst expose the rotten and impotent nature of godlessness; therefore we proclaim thee a victorious bearer of victory, O martyr.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst escape the muddy and deceitful drink and the mire by the enlightenment of piety, having illumined thy mind with radiant beams, O martyr Acacius.

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived Life, O Mother of God, thou didst abolish the dominion of death and the condemnation of the ancient curse; therefore we honor thy most pure childbearing.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine, hath come with incorruptible hand and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!*

*Troparion:*
Invincible as a pillar thou didst remain in faith, shaken neither by showers of torments, nor by the inflicting of wounds, nor by the winds of beatings, but didst cry with a loud voice: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Steered by the sails and breathings of the Holy Spirit, O blessed one, thou didst come to rest in a quiet haven, and wast not terrified by the surging of the waves and winds, O all-honorable martyr of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Having the invincible divine might, thou didst overcome all the adversary's power, and rejoicing, didst take away all their armor, as a praiseworthy victor.

*Troparion:*
Guided by divine manifestation, making the city of the king the end of thy course thou didst make the imperial city the end of thy course; and having become a citizen thereof, O most blessed one, thou beatest back every barbarian assault.

*Theotokion:*
Raise me up, slain and held fast by the inescapable bonds of sin, O Virgin, thou alone Mother of God, who didst conceive the eternal Life that gives deliverance unto all the faithful.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, waking early from the night, hymn Thee, the Only-Begotten, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst take up the cross upon thy shoulders, unswervingly following Christ, Who leads Thee unto incorruptible life and brings Thee unto never-setting light, O most wealthy one.

*Troparion:*
The bitter wounds of the body and the severed limbs, O all-blessed one, obtained for thee unending delight, a martyr's crown, and angelic rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
A youth guileless, chaste, and meek, strong against bodily passions, fierce against demons, and unapproachable unto the unfaithful wast thou shown to be, O ever-memorable one.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth in the flesh for us unto true God, the Only-Begotten of the Father, we name thee the all-honorable Birthgiver of God, joining our voices together unto Him born of thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.*

*Troparion:*
By hunger they that starve for the divine gift condemn thee, O martyr, who art ever fed with heavenly gifts and hast unending food.

*Troparion:*
Having stripped the godless arrogance of the tormentors and the author-of-evil serpent, thou didst drown them in the deep of thy blood, and as a victory-bearer didst take flight unto heaven, O Acacius.

*Troparion:*
By thy death imitating the Master's death, O most blessed martyr, thou reignest with Him eternally, filled with the vision of Him and with joy.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst loose the condemnation of death and the curse of the Law, O pure one, having given birth unto Christ, the Destroyer of death, Who poured forth blessing upon all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Quickly Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
In the streams of thy blood, O martyr Acacius, having drowned the enemy, thou didst put to shame all the tormentor's armaments; having attained the immaterial choirs of the angels, thou didst receive the unfading [crown] of incorruption *Sessional Hymn (continued):*
...unfading crown of incorruption; therefore by thy supplications save us from perils.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously ensnared: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
Beheaded in the head, thou didst take flight unto the spiritual Head of the Church, adorned with a crown of victory, and crying, O most wealthy one: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
With Christ's martyrs, as an all-true martyr, thou wast joined in patient endurance, having conquered the godless tormentors, crying out, O Acacius: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Giving wings to thy mind unto the blessed hope kept for the faithful, thou didst enter the tormentors' tribunal, singing in the Spirit: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
The sayings of the prophets, O Virgin, and the ancient types clearly prefigure thy pure childbearing, O Mother of God, which we hymn as we cry: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Stretching forth his hands, Daniel shut the lions' open jaws in the pit; and girded with virtue, the children, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Into the heavenly metropolis, which truly reigns, thou didst take up dwelling, O most wise one, which the Lord founded and prepared beforehand for the saints who hope in Him and cry in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Through love thou didst endure the torment of tortures, and wasting away with hunger, thou didst especially remember the everlasting delight, and the abiding food and joy, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Easily and right swiftly thou didst wage war against thine adversaries, having Christ as an invincible defender, helper, and protector, and Fellow-Sufferer, O most wealthy one, unto Whom we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*[^53]
Having been named Acacius, O blessed one, thou didst lead a guileless and blameless life, adorned with the blood of suffering, O all-praised one, embellished with the virtues of martyrdom, and crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
A marvelous mystery truly beneath the sun hast thou alone shown forth: for thou, O pure one, didst give birth unto God, invisible and without beginning, uncontainable by all, and incomprehensible unto Whom we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding of the fruit of thy womb, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.*

*Troparion:*
Having, O blessed one, the brightness that comes from victory in the martyr's resistance, thou didst stand before the Master's throne, rejoicing with the angelic hosts, and asking salvation for all who call thy memory blessed.

*Troparion:*
Radiant against the enemies, O most wealthy one, were thy victorious deeds of valor, and much greater honors did the Bestower of the contest grant thee, and the fullness of divine radiance; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Near wast thou unto Christ, O all-valiant one, for thou didst endure passion and affliction; therefore He vouchsafed thee clearly vision and the vision of God, whereof thou didst acquire the causes on earth, greeting them from afar.

*Theotokion:*
Fairer in beauty than all was shown to be thy Fruit, O all-immaculate one, adorning creation with the dawn of beneficent beauty; entreat Him fervently to save those who unceasingly magnify thee.

*Stichera of the Holy Martyr Acacius, Tone 2, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having taken up wrath against the single serpent, and having girded thyself with Christ's insurmountable and invincible power, O godly-wise Acacius, thou didst cast down to the earth the inventor of evil, laying low his haughty power, and didst put to shame the servants who act with him.

*Sticheron:*
Instructed from on high in alienation from evil and appropriation of good, thou wast in deed that which thou wast called before, O most wise Acacius, even though called after that which came later; for Providence ineffably and invisibly sets forth from afar the cause of great things.

*Sticheron:*[^54]
Great among cities and reigning over all, [the imperial city] hath thy honorable church as an imperial crown, and offers thee as a pillar to those who have acquired thee, a wise citizen, an acceptable guardian, and a valiant soldier, driving away the sudden assaults of enemies and adversaries.

*Theotokion:*
I call thee blessed, O all-pure one, who hast snatched men from the depth of evils and despair; I hymn thee in song, O Bride of God, ever-blessed, and I glorify thy incomprehensible childbearing, O pure one, for thou didst give birth unto the Savior of the world, and from the ancestral curse hast delivered mankind, O Virgin.

*Staurotheotokion:*
When the Ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she marveled and cried out: What is this sight, O beloved Son? Hath the unfaithful, lawless assembly repaid Thee with this, having enjoyed Thy many marvels? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos to 6, and of the Cross to 6. Tone 4.*[^55]  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The chariots of Pharaoh and his power He plunged into the sea, He Who is mighty in battle: let us sing a new song, for He hath been glorified!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst manifest upon earth the radiance of the Cross, whereby having cast down the devil, Thou didst save the human race, O Lord; therefore we sing Thy glory.

*Troparion:*
The Savior broke down the enmity of the disobedient mind by the Cross, through the Father's good pleasure; let us sing unto Him a new song, for gloriously hath He been glorified.

*Troparion:*
As in Eden Thou didst plant the tree of life, O Christ, so the victorious sceptre, Thy Cross, Thou didst cause to shine forth in luminous form upon the holy mountain.

*Theotokion:*
Thee whom the prophetic choir foresaw as the heavenly gate and the unburnt bush, O Virgin pure Mother, we have known as having given birth unto God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with power: therefore my heart hath been established in the Lord.*

*Troparion:*
Protected as with a shield by the sign of the invincible Cross, O Master, unable to endure it, the abominable one vanishes with the multitude of demons.

*Troparion:*
Thy Cross hath gladdened those who confess Thee as crucified God, and hath shone forth as with exceedingly bright and unapproachable beauty.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother who knewest not wedlock, who didst contain God the Word in thy womb, and having given birth unto God incarnate, at once also man.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having shown forth the divine image of the Cross in heaven, to guide those upon earth unto the knowledge of Thee, O Christ God: today the never-setting brightness shone forth, and the light of Thy countenance was signed upon us. Therefore all of us, the faithful, have an invincible victory.  
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard Thy report, O Lord, that Thou didst appear upon earth, and I glorified Thy power.*

*Troparion:*
Mystically indeed of old, but sensibly today, Thou didst cause to shine forth, O Christ, the image of Thy most pure Cross.

*Troparion:*
Having enlightened the presumption of the lawless deicides by the image of the Cross, Thou didst convict them, O compassionate Savior.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst establish the Christian faith by Thine immaterial light, delineating the Cross unto us, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
O pure Mother, blessed Mistress, by thy supplications make God, Whom thou didst bear, propitious unto us.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst cause the light to shine, and didst enlighten the morning, and show forth the day: Glory to Thee, glory to Thee, O Jesus, Son of God!*

*Troparion:*
The death-bearing tree of old brought death unto Adam who transgressed in Eden; but the life-bearing Cross hath now appeared, to enlighten the ends of the world.

*Troparion:*
Having established the primordial brilliance by His word, the Father now delineates for the Son the radiant Cross, testifying unto the divinity of Him Who was crucified.

*Troparion:*
In the never-setting light, the exalted Light, in the Father shall we behold Thee by faith, O Christ, the Son, and Thy Spirit, having shone forth unto our souls.

*Theotokion:*
Pains of motherhood thou didst not know, O Maiden, having supernaturally conceived God, and given birth in the flesh; therefore we all worthily glorify thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*When I was in affliction, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me.*

*Troparion:*
Let the eyes of the lawless be covered, for they are unable to gaze upon the beauties of the Cross shining forth.

*Troparion:*
Let those who know God, Who was crucified in the flesh upon the Tree, delight in the dawn of the most pure Cross.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, who didst bear God, cease not to entreat thy Son for us.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having opened the closed heavens, shedding exceedingly bright rays in the sky, the most pure Cross shone forth upon earth; therefore, having received the radiance of its operation, we are guided toward the never-setting Light, and in battles we possess it as a weapon of peace, an invincible victory.

*Ikos:*[^56]
This awesome sign of the great God the great Constantine beheld, having heard *Ikos (continued):*
...Jesus speaking within it, Who had spoken of old unto the faithful autocrat of Christians, that even unto the Second Coming He would preserve his city as His inheritance, and it shall abide even unto the consummation, holding the primacy over all cities; and its wall shall be the Cross, a weapon of peace, an invincible victory.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously ensnared: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
When the Sun was lifted up upon the Cross at midday, beholding Him, the sensible sun hid its rays: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
The God-seer conversed of old in fire and darkness; but by the Cross-manifesting lightning, as God He is glorified today: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
From Light and in Light shining forth as the co-everlasting Light, by Thy most pure light Thou didst show forth the victory of Thy Cross: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably and supernaturally received in her womb the immutable God, Who through mercy came unto men, let us piously hymn her as the Mother of our God unto ages.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who appeared in the image of an angel in the fiery furnace unto the hymn-singers, Christ God: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Beheld upon the throne of glory, and nailed upon the tree of the Cross in the reality of the flesh: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Arming the Cross with most pure light upon the Place of the Skull, upon the holy mountain: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Glorifying the One of the Trinity, Who enlightens the universe with the radiance of the Cross: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Her who ineffably and without seed conceived and gave birth unto the Joy of the universe, Christ God: praise Him, ye children; bless Him, ye priests; highly exalt Him, ye peoples, unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A stone cut without hands from an uncut mountain, O Virgin, the cornerstone Ode 9. Heirmos (continued):*
*...was cut off, Christ, Who joined together divided natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparion:*
Of old in fire and darkness Thou didst give the Law unto the disobedient; but now unto Thy faithful people Thou hast delineated the image of Thy Cross, shining brighter than the luminaries: which we worthily magnify.

*Troparion:*
With darkness creation lamented the audacity of those who crucified Thee; but now with most majestic light Thy life-bearing Cross hath shone forth, O Christ, revealing the hidden beauty of the Church.

*Troparion:*
Having lifted up their hope in Thee, Thy wealthy people offer unto Thee for supplication the Cross and Thy awesome blood; but graciously grant us deliverance, O Word of God.

*Theotokion:*
Enlighten us, O Virgin who didst bear the unapproachable Light, and fill us with gladness and joy, and divine understanding, who with a pure heart discreetly magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "With the Disciples" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ зрѧ́щымъ):*
The Cross is the guardian of the whole universe; the Cross is the beauty of the Church; the Cross is the majesty of kings; the Cross is the confirmation of the faithful; the Cross is the glory of angels and the wound of demons. *(Twice)*  
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
Exalt the Lord our God, and worship the footstool of His feet, for He is holy. *Verse:* The Lord reigns, let the peoples rage.  
*Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 125.*[^57]  
*Alleluia, Tone 1:*
Remember Thy congregation. *Verse:* But God is our King.  
*Gospel according to John, pericope 60.*[^58]  
*Communion Hymn:* The light of Thy countenance hath been signed upon us...

## May 8. Holy Apostle and Evangelist John the Theologian[^59]

*Commemoration of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostle and Evangelist, the beloved bosom friend, the virgin, John the Theologian; and of our venerable father Arsenius the Great.*

*At Small Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Come, ye faithful, let us today crown with divine hymns the depth of wisdom and the writer of Orthodox dogmas, the glorious and beloved John; for he thundered forth: *In the beginning was the Word.* Whence he was shown to be loud-voiced, shining as a herald of glad tidings unto the world, most sighted and all-praised.

*Sticheron:*
In truth thou wast shown to be a true friend and great bosom companion unto Christ the Teacher: for leaning upon His breast, from thence thou didst draw forth the dogmas of wisdom, wherewith thou dost enrich the whole earth all around, as God's divine speaker. Therefore Christ's Church, holding these beautiful [teachings], now rejoices in gladness.

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, O Theologian in truth; rejoice, all-beloved son of the Lord's Mother! For standing beside Christ's Cross, thou didst hear the Master's divine voice, crying unto thee: *Behold now thy mother.* Whence worthily all of us call thee blessed, as a great and beloved apostle of Christ.

*Glory... Tone 2, Idiomelon:*
Come, ye faithful, let us call blessed the ever-memorable John, the summit of the Apostles, the trumpet of theology, the spiritual warrior who subjected the universe unto God; who rises from the earth, yet departs not from the earth, but lives and awaits the Master's awesome Second Coming. Ask that we may meet it uncondemned, O mystical friend, Christ's bosom companion, for those who celebrate thy memory with love.

*Both now... of the Feast.*  
*At the Aposticha, of the Feast.*  
*Glory... of the Apostle, Tone 4:*
Disciple of the Savior, virgin... *(written at the Litiya).*  
*Troparion, Tone 2:*
O apostle beloved of Christ God, hasten to deliver a defenseless people; He Who allowed thee to recline upon His breast receives thee as thou fallest down before Him; Whom do thou entreat, O Theologian O Word, and to disperse the looming cloud of the gentiles, asking for us peace and great mercy.
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*  
*Litany, and Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the first antiphon.*  
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 4; and of the Venerable One, 3.*

*Stichera of the Apostle, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Beholder of ineffable revelations and narrator of the mysteries of God that came to pass, the son of Zebedee, having written for us the Gospel of Christ, taught us to theologize the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron:*
A God-moved harp of heavenly songs, being a mystical writer, a God-speaking mouth, sings a melodious song beautifully: for moving his lips like strings, and moving his tongue like a plectrum, he prays that we may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
With thy loud-thundering tongue, having made known the voice of wisdom, the hidden Word, O beloved of Christ, thou dost ever cry, moving thy lips purely: *In the beginning was the Word,* and thou dost teach every man the knowledge of God.

*Stichera of the Venerable Father Arsenius the Great, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^60]
O godly-wise father Arsenius, having enlightened thy mind with tearful streams, thou didst converse with God through unceasing cleansings; and a brilliant pillar of piety, shining with fire, wast thou shown to be, O blessed one, boast of monks; and now preserve us all by thy prayers.

*Sticheron:*
O godly-wise father Arsenius, with thy mind girded on every side with self-control, thou didst cause it to ascend unto the First Cause; and having left behind worldly turmoil, and shaken off clamor, by purity of mind thou didst unite thyself, O most wise one, through truly utmost desire, O blessed one.

*Sticheron:*
Venerable father Arsenius, thou didst seek God, and devoutly desiring to be enlightened by His radiant beams, having left all the brilliance of majesty and the imperial courts, thou didst receive an immortal inheritance, now taking up abode with Christ, O God-blessed one.

*Glory... Tone 2, Idiomelon:*
The son of thunder, the foundation of divine words, the chief of theology, and the foremost preacher of the true dogmas of God's wisdom, the beloved John and virgin, let the human race worthily praise in fitting manner: for he unceasingly *Idiomelon (continued):*
...having unceasingly within himself the divine [word], he said: *In the beginning was the Word.* And straightway [showing Him] inseparable from the Father and equal in power to the Father's essence, revealing unto us through himself the Orthodoxy of the Holy Trinity; and being the Fashioner with the Father, bearing life and being the true Light, he showed Him forth unto us. O awesome marvel, and matter exceedingly worthy of wisdom! For being full of love, he was also filled with theology: through glory and honor and faith being the founder of our pure faith, for the sake of which we shall obtain eternal blessings on the day of judgment.  
*Both now... of the Feast.*  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And three readings.*

*Reading from the General Epistle of John:*[^61]
Beloved, if our heart condemn us not, then have we confidence toward God. And whatsoever we ask, we receive of Him, because we keep His commandments, and do those things that are pleasing in His sight. And this is His commandment, That we should believe on the name of His Son Jesus Christ, and love one another, as He gave us commandment. And he that keeps His commandments dwells in Him, and He in him: and hereby we know that He abides in us, by the Spirit which He hath given us. Beloved, believe not every spirit, but try the spirits whether they are of God: because many false prophets are gone out into the world. Hereby know ye the Spirit of God, and the spirit of deception. Every spirit that confesses that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is of God. And every spirit that confesses not that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is not of God: and this is that spirit of antichrist, whereof ye have heard that it should come; and even now already is it in the world. Ye are of God, little children, and have overcome them: because greater is He that is in you, than he that is in the world. They are of the world: therefore speak they of the world, and the world hears them. We are of God: he that knows God hears us; he that is not of God hears not us.

*Reading from the General Epistle of John:*[^62]
Beloved, if God so loved us, we ought also to love one another. No man hath seen God at any time. If we love one another, God dwells in us, and His love is perfected in us. Hereby know we that we dwell in Him, and He in us, because He hath given us of His Spirit. And we have seen and do testify that the Father *Reading (continued):*
...sent the Son to be the Savior of the world. Whosoever shall confess that Jesus is the Son of God, God dwells in him, and he in God. And we have known and believed the love that God hath to us. God is love; and he that dwells in love dwells in God, and God in him.

*Reading from the General Epistle of John:*[^63]
Beloved, if a man say, I love God, and hates his brother, he is a liar: for he that loves not his brother whom he hath seen, how can he love God Whom he hath not seen? And this commandment have we from Him, That he who loves God love his brother also. Whosoever believes that Jesus is the Christ is born of God: and every one that loves Him that begat loves him also that is begotten of Him. By this we know that we love the children of God, when we love God, and keep His commandments. For this is the love of God, that we keep His commandments: and His commandments are not grievous. For whatsoever is born of God overcomes the world: and this is the victory that overcomes the world, even our faith. Who is he that overcomes the world, but he that believes that Jesus is the Son of God?

*At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple or of the Feast; and of the Apostle, Tone 1:*  
*By Germanus:*  
Rivers of theology flowed forth from thy honorable lips, O Apostle, wherewith the Church of God being richly watered, worships in Orthodox manner the consubstantial Trinity. Entreat It even now, O John the Theologian, to establish and save our souls!

*By Andrew Pyros:*[^64]  
A garden of purity, a fragrant myrrh hath again shone forth unto us in the present feast, to cry unto him: O thou who didst recline upon the Master's breast and didst rain down the Word upon the world, O Apostle John, who didst guard the Virgin as the apple of an eye, ask for us great mercy from Christ!

*Sticheron:*
O disciple of the Savior, virgin and Theologian, unto thee as a virgin, Christ God, when being crucified, committed the Virgin and Birthgiver of God, and thou didst guard her as the apple of an eye; therefore pray that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron:*
As an eyewitness of ineffable mysteries, crying out thou didst proclaim the pre-eternal Word to be in the beginning with God, and that He was God, O Apostle John, bosom friend of Christ, and constant companion, and delight of the Trinity, establishing Ephesus and Patmos immovably, and being a help unto us. Pray, O most blessed Theologian, that from impious enemies, visible and invisible, those people may be delivered who ever faithfully celebrate thy memory.

*And of the Venerable One, Sticheron, Tone 5:*
Venerable father, having heard the voice of the Lord's Gospel, thou didst leave behind the world, counting wealth and glory as nothing; therefore thou didst cry out unto all: Love God, and ye shall find eternal grace; prefer nothing before His love, so that when He comes in His glory, ye may find rest with all the saints. By his prayers, O Christ, preserve and save our souls.

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Having reclined upon the breast of Christ the Teacher at the Lord's Supper, O beloved disciple, from thence thou didst understand ineffable mysteries, and didst thunder forth the heavenly voice unto all: *In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God,* the true Light that enlightens every man coming into the world: Christ God and the Savior of our souls.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Having theologized the Son of the Most High, co-everlasting with the Father and consubstantial, immutable Light from Light, and the express image of the Parent's Hypostasis, shining forth timelessly and impassibly from Him, the Fashioner and Lord of all ages: O beloved one, thou didst preach Him unto the world, as Christ our God, Who brought forth light out of darkness; Whom do thou entreat to save and enlighten our souls.

*Verse:* Their proclamation hath gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the universe.

*Sticheron:*
Having received the Light of the Comforter, whereby also being enlightened, thou didst theologize Him as proceeding from the Father, and through the Son revealed unto mankind, being of equal power, co-enthroned, and consubstantial with the unoriginate Father and God the Word, O beloved one, thou didst proclaim unto all; therefore in hymns we honor thee as the divine foundation of the faith, which do thou preserve unshaken by thy prayers unto the Lord.

*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.

*Sticheron:*
Having ascended unto the height of theology, thou wast taught the ineffable mysteries of God concerning the consubstantiality of the Godhead, and the single glory and kingdom and dominion, ever divided in three Hypostases, yet in essence undivided and united in unconfused divine unity: thus gloriously thou didst proclaim, O Theologian, the indivisible Trinity; Whom do thou entreat to save and enlighten our souls.

*Glory... Tone 6. By John the Monk:*[^65]
Apostle of Christ, Evangelist and Theologian, having been an initiate of ineffable mysteries, thou didst thunder forth unto us the unutterable teachings of wisdom, explaining unto the faithful *He Who was in the beginning,* and casting aside *that which was not,* cutting off the words of heretics;[^66] having shown thyself to be a bosom companion and beloved friend: like Isaiah of majestic voice and Moses the God-seer, having boldness before God, fervently pray for our souls.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 2 (twice), written at Small Vespers.*

*And "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin" (once), and the blessing of the loaves according to the custom; and a reading.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, 4; and of the Apostle, 4.*

*Glory... of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*
With the streams of thy tears thou didst cultivate the barren desert, and by sighings from the depths thou didst make thy labors fruitful an hundredfold, and thou becamest a luminary unto the universe, shining with miracles, O Arsenius our father: entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Having become a disciple of Christ, the Master of all things, thou didst teach the people and enlighten the world, guiding it unto the knowledge of Him, O Apostle; therefore all of us name thee a preacher of truth and a Theologian, a friend and bosom companion of Christ. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word Co-Unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Let us all hymn with songs and sacred praises the all-honored John, the apostle and disciple of Christ, who proclaimed unto all the divine nativity from the Father of our Deliverer and King, Christ; for he unceasingly prays that our souls may be shown mercy. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having reclined upon the breast of Jesus, thou didst obtain boldness, as a disciple inquiring: Lord, who is he that betrays Thee? Being exceedingly beloved, O all-praised one, by bread *Sessional Hymn (continued):*
...by bread He clearly revealed unto thee; therefore, as having been an initiate of ineffable mysteries, thou dost teach the ends [of the earth] the Incarnation of the Word, O Theologian and apostle: entreat Christ God to grant remission of offenses unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Or this Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the heavenly gate and the ark, the all-holy mountain, the radiant cloud, the unburned bush, the spiritual paradise, the deliverance of Eve, the great vessel of the whole universe; for salvation was wrought in her for the world, and remission of ancient offenses; therefore also we cry out unto her: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of offenses unto those who piously worship thy most holy Offspring.

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the first antiphon of Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
Their proclamation hath gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the universe.
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God...

*Let every breath...*

*Gospel according to John, pericope 67.*[^67]

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 2:*
O virgin Theologian, beloved disciple of the Savior, by thy prayers save us, we beseech thee, from every harm, for we are thy flock.

*Canon of the Feast with the heirmos to 4, and of the Apostle to 6, and of the Venerable One to 4.*

*Canon of John the Theologian, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The wonder-working rod of Moses, striking crosswise of old and dividing the sea, drowned the chariot-driver Pharaoh, yet saved Israel the fugitive traveling on foot, as he sang a hymn unto God.*

*Troparion:*
As pure and beloved wast thou unto the imperishable Light, and reclining upon His breast thou didst rest, and with bold soul thou didst draw forth the light of understanding from the abyss of wisdom, O blessed Apostle John.

*Troparion:*
Supernaturally thou didst thunder forth theology, O beloved of Christ: *In the beginning was the Word,* living and hypostatic with His Parent, *and the Word was God; and the Word was made flesh,* yet remained immutable God.

*Theotokion:*
Fleeing under thy protection, O pure one, we are shielded from all assaults that come upon us, O most pure one; therefore even now we pray: Raise us who have fallen from the abyss of transgressions, by thy divine prayers, O pure one.

*Another Canon of the Venerable One Tone and heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Submitting to the better divine command, O Arsenius, thou didst leave behind the glory of imperial wealth, as did Moses the lawgiver; and fleeing from men, thou becamest a fellow dweller with angels, rejoicing together with them.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst flee from the murky darkness of turmoils, O Arsenius, as from sources of sin, and by silence thou didst bridle thy tongue; therefore in stillness thou didst preserve an undisturbed mind, having become a most seemly resting house of the Divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Exceedingly glorious things were spoken of thee throughout generations of generations, O Mary Birthgiver of God, who didst contain God the Word in thy womb, yet remained pure; therefore all of us honor thee, our true protection under God.

*Katavasia of the Feast.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Christ, Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me upon the rock of Thy commandments, for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*
From a fisherman thou becamest a catcher of men and a Theologian, O wise John, despising fatherly and worldly love, and following the Master Word with divine desire.

*Troparion:*
Purified in the eye of the soul, O Theologian, thou didst behold the ineffable glory of the only-begotten Word and Son of God from the Father, through Whom all things were wisely made by the Divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Under thy saving refuge we run, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, and by thy prayers we are saved from the enemy's assault, and are delivered from future torment, O pure one.

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
O father Arsenius, Christ hath covered the rough and exceedingly coarse garment of thy poverty that imitated Him with the glorious first robe, preparing for thee a vesture of ineffable glory.

*Troparion:*
Having received help from God through contrition of heart, O Arsenius, thou didst overthrow the stumbling-blocks of the enemy and of proud-boasting spirits, having become a victor through humility, O father.

*Theotokion:*
Higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim wast thou revealed to be, O Birthgiver of God; for thou alone didst receive the uncontainable God in thy womb, O undefiled one; therefore all the faithful bless thee with hymns, O pure one.

*Kontakion of the Venerable One, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
From Rome thou didst shine forth as a great sun, and didst reach the imperial city, O most blessed one, enlightening it by thy words and deeds, driving away all mist of ignorance; therefore we honor thee, O glory of the fathers, venerable Arsenius.

*Ikos:*
Having heard the voice of the Lord, O venerable one, Thou didst leave worldly beauties behind and embraced the monastic life, emulating the angelic conversation, O godly-wise one; therefore Thou wast revealed as wholly transformed, an angel in the body, offering unto Christ fasting and tears, blameless prayer, and all-night standing; therefore Thou becamest a temple of the Trinity, a pattern for monks, a guide of virtue, and a fervent intercessor for those who ever honor thee: therefore we honor thee, O glory of the fathers, venerable Arsenius.

*Then Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having reclined upon the breast of Wisdom, and learned the knowledge of existing things, thou didst divinely thunder forth: *In the beginning was the Word;* having well written first of the unoriginate Nativity, thou didst proclaim unto all the Incarnation of the Word; therefore having caught the gentiles with thy tongue as with a net, by the grace of the Spirit thou dost teach the ends [of the earth], and enlighten them with miracles, O Theologian and apostle: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn of the Venerable One, Tone 8, and the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Having fled the clamor of life, and accomplished thy life in stillness, thou didst mortify the passions, and winged thy mind toward God, and wast shown to be a pillar of fire and a luminary unto all the faithful who honor thee; and watered with showers of tears, thou wast revealed as a fruitful tree, O God-bearing Arsenius: entreat Christ God to grant remission of offenses unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the Father's bosom didst visit our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!*

*Troparion:*
The Lord called thee a son of thunder,[^68] and accounted thee worthy to thunder forth by the Spirit the ineffable mystery of His divine and unsearchable nativity from the Father; therefore with hymns we worthily glorify thy all-festal memory, O Theologian.

*Troparion:*
By virtues thou didst ascend unto the height, O Apostle; thou didst behold the only-begotten Word shining with glory in the flesh sitting timelessly in the Father's bosom; and thou wast accounted worthy to hear the Father's voice, testifying to His sonship.

*Theotokion:*
Now fleeing under thy protection, may I, thine unprofitable servant, O all-immaculate one, not be put to shame; for I have thee as an intercessor before God and an unassailable wall: through thee may I enjoy future stillness and divine life, I pray.

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be an overcomer of nature, O Arsenius; thou didst subject the passions, O father, to the dominion of the mind, commanding sleep as a servant;[^69] therefore in all-night vigils thou didst cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
With desire thou didst seek God, O venerable one; thou didst desire Him still more Who appeared unto thee, and being wholly God-like through purity, thou didst converse with the Desired One, having understood through union Him Who is beyond all comprehension, O father Arsenius.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the boast of the faithful, O unwedded one; thou art the intercessor, thou art also the refuge of Christians, a wall and a haven; for unto thy Son thou bearest supplications, O all-immaculate one, and dost save from perils those who with faith and love acknowledge thee as the pure Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light never-setting? And a strange darkness hath covered me, the wretched one; but convert me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.*

*Troparion:*
Having seen the most pure Light that shone forth in the darkness of the world, thou wast mingled with love; and having become a luminary of piety, O John, by the light of theology thou didst illumine the races of many-seeded nations.

*Troparion:*
Having learned that the shadow-like Law was given through Moses, but divine grace came through Christ our God,[^70] with theological tongue, O beloved son of thunder, thou didst proclaim the glad tidings of His Godhead.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art my protection and strength, O most pure one, and confirmation, and an unbreakable wall; and invoking thee, do thou preserve me by night and by day from every assault of Belial that comes upon me, that with faith and love I may glorify thee.

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Piously thou didst reject the mediator of the first transgression of first-fashioned Adam, O father, well perceiving the craftiness of the enemy; therefore, living in blameless monasticism, in eternal nourishment [by fasting] poverty, entreat Christ the Master.

*Troparion:*
Denying thyself, thou didst renounce all voluptuousness; and bearing thy cross upon thy shoulder, O Arsenius, and desiring Christ, thou wast shown to be a worthy co-heir with Him in the heavens.

*Theotokion:*
Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, disregard not the kindred providence for us, we pray; for we Christians mercifully offer thee alone as cleansing before the Master.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The abyss of sins and tempest of transgressions confounds me, and drags me into the deep of violent despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me as unto Peter, O Pilot, and save me!*

*Troparion:*
Isaiah figuratively foresaw the live coal of divine fire,[^71] and having cleansed his lips, he was accounted worthy of prophecy; but of the incarnate God the Word, O blessed John, thou wast shown to be the bosom companion.

*Troparion:*
The Word being the Only-begotten, Who sits in the Father's bosom, was beheld incarnate, O blessed John, and as unto a beloved initiate He told thee: In incorporeal manner no man hath seen God at any time.[^72]
