*Theotokion:*
Enlighten my soul, O Virgin all-hymned Birthgiver of God, who didst bear the Radiance of the Father's glory; for possessing thy true grace, we are restrained from speech that harms the tongue.

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Piously thou didst mortify the carnal mind through impassibility, not having loved earthly inheritance, O venerable one, desiring from Christ, O father Arsenius, to receive inheritance in the highest.

*Troparion:*
The sun that appeared ended thy all-night standing, and the incomparable radiance of the spiritual Sun, after the nighttime labors of thy life, shone round about thee, enlightening thee, O Arsenius.[^73]

*Theotokion:*
Possessing compassion, as having borne the Word Who loves mankind, save us from bitter and cruel misfortune; for we the faithful have acquired thee alone, O most pure Sovereign Lady, as an invincible protection.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Who can tell thy majestic deeds, O virgin? For thou dost pour forth miracles and streamest healings, and prayest for our souls, as a Theologian and friend of Christ.

*Ikos:*
To know the heights of heaven and to search the depths of the sea is daring and incomprehensible; for even as it is utterly impossible to number the stars and the sand of the sea, so is it impossible to tell the righteous deeds of the Theologian *Ikos (continued):*
...it is impossible to express them adequately. With so many crowns Christ, Whom he loved, crowned him; for he reclined upon His breast and ate with Him at the Mystical Supper, as a Theologian and friend of Christ.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The fire was ashamed before the condescension of God in Babylon of old; therefore the Children in the furnace, dancing with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
Fervently thou didst go forth from the bonds of matter, O all-blessed one; as God's intercessor and herald of God-inspired theology, thou didst teach [us] to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst count gold and wealth as grass, O all-blessed one, and hay became as gold unto thee by divine operation, as thou didst theologize the Fashioner and Lord: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The Holy Spirit proceeding from the unoriginate Father, and indivisibly received from Thee, O only-begotten Word of God, Thou didst proclaim unto the Apostles to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the temple and chamber of Christ the King, and the one bed of Solomon, which, as the Scripture says, threescore valiant men surround;[^74] with thee now, O Virgin, they cry out and sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Counting the ancient perfumes as ruin, O father Arsenius, enduring malodor and becoming the sweet fragrance of Christ by participation in grace, thou didst say: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^75]

*Troparion:*
Having entered into the divine immaterial darkness, O father Arsenius, in Christ thou wast marvelously led to know the unoriginate Father and the Spirit, chanting the Unity in the Trinity: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Triadikon:*
The Lord indeed of all, the one Father of the one only-begotten Son, theologizing in Orthodox manner, we proclaim Thee; and knowing Thine upright Spirit proceeding from Thee alone, consubstantial and co-everlasting.

*Theotokion:*
In time thou didst bring forth without wedlock the Timeless One, O pure one, and through the assumption of flesh, O Virgin, thou didst bring forth the Word Who before was incorporeal; unto Whom we sing, crying in concord: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Sevenfold the Chaldean tyrant fiercely kindled the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos (continued):*
*...ye priests, sing praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
God-adorned and auspicious hath thy most glorious memory shone forth in the Church, breathing forth divine fragrance, O herald of the glad tidings of Jesus' Godhead; therefore rejoicing, we the faithful sing: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having the Theologian as leader of the choir, through him we theologize Thee, O Christ, proclaiming Thee God with the Father and the Spirit, and singing in monarchical praise we cry: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bring forth the incarnate God the Word, O all-immaculate one; entreat Him to deliver me from the fire; for lo, my whole life is weighed down, all enslaved to cruel sins; therefore unto thee, O Virgin, I flee, and call upon thee who art full of grace.

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
With good understanding, by streams of tears, O Arsenius, thou didst extinguish the burning of soul-destroying lusts and the kindle of tormenting Gehenna; but cease not to pray Christ the Savior of the world to snatch us away from both: Whom we bless unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Sevenfold, O venerable one, thou didst kindle for thyself the Babylonian furnace of compunction, wherein bedewed by the illumination of grace, thou didst cry aloud unto the Fashioner and Savior: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Triadikon:*
Thrice-radiant Godhead, shining a single ray from the single three-hypostatic essence, the unoriginate Parent, the Word consubstantial with the Father, and the reigning consubstantial Spirit: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praise; ye peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Heaven was dismayed at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparion:*
Standing beside the Master's Cross with the Virgin and pure Birthgiver of God, the beloved disciple, pure instrument of theology, as a virgin received her betrothal,[^76] and having become the blameless guardian of the Mother of God, is now worthily blessed.

*Troparion:*
Worthily having loved thy most pure, honorable, and blameless life, who didst live angelically upon earth, [the Lord] received thee in the place of a brother, O blessed John, and as a son unto His own Mother, and proclaimed thee a son unto His Mother, the Birthgiver of God; with whom all of us bless thee.

*Theotokion:*
Be unto me a saving pillar, O pure one, render the hosts of demons powerless, driving away the turmoil of temptations and perils, and casting far away the assaults of passions; granting pure freedom, and bountifully bestowing upon me divine gifts.

*Another [Canon], the heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Moses was astonished, having been accounted worthy to see in figure the Virgin's birthgiving, the bush prefigured fiery-bright; and thou, wholly united unto the Holy Spirit in ecstasy, wast seen as fiery-bright;[^77] therefore also we worthily bless thee, O Arsenius.

*Troparion:*
Christ the never-setting Light shone forth upon thee, and granted thee joy in the heavens; for by perpetual remembrance of death, thou didst diligently offer unto thyself an undefiled life; therefore also thou didst worthily receive the fruits of thy labors, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast revealed, O Virgin Mother of God, having brought forth in the flesh beyond nature God the Word, Whom the Father as Good brought forth from His heart before all ages;[^78] Whom now we understand to be higher than bodies, even though He clothed Himself in a body.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Hear, Ye Women" (Жє́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Having become a son of thunder, thou didst theologize unto men *He Who was in the beginning, the Word,* O Apostle John; for reclining faithfully upon the breast of thy Master, and from thence drawing forth the streams of theology, thou dost water all creation.

*Glory... of the Venerable One:*
Thou didst turn away from worldly attachment as from defilement, and having withered the flesh by fasting, thou didst renew the strength of the soul, O venerable one, and wast enriched with heavenly glory, O Arsenius; therefore cease not to pray unto the Lord for us.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 3, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O blessed and all-wise John, through the abundance of warmth for Christ's love, more than all didst thou become the beloved disciple unto the all-seeing Word, Who judges the whole universe with righteous measures; having been illumined with purity and the beauties of virginity in body and in thoughts, O God-blessed one.

*Sticheron:*
Flower of virginity, pleasant dwelling of honorable virtues, instrument of wisdom spiritual instrument, radiant temple, lips of grace, most luminous Tserkovne Oko: with spiritual hymns let us now praise the all-honorable John, as a servant of Christ.

*Sticheron:*
Having reclined upon the breast of Wisdom, thou didst draw forth the waters of theology, and hast given the world to drink, O John, most excellently with paternal understanding; thou didst dry up the sea of godlessness, and having become a pillar and animate cloud, thou dost guide us unto the heavenly inheritance.

*Glory... Tone 8. Idiomelon:*
O herald John, equal to the angels, virgin, theologian, taught of God! In Orthodox manner thou didst proclaim unto the world the most pure rib that poured forth blood and water,[^79] whereby we receive eternal life for our souls.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Apostle; Glory... of the Venerable One; Both now... of the Feast. Litanies, and Dismissal.*

*And the departure into the narthex, and the First Hour. At the First Hour, Troparion of the Feast; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Feast, and the rest, and the complete Dismissal.*

*And at the Hours, we say the Kontakion of the Feast and of the Apostle, alternating them.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, the appointed Ode; and of the Apostle, Ode 6.*

*Prokimenon, Tone 8:*
*Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the universe.[^80]*  
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God.*

*Epistle: The General Epistle of John, pericope 73 [1 John 1:1–7].[^81]*

*Alleluia, Tone 1:*
*The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.[^82]*

*Gospel according to John, pericope 67 [John 21:15–25].*

*Communion Hymn: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth...*

*Instruction concerning the Feast and the praise of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist John the Theologian:*
*It begins from the Saturday of the 2nd Week after Pascha, and ascends unto the 7th Sunday, of the Holy Fathers.*

*It should be known that if the feast of the holy Apostle John the Theologian falls on the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers, or of the Paralytic, or of the Samaritan Woman, or of the Blind Man:*
*On Saturday, at Small Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection; and of the Apostle, the Aposticha of Great Vespers, with his verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion. Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion. Little Litany, and Dismissal.*
*At Great Vespers, after the customary Kathisma, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection of the Tone, 3; and of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle Typikon Instruction (continued):*
*...and of the Apostle, 4; Glory... of the Feast, Idiomelon from the Triodion; Both now... the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance. Prokimenon of the day; and 3 readings of the Apostle. At the Litiya, Sticheron of the temple, and of the Apostle, Idiomela; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection, and of Pascha; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Triodion. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion, "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," twice; and of the Apostle, once; and a reading from the Acts of the Apostles. If it is the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection: "When Thou didst descend unto death," once; "The noble Joseph," once; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Myrrh-bearers. But if it is the other Sundays, Troparion of the Resurrection, twice; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... the 1st Theotokion in the same Tone. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Resurrection, and their Theotokia; and a reading from the Commentary on the Gospel. Polyeleos, and both Sessional Hymns of the Apostle; Glory... Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now... Theotokion; and a reading of the Apostle. Graduals, and the Prokimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Resurrection. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," thrice. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Resurrection. Canon of Pascha with the heirmos and with their Theotokia, for 6; and of the Apostle, for 4; and of the Triodion, for 4. And we chant thus: at each ode, Troparion of the Resurrection, one; and of the Feast, one; and of the Trinity, and Theotokion. Katavasia: "The Day of Resurrection." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Apostle, and Ikos, and his Sessional Hymn, two; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; and a reading of the Apostle. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable."[^83] Exapostilarion of Pascha; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, 4; and of the Apostle, 4, with "Glory...", and with his verses; Glory... of the Feast; Both now... "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Resurrection, litanies, and catechesis, and Dismissal, and the customary departure into the narthex. Glory... Both now... Gospel Sticheron, and the First Hour. At the Hours, Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we say the Kontakia, alternating them. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone, for 4; and of the Triodion, Ode 3, for 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, for 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Apostle; Glory... Kontakion of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn, first of the day, and of the Apostle. But the service of Saint Arsenius is omitted, and is chanted when the superior or the head of the church wishes.*

*Concerning that same feast it should be known, if the feast of the holy Apostle John the Theologian falls on Mid-Pentecost Wednesday:*

*Typikon Instruction (continued):*
*On Tuesday at Small Vespers, Stichera of the Apostle, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Apostle from Great Vespers, with their verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; Little Litany, and Dismissal. But if there be no Vigil: at Compline, Kontakion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At Great Vespers, we chant "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, for 4; and of the Apostle, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Entrance. Prokimenon of the day; and readings of the Feast, and of the Apostle. At the Litiya, Sticheron of the temple, and of the Apostle, Idiomela; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, with their verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Feast, twice; and of the Apostle, once; and a reading of the Apostle, in the Apocalypse.[^84] At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Feast; and a reading of the Feast. Polyeleos, and both Sessional Hymns of the Apostle; Glory... Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now... the same of him; and a reading of the Apostle. Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokimenon of the Apostle. "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Apostle. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Apostle. Canon of the Feast, Tone 4, with the heirmos, for 4; and of the Apostle, for 6; and the other Canon of the Feast, Tone 4, for 4. Katavasia: the heirmos of the Feast, and of that Tone, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Apostle and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, two; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; and a reading of the Apostle. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 3; Glory... of the Apostle, Tone 8: "O herald John..."; Both now... of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Litanies, and catechesis, and Dismissal, and the customary departure, and the First Hour, in the narthex. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we say the Kontakia, alternating them. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, from the Canon of Tone 4, Ode 3, for 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, for 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Feast, and of the Apostle; Glory... Kontakion of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel, and Communion Hymn, first of the Feast, then of the Apostle. But the service of Saint Arsenius is chanted on another day.*

*Typikon Instruction (continued):*
*Concerning that same feast it should be known, if it falls on the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman: the service of Mid-Pentecost is omitted, and the service of the Resurrection, of the Samaritan Woman, and of the Theologian is chanted, as was indicated above for the other Sundays.*

*Concerning that same feast, if the feast of the holy Apostle John the Theologian falls on the Wednesday before Ascension (for it is the Apodosis of Pascha):*
*On Tuesday evening, at Small Vespers, Stichera of the Apostle, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the day. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Apostle from Great Vespers, with their verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the day. Troparion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... "All beyond [understanding]"; Little Litany, and Dismissal.*
*If there be no Vigil: at Compline we say the Kontakion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave." At Great Vespers, after the customary Psalm [103], we chant "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Blind Man, for 6; and of the Apostle, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the day: "Who can speak of Thy mighty acts..." Entrance. Prokimenon of the day; and readings of the Apostle. At the Litiya, Sticheron of the temple, and of the Apostle, Idiomela; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... "Lo, it hath been fulfilled." At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection, Tone 5: "Thee, incarnate..."; then Stichera of Pascha, with their verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of Pascha: "The Day of Resurrection"; and "Christ is risen," once. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Apostle, twice; and "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," once; and a reading from the Revelation of the Apostle John.*
*If there be no Vigil: after "Now lettest Thou depart," and after the Trisagion, Troparion of the Resurrection of the Tone; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion; and Dismissal.*
*And we chant the Midnight Office according to custom. After the 1st Trisagion, instead of "Behold, the Bridegroom," we say the Troparion of the Resurrection: "The Word co-beginningless"; Glory... Both now... its Theotokion. After the 2nd [Trisagion], instead of "Remember, O Lord," Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave"; thereafter, "Lord, have mercy," 12 times; and Dismissal. But the prayer "Remember, O Lord," we do not say.*
*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, twice, Tone 5: "The Word co-beginningless"; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... "All beyond comprehension." After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Resurrection; and a reading. After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Blind Man; and a reading. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymns of the Apostle, all once each; and a reading. Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokimenon of the Apostle. "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Apostle. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Apostle. Canon of Pascha without Theotokia, with the heirmos, for 6; and of the Apostle, for 4; and of the Blind Man, for 4. And we chant at each ode: Troparion of the Blind Man, by two; Glory... Triadikon; Both now Typikon Instruction (continued):*
*...Both now... Theotokion. Katavasia, heirmos: "Unto God the Savior," both choirs together. [The Forefeast Canon here is omitted, and we chant it on Tuesday, beforehand.][^85] After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Apostle, and Ikos. Then Hypakoe of Pascha: "Anticipating the morning"; Glory... Sessional Hymn of the Apostle; Both now... of the Blind Man; and a reading of the Apostle. After Ode 6, Kontakion of Pascha, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable." Exapostilarion of Pascha; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Blind Man. At the Praises, Stichera of the Apostle, for 4. Then, "Let God arise," and Stichera of Pascha, with their verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of Pascha, Tone 5: "The Day of Resurrection"; "Christ is risen," once. Great Doxology; Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... "All beyond comprehension"; litanies, and catechesis, and Dismissal, and departure, and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours, Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we alternate the Kontakion of Pascha and of the Apostle. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of Pascha, Ode 3, for 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, for 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Apostle; Glory... Kontakion of the Apostle; Both now... of Pascha. Prokimenon and Alleluia of Pascha, and of the Apostle. Epistle and Gospel of the day, and of the Apostle. Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ"; another: "Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth."*

*Concerning that same feast it should be known: if the feast of the holy Apostle John the Theologian falls on Thursday of Ascension:*
*At Small Vespers, Stichera of the Feast, 3, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Apostle, with their verses, from Great Vespers; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; Little Litany, and Dismissal. If there be no Vigil: at Compline, Kontakion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At Great Vespers, we chant "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, for 6; and of the Apostle, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Entrance. Prokimenon of the day; and readings of the Feast, and of the Apostle. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, and of the Apostle; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, with their verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Feast, twice; and of the Apostle, once; and a reading of the Feast. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Feast, and a reading. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast, and a reading (of the Feast). Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokimenon and Gospel of the Feast Typikon Instruction (continued):*
*...of the Feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast.*
*Canon of the Feast, Tone 5, with the heirmos, for 6; and of the Apostle, for 4; and the other of the Feast, for 4. Katavasia: "Covered by the divine cloud," both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Apostle, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; and a reading of the Apostle. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not chant "More honorable," but we chant the Refrains of the Feast; and unto the troparia of the Apostle: "Holy Apostle and Evangelist John the Theologian, pray unto God for us." Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera for 6: of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 3; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast.*
*Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Apostle; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Litanies, and catechesis, and Dismissal. And holy oil from the lamp of the Saint is given unto the brethren. And the customary departure. And the First Hour, in the narthex. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we alternate the Kontakia of the Feast and of the Apostle. At the Liturgy, the Typika; and from the Canon of the Feast, Tone 5, Ode 3, for 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, for 4. Introit of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast, and of the Apostle; Glory... Kontakion of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn, first of the Feast, then of the Apostle. But the service of Saint Arsenius is omitted, and is chanted whenever the Ecclesiarch decides.*

*Concerning that same [feast] it should be known, if the feast of the holy Apostle John the Theologian falls on the 7th Sunday after Pascha, of the 318 Holy Fathers:[^86] the service of Ascension is omitted, and the service of the Resurrection, and of the Theologian, and of the Holy Fathers is chanted.*
*On Saturday at Small Vespers, Stichera of the Resurrection, for 4; Glory... of the Apostle, Tone 2: "Son of Thunder"; Both now... Theotokion of Small Vespers. At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection, one; then the Aposticha of the Apostle from Great Vespers, with their own verses; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion, according to the Tone. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Fathers; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Little Litany, and Dismissal.*
*At Great Vespers: after the customary Psalm [103], and after the Kathisma; on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and of the Fathers, 4; and of the Apostle, 3; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance. Prokimenon of the day; and readings of the Fathers, and of the Apostle. At the Litiya, Sticheron of the Fathers, Tone 3; and of the Apostle, Idiomela; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast Typikon Instruction (continued):*
*At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion, "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," twice; and of the Apostle, once; and a reading. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Fathers; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Resurrection, with their Theotokia; and a reading from the Commentary on the Gospel. Polyeleos; and all the Sessional Hymns of the Saint; and a reading of the Apostle. Graduals, and the Prokimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Resurrection. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Resurrection, and the rest. Canon of the Resurrection with the heirmos, for 4; and of the Apostle, for 4; and of the Fathers, for 6. Katavasia: "Covered by the divine cloud." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Apostle, and Ikos; and Sessional Hymn of the Fathers; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion; and a reading of the Apostle. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Fathers, and Ikos, and Prologue. Exapostilarion of the Resurrection, and of the Fathers; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... Theotokion. At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and of the Fathers, 2; and of the Apostle with the Doxastikon, 3; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology. Troparion of the Resurrection, litanies, and catechesis, and Dismissal. And the customary departure. Glory... Both now... Gospel Sticheron; and the First Hour, in the narthex. At the Hours, Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Resurrection. At the Third Hour, Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Apostle. After "Our Father," we say the Kontakia of the Apostle and of the Fathers, alternating them.*
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone, for 4; and of the Fathers, Ode 3, for 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, for 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Fathers, and of the Apostle; then Kontakion of the Fathers; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast. Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel, of the Fathers, and of the Apostle. Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord from the heavens"; another: "Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth."*
*(Tailpiece — Decorative woodcut engraving of the Holy Name IHS supported by cherubim and floral vines.)*

## May 9. The Holy Prophet Isaiah; and the Holy Martyr Christopher; and the Translation of the Honorable Relics of Our Father among the Saints, Nicholas the Wonderworker, from Myra to the City of Bari
*His service is written after this service.[^87]*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Prophet, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Cleansed by the coal of the divine, incarnate Word,[^88] the great and glorious Isaiah was entrusted with prophecy, and was accounted worthy to behold the divine glory; having become a herald through his rich wealth of virtue and purity, he foreannounced the coming salvation unto the nations and the divine manifestation.

*Sticheron:*
Having cleansed thy mind and thought, O marvelous Isaiah, thou wast shown to be a well-pleasing instrument of the Spirit, ever struck by His workings and inspirations, and God-wisely attuned, O most glorious one, as thou beheldest Him Who sits upon the exalted throne,[^89] and wast instructed in divine things.

*Sticheron:*
Having seen the divine Seraphim ceaselessly glorifying with thrice-holy understanding the one Lordship of the tri-hypostatic Godhead, O blessed one, and having been instructed, thou wast entrusted with prophetic grace, O marvelous one; therefore, as we celebrate thy all-sacred memory, we bring thee unto Christ as a most fervent intercessor.

*Other Stichera of the Martyr, the same Tone, Podoben: "As Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
O most glorious Christopher, pillar named after Christ,[^90] honorable adornment of grace! Thou didst cast down the dominion of the mighty, and put the tormentors to shame, and didst proclaim Christ as the true King and thy Creator; by Whose divine power thou didst receive from heaven a divine crown and abiding glory.

*Sticheron:*
Illumined by the unapproachable Godhead, ineffable goodness, and steadfast radiance, having dispelled the darkness of idols, thou didst radiantly overthrow the arrogance of the lawless; and encompassed by fire and wounds, having cast down the adversary archer in the judgment court, thou wast revealed as victorious and a triumphant conqueror.

*Sticheron:*
The powers of the heavenly hosts were astonished at thee, and the ranks of martyrs and the ranks of martyrs rejoiced together, the assemblies of the righteous cried aloud in concord, and with the all-originating hand God crowned thee from on high. Therefore we cry: Through the prayers of Christopher, as Thou art bountiful, save all who celebrate his divine solemnity!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion of the Prophet, Tone 2:*
Celebrating the memory of Thy Prophet Isaiah, O Lord, therefore we entreat Thee: Save our souls!

*Another Troparion of the Martyr, Tone 4:*
Adorning thyself with garments of blood, thou standest before the Lord, the King of hosts, O ever-memorable Christopher; whence with the bodiless hosts and the martyrs thou singest with thrice-holy and awesome sweet melody; therefore by thy prayers save thy flock!

*Canon of the Saints, the Prophet and the Martyr, the composition of Theophanes, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou didst lead forth the people from the bondage of Egypt, and didst cover the chariots of Pharaoh and his power.[^91]*

*Troparion:*
Enlighten me, O Lord my God, and direct me radiantly to praise the glorious and honorable solemnity of Thy divine prophet.

*Troparion:*
The God-proclaiming trumpet of Isaiah, having sounded throughout the earth, summoned the nourishees of the Church to celebrate his memory.

*Troparion:*
Having steadfastly subjected the carnal passions to the soul's reasoned thought, O God-bearer, thou wast accounted worthy to behold the glory of the Almighty.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Come, all ye Christ-bearers today, let us piously hymn the memory of Christopher, the God-wise witness of truth!

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Having manfully striven against deceit, O passion-bearing martyr, strengthened by the might of Christ, thou didst trample down the threats of the tormentors.

*Theotokion:*
Foreseeing thee as a rod, O Birthgiver of God, and the incarnate Word as a flower from thee,[^92] the glorious Isaiah proclaimed [thee], O all-pure one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.[^93]*

*Troparion:*
Having received the full spiritual radiance, O glorious one, thou didst proclaim the ineffable mystery of the Word's dispensation.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst ascend unto the height of heaven, and didst display the radiance of piety, having exposed the weakness of idols' delusion.

*Troparion:*
Isaiah prophesies the most honorable Cornerstone being laid in Zion,[^94] holding together the universe, Christ the Master.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Protected by the weapon of the Cross, O martyr Christopher, thou didst endure wounds, and rejoicest as thou enjoyest Him Who is ever desired.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Thy flesh was indeed cut by wounds, but thy God-loving soul was nourished with love, sensing the enjoyment of the Kingdom, O Christopher.

*Theotokion:*
The saying of Isaiah is truly fulfilled: for the Virgin brought forth the incarnate Giver of life and Savior of our souls.[^95]

*Sessional Hymn of the Prophet, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Having cleansed thy soul exceedingly from carnal passions, thou didst behold the glory of God Who is over all, O most wealthy prophet, shining forth prophecy with radiant beams: As a young child, thou didst say, the Creator is born,[^96] saving us from corruption through thy prayers.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn of the Martyr, the same Tone, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Having accomplished the contest of martyrdom, O glorious one, thou didst receive the crown of victory from God, O blessed Christopher: entreat Him to deliver us from misfortunes!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Proclaiming the coming of the divine manifestation upon earth, O Christ God, the prophet cried aloud with joy: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^97]*

*Troparion:*
Having ascended unto the summit of virtue, thou didst purely converse with the Desired One, and joyfully didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Isaiah was entrusted with prophetic grace, and was shown to be a dwelling of God, and was accounted worthy of heavenly mansions, of incorruptible glory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst say figuratively: Let the islands be renewed,[^98] referring to the churches of the gentiles; filled with the Holy Spirit, O glorious one, Thou wast an initiate of ineffable mysteries.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Thou didst receive another inheritance, O martyr, having striven on earth after Christ; and thou didst manfully quench the idolatrous delusion of the cruel demon.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Every temptation the serpent brought against thee, O martyr, even as against forefather Adam of old; but being overcome he was put to shame, as he stretched out snares against thee.

*Theotokion:*
[...from the prophet...] A Child is given unto us from the Prophetess,[^99] born without travail of the Birthgiver of God, Whose government is upon His shoulder,[^100] as the divine Isaiah proclaimed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thy enlightenment, O Lord, send down upon us, and from the gloom of transgressions deliver us, O Good One, granting Thy peace.*

*Troparion:*
Beholding the mystery hidden from on high, and the ancient counsel of the Most High, even the dispensation concerning us, O speaker of divine things, thou didst proclaim.

*Troparion:*
The ever-flowing fountain of Thy passions, O Lord, washes away our passions; for Thou hast come, O Savior, as Isaiah the prophet taught.

*Troparion:*
Pious Isaiah, having gone forth from God, proclaimed unto those who knew not the Father, the Word, and the Holy Spirit, in one nature, one Trinity.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
By the illumination of grace thou didst catch men, and the frenzy of godless tormentors didst thou manifestly destroy on earth, O wise one, clothed with working from on high.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Having manifestly chosen another delight rather than that of earth, O wise Christopher, thou didst steadfastly endure the pains of torment, strengthened by the Cross.

*Theotokion:*
Purely seated upon a cloud, O Lord, Mother sung of in hymns, Isaiah clearly saw Thee casting down the Egyptian idols.[^101]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah, praying in the sea-monster, cried aloud: From corruption deliver me, O Jesus, King of powers!*

*Troparion:*
Exalted in spirit, Isaiah beheld upon the throne of Lordship the glory shining forth,[^102] hymned with a thrice-holy voice in threefold number.

*Troparion:*
Unto thy lips which were in pain, a Seraph brought a coal, O Isaiah, taking it with tongs, and dedicated a prophet unto God, mystically consecrated.[^103]

*Troparion:*
Beholding from afar the fulfillment of his prophecies, Isaiah noetically rejoices with the angels, and exulting glorifies Him Who fulfilled his proclamation.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Taking up battle not against blood and flesh, the noble one conquered principalities and demons, strengthened by the invisible power of the Son of God.[^104]

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Having fittingly loved the summit of desires, O passion-bearer, being scraped thou didst not feel the pains, ever lifting up thy thought unto thy Master.

*Theotokion:*
Beholding the Virgin who conceived in her womb and brought forth the incarnate Word, the Son the Son of God, glorious Isaiah the prophet now rejoices.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "In Prayers" (Въ моли́твахъ неусыпа́ющую):*
Having received the gift of prophecy, O prophet-martyr, God-proclaiming Isaiah, thou didst explain unto all the Incarnation of the Lord, crying aloud with all thy voice unto the ends of the earth: Behold, the Virgin shall conceive in the womb![^105]

*Ikos:*
Come, all ye faithful who are in the regions, let us worthily praise Isaiah, marvelous among the prophets: for having received the culmination of prophecy, he enlightens the world, having proclaimed unto all together the mystery of the divine union, crying out with all of them: God is with us, invisible![^106] Behold, the Virgin shall conceive in the womb!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Abrahamic children of old in Babylon trampled down the furnace flame, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
Beholding our saving deliverance coming, Isaiah cried aloud: He shall come; blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Crowned with the grace of prophecy and the crown of martyrdom, Isaiah [proclaimed]: My strength and my song;[^107] blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The boundaries of piety and the laws of life didst thou set down with a God-bearing tongue, O God-inspired prophet: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Running the path of martyrdom with endurance, Christopher dwells in the heavenly mansions, chanting: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Christopher smoothed down the flame of impiety with the dew of piety, calling out in hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Having seen the birthgiving transcending nature and the brightness of purity, Isaiah cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*In Babylon the children, inflamed with divine zeal, manfully trampled down the threat of the tormentor and the flame, and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Clothed figuratively with the bridal robe of joy, glorious Isaiah now gladly rejoices, and enjoying divine food, with the angels he declares: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Those who were formerly separated, deceived by demonic words, obstinately departed from the pure land, the wretched ones, sequentially following the voices of the prophet, and they are not able to chant: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Leaving deceit behind, the multitudes of the nations ran unto the divine radiance, and were betrothed unto the Church of Christ,[^108] as Isaiah of old said, and now in hymns they chant: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
The impetuously raging wave of idol frenzy was cut off by martyr-like, firm, steadfast resistances of those who accept tribulations for Christ and cry out: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Beautifully arrayed now in a royal purple robe dyed with thy blood, O martyr, invincible Christopher, thou dancest with the angelic choirs and singest unto thy Creator: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
The Virgin Maiden, Mother of God, holds as an infant the Word Who gives life unto all and hath created all things, as Isaiah said beforehand,[^109] and rejoicing she cried out: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve indeed, through the affliction of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, through the bud of thy childbearing, didst cause blessing to blossom forth upon the world: therefore we all magnify thee.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst radiantly stand before the throne of the Master of powers, O God-accepting one; and having enjoyed His glory, gathering the fruits of thy piety, O all-blessed and glorious one: therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Come now, ye who desire the light of the prophetic gift, read zealously the book of Isaiah, filled with divine understanding; let us attend with steadfast desire, that we may be filled with divine beams!

*Troparion:*
Manifestly illumined with the spirit of brightness, O God-bearing Isaiah, pray that all who in faith celebrate thy honorable and light-bearing memory may obtain salvation: that we all may magnify thee!

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Having received a radiant crown as a victor, O all-glorious Christopher, thou reignest together with the Master Who strengthened thee in torments and stretched out His life-bearing hand to overcome the author of evil.

*Troparion of the Martyr:*
Having been radiantly washed with martyr's blood, thou wast illuminated, O most marvelous one; and now rejoicing, thou hast entered into the virginal bridal chamber with the Bridegroom: cease not, O glorious martyr, to ask peace for the world!

*Theotokion:*
The noetic Coal whom Isaiah saw,[^110] O pure Bride of God, thou bearest in thy arms, O Mother of God, united unto our likeness, and radiantly granting salvation unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee.

## May 9. The Translation of the Honorable Relics of Our Father among the Saints, Nicholas the Wonderworker, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia

*(Headpiece — Decorative woodcut engraving spanning both columns.)*
*Service of Our Father among the Saints, Nicholas, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia, the Wonderworker.*

*At Vespers, we sing: "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon.*

*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast; and of the Saint, 5, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Running Unto Heaven" (На не́бо текꙋ́щее):*
Ascending unto the heaven of most exalted virtues, thou wast revealed as a wonderworker in Myra, O glorious one; resting in the body in Bari, in spirit thou visitest the faithful everywhere, and deliverest all from misfortunes, worthily receiving honor, and art glorified among hierarchs, O blessed favorite of Christ; calling upon thee now in supplication, we say with compunction: Pray, O Hierarch Nicholas, unto the Savior of all, to pacify the world and save our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Assembled with suppliant hymns, O lovers of the feast, let us joyfully praise the honorable translation of our protector, who enlightens all lands with wonders, and divinely comforts the sorrowing, and delivers the wronged; who by almsgiving placed God in his debt,[^111] and for this cause received a hundredfold recompense; unto whom we all cry aloud: Pray, O Hierarch Nicholas, unto the Savior of all, to pacify the world and save our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Having attained the angelic habitations by virtues, thou standest before thy Master Christ, unto Whom thou wast a well-obedient servant; and pouring forth myrrh from thy shrine, aiding the faithful by thy wonders, and bringing the pious unto God, not from Bari do we now call upon thee, but from the Jerusalem on high, where thou joyfully keepest festival with apostles, prophets, and hierarchs: Pray, O God-bearing Nicholas, unto the Savior of all, to pacify the world and save our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
The adornment of hierarchs and glory of the fathers, the fountain of wonders and great protector of the faithful: having assembled, O lovers of the feast, let us hymn with praises in song, saying: Rejoice, keeper of the people of Myra, and honorable primate, and immovable pillar! Rejoice, all-radiant luminary, who dost enlighten the ends of the world with wonders! Rejoice, divine joy of the sorrowful, and fervent intercessor for the wronged! And now, O all-blessed Nicholas, cease not to pray unto Christ God for those who honor with faith and love *Glory... (continued):*
...honor with faith and love thy ever-joyful and all-festal memory.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Entrance. Prokimenon of the day; and the Readings.*

*The Reading from Proverbs. [Chapter 10, Composite]:*[^112]
The memory of the righteous is with praise, and the blessing of the Lord is upon his head. Blessed is the man that finds wisdom, and the mortal that knows understanding; for it is better to buy her than treasures of gold and silver. And she is more honorable than precious stones, and nothing honorable is worthy of her. For length of days and years of life are in her right hand, and in her left hand are riches and glory. Out of her mouth proceeds righteousness, and she bears law and mercy upon her tongue. Hear me, therefore, O children, for I will speak honorable things; and blessed is the man that shall keep my ways: for my goings forth are the goings forth of life, and desire is prepared from the Lord. For this cause I entreat you, and put forth my voice unto the sons of men: for I, wisdom, have built; counsel, understanding, and knowledge have I called upon. Counsel is mine, and security; mine is understanding, and mine is strength. I love them that love me, and those that seek me shall find grace. Understand craftiness, therefore, ye simple ones, and apply your hearts, ye uninstructed. Listen unto me yet again, for I will speak honorable things; and I will open right things from my lips, for my throat shall meditate truth, and lying lips are an abomination before me. All the words of my mouth are with righteousness; there is nothing crooked or perverse in them. They are all right to them that understand, and plain to them that find knowledge. For I will teach you the truth, that your hope may be in the Lord, and ye may be filled with the Spirit.

*The Reading from Proverbs. [Chapters 10 and 11]:*[^113]
The mouth of the righteous drops wisdom, but the tongue of the unrighteous shall perish. The lips of righteous men drop grace, but the mouth of the impious is perverse. A false balance is an abomination before the Lord, but a just weight is acceptable unto Him. Wheresoever insolence enters, there also is dishonor; but the mouth of the humble meditates wisdom. The perfection of the upright shall guide them, and the slipperiness of the overthrowers shall shepherd them. Riches shall not profit in the day of wrath, but righteousness shall deliver from death. When the righteous man dies, he leaves regret; but the destruction of the impious is sudden and a subject of mockery. The righteousness of the blameless *Reading from Proverbs (continued):*
...directs his ways, but unrighteousness falls into impiety. The righteousness of upright men shall deliver them, but the lawless are caught in their own lack of counsel. When a righteous man dies, his hope does not perish; but the boast of the impious perishes. The righteous man escapes from the hunt, and in his place the impious is delivered up. In the mouth of the impious is a snare for the citizens, but the perception of the righteous is prosperous. In the good fortune of the righteous the city prospers, and at the destruction of the impious there is rejoicing. In the blessing of the upright the city is exalted, but by the mouths of the impious it is overthrown. He that is void of understanding mocks his citizens, but a wise man keeps silence.[^114]

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon. [Chapter 4]:*[^115]
Though the righteous man happen to die early, he shall be at rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is the stature of old age. Having been well-pleasing unto God, he was beloved; and living among sinners, he was translated. He was taken away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the fascination of wickedness darkens good things, and the roving of desire perverts an innocent mind. Having been perfected in a short time, he fulfilled long years; for his soul was pleasing unto the Lord: therefore hastened He to take him away from the midst of wickedness. But the peoples saw, and understood not, neither did they lay this in their mind: that grace and mercy are in His holy ones, and visitation among His elect.

*At the Litiya, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 2:*
O Father Nicholas, though the land of Myra be silent, yet the whole world, enlightened by thee, through fragrant myrrh and a multitude of wonders, cries aloud with grateful hymns; and we also, the condemned who were saved by thee, together with those who dwell in Myra, singing cry aloud: Pray that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 4. Idiomelon:*
O Father Nicholas, being a vessel of myrrh of the All-Holy Spirit, thou dost breathe fragrance in Myra with divine aromas; for having become an imitator of the apostles of Christ, thou dost compass the universe with the accounts of thy wonders; therefore, appearing in a dream unto those afar off as though near, thou dost deliver from death those who were about to die by unjust judgment *Tone 4. Idiomelon (continued):*
...saving gloriously from many misfortunes those who call upon thee; therefore free us also from the cruel things that assail us, by thy prayers, who ever praise thee in God.

*Tone 4. Idiomelon:*
The fruit of thy virtues, O venerable father, has enlightened the hearts of the faithful; for who, having heard of thy boundless humility, did not marvel at thy patience? Thy gentleness toward the poor, thy consolation unto the sorrowing: thou didst teach all things in a God-befitting manner, O Hierarch Nicholas; and now thou art crowned with an unfading crown, praying for our souls.

*Tone 4. Idiomelon:*
Thou didst run for the praise of the Lord, O Nicholas, in temporal life, and He glorified thee in the heavenly and true life; therefore having gained boldness toward Him, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O good and faithful servant, laborer in the vineyard of Christ! Thou didst bear the burden of the day,[^116] thou didst increase the talent given unto thee,[^117] and unto those who came after thee thou didst not bear grudge; therefore the heavenly gates were opened unto thee; enter into the joy of thy Lord,[^118] and pray for us, O Holy Nicholas!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
As an unsetting star* of the all-radiant Sun,* God-inspired,* telling the saving glory of God,* a divine herald,* an enlightener of the nations,* a river pouring forth waters of understanding* and giving the hearts of the faithful to drink:* let us call Nicholas the most glorious hierarch blessed!

*Verse: Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^119]*

*Sticheron:*
Thy journeys were holy across the sea,* from Myra of Lycia unto the city of Bari;* for thy shrine was taken from the tomb,* and from the East came unto the West,* as monks piously followed,* who beside thy tomb* reverently served,* by the nod of the Master of all,* O most glorious Nicholas!

*Verse: Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.[^120]*

*Sticheron:*
By thy seafaring,* thou didst make the water blessed,* and having arrived at the city of Bari,* thou didst pour forth myrrh, O glorious one,* and healing incurable afflictions,* thou wast a refuge unto those there,* and a protector and deliverer,* praying unto the King and Savior of all,* O Hierarch Nicholas!

*Glory... Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
O man of God *Glory... (continued):*
...O man of God and faithful servant, minister of the Lord, man of desires,[^121] chosen vessel,[^122] pillar and ground of the Church,[^123] heir of the Kingdom: cease not crying aloud for us unto the Lord!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
The day of radiant solemnity has arrived; the city of Bari rejoices, and with it the whole universe keeps festival with songs and spiritual hymns; for today is the sacred solemnity in the translation of the honorable and plenteously healing relics of the Hierarch and Wonderworker Nicholas: for like an unsetting sun he has shone forth with light-beaming rays, dispelling the darkness of temptations and misfortunes from those who cry aloud in faith: Save us, O great Nicholas, our protector!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, and of the Saint.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Feast, twice.*
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph Was Amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Thou hast made Thy saint marvelous in the lands, O Master, granting him the grace of wonders, to drive away various afflictions from men, and to deliver those in bonds, and to rescue the wronged from cruel tribulations; unto whom we cry aloud in supplication: O Hierarch Nicholas, deliver us from our enemies! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Who can recount the multitude of my unclean thoughts and the burden of unseemly imaginings, O all-immaculate one? And the assaults of my bodiless foes and their malice, who can declare? But by thy supplication, O Good One, grant me deliverance from them all!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Being perfect in thyself through life, thou wast revealed as a hierarch of God the Most High, strengthened by Whom thou didst stop the mouths of heretics; therefore we radiantly celebrate thy translation, magnifying Christ God. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
O unwedded, pure Virgin Birthgiver of God, sole protection and shelter of the faithful: deliver from misfortunes, sorrows, and cruel circumstances all those who have their hope in thee, O Maiden, and save our souls by thy divine supplications!

*Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokimenon, Tone 4:*
*Honorable in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?[^124]*

*Prokimenon Verse (continued):*
*...unto me? "Let every breath..." Gospel according to John, pericope 35, from the midpoint [John 10:9–16].[^125]*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Heir of God, joint-partaker with Christ, minister of the Lord, Holy Nicholas: according to thy name, so also is thy life; for gray hair judged understanding,[^126] the brightness of thy face bore witness, the inward guilelessness attested gentleness, thy word was quiet, thy life glorious, and thy dormition with the saints: pray for our souls!

*Canon of the Feast with the heirmos, for 6; and of the Saint, for 8.*
*Canon of the Saint, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us send up a song, O ye peoples, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!*

*Troparion:*
Receive the hymn of my lips, O Benefactor Christ, and make clear my tongue, not remembering my many sins, that I may hymn the honorable life of Thy hierarch, in memory of the translation of his relics.

*Troparion:*
Having received the gift of wonderworking and crowns of endurance, adorned with the hierarchy, pray unto God, O father, to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto us who glorify thy translation.

*Troparion:*
O shepherd of the flock of Christ, father, thou art sent unto other sheep, unto the Latin people,[^127] that thou mayest amaze all by thy wonders and bring them unto Christ, O blessed one: unceasingly pray unto Him for us also!

*Theotokion:*
Having once been far off, we gentile peoples, through thy childbearing, O Birthgiver of God, have drawn near unto God: for thou didst bring forth Him Who deified me and freed me from the ancient curse by His blood.

*Another Canon of Nicholas, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word for the Queen and Mother; and I will be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I will sing her wonders.*

*Troparion:*
Enlighten my soul and heart, I pray Thee, O Light-Giver, Creator of creation; grant me the gift of singing to praise Thy most honorable favorite, through whom deliver the world from sorrow!

*Troparion:*
Having found divine wisdom, thou didst put to good use the talent entrusted to thee; for by it having destroyed crafty deceit, thou didst make the people wise by divine teaching.

*Theotokion:*
The Pre-eternal is conceived, and the Perfect is made new; from the Maiden a Child is born, Whom thou didst proclaim in two essences, but in one Hypostasis, O God-blessed one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Builder of the vault of heaven and Founder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!*

*Troparion:*
Guileless in disposition and gentle in manner, having an angelic life, O God-blessed Nicholas: cease not praying for us unto the only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
What city, O blessed one, has thee not as a helper? What soul in grief calls not upon thy name? Or what place dost thou not visit in spirit, amazing all by thy wonders, O Nicholas?

*Troparion:*
The city of Bari indeed received thy body, but thy spirit is in the Jerusalem on high, where thou keepest festival together with the prophets, apostles, and hierarchs, praying for us unto the only Lover of mankind.

*Theotokion:*
Ask enlightenment for me, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, who am darkened by many sins, O thou who didst bring forth the Light for the whole world; and drive far away the dark mist of passions, O only God-blessed one!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*Establish Thy singers, O Birthgiver of God, thou living and plentiful fountain, who have gathered as a spiritual choir; and in Thy divine glory vouchsafe them crowns of glory!*

*Troparion:*
In sorrows we have found thee a strong consolation; in darkness, light; and from evils, deliverance, O we who are held fast by afflictions, most sacred father!

*Troparion:*
O Hierarch Nicholas, having become the mouth of God, from the mouth of the hostile wolf thou didst deliver the people, O glorious one, and didst bring a gift unto the Creator, giving healing unto all.

*Theotokion:*
The one Son and Word, beyond word, willed to be born in the flesh from thee, O all-pure Mother of God, having chosen for Himself a servant and teacher for the peoples, the Hierarch Nicholas.

*Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos.*
*And Sessional Hymn of the Hierarch, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
The translation of thy honorable relics became for us the institution of a radiant festival, O most lauded hierarch of the Lord; which we piously praise, joyfully honoring thee as the beauty of the faithful of the unsetting Sun of Light *Sessional Hymn (continued):*
...adornment, O Nicholas! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou didst mount Thy horses, Thine apostles, O Lord, and didst take their reins into Thy hands, and Thy riding became salvation unto those who faithfully sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Illumined by the light of the Most High, O hierarch, enlighten my heart and drive away the darkness of sin, that rejoicing I may sing of thy holy translation.

*Troparion:*
Having ascended the ladder of virtues, O blessed one, thou wast revealed as a wonderworker throughout all the earth, O Nicholas; and therefore the people of Bari receive thy holy relics from Myra.

*Troparion:*
Faithful kings boast in thee, and Christ-loving princes call upon thee as a helper in battles; and we, the unworthy, stir thee up to pray unto God for us, O Nicholas.

*Theotokion:*
The depth of thy mystery amazes the angelic ranks, O Birthgiver of God, and thy icon confounds the demons; which icon we venerate, honoring thee as the Mother of God.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*Perceiving the unfathomable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the Prophet Habbakuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
By communion in the primordial divine light, O God-proclaimer, Thou wast revealed as a second light, enlightening the darkened with radiance, as a lover of virtue preeminent in glory.

*Troparion:*
Deliver thy flock from the tempest of sins, and from misfortunes and adverse waves, ever leading us unto painless havens by thy saving prayers.

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure one, who ineffably didst bear the Radiance of the ineffable Light from on high, Who is exceedingly rich in mercy: enrich us who have grown impoverished with divine gifts!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Dispel the darkness of my soul, O Christ God, Giver of light, having cast out the primordial darkness of the abyss; and grant me the light of Thy commandments, O Word, that rising early I may glorify Thee!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst have thy throne in Myra, thou didst translate thy body to Bari, and in spirit thou abidest in heaven, O Hierarch Nicholas, with the apostles as their successor; together with them pray for us who sing of thy translation.

*Troparion:*
Hope of all Christians, and great protector of the wronged, healer of the sick, consoler of the sorrowful, and mediator from men unto God:

...ask peace for our king,[^128] and save us from the invasion of the heathen!

*Troparion:*
My soul, submerged in many passions, O Nicholas, and slain by the fall into sin, do thou revive by thy prayers and direct unto repentance, that with all the faithful I may glorify thy translation.

*Theotokion:*
Him before Whom the myriads of angels and the multitudes of seraphim stand in awe, and Whom rational creation trembles before, Him thou dost nourish as an Infant, O Virgin; Whom seeing iconographically in thine arms, we ever venerate.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.*

*Troparion:*
Having gained beautiful feet through much wonderworking, thou didst proclaim good tidings of good things unto all,[^129] making men free from the ancient enmity; by thy divine teaching, save us, O chief hierarch!

*Troparion:*
By God's good pleasure mounting upon the sea's back, and with reverent men traversing the wide deep, thou didst come unto the city of Bari, O blessed Nicholas.

*Theotokion:*
A shield unto the faithful, O Virgin Mother of God, we truly glorify thee, crying out unto thee with the angels: Rejoice, blessed and full of grace, awesome tidings and proclamation, and marvelous resting place of every good creation!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*As Thou didst deliver the prophet from the nethermost depth,[^130] O Christ God, so deliver me also from my sins, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, and guide my life, I pray Thee!*

*Troparion:*
As from a vessel of ointment, from thy divine church we abundantly receive health for bodies and purification for souls, O Hierarch Nicholas; for by grace thou pourest forth wonders unto those who hope in thee with unwavering faith.

*Troparion:*
From various tribulations, cruel perils, and sorrows that encompass us, and from various evils, O father, as Christ's most glorious favorite, deliver thy servants who dutifully honor thy holy translation.

*Troparion:*
Numbed with the frost of sin and buffeted by worldly wind, having seen the likeness of thine image as another sun, O father, may I be warmed by thy prayers, O minister of Christ, Nicholas!

*Theotokion:*

...The earthly mind knows not heavenly mysteries, nor can a creature comprehend the mind of the Creator; for the Lord's nativity from a virgin surpasses the human mind; praying unto her in faith, let us receive forgiveness of sins.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*Celebrating this divine and all-honorable festival of the Mother of God, O ye God-wise, come, let us clap our hands, glorifying God Who was born of her!*

*Troparion:*
Having prepared God's people for salvation through Him Who appeared in the flesh because of His great goodness, O proclaimer Nicholas and teacher of the gentiles: save us by thy guidance!

*Troparion:*
Having had the sea as a way and waters as paths,[^131] beseech the Lord that we may cross the sea of this life without harm, and by the path of tears reach the heavenly Kingdom.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the gate of the King of heaven, and the temple of His glory, O all-hymned Virgin; open unto us the doors of mercy, and lead us by thy prayers into the mansions of heavenly glory!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Thy relics arose like a star from the East unto the West, O Hierarch Nicholas; and the sea was sanctified by thy journey, and the city of Bari receives grace through thee; for for our sake thou hast been revealed as an eminent, marvelous, and merciful wonderworker.

*Ikos:*
Let us now hymn the hierarch with songs, the shepherd and teacher unto the people of Myra, that by his prayers we may be enlightened; for he was revealed entirely pure, incorrupt in spirit, offering unto Christ an unblemished, pure sacrifice, acceptable unto God, as a hierarch purified in soul and body. Therefore being truly the defender of the Church and her champion, he is an eminent, marvelous, and merciful wonderworker.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the plain of Dura, of old the tormentor set up a furnace for the torture of the God-bearers; wherein the three children hymned the one God, singing triply, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^132]*

*Troparion:*
Today the angels of God rejoice, and the host of demons laments; for thou hast saved people from calamity, and driven away dark spirits. Therefore worthily art thou called the conqueror. For this cause we cry aloud unto the Lord: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Those held fast by sorrow of infirmity, afflicted with various diseases, with faith unto thy shrine hasten, and having received thy mercy, they depart swiftly, joyfully singing unto the Lord: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Before thee stand the assemblies of Orthodox people, O blessed one, and the teachers of the Church boast in thee; kings also glorify thee as a protector; and we pray unto thee: Save us, O Nicholas, who sing unto the Lord: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
How shall I hymn my Savior, Whom the angels praise with trembling? But do thou, O Mother, instruct me, and by thy compassion move the merciful heart of thy Son to have mercy on us who hope in thee, that praying unto Him for us, thou mayest deliver us from torment.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*The God-wise worshipped not the creature rather than the Creator,[^133] but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
When darkness encompassed all human understanding, do thou by thy word grant healing as with salt,[^134] and teach us to sing unto the Lord: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
O thy most glorious wonders which thou didst work! For thou healest diseases and deliverest from tribulations, bidding all to cry aloud unto the Lord: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
A protector and a wall unto our humility art thou, O pure Mother of God; therefore we who are thy servants ever cry aloud unto the Lord: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Ye angels and heavens, bless, sing, and exalt throughout the ages Him Who sits upon the throne of glory and is unceasingly glorified as God!*

*Troparion:*
The angels of heaven receive thee as a minister, the prophets and apostles meet thee as a fellow-servant, the Lord Himself receives thee; and we sinners ask for mercy:

*Troparion:*
Great authority was given thee by God, O Nicholas: the sea obeys thee, the air heeds thee, and the nations submit, beholding thy most glorious wonders.

*Troparion:*
Unto the new Israel, the Christian people, O Savior, Thou hast shown Thy hierarch Nicholas to be more glorious than Moses, delivering us from all evils.

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother of God, ask purification of sins, and stretch forth thy hand unto me, O Sovereign Lady, who am submerged in the depth of grief, that rising in hope, I may exalt thee throughout all ages!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*The Nativity of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious children in the furnace; prefigured then, but now actualized, it stirs the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him throughout all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst spring up as a fragrant flower, O glorious one of the land of Myra, offering unto all from on high fragrant, healing gifts, unto those who praise thee and sing: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him throughout all ages!

*Troparion:*
Distilling the sweetness of God's grace as the Mount of Olives distills oil, thou didst have an untroubled path across the sea by the grace of the Lord, unto Whom we sweetly cry: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him throughout all ages!

*Theotokion:*
With the wonder of transcendent choirs, thou givest birth and remainest ever virgin; for thou didst bear the Word Who became man, immutable in His divinity, unto Whom we all sing: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him throughout all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who hath raised up a horn of salvation for us in the house of His servant David; wherein the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and hath guided us into the way of peace.[^135]*

*Troparion:*
Blessed be the Lord our God, for He has glorified the saint in distant lands, overflowing with streams of wonders, healing all in Myra and among the Latins, and visiting Rus'[^136] by prayer!

*Troparion:*
With a marvelous sleep thou didst fall asleep, and for the benefit of Bari thou didst dispatch thy body, having become a sleepless protector unto all who call upon thee in faith by thy prayers, O Nicholas.

*Triadikon:*
Blessed be the Creator of heaven and earth, God the Almighty, the unoriginate and all-powerful Father, and the Son Who shone forth before the ages from the bosom of the Father, the co-eternal Word through Whom all things were made that concern us, and the Holy Spirit Who proceeds: O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee!

*Theotokion:*
By rhetorical artistry I attain not to praise thee worthily, who art greater than the heavenly hosts and more honorable than all creation, having borne in the flesh God and Creator; beseech Him, O Birthgiver of God, that He may have mercy on us on the Day of Judgment.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
*Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit, being enlightened Another [Canon]. Heirmos (continued):*
...and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and crying aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Birthgiver of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:*
Thy memory today brings festival; the apostolic choir and the assembly of martyrs rejoice, as do the spirits of the righteous; and we faithful glorify thee with songs, crying aloud: Deliver us from all tribulations, O hierarch of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Thy most glorious memory has shone forth upon us, brightly enlightening the faithful, O saint of Christ, wise Nicholas. Therefore we beseech thee: Remember us all, and deliver us from every adverse tribulation by thy prayers.

*Theotokion:*
Mortify our carnal passions and lusts, O pure Mother of God; calm the tempest of our passions, still the tossing of our thoughts, and strengthen the hearts of those who faithfully honor thee as protector, O blessed and all-pure one!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Жє́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Great are thy wonders, O most holy Nicholas: for thou didst appear in a dream unto the God-wise Constantine, and didst save the three soldiers condemned without guilt unto death;[^137] so also save us from sudden and eternal death, O most holy Nicholas, hierarch of the Lord! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera for 4, Tone 1. Idiomela:*
Gazing steadfastly upon the height of understanding, and beholding the unrevealed depth of wisdom, thou hast enriched the world with teachings, O father; ever pray unto Christ for us, O Hierarch Nicholas!

*Sticheron:*
O man of God and faithful servant, and steward of His mysteries, and man of spiritual desires, living pillar and inspired image:[^138] for the church of Myra, wondering, received thee as a divine treasure, and an intercessor for our souls.

*Tone 2:*
A rule of faith and image of meekness Christ God revealed thee unto thy flock, O Hierarch Nicholas; for while thou sheddest fragrance in Myra, thy divine achievements shine brightly everywhere, O protector of orphans and widows. Therefore pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
O Father Nicholas, the reliquary of thy relics enriches Myra with myrrh, wherein also the bound men condemned unjustly who were condemned, thou didst appear in a dream unto the emperor, and by thy appearance didst deliver them from death, from bonds, and from prison; so now also, as then and always, appear by thy presence, praying for our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Let us trumpet with the trumpet of songs, let us dance festively, and let us exult, rejoicing in the solemn festival of the God-bearing father! Let kings and princes come together, [praising him] who through a fearful appearance in a dream persuaded the emperor to release the three commanders held unjustly. Let shepherds and teachers hymn him, gathering together to praise the shepherd who is a zealous follower of the Good Shepherd; the sick, a physician; those in perils, a deliverer; sinners, an advocate; the poor, a treasure; those in afflictions, a consoler; travelers, a companion; those upon the sea, a pilot; and all, everywhere warmly anticipating, praising the great hierarch, let us say thus: O most holy Nicholas, come quickly, deliver us from present calamity, and save thy flock by thy prayers!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint.*
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*
*Great Doxology. Litanies.*
*And the Dismissal.*
*The brethren are anointed with holy oil from the lamp of the saint.*
*And the First Hour, and the final dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Feast, the appointed ode; and of the Saint, Ode 3, for 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.[^139]*
*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song, for marvelous things hath the Lord done.[^140]*
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 335 [Hebrews 13:17–21].[^141]*
*Alleluia, Tone 2: Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.[^142]*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 24 [Luke 6:17–23].[^143]*
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^144]*

## May 10. The Holy Apostle Simon the Zealot

*At Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon. Stichera for 8: of the Feast, 3; and of the Apostle, 5, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Marvelous Simon,* praise of the apostles,* like a shining ray traversed the ends of the earth,* driving away the darkness of polytheism* with his saving and light-bearing* divine teachings,* and enlightening all* to glorify the one Godhead in three Hypostases,* undivided and unsevered. *Twice.*

*Sticheron:*
Simon the Apostle,* having traversed the ends of the earth* like a fragrant wheel,[^145]* burned up all the frenzy of idolatry,* and having enlightened all with piety,* kindled bright rays of grace for the faithful;* for he enlightened all* to glorify the one Godhead in three Hypostases,* undivided and unsevered. *Twice.*

*Sticheron:*
