Simon the Apostle,* most radiant and most marvelous,* teacher of the gentiles,* divine net* that drew us up from the abyss of perdition* by lofty theology,* true preacher,* wisely brought all unto the knowledge of Christ today,* and set forth his achievements as a splendid banquet.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Named after zeal wast thou called, O Simon, most marvelous; zealous with zeal wast thou for God the Almighty, and He showed thee full of divine wonders; for having ancient zeal, O blessed one, thou wast named after zeal,[^146] and didst acquire a name concordant with thy character. Therefore being on high, dwelling with the heavenly choirs, pray unto the transcendent Word, Christ our God, to grant us great mercy!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day. And the three readings of the 8th day of this month [May 8, Saint John the Theologian].[^147] Litiya of the Apostle.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "O All-Lauded Martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*
Thou art full of the torrent of delight,[^148] and having wisely given those who warmly desire it to drink of the knowledge of God,* imitating Christ:* unto Whom thou hast now joyfully departed,* clothed in the all-radiant glory of the Trinity* and in peace passing all telling.*

*Verse: Into all Verse (continued):*
*...the earth hath their sound gone forth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^149]*

*Sticheron:*
O most marvelous Simon,* contemplating the beauty of God,* and beholding it as far as is attainable,* enjoying this by superior union,* and deified by adoption,* thou didst reach the One desired and blessed,* rejoicing, O ever-memorable preacher of Christ,* dwelling now with the angels.*

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.[^150]*

*Sticheron:*
All-blessed Simon,* thou wast shown to be a faithful mediator between God and men;* and now make Christ merciful unto us* by thy bold prayers;* cease not ever praying unto the Lord* to grant our souls peace* and great mercy!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Like a high-soaring eagle thou didst traverse creation, O most sacred Simon, disciple of the Savior and apostle; and having consumed all the idol's deceit like matter by thy fire-speaking dogmas, thou didst bring the nations out of the depth of ignorance unto the knowledge of God. And now pray unto Christ, that He may be merciful unto us on the Day of Judgment.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle Simon, entreat the merciful God to grant our souls forgiveness of transgressions.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice.*
*Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*
Having netted the nations, the glorious apostle taught the ends of the earth to worship Thee together with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God; therefore establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O Thou Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind! *Twice.*
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
As the Sun of Righteousness Christ sent thee forth to enlighten all the earth, O glorious Apostle Simon; by thy divine prayers thou illuminest and with the unwaning divine light enlightenest all who in faith celebrate thy holy memory. *Twice.*
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding Unto Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости наста́вниче):*
With the net of divine words, having caught rational fish, thou didst offer these as first-fruits unto our God; and desiring to be clothed in the wounds of Christ, an imitator of His passion *Sessional Hymn (continued):*
...thou wast revealed; therefore, gathering together, we dutifully honor thy all-festal memory, O glorious apostle, and concordantly cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^151]*
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God.*
*Let every breath...*
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 67 [John 21:15–25].[^152]*

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Having clearly received the grace of the Holy Spirit, Simon, and having become a partaker of the sacred apostolic choir: therefore, having breathed in through a fiery tongue the fiery breath once borne from heaven,[^153] thou didst burn up the thorny godlessness of the pagans; O preacher of Christ God, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Canon of the Feast with the heirmos, for 6; and of the Apostle, for 8.*
*Whose acrostic is:*
*I sing the glory of the Apostle Simon.*

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us send up a song, O ye peoples, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!*

*Troparion:*
Standing on high before the Master, O marvelous apostle, Who glorified thee and showed thee forth as His radiant disciple: enlighten my soul, that I may divinely sing of thy memory!

*Troparion:*
Bestowing an abundance of good things upon thee, Christ showed thee to possess all divine gifts, O apostle, by righteous judgment, as the only righteous One, O God-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Having received the all-spiritual ray that descended upon thee from heaven, O apostle, thou wast fire-inspired, burning up all the delusion of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*
Having shown my mortal and corrupt nature to be immortal and incorrupt, O Savior, Thou didst dwell in the womb of the holy, all-pure Virgin without wedlock, being formed into human nature.

*Katavasia of the Feast.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*None is holy as the Lord, and none is righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns; none is righteous more than Thee, O Lord![^154]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst truly know the divine mystery of the Incarnation, O God-welcoming Apostle Simon, having received the transcendent ray from the Savior Himself.

*Troparion:*
The unoriginate and co-eternal Word [showed] thee, His minister, O marvelous Simon, with bright splendors richly to enlighten [the world] by divine grace.

*Theotokion:*
The prophet prefigured thee, O Birthgiver of God, as an all-golden lampstand bearing the unwaning Light, Christ our God, Who enlightens the world with the rays of His divinity.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, and the Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
By the illumination of the Holy Spirit, thou didst dispel the darkness of polytheism and didst enlighten the hearts of the faithful, singing the saving commandments, and didst make void the Hellenic fables, O glorious Simon; entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the prophet divinely foresaw Thee about to become incarnate of the only Birthgiver of God, and with fear glorified Thy power.[^155]*

*Troparion:*
Being wholly a treasure of evangelical gifts, full of grace, thou wast the light of the world and the salt of the universe, O all-blessed Simon.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst turn away from the manifestation of shameful matter, and wast accounted worthy to behold the immaterial Light of the Godhead, Who took on the substance of humanity, O right-marvelous one.

*Troparion:*
O Simon, disciple of incorruptible life, mortify our living sin by the life-bearing power of the Giver of life, whose operation thou didst receive.

*Theotokion:*
Being equal unto Thy Father in essence, Thou didst become equal unto men in nature, O Master, taking our flesh from the all-pure Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Having enlightened the ends of the earth from the night of ignorance by the knowledge of God, enlighten me with the morning of Thy love for mankind, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Together with the fire, O God-beholder, Thou didst zealously receive the visible tongue of the Spirit, sitting in the upper room.

*Troparion:*
Being exalted, dwelling in the highest mansions, thou didst bring us lofty and great teachings.

*Theotokion:*
The first law ceased by thy childbearing, O Virgin, and grace blossomed forth, and righteousness shone forth.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a radiant robe, O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God![^156]*

*Troparion:*
Called namesake of zeal, O right-marvelous Simon, thou wast zealous with zeal for the Lord God the Almighty.

*Troparion:*
The Savior showed Thee forth as a performer of divine wonders having given thee authority through the operation of His goodness.

*Theotokion:*
Let the mouths of the evildoers be stopped, who do not acknowledge thee as Birthgiver of God, O all-immaculate one, and let their faces be clothed with shame!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The Firm and God-Proclaimed Preachers" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Him who securely placed the teachings of wisdom in the souls of the pious, let us all bless in praise, as the God-proclaiming Simon; for he now stands before the throne of glory, and rejoices with the bodiless [hosts], praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:*
Let us all now praise the apostle's memory as a saving day, and bless it piously; for he shines forth unto the whole universe like the sun, dispelling all mist by the rays of light, and enlightens those who celebrate his memory with love and honor him affectionately. Therefore let us zealously assemble, singing of him; for he stands before Christ, praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trod down the flame and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^157]*

*Troparion:*
Having an extraordinary zeal, O Simon, thou wast called the namesake of zeal, O blessed one, and didst acquire a character concordant with thy name, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Dancing in chorus together with the Word, and having become His companion in discourse, thou art a partaker of the Kingdom there, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
By deceit of old the serpent drove out our forebears from the bowels of mercy, but thou, O Mother of God, hast recalled them: Blessed is the fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*When the musical instruments sounded in unison and countless peoples worshipped the image in Dura, the three children refused to obey, but hymned the Lord and glorified Him throughout all ages![^158]*

*Troparion:*
How beautiful are thy feet, O Simon; beautiful also is the tongue speaking the glory of Christ, and it taught us to cry: Sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him throughout all ages!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with radiant beauties and sending forth the splendors of wonders, thou wast made known unto the peoples as a blessed seed, O Simon: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him throughout all ages!

*Theotokion:*
The mind cannot declare thy childbearing, O Birthgiver of God, and the tongue is powerless to speak; for having conceived God, thou didst give birth, O Virgin, unto Him Whom we exalt throughout all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Let us people gloriously honor the pure Birthgiver of God, who received the fire of the Godhead into her womb without being consumed, and let us magnify her with songs!*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast revealed unto the ends of the earth, shining with light, and having received the spiritual fire, thou didst appear light-bearing; therefore we magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Wholly dedicated unto God, thou wast commingled with Him; pray now unto Him, O God-blessed one, for us who praise thee with faith and love.

*Theotokion:*
We magnify in songs her who appeared unto men as the mediatress of salvation for all, and who enlightened the world with the splendors of God-beholding purity.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "In the Spirit in the Sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во ст҃и́лищи):*
Having turned the course of thy beautiful feet, O Apostle Simon, thou didst ascend rejoicing unto the heavenly pathway, and standing before the Trinity, in the Father thou beholdest the Son and the divine Spirit. Therefore in faith we celebrate thy most sacred and divine memory.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera for 4, Tone 8, Podoben: "What Shall We Call You" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*
What shall we name thee, O apostle?* Heaven,* for thou didst declare the glory of God;* a river,* for thou dost mystically water creation;* a star,* illuminating the Church;* a chalice,* pouring forth holy drink;* the closest friend of Christ,* the equal citizen of the bodiless hosts:* pray for the salvation of our souls! *Twice.**

*Sticheron:*
O Simon, praise of the apostles,* that which adorned thy feet,* which trod well the path of preaching,* and narrowed all the enemy's ways* by the breadth of divine understanding,* was the Word Who appeared in the grossness of flesh* and chose thee as a most glorious disciple, O blessed one;* pray unto Him to save our souls!*

*Sticheron:*
O God-proclaimed Apostle Simon,* thou wast sent forth as a light-bearing arrow from Christ,* wounding the enemies,* and fitly granting healing unto wounded souls.* Therefore we dutifully bless thee,* and celebrate thy holy festival today;* pray that our souls may be saved!*

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Having left earthly things, thou didst follow Christ, and having been marked *Glory... Tone 2 (continued):*
...and having been sealed by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, thou wast sent by Him unto the perishing gentiles, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostle Simon; and having finished the struggles of thy divine suffering and diverse tortures, thou didst commit thy soul unto Christ. Entreat Him, O all-blessed one, to grant us great mercy!

*Both now... of the Feast.*
*Great Doxology. Litanies.*
*And the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, the appointed ode; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, for 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^159]*
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God.[^160]*
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 131 [1 Corinthians 4:9–16].[^161]*
*Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.[^162]*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 34 [Matthew 10:1–8].[^163]*
*Communion Hymn: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth...*

## May 11. The Renewal of the Imperial City, and the Holy Hieromartyr Mocius

*At Vespers, Stichera of the Hieromartyr, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant Among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Like the radiant sun,* thou didst shine forth from the West,* illuminating all that is under the sun* with the lightnings of suffering,* O God-wise, exceedingly rich one,* adornment of sufferers,* beauty of priests,* equal standing-companion of the holy angels,* who didst destroy the power of delusion,* O God-blessed Mocius!*

*Sticheron:*
Kindled by piety* and sharpened by suffering,* and anointed with courage, O Mocius,* thou wast an unbound sword, O glorious one,* utterly destroying the hosts of enemies.* Therefore today we celebrate thy bright, light-bearing,* and holy memory,* O glory and praise of martyrs!*

*Sticheron:*
Never broken,* though burned by fire,* cast to wild beasts, O blessed one,* afflicted by exile,* and severed by the sword,* thou didst not deny Christ,* nor didst thou sacrifice to idols.* Therefore thou hast received the crowns of victory,* and art numbered among the choirs of the bodiless hosts,* praying for the whole world.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*

*Troparion [of the Hieromartyr], Tone 4 (continued):*
Having become a sharer of the way and a successor on the thrones of the apostles, thou didst find action in contemplation, O God-inspired one, an ascent unto vision; therefore rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst suffer for the faith even unto blood, O Hieromartyr Mocius: entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Canon, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen Mother, and I will be seen keeping radiant festival, and will sing rejoicing of her wonders.[^164]*

*Troparion:*
Unto the breadth of thy praises, O blessed one, direct the narrowness of my mind, and send down light upon my heart as I sing of thy light-bearing and glorious memory, O all-lauded martyr!

*Troparion:*
The Church acknowledges thee as a radiant star brightening her firmament with the brilliance of thy sufferings, O all-blessed one, adornment of martyrs, all-wise Mocius!

*Troparion:*
By thy blood thou didst destroy godlessness; by warlike struggles thou didst crush the enemies; consumed by fire, thou didst burn up the entire assembly of the impious.

*Troparion:*
Scraped with iron and afflicted by fire, thou didst become brighter than gold, O Mocius, an image of the sufferings of Christ and a champion of the faith, O God-blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast appeared unto us as the mediatress of joy, O all-immaculate one, having received the fullness of all the Godhead; therefore we all concordantly cry unto thee, as did the angel: Rejoice, O thou who art full of grace!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for none is holy more than Thee, O Lover of mankind![^165]*

*Troparion:*
Stripped bare for struggles and combats, thou wast prepared, strengthening thy weakness by the power of the Cross; therefore thou didst cast down the boastful serpent.

*Troparion:*
Through temporal death thou didst find immortal glory, and for the sake of bodily pains, eternal delight, as thou didst desire, O invincible martyr!

*Troparion:*
Truly thou hast passed from corruption unto incorruption; through supreme love thou didst enter with joy, having become a fellow partaker with the angels, O Mocius.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee as a most pure dwelling, the Wisdom of God became incarnate from thy precious blood, and showed thee forth as incorrupt after childbearing.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Let the renowned passion-bearer of all, who humbled the enemy unto the end and steadfastly endured fiery torment, the glorious Mocius, be honored with praises; for having endured unbearable torments and having been beheaded in the head, he ascended rejoicing unto Christ as a crown-bearer.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead on a light cloud, Jesus the transcendent in divinity came by an incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^166]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst strip off the coats of skins of the ancient mortification, O blessed one, being scraped everywhere on thy body, and didst clothe thyself in the garment of incorruption, and rejoicing, thou dost enjoy the Tree of Life.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast adorned with the fair appearance of virtues, O most glorious one; thou wast made radiant by the pains of beneficent suffering; thou wast likened unto the angels, for as if bodiless thou didst endure fractures and wounds.

*Troparion:*
The abyss of godlessness in no wise submerged thee, for the Creator saved thee with a mighty hand; the rivers of torments did not shake the unshakable pillar of thy soul, O wise one.

*Theotokion:*
He Who created all things by His will and desire formed flesh for Himself from thy blood; and in two natures, He comes forth as One, O Mother and Virgin, saving us by His goodness.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a Light, a holy Light, turning those who sing of Thee in faith from the darkness of ignorance.[^167]*

*Troparion:*
Having acquired a mighty power against the invisible enemy, Thy martyr cast down his frail strength, O Christ.

*Troparion:*
The earth was sanctified by thy blood, O blessed one; by thy wounds the demonic legion was wounded, O God-wise, right-marvelous one.

*Troparion:*
Possessing radiant and precious pearls in her adornment, the honorable Church is adorned, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear in time the Timeless Light, the Effulgence of the Father, Who became [flesh] through the wealth of His goodness, O all-immaculate one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.[^168]*

*Troparion:*
Armed with love, hope, and faith, the author of contests the great Contest-Master [armed thee as] a mighty champion of piety; therefore did he overthrow the stronghold of delusion.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst withdraw thy mind from carnal love, and beholding the ineffable beauty of the Bridegroom, thou didst count torments as nothing, O all-lauded one!

*Troparion:*
Without being engulfed thou didst cross the gulf of martyrdom, and didst hasten with all thy wealth unto the heavenly havens of good things, having received the crown of victory.

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, who alone didst bear Christ, as the only merciful one draw me up from the depth of sins, and save me from the tempest of adverse thoughts!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The Firm and God-Proclaimed Preachers" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Armed with the shield of faith, thou didst crush the hosts of the impious, O hieromartyr, and didst receive a crown from Christ, O Mocius; therefore, rejoicing with the angels, deliver from perils those who sing of thee, praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:*
Having a defiled soul and a body [stained] by countless stumbling-blocks, and being grievously submerged by a tempest, I dare not speak, nor am I able, of thy praises, O blessed hieromartyr. But as thou art a fountain of abundant mercy, pray that a word may be given to open my mouth [against] stumbling-blocks; for thou prayest unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously snared: O supremely praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^169]*

*Troparion:*
Cast into the furnace, thou didst find the Word bedewing thee in the likeness of an angel, Who saved the three youths, as thou didst cry, O all-blessed one: O supremely praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Against swords, against fire, and against incandescent vessels thou didst stand manfully, O martyr, strengthened by divine power, and didst cry out rejoicing: O supremely praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
With the streams of thy blood thou didst extinguish the flame of delusion, and by the fire of endurance thou didst burn up the kindled brushwood of idols, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Thee, who alone didst pour forth for us the Water of forgiveness from the immortal Wellspring, Christ Jesus, O Ever-Virgin, we hymn, crying aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Nativity of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious children in the furnace; prefigured then, but now actualized, it stirs the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him throughout all ages![^170]*

*Troparion:*
Having dyed a purple robe crimson from thy blood and being clothed therewith, bearing also the invincible weapon instead of a scepter, thou reignest with the King of all, O Mocius, crying: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him throughout all ages!

*Troparion:*
Today the memory of thy suffering shone forth like the sun upon the world, illuminating all and dispelling the darkness of demonic malice, O glorious Mocius; wherein rejoicing we sing of thee, blessing Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Having prepared a chariot by the blood of thy suffering, O God-wise one, to be taken up unto the heavens, thou didst reach the ranks of the spiritual hosts and the choirs of sufferers; standing with them before the Trinity, ever pray for those who sing of thee.

*Theotokion:*
In thee were seen the goings of the Son of the great King, O heavenly gate, maiden unwedded, who didst bring men's nature into heavenly citizenship; therefore we sing of thee as Virgin, and exalt thee throughout the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve indeed brought in the curse through the disease of disobedience; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, didst cause blessing to blossom forth for the world through the bud of thy gestation; therefore we all magnify thee.[^171]*

*Troparion:*
Having become exceedingly rich and full of enlightenment, and adorned with holiness, thou didst enter inside the bridal chamber, rejoicing with the wise virgins, and thou beholdest the inconceivable beauty whose brightness thou didst acquire beforehand.

*Troparion:*
Having suffered beforehand in other cities, O glorious one, thou didst reach the city of Byzantium, O martyr; wherein thou didst receive the end of thy struggles and the crown of victory as an invincible sufferer; which [city] thou protectest by thy supplications.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast truly found the recompense of thy pains: an unshakable kingdom; thou didst stand by desire, O all-blessed one, having approached the Tree of Life, and becoming god by participation, O Mocius, right-marvelous martyr!

*Theotokion:*
Following thy words, we ever bless thee, O Virgin; for ineffably thou didst bear for us the Word Who shone forth from the Father before the ages, as He Himself was well pleased; Whom we worthily magnify.

*At the Liturgy, the Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.[^172]*
*Verse: Hear, O God, my voice, when I make my prayer unto Thee.[^173]*
*Epistle to Timothy, Pericope 292 [2 Timothy 2:1–10].[^174]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.[^175]*
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 51 [John 15:17–16:2].[^176]*
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be...[^177]*

## May 12. Our Fathers Among the Saints, Epiphanius, Bishop of Cyprus, and Germanus, Patriarch of Constantinople

*At Vespers, Stichera of Saint Epiphanius, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O Container of Ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*
Rejoice, in truth [adornment] of ascetics,* who with the cord of thy most wise teachings* didst pasture this lamb* upon the pastures of orthodoxy,* O Epiphanius,* the meek sheep of Christ the Chief Shepherd,* which was reared on the grass of abstinence,* and now hath made its fold in the sheepfold of the life on high,* where dancing in chorus with the saints and martyrs,* ever pray for all who with love celebrate thy memory, O God-wise one,* to grant us peace* and great mercy!*

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, luminary of piety,* who with the rays of thy fiery words* didst enlighten the universe* and didst burn up the brushwood teachings of the enemies by fire, O Epiphanius,* calling those who are in the tempest of ignorance* unto the calm haven of the knowledge of God;* instrument sounded by the breath of the Spirit,* unceasingly pouring forth an abyss with the flower of wonders* and delivering from death:* entreat Christ* to grant our souls great mercy!*

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, mystical river* filled with spiritual waters,* adornment of the Church,* rule of the sacred hierarchy,* pillar of monastics, O Epiphanius,* foundation of the orthodox,* vigilance of the Church,* pillar of strength* against contrary impiety,* God-sounding trumpet,* much-speaking nightingale,* clear mind,* watchful tongue,* who didst wonder at the divine law through the grace of Christ:* entreat Him* to grant our souls great mercy!*

*Other Stichera, of Saint Germanus, Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Lauded Martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*
The scheming of Leo* thou didst firmly overthrow, O Germanus:* full of evil belief, the wretched one* rejected the veneration of the honorable icons of Christ* and of all the saints;* but by thy words, O God-manifested one,* he was refuted as insane* and remained senseless.*

*Sticheron:*
Leo, the godless beast,* who, hating God,* was the forerunner of the Antichrist,* having cast aside the veneration of Christ's image,* was expelled from the heritage of the faithful.* Therefore we beseech thee:* as [thou didst dispel] him by thy prayers,* drive away the present turmoil!*

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst attain thy desire which, O God-manifested one,* was clearly desired by thee from afar;* for unto thy Master with boldness, O blessed Germanus,* thou hast now entered in holy dignity;* standing before Him,* thou dost enjoy deification:* ask peace for the world!*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*
*Troparion [of the Saints], Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, withdraw not Thy mercy from us; but by their prayers direct our life in peace.
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Canon of Saint Epiphanius, the work of John the Monk.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in power in a manner befitting God; for it, O Immortal One, being all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, fashioning a new path through the deep for the Israelites.[^178]*

*Troparion:*
Having lived by faith in the acceptable law of the Scriptures, O blessed one, thou didst submit unto the yoke; and being adorned, O exceedingly rich one, thou wast shown forth as a luminary, enlightening the ends of the earth.

*Troparion:*
Embraced by the desire of the divine law and discerning the dispute of the unrighteous, O Father Epiphanius, thou didst typically receive the saving paternal faith, as from divine foreknowledge.

*Troparion:*
A God-beholding man became for thee a healer of flesh and soul; through thy gifts he slew the passionate impulse and healed the unbelieving mindset of thy soul.

*Theotokion:*
Having taken on my whole form, He Who was formerly beheld in the form of God renewed it entirely, having dwelt in thy womb in a manner befitting God, O honorable one; therefore in faith we all glorify thee as Birthgiver of God.

*Another Canon, of Saint Germanus, the work of Theophanes. Tone 4. Ode 1. The same Heirmos.*

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the vesture of the priesthood, O blessed one, thou didst live; and by the word of grace, O God-manifested one, thou wast shown forth enlightening the most honorable assemblies of the faithful with teachings.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a chariot of virtues, mounting upon which thou didst depart unto God, having left the beauty of the world and corruptible glory, having become a heavenly initiate through thy wisdom.

*Troparion:*
Having been foreknown by Providence as a God-voiced priest, O Father, thou didst escape the defiled murder of the tormentor, O venerable one, while God preserved thee by His all-ruling right hand.

*Theotokion:*
What worthy song shall our weakness bring unto thee, save that joyful salutation wherewith Gabriel taught us: Rejoice, O Birthgiver of God, Virgin, unwedded Mother!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature and hast mercifully taken form therein, gird me with power from on high,[^179] that I may cry unto Thee, O Holy One: Living temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!*

*Troparion:*
Having beheld with bodily eyes the black garment of him who gave when mercy was asked, yet having perceived him spiritually as clothed in a radiant robe, through divine grace thou wast enlightened unto the blameless faith.

*Troparion:*
Having heard the pious words, thou didst truly despise material things and didst desire an immaterial life; therefore, taking thy sister who shared thy blood and making her a partaker of divine understanding, ye were shown forth as a blessed pair.

*Troparion:*
Having turned away from dead thoughts, O most blessed Epiphanius, thou didst desire to serve the living God; and having faithfully hastened unto the divine temple and received the radiant monastic habit, O exceedingly rich one, thou didst manifest the casting off of thy dead works.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst holily give birth unto Christ, the Holy of Holies, the holy habitation of holiness, Who rests in the saints, unto Whom we cry: Holy is the living temple of Thy glory, O pure Lover of mankind!

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
God, Who through divine foreknowledge knew the freedom of thy character and the nobility of thy soul, O wise one, prevented thee from being slain by the hands of the lawless, and most wisely refuted the castrated condition imposed upon the virgin order.[^180]

*Troparion:*
Desiring clearly to behold the spiritual splendor of divine deification, O blessed one, thou didst adorn thy hierarchal office with a chaste life, and with wise teachings thou didst guide thy flock orthodoxly.

*Troparion:*
Having ascended the great cathedra, thou didst greatly enlighten the fullness of the Church, O Father, with the hymns of thy teachings, O most lauded Germanus, venerable hierarch and most honorable luminary of the universe.

*Theotokion:*
From thee, O pure one, blossomed forth the unfading Flower, filling all mankind with fragrance through the divine myrrh of His nature—He Who is co-unoriginate with the Father, and from thee was born in time, O all-blameless Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn [of Saint Epiphanius], Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having cast off the shoes from thy feet, even as Moses did in the holy place,[^181] thou wast instructed in divine spiritual mysteries; and having put to death thy body by the labors of abstinence, thou didst fill creation with most glorious wonders. Therefore thou didst also explain unto all the ineffable things of divinely-inspired Scripture, O our God-bearing Father, Hierarch Epiphanius: entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who with love honor thy holy memory!

*Glory... Another Sessional Hymn, of the Hierarch [Germanus], the same Tone [Tone 8]:*
Having vested in the sacred garment, thou wast shown forth as a helper of Christ, not having feared the lawless emperor,[^182] O our Father, and remaining unyielding unto the godless command, enduring evil malice, reproaches, and threats. Therefore the savage man, inflamed with wrath, shut thee up in prison until thy demise, O blessed one. Therefore we cry unto thee: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto us who with love honor thy holy memory!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Having with prophetic eyes perceived thee as the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God,[^183] Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should go forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.*

*Troparion:*
Having approached baptism as a servant seeking refuge, thou wast granted adoption as a true heir thereby, O Epiphanius, and didst become a joint-heir with Christ, having lived unto Him in a manner befitting God.

*Troparion:*
The Shepherd beholds thy face adorned with many virtues and ineffable glory, and thy glorious head crowned beyond nature with a beauteous wreath, O glorious one—the representations of thy pure heart.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a vessel of divine grace, O venerable one, and didst wisely distribute temporal wealth unto Christ;

and having become enriched with heavenly possession, thou didst store up an unstealable treasure, even as a wise judge, O Epiphanius.

*Troparion:*
In youth thou didst cast aside the mindset of youth, being strengthened in the divine fear by elderly, wise, and prudent men, O blessed one; through instruction in spiritual words, thou didst become a lover of wisdom.

*Theotokion:*
O Holy of Holies, all-hymned Birthgiver of God, expectation of the nations and salvation of the faithful: from thee shone forth the Deliverer, the Giver of life, and the Lord: Him do thou entreat, that thy servants may be saved!

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
Having most wisely put to death the impulses of the flesh, O sacred initiate Germanus, by the enlightenment of divine philosophy thou didst clarify thy mind; adorned mystically by spiritual instruction, thou didst cry unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Fearing the most honorable depictions of Christ, the impious enemy compelled thee; for having cast himself into folly, he was clearly refuted by thy teachings, O glorious one; yet, being incurably diseased, he remained unfeeling.

*Troparion:*
Seeing the refutation of thy words, the beast-named ruler, with a bestial mind deposed thee from the sacred throne; but in place thereof thou wast enriched, O all-blessed one, with heavenly priestly ministry.

*Theotokion:*
Bringing joy unto thee clearly from the heavens, O pure one, the Chief Commander announced good tidings, saying: God shall come in the flesh from thee, O most pure Virgin, unto the salvation of those who with love sing of thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Christ, Who hast enlightened with the splendor of Thy coming and illuminated the ends of the world by Thy Cross: enlighten the hearts of those who orthodoxly praise Thee with the light of Thy divine knowledge.*

*Troparion:*
Fastingly thou didst constrain thy belly with bread and water, having salt as cooked food, and thou didst hold back the passions of the flesh, while in the flesh imitating the life of the bodiless angels.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst have compassion on those languishing in the heat of thirst, and didst transform the heat: beginning signs in a manner befitting God, as an eminent servant imitating thy Master, thou didst turn water into wine, O blessed one.[^184]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst flee the vain glory of men, and into the wilderness, even as thy Master, didst settle, strengthening thy weakness unto the combat with invisible foes amidst deceitful temptations, O wise one.

*Theotokion:*
O Birthgiver of God, beyond word thou didst contain in thy womb the divine Word: Him do thou beseech to deliver thy servants from irrational deeds and deadly pleasures.

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
Having loved Christ unto the end, O God-receiver, thou wast worthily loved by Him, and didst receive an abyss of gifts; for with thy wise teachings thou didst enlighten the Church.

*Troparion:*
With divinely-inspired hymns, O Germanus, thou didst clarify the choirs of the faithful and the divine assemblies; for the whole worthy steadfastness of thy heart, O Father, enlightened the orthodox.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, thou didst depict the suffering and victories of the martyrs with composed praises and words, and clearly, O glorious one, didst join both together by figurative painting.

*Theotokion:*
The heavenly hosts make merry, beholding thee; the assemblies of men rejoice with them; for by thy childbearing, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, they were united: Him do we worthily glorify.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The uttermost abyss hath encompassed us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter.[^185] Save Thy people, O our God: for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.*

*Troparion:*
The Hagarene descendants, imitators of demons, surrounded thee; but imitating thy Master, thou didst bestow benefits upon him who opposed thee, and brightly restored his other eye, O Father.[^186]

*Troparion:*
Thy Creator Who rests in thee made thee an instrument of the Spirit and a god unto the Arabian dwellings, even as Moses was unto Pharaoh;[^187] for in truth thou wast shown forth imitating his example.

*Troparion:*
The offspring of the Assyrian king, whom the spirit drove out, became a messenger of thy virtue and divine goodness, O Father; for God knows how to glorify those who glorify Him.[^188]

*Theotokion:*
The forebears of our race rejoice in thee, O most pure Virgin, having through thee regained Eden, which they lost through transgression; for thou art pure, both before childbearing and after childbearing.

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
The hierarchy of the Church is watered with thy teachings and words, O all-wise one, and the beloved, God-called people of Christ are guided unto God by thine instructions.

*Troparion:*
Remember those who sing of thee, O venerable one, as thou now standest before God, filled with divine goodness and incorruptible beauty, and unto life guide them by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst set ascents in thy life, O wise one; from glory and power unto heavenly glory and strength Thou didst pass through abstinence, O glorious one,[^189] until rejoicing Thou didst converse with Christ Whom Thou desiredst.

*Theotokion:*
Deliver now thy servant from every adversity, O most pure one, from the surging waves and tempest of passions, and guide me unto thy haven; for thou art my protectress and restoration.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на Крⷭ҇тъ):*
Let us, the faithful, duly praise the marvelous pair of hierarchs, the divine Epiphanius with Germanus; for they burned up the tongues of the godless, setting forth most wise dogmas unto all who ever orthodoxly sing of the great mystery of piety.

*Ikos:*
As the beauty of the sacred and the glory of the orthodox, let us worthily honor the holy pair. For they were shown forth unto us as pillars of confirmation against foes invisible and visible at all times, unyielding pillars of the faith, and a beauteous adornment of the Church. Wearing this adornment, she straightly rules, crying spiritually: In truth my spirit rejoices within me, having received back my robe as before, which the heretics rent asunder, not singing of the great mystery of piety!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Let us, the faithful, contemplate thee, O Birthgiver of God, as a spiritual furnace; for even as the Supremely Exalted One saved the three children,[^190] He entirely renewed the world in thy womb: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.*

*Troparion:*
Having received the gift of wonders from the divine right hand, O Father, thou didst freely distribute healings unto all who asked of thee, even as the Master commanded thee: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
The Assyrian king prostrats himself, rolling at thy feet; for even the adversary knows how to revere virtue; yet He Who alone rests in the saints protects everyone: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Speech falls silent, and can in no wise recount thy righteous deeds and the multitude of thy wonders, O all-blessed one; for that which Christ granted unto thee exceeds the sand of the sea:[^191] the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Triadikon:*
O thrice-radiant Unity, inexhaustible Light unto those in measureless distress, Thou overseest Thy hymnographers, O supremely exalted One: unoriginate Father, and co-unoriginate Son, and co-ever-existing Spirit: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the Source of life, O pure and all-blameless one, quicken me who am grievously slain by demonic assaults, by thy prayers, as I sing unto thy Son: the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
The fountain of the Holy Spirit was wholly poured out in thee; for thou dost pour forth saving commandments and divinely-beauteous teachings like rivers, O marvelous Father, magnifying the supremely praised God unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst piously elucidate hymnody, chanting in song to celebrate feasts, having been deified; and through thy melodies thou didst wisely anoint all the saints unto virtue, as they sing in faith: Blessed and supremely glorified art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Crying aloud with a theological tongue, thou didst proclaim unto all one Principle in undivided unity in three Hypostases: the unoriginate Begetter, the only-begotten co-unoriginate Son, and truly the connatural and consubstantial divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
In thee, O pure Birthgiver of God, have I placed all my hope, and unto thee do I ever flee; save me, O all-blameless one, and by thy prayers deliver me from the prevailing tumult and confusion of my grievous passions and falls.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The children of Israel shone from the furnace through the beauty of piety purer than gold, as in a crucible, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^192]*

*Troparion:*
By thy word thou didst wisely refute the delusion of heresies, and by the radiance of piety thou didst drive away darkness, crying, O Epiphanius: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having ascended a lofty throne, for thou didst reign over passions, and having restrained the flesh, thou didst shepherd Christ's flock, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Let every spiritual and rational being dance in chorus in memory of the hierarch and pleaser of Christ, crying in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having in a God-pleasing manner spoken of God as the foundation of teachings, thou didst leave unto all thy theology, whereby, O Father, we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, glorious throne of God; rejoice, wall of the faithful, through whom Christ shone as a light unto those in darkness; and blessing thee we cry: Bless, all ye works, and praise the pure childbearing of the Virgin!

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
Enjoying the radiant brilliances of the thrice-sunlit Light as a torch, O sacredly-manifested one, thou dost now cry, rejoicing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having been accounted worthy of much light and radiance in the heavenly courts and habitations, O Father, remember us who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing unto the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having preserved that which is God-like and according to God's image, thou didst raise up the icons of the saints, knowing that all honor and glory passes unto the prototype,[^193] O most praised one, crying aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
In a new manner beyond nature, O Virgin, having conceived the Word, the Son consubstantial with the Father, thou didst remain a virgin; therefore, all we works praise thee and cry unto thee: Rejoice, and we supremely exalt thee, O pure one, unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The burning bush showed forth unconsumed an image of thy pure childbearing;[^194] and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparion:*
Thy life as a rule of action and thy words of divine contemplation, O Father, were truly left unto the God-wise people, O Epiphanius, blessed and glorious one; therefore as is worthy, praising thee we magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Stand before the Church, the bride of Christ, O glorious Epiphanius, and by thy prayers calm the fierce tempest raised against her, as one having boldness before the Master and Lover of mankind!

*Triadikon:*
O King of kings, Holy Consubstantial Trinity, ruling over all: grant forgiveness of transgressions and the unshakable ordering of our whole life unto those who sing of Thee, through the prayers of Epiphanius!

*Theotokion:*
Thy wonders surpass understanding! For the Word of God having become flesh, thou alone didst bear for us beyond nature, O Virgin Mother—Him Who clearly upholds all things by His divine will: Him do thou entreat for all.

*Another Canon, [of Saint Germanus]. The same Heirmos.*
*Troparion:*
Having truly received the authority to bind and loose transgressions,[^195] according to the law as a priest, O most blessed one: grant remission unto those who sing of thee, O Father, through thy prayers, that we all may call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst find the reward of thy labors, O sacredly-manifested Germanus, all-blessed one, having received a blessed end and inherited eternal life; and thou dost unceasingly enjoy divine contemplation.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst wholly commit thyself unto the all-seeing Word and Almighty, and wast shown forth as a good and sacred ministry, offering unto the Lord an acceptable and well-pleasing sacrifice: the songs of thy labors.

*Theotokion:*
Put an end to carnal-mindedness, and calm the turmoil of passions, O Virgin, for him who flees unto thy protection, O Birthgiver of God, and hath thee as the foundation of hope, and was enriched by thy divine intercession and protection.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жє́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Come, ye faithful, let us in hymnic songs honor the divine and all-sacred pair of hierarchs, the divine Epiphanius and Germanus, who destroyed the gloom of heresies and enlightened all creation with the radiance of piety.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 1:*
My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart understanding.[^196]
*Verse:* Hear this, all ye nations; give ear, all ye that inhabit the world.

*Epistle unto the Hebrews, Pericope 318 [Hebrews 7:26–8:2].[^197]*

*Alleluia, Tone 2:*
The mouth of the righteous shall meditate wisdom.[^198]
*Verse:* The law of His God is in His heart, and His steps shall not trip.

*Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 11 [Matthew 5:14–19].[^199]*

*Communion Verse:* In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^200]

## May 13. The Holy Martyr Glyceria

*At Vespers, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Martyrs, O Lord" (Мч҃ницы твоѝ Гд҃и):*
Giving heed, O divinely-blessed one, unto the sweet words of the Holy Spirit, thou didst abandon all bitterness of carnal passions, and through death didst pass unto immortal glory, O Glyceria, entreating Christ to grant great mercy unto all!

*Sticheron:*
Glyceria was wounded with Thy sweet love, O Master, and endured the bitter pain of wounds, O Word of God; therefore she is translated unto painless delight, as one undefiled and incorrupt: by her prayers, O Word, grant great mercy unto all!

*Sticheron:*
Having well confessed the name of Christ thy Bridegroom before the lawless enemies, thou didst lawfully suffer for His sake, O honorable one, being severely severed limb from limb and courageously conquering wild beasts. And now thou makest supplication to grant great mercy unto all.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Glyceria, O Jesus, cries with a great voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee do I endure suffering, and am crucified and buried with Thy baptism; and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may live with Thee; but receive me as a blameless sacrifice offered with love unto Thee. By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*Canon, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God![^201]*

*Troparion:*
Deliver me from the bitterness of passions by Thy prayers unto God, O Glyceria, that I may hymn Thy memory and praise Thy noble struggles.

*Troparion:*
Having been wounded with the love of Christ, by the panoply of the Cross thou didst conquer the enemy, and didst receive the crown of victory, O Glyceria, all-praised virgin.

*Troparion:*
They who paid honor unto stones stoned thee with stones,[^202] as thou from thy soul didst look unto the living Stone and Master, Who granted thee victory, O bride of God.

*Theotokion:*
Having truly given birth unto God in nature, thou art truly called the Birthgiver of God, bearing truly the name corresponding to each, O pure one, through divine childbearing.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Fashioner of the heavenly vault and Builder of the Church: confirm me in Thy love, Thou summit of desires, firm support of the faithful, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind!*

*Troparion:*
Taking neither shield nor spear, but the victory of the Cross with unhesitating faith, thou didst zealously ascend unto praises and noble struggles against the wiles of the ruler of this world.

*Troparion:*
Desiring Thee, O Bridegroom, I fear not death; enjoying Thy beauties, rejoicing I set aside bitterness, torment, and grievous tortures, O Glyceria, as she truly cried while suffering.

*Troparion:*
Suspended by the hair and scraped with iron claws, she endured pains in the body, foreseeing painless delight and the beauty of the Bridegroom through purity of mind, O all-honorable one.

*Theotokion:*
Me, who was slain of old, thou alone didst lead back unto life, O most pure one, having given birth unto personal Life: striking against Whom, hostile death was clearly shattered.

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Loving the Virgin and Birthgiver of God Mary, thou didst keep thy virginity incorrupt; and being zealous in love for the Lord, thou didst suffer courageously even unto death. Therefore with a twofold crown, O virgin martyr, Christ God crowns thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на Крⷭ҇тъ):*
Brightly adorned with the garments of thy sacred and marvelous struggles, as a virgin bride of Christ, thou didst ascend unto His incorruptible bridal chamber, and dost enjoy His beauty. But entreat that we who with love sing of thee may be saved from every distress!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy divinity.[^203]*

*Troparion:*
Depicting Thy blessed passion, O Almighty One, the God-wise martyr endures hanging and the pains of wounds.

*Troparion:*
By the drops of thy blood the fire of vanity was extinguished, and the torrents of polytheism were drowned polytheism, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
By the buffeting of thy cheeks, O martyr, thou didst buffet the faces of the demons; and the bruising of thy face ground error into dust like powder.

*Troparion:*
They who wounded thy body as thou didst emulate the life of the bodiless powers, fell and were slain by the divine beckonings of the bodiless angels.

*Theotokion:*
O bride of God, who didst give birth unto the Master and Helmsman: calm the tumult of my passions, and the tempest of my transgressions!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray Thee![^204]*

*Troparion:*
Delighting in beauties, in the loveliness of the Benefactor Jesus, Thou didst run by sufferings unto the fragrance of His myrrh, ignited with desire, kindled with His love, O radiant Glyceria.

*Troparion:*
Having drawn from the fountain of salvation ever-living healing, thou wast inflamed with a thirst for martyrdom; and having hastened thou didst run, and having drunk thou didst stand, having found rest in the desire for consummate love, O bride of God.

*Troparion:*
With heavenly food angels fed thee in prison, O martyr; for thou didst desire the food not of the world, but the living life; unto which thou didst truly present thyself, adorned in wounds with the wounds of thy suffering.

*Theotokion:*
Having motherly boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, disregard not, we pray, the kindred providence that is over us; for as Christians we mercifully offer thee as the sole cleansing before the Master.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation![^205]*

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth like the sun from the West, the martyr enlightens the hearts of the faithful with grace, and burns up the hosts of dark demons with the rays of suffering.

*Troparion:*
Enlightened with radiant rays, thou didst flee the darkness of folly; and thou didst enlighten the hearts of those who cry aloud in faith, O passion-bearer Glyceria.

*Troparion:*
The glorious martyr stripped off the coats of skins of corruption,[^206] being struck with a spear in the face and head through the cruelty of the torturer, gratefully singing unto Thee, O Christ, Who grantest strength:

*Theotokion:*
Bearing as a new babe Him Who is God before the ages, incarnate from thee, O all-blameless one, cease not to pray that He may save those who sing of thee!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Judean youths who came of old to Babylon, by faith in the Trinity trampled the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^207]*

*Troparion:*
Unharmed thou didst sail across the abyss of suffering, and calmly didst pass through the billow of pains; and thou didst reach tranquil havens through divine refreshment, singing having sailed well: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Emulating the persecutions of Paul, O God-wise one, by the command of the torturers thou didst often pass from city to city, struggling against the rulers of darkness, and thou didst reach the city on high, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Cast into the furnace, thou wast not burned; for He Who saved the three youths in Babylon transformed the fire into dew,[^208] O most glorious one!

*Theotokion:*
As an animate ark thou didst receive the unoriginate Word; as a holy temple thou didst contain the Creator; as a fiery throne thou bearest the Master of all creation, O Mother of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^209]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst truly show thyself, O Glyceria, as a fruitful vine of the Planter of creation, bearing clusters of suffering pressed, O martyr, by the winepresses of martyrdom, and pouring forth the wine of compunction unto those who sing in faith unto Christ: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Spread out upon sharp stones, O glorious one, thou didst endure lacerations with an unbending mind; and the flatterer having been caught and bound with divine fetters, the delusion of idols was destroyed, and by thy word thou dost lead the martyr bravely to sing: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Even as Daniel shut the mouths of the wild beasts,[^210] by the divine hymns of thy prayers, O all-honorable one, thou didst extinguish the fire, having reproved the lawless; and with a mortal body thou didst acquire immortality, having become like unto the angels, with whom thou singest: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Following thy voice, O all-pure one, we call thee blessed, the blessed one who didst truly bear in the flesh Him Who is blessed, Who dwells in unapproachable light, the Giver of light, Giver of life, and Master: Whom, ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from a virginal womb. Therefore, the faithful, we magnify the all-pure Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparion:*
Thy mind delighted by the vision of God, thou didst keep thyself without pain, O virgin, in thy much-suffering body; when struck before the judgment seat of the torturers, thou didst suffer firmly, conquering the bodiless foes along with the body.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast betrothed, O incorrupt virgin, unto Him Who was born of the Virgin Maiden of God; and as a gift unto Him, O martyr, thou didst offer suffering, an unjust death through manifold wounds. Therefore He truly accounted thee worthy of the heavenly bridal chamber.

*Troparion:*
Having golden wings shining with the radiance of the Spirit, O martyr, thou didst fly up, adorned with flowers, with divine splendors; and in the very heavenly divine habitations thou hast truly found rest, clearly enjoying deification.

*Troparion:*
Thou dancest now joyfully with the angels before the face of thy Creator, illumined by the rays that ever stream from thence; but remember us who in faith celebrate thy glorious memory, O all-praised passion-bearer Glyceria!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the dwelling of God, having contained in thy womb Him Who cannot be contained, having in an awe-inspiring manner given birth incarnate unto Him Who was formerly bodiless. Him do thou entreat, O pure one, to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto all who ever magnify thee in faith.

## May 14. The Holy Martyr Isidore of Chios

*At Vespers, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Martyr, 3; Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Torn with stripes, thou didst strip off the coats of skin of deadness; and clad in the garment of incorruption and a bright purple robe, thou didst swiftly come unto the tree of life, having truly received indestructible life and unending glory: whereof now taking thy fill, preserve those who sing of thee!

*Sticheron:*
Kindled with zeal for piety, O Isidore, thou didst enter the judgment hall of fervent sufferings, and didst receive the crown of victory, O martyr, adorned with God-submissive teachings against delusion; and strengthened with the whole armor of the Cross,[^211] thou didst cast down the deceitful tyrant, shown forth as a crown-bearer.

*Sticheron:*
Truly the sufferings of the martyrs surpass the praise of comparison, and of word and thought; for being firm pillars of piety, clothed like us in a mortal and passible body, yet they dared against fire and endured wounds, as though suffering in another's body, O glorious ones!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Isidore, O Lord, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers, and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: by his prayers save our souls!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Canon, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God![^212]*

*Troparion:*
By thy prayers, O martyr of Christ, blessed Isidore, grant me enlightenment and grace from heaven, to praise thy honorable feast!

*Troparion:*
Having a heart and soul perfectly directed unto God, O martyr Isidore, and having acquired a blameless life, thou didst bravely contend with torments.

*Troparion:*
Having lawfully ordered thy life, O martyr Isidore, as an unstolen treasure, thou didst hold the unshakeable faith of salvation, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Having truly given birth in nature unto God, thou art truly named the Birthgiver of God, bearing the title corresponding unto each through divine childbearing, O pure one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee![^213]*

*Troparion:*
Guided by the Word, O glorious martyr of Christ, passion-bearer, by fasting virtues thou didst wither the passible impulses.

*Troparion:*
Having an unashamed spiritual desire, O glorious one, thou didst preserve the most godly confession unharmed.

*Troparion:*
Render the Judge of all merciful by thy prayers, O passion-bearer, unto those who godlily praise thy celebration!

*Theotokion:*
Put to death the wisdom of our flesh, O Sovereign Lady, and quicken our souls, O Virgin, who in faith sing of thee!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
A most great governor of the universe wast thou shown forth, O holy one, by thy prayers unto God; therefore we sing of thee today, O God-wise martyr Isidore, most glorious one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дв҃ства):*
Rousing thyself against the serpent, thou didst set up a trophy of victory, O martyr Isidore; shining as a lantern from Egypt to illumine the world under the sun, thou didst shine forth unto all, directing thy movements unto Him Who shone forth from the Virgin Maiden of God: for Whose sake having been slain, O passion-bearer, thou becamest a sweet-smelling sacrifice.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, not leaving the Father's bosom and visiting our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^214]*

*Troparion:*
Having the whole Word dwelt within thee, as an unconquerable warrior thou didst put to shame the tyrant's pride; and having become a victor on this account, O blessed one, thou didst receive an unfading crown from God, O all-glorious martyr Isidore.

*Troparion:*
Youthfully didst thou chase thy foes, O Isidore; thou didst crush them, shielded with the dread weapon of the Cross; and girded with strength, thou didst destroy those who hated thee, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind! calling aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Following the death of the Master, for His sake thou didst endure passions; therefore the Lord of glory accounted thee worthy of incorruptible life, O martyr: into Whose hands thou didst commit thy soul, O most blessed Isidore.[^215]

*Theotokion:*
Becoming incarnate, He renews all mankind, O all-pure one; united unto him, not leaving the Father's bosom, He willed to dwell in thy womb; and having willingly become poor through the wealth of His mercy, He enriched the world with divinity.[^216]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray Thee![^217]*

*Troparion:*
A blameless and perfect sacrifice was offered unto thy Master Christ; and having been zealously slain, thou didst pour forth thy blood, O Isidore, having partaken of His passions, and having become a worthy heir of His kingdom.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened, O Savior, by Thine all-powerful might, the victorious martyr Isidore extinguished the gloom of delusion; for beheaded in the head, with laud he gave up his soul unto Thee, the Giver of life, O Master.

*Troparion:*
The Church of Christ is adorned with thy honorable blood, O blessed one, and is made bright by the resistance of thy sufferings against sin, O glorious one; for thou didst valiantly resist even unto blood, O Isidore.[^218]

*Theotokion:*
Human nature, defiled by the first transgression and grievously corrupted and deadened, thou didst raise up, having given birth beyond understanding unto the Source of immortality, O all-blameless Sovereign Lady, Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my griefs, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^219]*

*Troparion:*
Having acquired perfect love for God, O most blessed passion-bearer, for the sake of incorruptible goods thou didst truly forsake the love of earthly things, and didst ascend crying: I have cleaved after Thee, O Master![^220]

*Troparion:*
As a comely bridegroom, O Isidore, adorned with martyr-like beauties, rejoicing thou didst ascend unto the heavens, to stand before the beneficent radiance, ever praying for those who celebrate thy memory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endure slaughter, O sufferer, foreseeing for thyself eternal delight and truly ever-living food; as an unconquered sufferer with the martyrs, thou didst truly receive the crown, O most blessed Isidore.

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, dwelling place of the Master, shining radiantly like a lily with the bright rays of virginity in the midst of the confusion of thorns:[^221] I pray thee who art good, grant me forgiveness of transgressions!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled the flame, and transformed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^222]*

*Troparion:*
Now illumined with the spiritual dawn, O thrice-blessed one, be pleased that those who sing of thee may be illumined with thee, as they cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages! *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
O thy most noble struggles, whereby thou didst destroy the delusion of idols, and didst put the cruel torturers to shame, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Heal the wounds of my transgressions with the oil of thy mercy, O Sovereign Lady Virgin, for him who cries: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the fruit of thy womb![^223]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^224]*

*Troparion:*
With flatteries the torturer attempted to steal away the strength and power of thy patience unto his own wicked folly, O all-blessed Isidore; but thou, O God-wise one, fearing Christ Whom also I serve, didst cry aloud: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
All-radiant as the sun, thy calm face was shown forth by the rejoicing of martyrdom; for having been slain like a blameless lamb, thou didst offer thyself unto God,[^225] unto Whom as Creator thou didst cry aloud with hymns: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thy body was shown forth radiant unto those who love thee, borne in escort by the angelic ranks, shining, O Isidore, with the brightness of wonders and the diverse graces of wonderworking thou dost grant abundant healings unto those who cry: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Childish tongues cannot praise thee, O Parent of God, Mary, bride of God; for thou didst bear God Who is over all, O pure maiden, from a virginal and unwedded womb: Whom the youths bless, the priests praise in hymn, and the people supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Heaven was terrified at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.*

*Troparion:*
Having come down from heaven through the wealth of mercy, Thou didst willingly endure the Cross and death for our sake, as Thou art good; therefore Isidore, looking unto Thee, cried aloud: I will die with Thee, that I may live; I will be crucified with Thee, that I may reign with Thee![^226]

*Troparion:*
Lawfully poured out upon earth, the drops of thy blood, O Isidore, dyed for thee in heaven a comely robe woven with grace: clad wherein thou now standest before Christ in the heavenly chambers, O most blessed one, wearing a crown, O glorious martyr!

*Troparion:*
As thou hast boldness toward Christ, cease not to pray, O Isidore, to deliver from the tempest of misfortunes, griefs, and passions those who worthily celebrate thy sacred memory; and asking spiritual salvation, O thrice-blessed one, account us worthy of thy portion!

*Theotokion:*
As having given birth unto the Savior and Deliverer, account me worthy to obtain salvation, O all-pure maiden, loosing the fetters of my transgressions by thy prayers; for all things thou directest as thou wilt, since with incomparable goodness thou didst bear in thy womb the King of all!

## May 15. Our Venerable Father Pachomius the Great

*At Vespers, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Venerable One, 3; Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
Having stretched out all thy desire unto God, thou didst forsake worldly beauty as passing away, and didst commit thyself wholly unto Christ, O all-wise one; having cleansed thyself in every way by abstinence, O Pachomius, thou didst show forth patient resistance against the inflamed passions, and undergoing the martyrdom of conscience, O God-wise one, as a victor thou didst receive an unfading crown!

*Sticheron:*
Unto thee it has now come from active contemplation, by putting off the body, the gross veil, and casting away carnal attachment, to be united unto God, illumined by otherworldly rays and graces and beneficent splendors, and deified by divine beckoning, and to ascend unto that which is higher; and now, having become blessed, O God-bearer, pray with boldness unto Christ for our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Having surpassed all sensation, thou didst converse purely face to face, having been outside the flesh, O all-honorable one; having restrained the passions by better contemplation, and destroyed the raging of demons, thou didst trample them underfoot, O Pachomius; and now, dwelling in the heavenly habitations, remember all who honor thy memory, praying with boldness unto Christ for our souls!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Having preserved unharmed that which is according to the image,[^227] having set the mind as master over ruinous passions in ascetic struggle, thou didst ascend as far as is possible unto that which is according to the likeness; for having manfully constrained nature, thou didst hasten to subordinate the worse unto the better, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit. Therefore thou wast shown forth as the summit of monastics, a desert-dweller, instructor of those who run well, a most certain rule of virtue; and now in the heavens, the mirrors having been loosed, O our father Pachomius, thou purely beholdest the Holy Trinity, praying directly for those who honor thee in faith and love![^228]

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, of the Feast.*
*Glory, Tone 6:*
O venerable father, into all the earth has gone out the sound of thy righteous deeds;[^229] therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors thou didst destroy the hosts of demons, and didst reach the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate; having boldness toward Christ God, entreat peace for our souls!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
With the streams of thy tears thou didst cultivate the barren desert, and by sighings from the depth thou didst bring forth fruit a hundredfold in thy labors; and thou wast a beacon unto the universe, shining with wonders, O Pachomius our father: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved![^230]

*Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos, to 6; and of the Saint, to 6.*
*Whose acrostic is: "To Pachomius lovingly gives praise."*

*Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Of old an almighty power laid low the whole host of Pharaoh in the deep; and the incarnate Word consumed all-wicked sin: the supremely glorified Lord, for gloriously is He glorified![^231]*

*Troparion:*
Relying upon the goodness of thy ways, with zeal I raise a song as a servant unto thee; but illumine my mind, O God-proclaiming voice, with light-streaming splendors through thy prayers, O preeminent Pachomius!

*Troparion:*
Held by the warm desire for dispassion, O father, thou didst wither the material waves of passions, O Pachomius; and winged with love, thou didst attain unto the flowing radiance of this Godhead, O most blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst fill the heavenly ranks with joy, O wise one, having known the Creator of all creation; for in prison thou didst learn of Him Who has ineffable power, being faithfully instructed by His teachings, O God-bearer.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast higher, O pure Ever-Virgin, than all invisible and visible creation; for thou didst bear the Creator, even as He was well pleased to become incarnate in thy womb: Whom do thou pray with boldness to save our souls!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The desert has blossomed like a lily, O Lord: the barren Church of the gentiles, through Thy coming, wherein my heart is established![^232]*

*Troparion:*
As a deer running unto water, O venerable one,[^233] having been sprinkled with holy baptism, thou didst receive the dew whereby thy heart was sweetened.

*Troparion:*
Not enduring to behold the firmness of thine abstinent life, O venerable God-bearer, the hosts of demons spread out diverse temptations before thee *Troparion:*
As having acquired a sacred life, O Pachomius, thou wast the lawgiver and first guide of fasters; thou didst bring these unto Christ, O glorious one, in Whom thy heart was established.[^234]

*Theotokion:*
The angelic ranks were amazed, O all-pure one, and human hearts were struck with fear at thy childbearing; therefore as the Birthgiver of God we honor thee in faith.

*Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos.*

*Then Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having richly watered thy soul with the showers of tears, O wise father, thou didst reap a fruitful ear of thy virtues; and having become a shepherd unto venerable sheep, thou didst nurture them upon the pasture of abstinence. Therefore having been loosed from life gloriously, O Pachomius, thou wast joined unto the choir of the fathers. Therefore we cry aloud unto thee: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now... of the Feast.*

*Or Theotokion:*
O cloud of the noetic Sun,[^235] wrought-gold lampstand of the divine Light,[^236] immaculate, undefiled, all-blameless Sovereign Lady: enlighten, I pray thee, my soul darkened by the blindness of passions with the dawn of dispassion; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance; and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that with love I may cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin, pray unto Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions, for in thee I, thy servant, have hope!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross,[^237] the ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting she cried: The world indeed rejoices receiving deliverance through Thee; but my heart burns beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the sake of the mercy of Thy lovingkindness, O long-suffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have mercy and grant remission of transgressions unto those who in faith hymn Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
