*Thou hast come from a Virgin, not an ambassador nor an angel, but Thyself the Lord incarnate, and hast saved me, the whole man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^238]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast an excellent ruler of monastic assemblies, O Pachomius; for by a vision, O all-wise father, thou wast certified to arouse care for virtue.

*Troparion:*
Having received the dawn of the Holy Spirit, Thou wast a most radiant star unto all, shining with grace, whom Thou didst guide unto the haven of salvation by thy teachings.

*Troparion:*
Thy life, O God-bearer, most blessed Pachomius, became a certain rule of monastics, whereby they who follow thy divine teachings now live, O father.

*Troparion:*
Wounded with love, O blessed one, thou didst extinguish the attachment of the flesh by abstinence, O Pachomius; thou didst offer all thy life as a sweet-smelling sacrifice.[^239]

*Theotokion:*
We all, the faithful, know thee as the haven of salvation and an immovable wall, O Birthgiver of God, Sovereign Lady; for by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from misfortunes.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast become a mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, from the night of ignorance we have access unto Thy Father, the Source of Light.[^240]*

*Troparion:*
With divine love, through the teaching of the Spirit thou didst converse; illumined by which, thou didst receive the perfection of virtue, having transformed the disposition of the soul from the passions.

*Troparion:*
We bring unto Thee, O Master, Thy faster as an intercessor, asking for his sake to be enlightened with the illumination of grace and to be preserved evermore by Thine all-armor.[^241]

*Troparion:*
Having strengthened the mind by the keeping of the commandments, O Pachomius, by thine abstinence thou didst wither the wantonness of the flesh, and wast shown forth as a shepherd of the God-beloved flock.

*Theotokion:*
Pray unto thy Son and Lord, O pure Virgin, to grant deliverance unto captives from adverse circumstances, and peace unto those who put their hope in thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy lovingkindness: from corruption, O God, lead me up![^242]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst trample down the soul-destroying guiles of the demons, O father, clad in the power of thy Master, and protected by the precious Cross.

*Troparion:*
Wisely discerning the eternal things above temporal things, thou didst endure the afflictions of abstinence, O venerable one, and wast a builder of souls.

*Troparion:*
Having become exceedingly poor in spirit, O father, thou didst receive immortal and unending delight in the heavens, and ineffable wealth.[^243]

*Theotokion:*
Without husband thou didst bear, O Virgin, and remainest a virgin, showing forth the images of the true Godhead of thy Son and God.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
A radiant beacon thou didst appear at the ends of the earth, and didst make cities of the desert with multitudes of monastics; having crucified thyself, taking thy cross upon thy shoulder, and by abstinence thou didst wear out thy body, praying unceasingly for us all.[^244]

*Ikos:*
The all-radiant memory of the blessed one has appeared as another day of salvation unto us, by the brightness of abstinence driving away the deep night of the passions, and shining radiantly with beneficent action, wherein a clear and bright star appeared. In faith let us offer hymns with gladness unto the divine Pachomius; for looking down from on high he enlightens us and gives unfailing peace, praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The ungodly command of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew unto the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified![^245]*

*Troparion:*
Clad in the power of the Sovereign Lady, like dust thou didst trample underfoot the sensuality of life, overcome by love of incorruptible life, of which thou hast now also become a partaker, dwelling with the angelic choirs, O father.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired a pure heart, thou wast accounted worthy to see Him Who is invisible to all; whence as a blessed servant, O father, thou didst become a field of the Benefactor and supremely glorified One.[^246]

*Troparion:*
Having acquired orthodox wisdom, thou didst preach the Trinity in three Persons, consubstantial, O most blessed one, and the awe-inspiring incarnation of the Word; thou didst teach all to hymn the Benefactor as supremely glorified unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
We know thee as holier than the holy ones, as having alone given birth unto the immutable God, O undefiled Virgin, unwedded Mother; for unto all the faithful thou didst pour forth incorruption by thy divine childbearing.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The fiery furnace in Babylon of old divided its action by divine command, burning the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^247]*

*Troparion:*
Having manifestly learned the God-fashioned rules of fasters from an angel by divine command, O wise one,[^248] fed wherewith now the nurslings of piety cry with hymns: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Like lightning with great flashes, the shining life of thine abstinence shone forth unto all, O all-wise one, unto those who piously cry unto the Creator: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Christ Who beyond comprehension was begotten of the unbegotten Father before the ages, glorious Pachomius preached as the glory, and the all-holy Spirit, knowing one nature and Godhead of the Trinity.

*Theotokion:*
Piously we name thee the gate of light, O Virgin Mother of God; for the Father's Radiance united to the grossness of flesh thou didst bear beyond nature, O all-pure one:[^249] Whom all works of the Lord hymn, as the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The Son of the unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of a Virgin has appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened and to gather together the dispersed. Therefore the all-hymned Birthgiver of God we magnify![^250]*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the rays of grace, O Pachomius, as a faithful servant thou standest before God, and wast accounted worthy to enjoy imperishable glory. Therefore we magnify thy venerable feast.

*Troparion:*
A crown was woven for thee as victor, O Pachomius, by the life-bearing and almighty right hand; and now account those who sing of thy glorious memory, O blessed one, worthy to obtain remission of transgressions, O ever-memorable one!

*Troparion:*
Now enjoying fruits beyond comprehension for all these thy painful struggles, O Pachomius, in joy: interceding to deliver thy disciples, entreat Christ, O praiseworthy one, for those who now glorify and honor thee.

*Theotokion:*
As the fleece, O all-immaculate one,[^251] receiving the heavenly dew in thy womb, thou didst bear for us Him Who gives food unto those who piously sing of Him, and confess thee as the all-hymned Birthgiver of God.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Heavens with stars" (Не́бо звѣзда́ми):*
Desiring the angelic life, thou didst withdraw thyself into the deserts, and didst subdue carnal passions, being shown forth as equal to the angels, O God-bearing Pachomius.

*Glory, and now... of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Glory, Tone 1:*
The multitudes of monastics honor thee as an instructor our father Pachomius: for by thy path we have learned truly to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst expose the enemy's power, companion of angels, partaker with the venerable and the righteous: with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may be shown mercy!

*And now... of the Feast.*

*At the Liturgy: Beatitudes of the Feast, the appointed Ode; and of the Saint, Ode 3.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^252] Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? Epistle to the Corinthians, composite reading, section 176 [2 Cor. 4:6–15]. Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man who fears the Lord.[^253] Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth. Gospel of Luke, section 77 [Luke 14:25–35]. Communion Verse: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be.*

## May 16. Our Venerable Father Theodore the Sanctified, Disciple of Venerable Pachomius the Great

*Stichera of Theodore, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
He Who of old as Master established the ascent of clouds,[^254] having now descended upon a light cloud into Egypt,[^255] foretold that the elect should shine forth, who are caught up on the clouds as God-wise ones;[^256] with whom Theodore, our sanctified father, an immovable pillar, unswerving toward virtue, shining with the most radiant splendors of Christ, with boldness prays for our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Egypt, which formerly raged with demonic sacrifices and passions, is now adorned with the ranks of fasters, and is shown forth adorned with diverse beauties and divine virtues; wherein shone forth Theodore the venerable, God-like, and acceptable to God, named after divine gifts,[^257] who by marvelous humility emulated Christ: with boldness he prays for our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Night-vigil, abstinence, humility, patience, avoidance of malice, the reception of virtue: from youth the wise Theodore, a great faster, was shown forth as God-wise, having become a fervent lover of divine love, and rejoicing, he surpassed all visible things, elevated by a marvelous manner of life, conversing with God: with boldness he now prays for our souls!

*Glory, and now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
A dweller of the desert, an angel in the body, and a wonderworker thou wast shown forth, O our God-bearing father Theodore; having received heavenly gifts by fasting, vigilance, and prayer, thou healest the sick and the souls of those who run unto thee in faith. Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who through thee works healings for all![^258]

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I hymn thee to God as a sanctified gift."*

*Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Of old an almighty power laid low the whole host of Pharaoh in the deep; and the incarnate Word consumed all-wicked sin: the supremely glorified Lord, for gloriously is He glorified![^259]*

*Troparion:*
Unto me who desire to sing of thy celebration now in hymns, as thou art named after divine gifts, grant me a light-bearing gift, drawing forth divine grace from inexhaustible treasures, O ever-memorable one! *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Meditating diligently upon the pure law, O father, thou wast all pure and immaculate; and joining thyself unto the all-pure Word, thou wast shown forth as a bright vessel of purity, and sanctified, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Having put thyself to death unto the world, O father, piously walking an angel-like life upon earth, thou didst follow Him Who shone forth from the virginal womb, taking thy cross upon thy shoulder, O God-wise and all-blessed one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The desert has blossomed like a lily, O Lord: the barren Church of the gentiles, through Thy coming, wherein my heart is established![^260]*

*Troparion:*
As a beloved gift unto the Birthgiver of God, O venerable one, thou didst offer thy soul saved, O Theodore, and transformed by abstinence from passions.

*Troparion:*
Sanctified unto God, thou didst surpass the perception of visible things, O all-wise one, and with pure mind didst converse with the Mind that ever abides, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst withdraw into the deserts, fleeing, O glorious one, having received God the Word Who shone forth from the Virgin: in Whom also thou wast saved, O wonder-worthy one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*

*Ikos (continued):*
In the house of God thou didst blossom like a palm tree,[^261] and by excellent fasting didst bring forth fruits of virtues unto the Lord, O venerable father; therefore thou art also called blessed as equal in standing unto the bodiless powers.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Clad in divine power, thou didst strip bare the hosts of adversary powers, O God-bearer; and having shone forth with the brightness of virtues, thou didst flash the dawn of healings, O venerable father: entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory, and now... of the Feast.*

*Or Theotokion:*
A divine tabernacle of the Word thou wast,[^262] O only all-pure Virgin Mother, having surpassed the angels in purity. Cleanse me, who have become dust above all men and am defiled by carnal transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, O pure one, granting great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him Who sprouted from her without pain suspended upon the Cross, lamenting as a mother, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast come from a Virgin, not an ambassador nor an angel, but Thyself the Lord incarnate, and hast saved me, the whole man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^263]*

*Troparion:*
Having become a co-dweller with the divine Pachomius, thou didst imitate the ways of his abstinence, O God-wise father Theodore, being conformed unto him by orthodox faith.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst worthily obtain the celebrated Kingdom and divine radiance, as having been skilled in purity, O father, unto the communion of ineffable beauty.

*Troparion:*
The Ethiopian settlements, the demonic hordes, were terrified at thy patience and humility, and at thy unceasing standing in vigils, O wonder-worthy one.

*Theotokion:*
In ecstasy the heads of the mighty were cut off in fear of thy childbearing,[^264] O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God; and the assembly of the venerable was worthily amazed by divine grace.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast become a mediator between God and men, O Christ God: for through Thee, O Master, from the night of ignorance we have access unto Thy Father, the Source of Light.[^265]*

*Troparion:*
Having fervently held venerable teachings, thou didst destroy the deceptive acceptance of all heresies, O wise one, by faith in the Trinity, protecting thy flock. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Truly as a light thou wast upon earth, O Theodore, having lived a blameless life, O ever-memorable one, adornment of fasters, praise of monastics.

*Theotokion:*
Watering thy heart with the stream of ascetic tears, thou didst show it forth fruitful unto Christ, Who shone forth from the holy Virgin, Mother of God, O most wealthy Theodore.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy lovingkindness: from corruption, O God, lead me up![^266]*

*Troparion:*
Thy bright and God-pleasing life showed forth unto thee the heavenly brightness of the saints in the heavenly mansions, O father Theodore. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
A conqueror prevailing over the craftiness of spirits thou wast, O Theodore, having acquired the Comforter Spirit as a helper, O recipient of God.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art a light cloud, O pure one,[^267] whereon sitting the almighty Lord came into Egypt, and made luminaries of the venerable.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The ungodly command of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread out spiritual dew unto the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified![^268]*

*Troparion:*
Understandingly, like a beholder of God, thou didst desire to see divine brightness, O Theodore; therefore thou didst leave behind all visible things, and having entered into darkness thou wast accounted worthy to behold the Incomprehensible.[^269] *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
In no way receiving the poison of the soul-corrupting serpent, O most blessed Theodore, thou didst destroy the memory of him, O father, having unceasingly acquired the memory of God in thy heart, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
The Holy of holies, Who rests in the saints, thou didst holily bear, O all-immaculate Virgin: Him Who for the sake of much mercy became man from thee, the Word previously bodiless incarnate.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The fiery furnace in Babylon of old divided its action by divine command, burning the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^270]*

*Troparion:*
Thou, radiant in form, conversing with the all-radiant Light wast shown forth, O most blessed Theodore. And deified beyond nature by a better union, thou criest now: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having become a steadfast martyr against sin, resisting even unto blood, O God-wise Theodore,[^271] thou didst adorn thy head with the crown of suffering, crying now: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
From thee, O Mother of God, shone forth unto us the bright Sun of righteousness,[^272] enlightening all things with the radiance of Godhead, having become incarnate: Whom now we hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The Son of the unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of a Virgin has appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened and to gather together the dispersed. Therefore the all-hymned Birthgiver of God we magnify![^273]*

*Troparion:*
Now not in riddles is Christ seen by Thee, but rather face to face is He seen in true manifestation,[^274] the shadow-like mirrors having been loosed, O namesake of gifts, of the Savior and our God! *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Thy divine desire was fulfilled, O Theodore, as thou didst attain the end which by fasting from afar thou didst behold with compunction; and having obtained it, thou didst stay thy desires, praise of fasters, splendor of monastics.

*Theotokion:*
The first Adam, who through disobedience was corrupted by diseases, was renewed through thee, O Mother of God; for having ineffably given birth unto the incarnate Word without corruption, having uprooted corruption, thou didst blossom forth incorruption.

## May 17. Holy Apostle Andronicus and Those with Him

*Stichera of the Saints, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
By the word thou didst stay the irrational darkness of godlessness, as an obedient hearer of the Word, and a glorious minister, and a most radiant luminary of the universe, O God-blessed one: therefore with divine words we glorify thee, and joyfully celebrate thy holy memory, offering praise unto the Almighty!

*Sticheron:*
Paul, the divine Apostle, praising you brightly with divine praises, presents your valiant fortitude unto the faithful,[^275] saying that before him you were shown forth as disciples of God the Word, O all-wise ones; and his kinsmen, the glorious Junia, as truly is so, and blessed Andronicus, pleasing servants of God!

*Sticheron:*
Having enlightened the earth by your all-sacred preaching, you drove away the mist of polytheism, and guided unto the light of the knowledge of God those who of old were in the darkness of godlessness, O divine Apostles, guides of salvation, and intercessors for all who ever honor you in faith!

*Glory, and now... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Holy Apostles, entreat the merciful God to grant remission of transgressions unto our souls![^276]

*Canon, the composition of Joseph.*
*Whose acrostic is: "Praise I offer to the wise Andronicus."*

*Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea: for He alone is gloriously glorified![^277]*

*Troparion:*
The venerable Church offers praise unto God in thy most holy memory, O blessed one, glorifying thy struggles in faith.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst direct all the movements of thy mind, O sanctified one, unto the fulfillment of divine desires, rejoicing, O Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
To cleanse diseases, to drive away evil spirits, thou didst receive divine grace from God, O God-wise Andronicus.

*Theotokion:*
Rain down upon me, O Virgin, deliverance from all transgressions and amendment of life, by thine intercession and grace.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as the Lord, and there is none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^278]*

*Troparion:*
Having put thy members to death by ascetic struggles,[^279] thou wast accounted worthy to see the Life of the living, Christ our God, bearing flesh upon earth, O Apostle Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast fire-breathing with the divine Spirit,[^280] renewing the warmth by thy pure thought, O Apostle, and consuming the thorns of delusion, O all-praised Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
The sound of thy words and the power of thy wonders ran through the ends of the world,[^281] O disciple of the Lord, teaching the faithful the graces of the divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
There is none all-pure above thee, O Sovereign Lady, and there is none immaculate besides save thee, all-immaculate one; for thou alone hast been a spacious dwelling-place of the God of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^282]
Let us praise the all-radiant star, who enlightened the nations with the light of the knowledge of God, Christ’s Apostle Andronicus, and with him all-wise Junia, who labored in the Gospel, crying: Pray unceasingly to Christ God for us all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Having become luminous, divine Andronicus enlightened hearts with the light of the knowledge of God to come unto the Lord; therefore even after his demise he pours forth healings unto those who draw near with faith to his honorable temple, and he entreats great mercy for all the faithful.

*Glory..., Now and ever... Of the feast, or Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou hast renewed the mortal nature of earthborn men that was corrupted in passions, and thou hast raised all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore as is our duty we all call thee blessed, O all-glorious Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.

*Staurotheotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she spoke: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, desiring to give life unto the dead, as Thou art merciful?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
The prophet, discerning with divine vision that from the shadowed mountain, the only Theotokos, Thou, O Word, wast to become incarnate, with fear gave glory to Thy power.

*Troparion:*
Paul salutes thee before the faithful as a kinsman, proclaiming thy divine praises, O divinely-speaking Andronicus, guide of light unto those who praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Having become a temple of the Most Holy Trinity, thou didst tear down heathen temples and sacrificial shrines, O blessed one; and thou didst build churches, O all-lauded one, for the salvation of our souls.

*Troparion:*
Having been strengthened by divine power, thou wast able to plunder the vessels of the strong one, O blessed one,[^283] and to make them a dwelling-place of the Holy Spirit, O Andronicus.

*Theotokion:*
Thee, O Virgin Theotokos, all we the faithful ever praise in unison, as our steadfast and champion intercession, and the salvation of our souls.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou Who hast enlightened the ends of the world from the night of ignorance by the knowledge of God enlighten me with the morning of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
As a kinsman to Paul, and having been a disciple before him,[^284] with him we honor thee, having assembled in faith, O Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Let us honor the sacred apostles as radiant stars: divine Junia and God-bearing Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Blessed Paul proclaims you in the Church, O blessed ones, as notable among the apostles.

*Theotokion:*
The choir of the prophets prefigured thee, O all-holy Maiden, by sacred types, to be the Bearer of the Lord.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
Thy house pours forth fountains of healings for those who hasten to it with love, O Apostle, washing away spiritual infirmities.

*Troparion:*
The multitude of the nations, corrupted by godlessness, thou didst renew by thy novel teachings, O true friend of incorruptible Jesus.

*Troparion:*
Like the sun hast thou traversed the earth, O all-wise Andronicus, announcing to the faithful the uncreated light of the thrice-radiant Godhead.

*Theotokion:*
The divine goings were made known in thee, O Sovereign Lady, of Him Who became poor in the flesh and enriched the faithful, O unwedded Bride.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the feast.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst lay the foundation of the earth and establish the heavens by Thy Word: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Grievous infirmities and every sickness thou drivest away from the souls and bodies of those who hasten into thy house, O wise disciple of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thou drivest away demonic assaults, and through fellow-suffering cleansest grievous passions for mankind, O eyewitness of Christ; therefore we faithfully call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Having cast down the rhetoric of philosophers by the simplicity of thy teachings, O wise one, thou didst proclaim God in the likeness of flesh, Who dwelt with earthly beings for the sake of goodness.

*Theotokion:*
Thou Who didst dwell within the womb of the Virgin and re-create Adam in her: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain and by fire in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: Praise ye the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having seasoned with divine salt by thy teachings the human hearts that were festering with evil corruption, O Andronicus, thou didst present them saved unto the outpoured Word, O thrice-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast known as a priest, sacrificer, and sacrifice unto Him Who was sacrificed out of mercy, and thou didst ascend unto Him, O Andronicus, having received from Him the crown of incorruption and righteousness.

*Troparion:*
A divine preacher and God-beholding apostle, a destroyer of pernicious carved idols, and an enricher of the poor didst thou appear, O glorious one, and a great citizen of Sion on high, O divinely-wise Andronicus.

*Theotokion:*
As with tongs in thy hands thou bearest the Coal, O Virgin, Who pours forth divine dew for those who are distressed in the flame of deception, and burns away the passions of those who hymn His kingdom.[^285]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The birthgiving of the Ever-Virgin revealed of old to the lawgiver on the mountain in the fire and the bush, unto the salvation of us the faithful, we magnify with unceasing hymns.

*Troparion:*
Having appeared as an inheritor of everlasting life, those who were deadened aforetime by demonic assaults hast thou made alive by thy life-bearing word, O right-blessed Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Having become a well-grown branch of the True Vine, O most blessed one, thou didst distill the wine of immortality and make glad the hearts of men, O all-lauded Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Having clearly burned up the stronghold of polytheism like lightning, thou didst show forth the light of the knowledge of God unto those who were in the darkness of this life; therefore we all glorify thee, O Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Thy sacred memory extended unto the ends of the earth today enlightens the hearts of those who sacredly praise thee therein, O right-blessed Andronicus.

*Theotokion:*
Entreat enlightenment for us, O thou who gavest birth to the Light, and remission of transgressions, and salvation of souls for those who know thee as the true Theotokos, O pure one, or all-immaculate one.

## May 18. Holy Martyr Theodotus of Ancyra; and Holy Martyrs Peter, Dionysius, and Those with Them; and the Seven Holy Virgins

*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron:*
Adorned with peaceable disposition, O most blessed Theodotus, thou wast piously chosen to serve all; and illumined with the martyr's crown, thou exultest in the heavens with those who suffered with thee; with them pray thou that we may be saved.

*Sticheron:*
Having offered thyself unto the Lord as a sacred vessel and obeyed His words, O divinely-blessed one, thou didst lavishly distribute undefiled wealth unto the poor, O wise martyr, wisely and marvelously making tortures thine own gain.

*Sticheron:*
Lo, a river of salvation, lo, a living fountain, the shrine of the holy virgins is revealed; therefore come ye eagerly, let us who are sick draw healing now, and joy for those who are in sorrow, through the prayers of the holy brides of Christ.

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron:*
Illumined by the radiance of the thrice-radiant Godhead, ye passed through the darkness of torments and were revealed as luminous stars, the divine establishment of the Church, ever guiding in light, O holy martyrs;[^286] therefore we celebrate your radiant solemnity, venerating your relics and the dust of your bodies.

*Sticheron:*
Peter, Dionysius, Paul, Andrew, and Benedimus, the valiant adamants, Christina, Heraclius, and Paulinus, let us all the faithful now call blessed with joy, who suffered steadfastly for the Trinity, and trampled underfoot all the wiles of the serpent, and drove away the darkness of idolatry by grace.

*Sticheron:*
With your blood, O blessed ones, ye purchased the unshakable kingdom of God, and ye reached the calm havens, having endured every tempest of cruel afflictions with manly resolve, O divinely-blessed ones; therefore ye are called blessed and glorified by all, ever praying that we may find mercy in the day of judgment.

*Glory..., Now and ever..., Of the feast.*

*Canon of the Martyr and the Holy Women, Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who marvelously drowned Pharaoh in the sea, a song of victory, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who strengthens human weakness against the invisible enemy and crowns it with victories.

*Troparion:*
Having received the victory given by God, O passion-bearer, thou didst appear invincible against adversaries, O glorious Theodotus, vanquishing them.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the beauty of divine purity, ye entered the judgment hall as into a bridal chamber, O virgins, hymning Christ the Bridegroom.

*Theotokion:*
By thy childbearing, O Theotokos, we have all become partakers of divine glory, who with love glorify thee as the Mother of the God of all.

*Another Canon of the Holy Martyrs, the same Tone.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
With a song I will sing unto Thee,[^287] O Lord my God, for Thou hast brought out the people from the bondage of Egypt, and hast covered the chariots of Pharaoh and his power.

*Troparion:*
O champions of the faith, deliver from passions and from every distress those who ever keep your sacred solemnity with faith.

*Troparion:*
Winged marvelously with the sail of the Cross, O God-bearers, ye crossed the abyss of tortures without becoming wet, having drowned the enemies.

*Troparion:*
Ye were joined unto the heavenly ranks, having endured wounds in the body, O holy ones; therefore today we honor your divine memory.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the divine law, O wise ones, ye brought to naught the counsels of the lawless; and having suffered lawfully, ye attained divine glory.

*Theotokion:*
As the throne of the King, O Virgin, upon which He sat and prepared a heavenly seat for the faithful, we honor thee and glorify thee with love.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
My heart is established in the Lord my God; therefore the weak have girded themselves with strength.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened in the Lord thy God, O martyr Theodotus, thou didst bring to naught the opposing enemies by the truth.

*Troparion:*
Having cast off the grossness of the flesh through abstinence, O passion-bearers, ye soared unto the height of suffering.

*Troparion:*
Not inclining your ear to the flatteries of the enemies, ye suffered lawfully, having held fast unto the divine laws.[^288]

*Theotokion:*
Established upon the rock of hope in thee, O Virgin, we shall in no wise be shaken by the surging tempest of temptations.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty is weakened, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having set the feet of thy soul upon the rock of faith, O blessed Peter, thou wast not overcome by the resistance of the godless, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thy city presents thee unto the world as a shining star, O Peter, driving away the night of godlessness by divine grace.

*Troparion:*
Gaining strength by looking unto God, O Peter, though broken by wheels and bonds and beaten incessantly, thou didst show thyself a crown-bearer.

*Troparion:*
Offering hymnody unto God, O passion-bearers, ye endured every onset of torments; therefore ye were accounted worthy of crowns.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone hast wrought wonder in giving birth; thou alone wast truly known as the minister of the great mystery, having given birth unto God, O Theotokos, Mother Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Anointed with the Divine Spirit, thou wast a servant unto all, O glorious one; and having suffered steadfastly, thou wast greatly glorified. Therefore having come together we honor thee, O Theodotus, keeping thy holy memory with faith, in which by thy prayers save all.

*Glory..., Tone 4, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Adorned with martyr’s blood, ye stand before the Lord, the King of all, O noble-victorious martyrs;[^289] therefore with gladness we celebrate your honorable memory today, asking to receive through you remission of debts and great mercy.

*Now and ever... Of the feast, or Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin who gavest birth to God the Most High, with the bodiless hosts unceasingly pray Him to grant remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end unto us who with faith and love praise thee as is our duty, O only all-hymned one.

*Staurotheotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she spoke: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, desiring to give life unto the dead, as Thou art merciful?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard the report of Thy dispensation, that Thou wast born of a Virgin Thou wast born of a Virgin, not consuming her who bare Thee, and I glorified Thy condescension, O Savior.

*Troparion:*
Having drowned the persecutor in thy martyr’s blood as in a sea, O martyr, thou rejoicest in incorruptible delight under Christ the Giver of crowns.

*Troparion:*
The well-praised choir of passion-bearers shone forth: having lit brightly-shining lamps, the seven wise virgins entered into Christ's divine bridal chamber.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as a spiritual crown, O Virgin, the virgin passion-bearers boast in thee, singing of thy Son and God, their Bridegroom and Master.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of love of Thine image, O Compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise.

*Troparion:*
Having shaken off from the eyelids of your souls the slumber that comes of sloth, by divine vigilance ye utterly quenched the evil design of the godless.

*Troparion:*
Like stones of the Word, having been put to death with stones, Andrew, Paul, and Dionysius were accounted worthy of everlasting life.

*Troparion:*
The most beautiful flowers of piety, and the true and divine fragrance of Christ, let us praise the martyrs today with faith.

*Troparion:*
Having despised earthly things through divine longing and endured bodily pains valiantly, rejoicing they passed over unto the life free of pain.

*Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Virgin, Birthgiver of God, save those who praise thee and place their hope in thee from every distress, tribulation, and sorrow.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou Who hast shone forth light, enlightened the morning, and shown the day: Glory to Thee, glory to Thee, O Jesus, Son of God!

*Troparion:*
Pouring forth the wine of teaching from the cup of thy soul, O wise one, thou didst make glad the hearts of the pious, O glorious Theodotus.

*Troparion:*
Having drunk of the honorable dew of abstinence, ye wisely put forth flowers of purity and were revealed as the sweet fragrance of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Accounting bodily sufferings as delight for the sake of the love of the future life, the virgins suffered valiantly for His sake.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered from the deception of idol-madness by thy divine childbearing, we praise thee, O pure one, the cause and mediatress of good things.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Send down Thy light unto us, O Lord, and loose us from the gloom of transgressions, O Good One, granting us Thy peace *Troparion:*
Sealed with intelligible light, O martyrs, by divine power ye passed without stumbling through the darkness of torments and the evil night of polytheism.

*Troparion:*
Shining with virginal virtues, O Christina, thou wast brightly adorned with the rays of thy wounds, and rejoicing didst dwell in the spiritual bridal chamber of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Those who frantically lusted after thy body, O martyr Christina, bound to the desire of God by thy sweet word, suffered steadfastly.[^290]

*Theotokion:*
O unwedded Bride of God, through whom, having been loosed from the ancient debt, we have been shown forth as sons of the Heavenly Father by grace: preserve those who sing of thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
As Thou didst deliver the prophet Jonah from the whale, O Christ God: lead me up also from the depth of transgressions, and save me, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having found thee as a divine haven, O Theodotus, the faithful turned aside from the surging waves of the wicked, praising Christ the Helmsman.

*Troparion:*
Through love of the Creator the virgin passion-bearers strengthened natural weakness for deeds beyond nature, and nobly endured therein.

*Troparion:*
Having lit lamps equal in number unto the sevenfold-radiant candlestick,[^291] ye entered the contest of suffering as into a bridal chamber, O virgins.

*Theotokion:*
The earth was delivered from the ancient curse with those who dwell upon it by thy childbearing, O Virgin; therefore we ever glorify thee as the cause of blessing.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the prophet Jonah cried aloud, praying in the whale: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparion:*
Glorious Heraclius, having contested valiantly, received great glory, becoming a citizen of paradise; and having joined the honorable choir of sufferers, he now rejoices.

*Troparion:*
With Paulinus let us sing of divine Benedimus, and Andrew, Heraclius, and Dionysius, Peter and Paul, and Christina, the martyrs of the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Torn asunder and unjustly beaten with stones, and consumed by fire, O glorious martyrs, ye were accounted worthy to dwell in the Church of the firstborn.[^292]

*Troparion:*
Unto Him Who showed forth the all-wise passion-bearers shining like stars upon the firmament of the Church, singing piously let us bow down unto the Son of God!

*Theotokion:*
As a fertile field, O Theotokos, thou hast sprouted the living Ear of corn Who nourishes the faithful with life, and truly turns away pernicious famine from the earth.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having contested well as a martyr with thy fellow sufferers, O Theodotus, ye received crowns of honor together with the venerable virgin passion-bearers; therefore unceasingly pray Christ God for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst speak with Moses on the mountain and show forth the type of virginity in the bush: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Exposing the wiles of the enemies by spiritual wisdom, O passion-bearer, thou didst save from their deception the martyrs who suffered with thee.

*Troparion:*
Winged in their thoughts with love for incorruptible goods, the virgins ascended unto them by struggles, praising Christ.

*Troparion:*
In no wise yielding to the deception of the author of malice, O passion-bearers, ye kept the commandment of the Creator whole, O glorious ones.

*Theotokion:*
To behold the glory of Him Who was born of thee, and to enjoy it with the martyrs,[^293] ever entreat Him for those who sing of thee, O all-pure one.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Of old in Babylon the Abrahamite children trampled down the flame of the furnace, crying aloud with songs: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Faithfully emulating the young children in Babylon, ye eagerly entered into the fire, crying aloud, O blessed ones: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Ye bowed not down unto carved idols, O glorious sufferers; fleeing them, ye endured ever-memorable death, clearly receiving immortal delights.

*Troparion:*
With manly soul and firmness of disposition the martyrs cast down the rages of the lawless, crying aloud faithfully: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the words of the divine martyrs,[^294] O Paulinus, thou didst choose to die, O all-glorious one, crying aloud unto Him Who made all things: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
The ark of God, the jar of divine manna, the golden candlestick and table, the holy mountain, the Theotokos, let all we the faithful praise with songs.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Christ the King, Whom the captive children confessed in the furnace in the furnace, saying with a great voice: Praise ye the Lord, all ye works!

*Troparion:*
Having Christ the Benefactor preserving and strengthening thee ever in all things in the pains of suffering, O Theodotus, thou didst accomplish deeds beyond nature, singing unto God and King Himself.

*Troparion:*
By the good pleasure of Christ, the good virgins, having sweetened their souls, ran after Him, having suffered valiantly, and in joy cried aloud: We exalt Thee above all, O Christ, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
O Creator of all things, Thou, O Word, didst strengthen the weakness of the female nature in sufferings as God; therefore having suffered valiantly, the holy women sang: Thee we praise as God and King.

*Theotokion:*
The Renewer of human nature came forth from thee, O pure one; for having taken on humanity, as the God and Creator Who loves mankind, He formed our nature anew, who confess thee to be truly the Theotokos.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O almighty Deliverer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst refresh those who lived piously, and didst teach them to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and praise Him!

*Troparion:*
By stripping off the earthen body, the sufferers were clothed in a robe woven of grace on high, crying aloud with love: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and praise Him!

*Troparion:*
Cleansed by the sprinkling of divine blood, O martyrs, ye poured out your blood for Christ, singing unceasingly: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and praise Him!

*Troparion:*
Ye appeared as beautiful clusters of grapes of the spiritual vine, O blessed ones, pouring forth for us the wine of piety, as we cry aloud with love: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and praise Him!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast become a spiritual place of propitiation, receiving the prayers of all, and offering them up unto thy Son, O all-pure one, for the redemption of those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and praise Him!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thy seedless childbearing, O pure Theotokos, Christ our God, we magnify with unceasing hymns.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst conquer the hosts of deception by spiritual power, O glorious one, and didst receive the crown of victory, O Theodotus.

*Troparion:*
Breathing forth wonders like fragrant ointments, O Theodotus, thou fillest with sweet fragrance all those who glorify thee with faith and love.

*Troparion:*
Shining with the dawn of purity, ye appeared beautiful in goodness in suffering, O noble virgins.

*Troparion:*
Having kindled a seven-lighted intercession, O honorable ones, to guide us to guide us unto the unwaning light of Christ God.

*Theotokion:*
Surpassing all love, the desire for thee, O all-pure one, draws me to cry aloud unto thee unceasingly: Rejoice, O Lady!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve, through the sickness of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the budding of the fruit of the womb, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Having willingly offered yourselves with desire, O noble martyrs, rejoicing ye ardently gave up your blood, and received wealth that cannot be taken away; and now ye are numbered among the choirs of martyrs, O all-praised ones.

*Troparion:*
Ye appeared as spiritual flowers, O passion-bearers, breathing forth the sweet fragrance of piety, and citizens of the divine city, salvific havens for those who are in the tempest, luminaries of truth and champions; therefore ye are worthily called blessed.

*Troparion:*
With Peter and Paul, let us honor wise[^295] Heraclius, Paulinus, and Benedimus, together with Andrew, Christina, and Dionysius, who suffered steadfastly for Christ, and destroyed deception.

*Troparion:*
The earth indeed received your blood that was poured out, O renowned ones,[^296] for the Benefactor of all; and the dwelling places of the righteous received your spirits; rejoicing with them, remember those who ever commemorate you.

*Theotokion:*
We the faithful joyfully offer unto thee the voice of the angels, O Bride of God: Rejoice, helper of mankind, confirmation of the martyrs and apostles, and proclamation of all the prophets, O all-immaculate Mother of God!

## May 19. Holy Hieromartyr Patrick, Bishop of Prusa, and His Companions

*Alleluia.*

*Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Laid Aside All" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

*Sticheron:*
Steered by fire, the blessed hieromartyrs easily sailed across the turbulent surging of sufferings; and by hot waters they were entrusted to heal the afflictions of men; whose relics, having been buried in sacred manner, pour forth healings richly unto those in need; let us worthily honor them, O faithful, who unceasingly pray with boldness for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Wearing a purple robe of martyr's blood, and holding the precious Cross as a scepter, the divine martyrs reign with Christ rejoicing, for Whose sake they willingly chose to suffer: sacredly-revealed Polyaenus, Acacius, and Menander, with God-wise Patrick; and now rejoicing they stand before the throne of Christ, praying with boldness for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Desiring the kingdom of Christ, the blessed martyrs accounted temporal death as delight, and having endured famine and the wounds of pain with manly courage, O noble ones; therefore they became protectors unto all the faithful, ungrudgingly procuring health for souls and bodies; whom now we the faithful worthily hymn with joy, as they pray unto the Lord for our souls.

*Stichera of the Theotokos. (If it be a fast and Alleluia is sung, then the Stichera of the Theotokos are sung first, then those of the saints; Tone and Podoben the same) (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, fulfillment of the law; rejoice, temple of the Holy Trinity, incorrupt Bride; rejoice, divine carriage of the King of all; rejoice, immaterial fire, bearing upon thy hands as with tongs the Live Coal; new paradise of the enclosed garden, divine and most radiant table; undefiled dove, throne of the Most High, spiritual couch of God, whom as a maiden the Holy Spirit overshadowed!

*Sticheron:*
Wounded by the robbers' demonic assaults, and lying altogether helpless, O all-immaculate one, upon the path of this ever-unstable life, and begging for mercy: visit me speedily, pouring out wine and oil upon my incurable wounds, and restore me to health, that I may glorify thee and worthily hymn thy great deeds with love, O pure Mother, Ever-Virgin!

*Sticheron:*
Having slipped into evil ways and been enslaved by crafty deception, O Bride of God, I the wretched one run to thy most marvelous mercy and fervent protection, O most holy Maiden. Deliver me from the bond of misfortunes and sorrows, and save me, O all-immaculate one, from demonic assaults, that I may glorify thee, and sing and bow down with love, and magnify thee, O most blessed Lady!

*Glory..., Now and ever..., Theotokion:*
Deliver me from the whale's belly of crafty sin, O Lady, who didst contain in thy womb Him Who cannot be contained;

deliver me from the grievous waves of temptations, snatch me away from the tempest of falls, O Maiden, drying up the deep of mine iniquities and quelling the assaults of demonic encampment by thy divine assistance, O pure one, that I may unceasingly glorify thee, the ever-blessed!

*Staurotheotokion:*
When the undefiled ewe and immaculate Lady of old beheld her Lamb upon the wood of the Cross, crying out maternally and marveling she cried aloud: O sweetest Child, what is this new and glorious sight? How hath the graceless council delivered Thee unto Pilate's judgment seat and condemned Thee to death, Who art the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*Canon, the work of Theophanes, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonderworking rod of Moses of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, drowned the chariot-driver Pharaoh, but saved fleeing Israel going on foot, as they sang a song unto God.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with all-various graces,[^297] with the vesture of the priesthood and immediately with the crimson dye of martyr's blood, ye were adorned, O glorious ones; and having been well adorned, ye were revealed as champions of piety and firm in faith.

*Troparion:*
Christ, the Ordainer of the contest, Who sits at the right hand of the Father, having opened the stadium of struggles unto all sufferers, gathered together the choir of four martyrs, joined in harmonious age, who magnify Him with songs.

*Troparion:*
As protector of the Church, O Patrick, like a good shepherd thou didst lay down thy life for her, O blessed one; with Acacius, Menander, and divine Polyaenus, enrich thyself with all-wise intercessors, ever sending up hymnody unto God.

*Theotokion:*
Following the words of the divine prophets, the divinely-speaking martyrs confessed the Word of God Who took flesh from the all-pure Virgin; glorifying her as the Theotokos, we ever bless her with songs.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and founded the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for none is holy save Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Having manfully trodden the path of martyrdom and finished the good course, let us the faithful bless them as victorious martyrs, and divine crown-bearers, and God-wise hierarchs.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with the zeal of piety, the God-proclaiming one withered the flame of deception, eloquently declaring[^298] the divinely-spoken commands with firmness, and bringing all unto the divine knowledge of truth.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the fierce governor slain by the drunkenness of impiety, Patrick cried aloud with a high voice the words of divine knowledge, sowing unto all the divine word of faith.

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing wedlock, O Theotokos, thou didst give birth unto the Word of God, the Son incarnate of thee; Whom when the martyrs manfully confessed, they became His soldiers and crown-bearers.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
With Menander, let us honor in songs wise Acacius, and Polyaenus, sacred through faith, and great Patrick; for having been priests of God the Almighty, they were well-pleasing unto Him through faith. Therefore at the close of life they were radiantly accounted worthy of martyrdom and joined unto the angels; unto whom we cry aloud with love: Pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who with love honor your holy memory!

*Glory..., Now and ever..., Of the feast, or Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the heavenly gate and the ark, the all-holy mountain, the radiant cloud, the unburnt bush, the rational paradise, the recall of Eve, the great treasure of all the universe; for salvation was wrought in her for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions. Therefore we cry aloud unto her: Pray thy Son to grant remission of transgressions unto those who piously bow down before thine all-holy childbearing!

*Staurotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd, and the Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting she cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb is inflamed, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest out of the tenderheartedness of mercy,[^299] O longsuffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain! Have mercy and grant remission of transgressions unto those who with faith hymn Thy divine sufferings!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the bosom of the Father, and hast visited our poverty. Therefore with the prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Clothed with the power of Christ, O Patrick, for thou wast His priest, and following His footsteps manifestly, thou didst offer thyself in martyrdom unto the only Benefactor, piously crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a divine celebrant of sacred mysteries, O Acacius; having been illumined with divine rays,[^300] and revealed as a true martyr of Christ, thou didst spit upon the deception of idols. Therefore also with a joyful conscience thou didst cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
In the assembly[^301] of Thy martyrs, O Christ, Menander, dancing and rejoicing, now shines with Thy rays, and is beautifully adorned with outpourings of light, and there rejoicing he cries unto Thee, O Master: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Having obtained Thine all-immaculate Mother as a weapon, O Christ, the wise martyrs vanquished the crafty wiles of deception and manifestly cast away the flatteries of the torturers. And now having appeared as crown-bearers, they cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and a strange darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
Having died unto the world through the pains of abstinence, O God-wise martyrs, ye ardently offered yourselves unto Christ as perfect sacrifices, having offered yourselves as lambs, ye submit unto the Master's divine commands.

*Troparion:*
Having suffered lawfully, O all-praised martyrs, ye were crowned; for having endured bitter pains and wounds from the lawless, ye were accounted worthy to enjoy incorruptible life in the heavens.

*Troparion:*
Noetically dwelling now with the angels, O blessed Patrick, remember those who celebrate thine honorable memory, fervently praying by thy supplications unto the Master to deliver us from misfortunes, O all-praised one!

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee to be truly the Theotokos, O glorious martyrs, knowing the Word of God born of thee they proclaimed Him, and valiantly resisted sin even unto death, O all-hymned Maiden!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The abyss of sins and the tempest of transgressions trouble me, and cast me into the depth of violent despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me, as unto Peter, O Helmsman, and save me!

*Troparion:*
The choir of sufferers composed of four, a martyr and a hierarch, worthily and splendidly illumined: let us pious people, having now formed a choir, joyfully praise them!

*Troparion:*
The outpourings of hot waters from your tombs, O sufferers, loose bodily diseases for the sick; and the passions of the soul are transformed by your divine prayers for those who draw near with faith.

*Troparion:*
Raise up for me a spiritual morning, lying as I am in the darkness of transgressions and abiding in defiled life, O blessed Patrick, by thy prayers, beseeching Christ the Deliverer.

*Theotokion:*
Illumine my soul, O Virgin Theotokos, all-hymned one, who didst bear the Radiance of the Father's glory; for having thy grace, the sufferers cast away the vain speech of the torturers.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
As one brightly shining with the beauty of the priesthood and preeminently adorned with the blood of martyrdom, standing before Christ with those who suffered with thee, O Patrick: remember us, as an honorable sufferer!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire was ashamed of God's condescension in Babylon of old; therefore the children in the furnace, dancing with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The glorious martyrs, adorned with the vesture of the priesthood, having made it yet more sacred with their own blood, rejoicing in divine vision sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Illumined with divine light, O blessed one, thou didst refute the ferocity of Annius,[^302] as in a furnace in the hot baths wherein thou wast not burned, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The proud governor could not endure thy wise words, O glorious one, whereby thou didst expose the all-defiled and falsely named gods; spitting upon them, thou didst sing rejoicing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Lo, now is fulfilled the divine prophecy of divine David,[^303] for the four martyrs lovingly entreat thy face, O pure one, crying aloud unto Him Who was incarnate of thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tormentor furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Come, O faithful, rejoicing let us honor the crown-bearing martyrs and God-wise hierarchs,[^304] magnifying Christ Who gave them strength not to fear the torments of the impious, as they sang: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
With the anointing of the priesthood and with the blood of martyrdom, the quartet of noble victors was adorned, and shines with wonders variously brighter than light, crying aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having conquered the demonic hosts, O glorious ones, and not fearing the threats of the torturers, ye received crowns from Christ, O all-praised ones, and as victorious sufferers ye sing: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Let Menander rejoice, let Acacius be glad, and radiant Patrick with Polyaenus, having formed a choir, let them unceasingly hymn the Father's Word incarnate from virginal wedlock-free wombs, having been shown forth as priests, supremely exalting Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
The tormentors marveled beholding the heads of the sufferers being cut off, and they patiently surrendering their souls with love. And Christ received them, giving them rest with Himself, through whom He also pours forth healings as from a vessel unto those who unceasingly run unto them.

*Troparion:*
Where ye beheld the Fountain of desires, O hierarchs and martyrs in the heavens, having left every earthly possession, ye were taken up unto the high majesty of the divine kingdom, accounted worthy of delight and blessedness beyond understanding.

*Troparion:*
Ye passed through the water of temptations and passed beyond the fire of tortures; and now, O sufferers, ye have clearly received everlasting rest in the heavenly tabernacles, wherein, dancing unceasingly as crown-bearers, deliver us from misfortunes!

*Theotokion:*
Like the sun, the memory of the sufferers illumines the faithful with wonders; for Christ God, born of thee, O pure Virgin, Whom the holy ones piously proclaimed, preeminently illumined them radiantly, as He promised to glorify those who with faith honor their course.

## May 20. Holy Martyr Thalelaeus

*Stichera of the Martyr, Tone 4, Podoben: "As Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron:*
When such great tempest held sway under heaven and mistreated the soul, O all-glorious one, guided by the helmsmanship of the high calling, unharmed thou didst pass through the much-afflicted deep of tortures; and having reached the high havens, thou wast filled with eternal calm, O martyr Thalelaeus!

*Sticheron:*
Thou didst depict the suffering of the Master of creation, when lifted up upon the wood, O glorious one, and torn in the ribs through the cruelty of the torturers, exposing their torn understanding, reproaching the deception of idols, and gloriously strengthening piety, O great martyr Thalelaeus!

*Sticheron:*
At the command of the tormentor, thou wast surrendered unto the deep of the sea, yet by divine grace thou didst remain undrowned, O all-wise one; and by the streams of thy blood thou didst drown the spiritual Pharaoh and his host, and didst deliver them unto utter destruction, O God-wise one, being mightily strengthened by Christ.

*Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone 4, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Sticheron:*
Deliver my humble soul from condemnation and grievous transgressions, O most holy Bride of God, and change death by thy prayers, and grant me to obtain justification in the day of trial, which the assemblies of the saints obtained; show me forth cleansed before the end by repentance and outpourings of tears!

*Sticheron:*
Dew my mind with the rays of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, who didst bear the Drop, Christ, Who ineffably washed away the immeasurable iniquities of men with compassions; dry up the fountain of my passions, and account me worthy of the river of the food of life ever, by thy prayers!

*Sticheron:*
Having given birth unto God my Savior, do thou grant me through thy prayers, O Virgin, the choice and salvific medicine of repentance, and streams of tears, and the remembrance of that dread and fearful hour, and of the inexorable judgment, that I may escape fearful torment and obtain divine grace!

*Glory..., Now and ever..., Theotokion:*
Ten thousand times have I promised, O all-pure one, to do penance for my transgressions; but the beloved habit of my wicked ways does not leave me. Therefore I cry unto thee, and falling down I pray: do thou, O Lady, deliver me from such tyranny, guiding me unto better things and those near unto salvation!

*Staurotheotokion:*
When the Ewe that bare Thee beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the tree, she wept and maternally said unto Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the wood of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured out Thy blood, O Master?

*Canon, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having crossed the water as though it were dry land, and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Clothed with a purple robe dipped in the blood of suffering, gracefully adorned, the Church of Christ boasts in the passion-bearers, and is made beautiful.

*Troparion:*
Having received invincible power from God, O passion-bearer, thou didst wage war alone against myriads of enemies in the struggles of martyrdom, O Thalelaeus.

*Troparion:*
Having cast away the matter of the flesh, and lived on earth the life of the immaterial angels, O Thalelaeus, thou wast numbered among the angelical choirs.

*Theotokion:*
We earthborn, through thy childbearing from the earth, have truly been lifted up unto that heavenly height,[^305] O pure Theotokos, and have been numbered among the angelical choirs.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparion:*
Christ's ointment anointed thy head, O glorious one; therefore thou didst not offer ointment unto deception; thy blood poured forth healing unto all men, and washed away the blood of impiety from the earth, O Thalelaeus.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst subdue the flesh unto Him Who accepted the Cross for thy sake, O martyr of Christ, and wast accounted worthy of incorruptible life, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
The tormentor fell under thy feet, O glorious one, cast down by thy patience and by resistance to adversaries, O Thalelaeus!

*Theotokion:*
Thy childbearing loosed the debts of our sins, and bound all the faithful unto righteousness.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having shown thyself radiant, O passion-bearer, thou didst cast away the gloom of godlessness, and didst hasten[^306] unto tortures and death. Therefore, having finished thy great struggles, thou didst receive a great inheritance and glory that never passes away, O noble sufferer, glorious Thalelaeus!

*Glory..., Now and ever..., Theotokion:*
Next to God, I the humble one flee unto thy divine protection, O Theotokos; falling down I pray: Have mercy, O all-pure one, for my sins have risen above my head, and I fear the torment, O Lady, and tremble; make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son, to deliver me from these!

*Staurotheotokion:*
She who in the latter times bare Thee in the flesh Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O my Son? I hymn Thee, O longsuffering One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Christ received Thee, O glorious one, illumined with the radiance of virtues, upon the judgment seat of suffering as in a bridal chamber, and He hath glorified Thee.

*Troparion:*
The Master gave thee as a healing unto all the faithful, O Thalelaeus, and by thy divine prayers thou healest the passions of our souls.

*Troparion:*
The streams of thy blood pour forth the myrrh of healing unto all, and drive away the stench of diseases and sorrows, O Thalelaeus.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as an inexhaustible treasure of gifts, O Lady, we all receive ungrudging gifts of wonders from thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Illumine us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
The cloud of pains and torments covered not the brightness of thy patience; for thou didst shine forth brighter than the sun, O passion-bearer.

*Troparion:*
Growing spiritually in the Lebanon of faith, thou didst breathe forth every sweet fragrance of martyrdom, O most glorious Thalelaeus.

*Troparion:*
Having clothed thyself with the weapon of suffering, O wise one, by an airy path thou didst ascend unto divine height of grace, O Thalelaeus.[^307]

*Theotokion:*
From the defilement of passionate thoughts cleanse my soul, O Bride of God, and by thy prayers present me pure unto God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Serving the living God and Creator of all creation, thou didst cast away the services of the worldly, O Thalelaeus; therefore the Establisher of the contest glorified thee in sufferings and wonders.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stretch forth thy shoots like a fruitful olive tree, O wise one of endurance, perceived in the height; whence thou hast poured forth the oil of wonders for the faithful, O Martyr Thalelaeus.

*Troparion:*
Having wisely adorned the lamp of thy soul with the oil of virtues, O glorious Martyr Thalelaeus, thou didst kindle it with the fire of suffering; therefore thou hast attained unto the divine bridal chamber.

*Theotokion:*
We the dead have become immortal by the divine grace of thy childbearing; and we have been delivered from the first corruption, O most pure Birthgiver of God, and have been adorned with the garment of incorruption.

*Kontakion, Tone 3. Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Thou hast appeared as a fellow-sufferer and armed companion of the martyrs, thou wast an excellent warrior of the King of glory; by temptations and torment thou didst trample down the pride of the idol-worshipers. For this cause we sing of thy honored memory, O wise Thalelaeus.

*Ikos:*
Let us all now sing in hymns and praises, and piously laud the valiant servant of Christ's truth, as a steadfast sufferer and preacher of the faith, who cast down the enemy's snare and exposed all the soul-destroying fury of the idols; for as a lamb standing in the midst of lawless wolves, he proclaims piously the law of the Lord; doing which with one mind, we sing of the honored memory of the all-wise Thalelaeus.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youth who came from Judea in Babylon once by the faith of the Trinity trampled down the furnace flame, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having ascended to the height of Christ's piety, O Martyr Thalelaeus, thou didst abhor the downward-dragging deceit of the idols, and unceasingly dost cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having planted thyself firmly upon the rock of the Creator's love, O Martyr Thalelaeus, by the thrice-painful throes of torments thou wast in no wise shaken from this divine faith, and thou dost gratefully sing of Him as Benefactor.

*Troparion:*
Having drawn from the ungrudging waters of spiritual grace of wonders, thou dost impart these ungrudgingly unto all that ask, O Thalelaeus, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
O Thou Who art marvelous in all Thy saints, O only Lover of mankind, show forth Thy rich mercies, O Bountiful One, through the prayers of Thalelaeus, unto them that sing of Thy dispensation, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
That He might draw all unto heavenly glory, the Creator of all things was well pleased to be born of thee, O Birthgiver of God, and as Lover of mankind and Benefactor God, to be joined in the flesh with us.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Overcoming the inconstant wisdom of the Greeks boasting in word-weavings, O glorious Martyr: by an unfailing mind and an unshaken word thou didst draw all and God unto thyself, crying unto Him unceasingly: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Being lifted up from earthly things in spirit, O Thalelaeus, beholding the Creator and Deliverer in purity of mind, thou didst endure afflictions, suffering with a valiant soul, singing and calling aloud: Ye priests, bless the Master; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having dyed thy garment with martyr's blood, O Thalelaeus, thou didst spiritually clothe thyself therewith as an adornment of the Church, and with all the sufferers and martyrs thou dost dance in the heavenly tabernacles, crying unceasingly: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
He Who by a word composed all things out of nothing took human flesh from thee, the only Immortal One, through tender compassion, O pure one, that as God the Benefactor He might renew all that sing: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every hearing was affrighted at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High of His own will came down even unto the flesh, from a virginal womb,[^308] becoming man; therefore the faithful magnify the most pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
With the blossoms of virtues, O passion-bearer, thou didst adorn the spiritual woven crown of suffering as with stones, and didst beautify it with comely wounds, O Thalelaeus, arrayed with which thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal chamber.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast wounded by the spiritual arrow of endurance, and the evil-originating enemy who wounded thy body grew faint and fell to the earth; but thou, strengthened in soul, didst ascend to the heavenly height, O Thalelaeus, singing of the Lord Who strengthened thee.

*Troparion:*
The drops of thy blood, O Thalelaeus, were revealed as healing unto the faithful, washing away infirmities and pouring forth the cures of wonders, extinguishing fierce fiery burning by the grace of Christ the Crowner, and dewing them that praise thee in song.

*Troparion:*
Thy rewards for suffering have been made known unto all earthborn, O Thalelaeus; exceedingly abundant beyond hope, they pour forth gifts, wherewith visit also us that piously and faithfully honor thy struggles, as we ask from the heart.

*Theotokion:*
Elevate, O Good and Pure One, the downward-dragging wisdom of my soul unto the all-radiant and divine desire of Thy Son and God, that being ever inflamed by His love and captivated toward Him, I may draw near through virtue.

## May 21. Holy Great Sovereigns and Equal-to-the-Apostles Constantine and Helena

*In the same month, on the 21st day: The Holy Great Sovereigns and Equal-to-the-Apostles, Constantine and Helena.*[^309]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 6, and of the Saints on 5,[^310] Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Thou hast given a most mighty weapon unto our sovereign: Thy precious Cross, wherewith he reigned righteously upon earth, having shone forth in piety, and was accounted worthy of the heavenly kingdom by Thy tender compassion; with whom we glorify Thy dispensation of love for mankind, O Jesus All-Powerful, Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*
Thou didst give, O Lover of mankind, unto Thy pious servant the wisdom of Solomon, the meekness of David, and apostolic Orthodoxy, as King of kings and Lord of all that rule. Therefore we glorify Thy dispensation of love for mankind, O Jesus All-Powerful, Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron 3:*
Thou wast the first, O ever-memorable sovereign, willingly to subject the purple unto Christ, having recognized Him as God and as the Benefactor of all, reigning over all, victorious over every principality and higher than every power. Whence Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls, established the kingdom for thee, O lover of Christ.

*Glory... Tone 3. By Byzantios:*
Having received the best of rich gifts from God, O most sovereign king, Constantine the exceedingly great, thou didst excellently prosper in them; for illumined by the rays of the All-Holy Spirit through baptism by Sylvester the hierarch, thou wast revealed as invincible among kings, and thou didst give the inhabited earth and the pious ruling city as a gift unto thy Creator. Therefore, as one who hast boldness, cease not to pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins and great mercy unto all that celebrate thy memory.

*Now and ever... of the Feast.*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*

*And the Readings:*

*The Reading from the Third Book of Kings [1 Kings 8:22–23, 27–30]:*[^311]
Solomon stood before the face of the altar of the Lord, before all the assembly of Israel; and having spread forth his hands toward heaven, he said: O Lord God of Israel, there is no God like Thee in heaven above, or on earth beneath. For if heaven and the heaven of heavens suffice Thee not, how much less this house which I have built unto Thy name? Yet look Thou upon my prayer, O Lord God of Israel, to hear the supplications and the prayer which Thy servant prays before Thee toward Thee this day: that Thine eyes may be open toward this house day and night, toward this place whereof Thou hast said: My name shall be there, to hear the prayer which Thy servant prays in this place day and night, and to give ear unto the supplication of Thy servant and of Thy people Israel. And whatsoever they shall pray toward Thee in this place, do Thou also hear in the place of Thy dwelling in heaven, and do Thou act and be merciful unto them.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Isaiah 61:10–62:5]:*[^312]
Let my soul rejoice in the Lord, for He hath clothed me with the garment of salvation, and with the robe of gladness hath He covered me; as upon a bridegroom He hath placed a crown upon me, and as a bride hath He adorned me with beauty. And as the earth causes her flower to grow, and as a garden causes its seeds to shoot forth, so shall the Lord cause righteousness to shine forth, and joy before all the nations. For Zion's sake I will not keep silence, and for Jerusalem's sake I will not hold My peace, until My righteousness go forth as light, and My salvation shall burn as a torch. And the nations shall see thy righteousness, and all the earthly kings thy glory; and thou shalt be called by thy new name, which the Lord shall name. And thou shalt be a crown of comeliness in the hand of the Lord, and a royal diadem in the hand of thy God. And thou shalt no more be called Forsaken, neither shall thy land any more be called Desolate; for thy name shall be called My Pleasure, and thy land the Inhabited Earth; for the Lord is well pleased in thee, and thy land shall be inhabited. And as a young man dwells with a virgin, so shall thy Creator dwell with thee; and thy sons shall be with thee; and it shall come to pass, that as a bridegroom rejoices over a bride, so shall the Lord rejoice over thee.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Isaiah 60:1–16]:*[^313]
Shine, shine, O Jerusalem, for thy light is come, and the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee! For behold, darkness shall cover the earth, and gross darkness the nations; but upon thee the Lord shall appear, and His glory shall be seen upon thee. And kings shall walk by thy light, and nations by the brightness of thy rising. Lift up thine eyes round about and see thy children gathered together; all thy sons shall come from afar, and thy daughters shall be borne upon the shoulders. Then shalt thou see, and rejoice, and fear, and be astonished in heart; when the wealth of the sea and of the nations and peoples shall turn unto thee. And herds of camels shall come unto thee, and the camels of Midian and Ephah shall cover thee; all they from Sheba shall come, bearing gold and frankincense unto thee, and they shall bring precious stones, and shall proclaim the good tidings of the salvation of the Lord.
