And all the flocks of Kedar shall be gathered together unto thee, and the rams of Nebaioth shall come unto thee, and acceptable offerings shall be offered up upon Mine altar, and the house of My prayer shall be glorified. Who are these that fly as clouds, and as doves with their young? Zion is with Me; the islands have waited for Me, and the ships of Tarshish among the first, to bring thy children from afar, and their silver and their gold with them, for the sake of the holy name of the Lord, and because the Holy One of Israel is glorified. And the sons of strangers shall build up thy walls, and their kings shall minister unto thee; for in My wrath I smote thee, and in My mercy have I loved thee. And thy gates shall be open continually, O Jerusalem, day and night; they shall not be shut, that men may bring unto thee the power of the Gentiles, and their kings led captive. For the nations and the kings that will not serve thee shall perish, and the nations shall be utterly laid waste. And the glory of Lebanon shall come unto thee, with the cypress, the pine, and the cedar together, to glorify My holy place, and I will glorify the place of My feet. And the sons of them that afflicted thee and provoked thee shall come trembling unto thee, and all they that provoked thee shall worship at the footsteps of thy feet, and thou shalt be called the City of the Lord, the Zion of the Holy One of Israel. Whereas thou hast been forsaken and hated, and there was none that helped thee; I will even make thee an eternal joy, a gladness from generation to generation. And thou shalt suck the milk of the Gentiles, and shalt eat the wealth of kings; and thou shalt know that I the Lord am Thy Savior, and Thy Deliverer is the God of Israel.

*At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple:*[^314]

*And of the Saints, Tone 2:*

*Sticheron 1:*
In due manner we celebrate thy memory, O Constantine equal-to-the-apostles, foundation and boast of all kings; for having been enlightened by the rays of the Spirit, thou didst elucidate the whole Church of Christ, having gathered councils of the faithful from all sides in the radiant city of Nicaea, where the insolent raging of the impious was extinguished, and the tongues of the heretics grew faint and foolish, while the crown of the Orthodox was exalted when the faith was revealed. Whence thou wast glorified among them as the most Orthodox, and wast proclaimed unto all kings, as thou art the first father, having received the purple from God. Unto them that with radiant titles celebrate thy memory in faith, [grant] cleansing of transgressions unto our souls!

*Tone 2. By Byzantios:*
Not having received the calling from men, but even as divine Paul, thou hadst this rather from on high, O most glorious Constantine, from Christ God. For having seen the sign of the Cross in the heavens, by this thou wast caught as a most excellent capture; by this also thou wast revealed as an invincible victor against visible and invisible enemies. Therefore we pray thee as a fervent intercessor, as we earthborn worthily honor thy memory, to ask with boldness for enlightenment, cleansing, and great mercy for us.

*Sticheron 3:*
The memory of pious Constantine hath shone forth today like poured-out myrrh; for having desired Christ, he disregarded the idols, and erected a temple on earth unto Him Who was crucified for our sake, and in the heavens he received the crown of hope.

*Tone 3:*
Passing through the age of youth, even as divine Paul, thou didst receive divine gifting from on high, and by the whole armor of the Cross thou didst overthrow the warfare of the fierce adversary; O boast of kings, apostle Constantine, pray unto the Lord for us, that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 4:*
Praise unto God is sung from earthen lips at thy divine memory, O all-praised Constantine; for thou wast revealed as an excellent warrior of the word of faith, having put to scorn the carved forms of idols; and now thou art numbered with the light of the Trinity, enlightening our thoughts by thy supplications.

*Glory... Tone 5:*
The Church today having adorned herself with thy power, O sovereign, mystically rejoices, and worthily and with highest praise honors thy most venerable memory, crying aloud: Rejoice, thou that didst emulate Paul, and didst take up the Cross of Christ, and didst break the snares of the adversary! Rejoice, splendor of kings, and equal in honor to the apostles! Rejoice, confirmation of the faithful, and mighty rampart of sovereigns, O blessed Constantine: cease not to pray unto the Lord for us, as one having boldness, O adornment of kings!

*Now and ever... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^315]
As the first king among Christians, O Constantine, thou didst receive the scepter from God; for unto thee was revealed the saving sign hidden in the earth, wherewith thou didst also subdue all nations under the feet of the Romans, having the life-creating Cross as an unconquerable weapon, O blessed one, whereby also thou wast brought unto our God.

*Verse:* I have exalted one chosen out of My people; I have found David My servant.[^316]

Truly blessed was the belly and sanctified was the womb that bare thee, O king longed for by the world, joy of Christians, God-crowned Constantine: boast of the Romans, wealth and champion of orphans and widows, shelter of the humble, and restoration of them that are perplexed in afflictions, truly also the deliverance of captives!

*Verse:* For this cause God, Thy God, hath anointed Thee with the oil of gladness...[^317]

Wounded by desire and love for Christ, the mother of the all-sweet offshoot with dili- gence came unto holy Zion, unto the holy place whereon our Savior willed to be crucified in order to save us; where, having taken up the Cross, rejoicing she cried aloud: Glory to Him that hath granted me that which I hoped for!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
An all-radiant light, an unwaning star unto the churches, having passed from unbelief to the faith of the Godhead, he was brought to sanctify the people and the city; and having seen the pattern of the Cross in the heavens, he heard from thence: By this conquer thine enemies! Whence having received the understanding of the Spirit, an ordained priest and king, thou didst confirm the Church of God with oil, O father of Orthodox kings! Whose shrine also pours forth healing, O Constantine equal-to-the-apostles, pray for our souls!

*Now and ever... of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*[^318]
Having seen the pattern of Thy Cross in the heavens, and like Paul having received the calling not from men, Thy sovereign apostle among kings, O Lord, placed the ruling city in Thy hand; which do Thou ever preserve in peace, through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, O only Lover of mankind.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice.*
*Glory... of the Saints; Now and ever... of the Feast.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
A new David wast thou in types, receiving the horn from on high upon the summit of thy kingdom; for the all-radiant Word and Lord anointed thee with the oil of the Spirit, O glorious one; whence thou didst also receive the royal scepter, O all-wise one: ask great mercy for us! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only most pure Virgin Mother, who by purity didst surpass the angels: cleanse me who beyond all am become dust, defiled by carnal transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6. Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃льскїѧ пред̾иди́те си́лы):*
Fastened upon the Cross, the Creator of the sun and creation drew thee like a radiant star from heaven with the stars, unto Whom as first sovereign He laid upon thee the rule through the Church; therefore we praise thee, O most pious King Constantine, with Helena thy God-wise mother. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
Gideon prefigures the conception, and David declares thy childbearing, O Birthgiver of God; for like rain upon the fleece, the Word descended into thy womb, and without seed thou didst bud forth salvation unto the world, Christ our God, O full of grace!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^319]
Thy feastful memory having appeared unto us, illumines the ends of the world with the light of the knowledge of God, O Constantine inspired of God; for thou didst show thyself pious among kings, having kept the laws of the heavenly King. Therefore by thy prayers deliver us from temptations! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou hast renewed the mortal nature of the earthborn which had decayed in passions, and thou hast raised all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore as is due we all call thee blessed, O all-glorious Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.

*Gradual Hymns: The 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
I have exalted one chosen out of My people.
*Verse:* For this cause God, Thy God, hath anointed Thee...

*Let every breath praise the Lord...*

*Gospel according to John, Pericope 36 [John 10:9–16].*[^320]

*After Psalm 50:*

*Glory:*
Through the prayers of the God-crowned sovereigns, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions!

*Now and ever:*
Through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions!

*Then:*
Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy...

*After the Verse:*
*The idiomelon sticheron: "The memory of pious Constantine..." (see fol. 83v).[^321]*

*Canon of the Feast on 6, and of the Saints on 8:*

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!

*Troparion:*
O only Heavenly King, from the sin that reigns now in me, by the supplications of Thy favorites, set my humble soul free!

*Troparion:*
Having become a lover of the heavenly kingdom, O blessed Constantine, with a pure mind having believed in the King and Master of all, thou didst serve Him.

*Troparion:*
Having been illumined with the light originating from God, thou didst truly leave behind the darkness of ignorance, O God-wise Helena, having sincerely served the King of the ages.

*Theotokion:*
O Gate of the Divine Orient, open unto me the gates of repentance, and from the gates of death-bearing sin deliver me by thine intercession, O Lady!

*Katavasia of the Feast.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Fabricator of the vault of heaven and Builder of the Church, do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst hasten to obtain heavenly rewards; therefore, O God-wise one, thou didst follow Him that called thee, and thou didst leave behind the darkness of deceit handed down to thee, O father, and wast a beacon by the Divine Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Having cleaved unto Christ, O all-venerable one, and having placed all thy hope upon Him, thou didst reach His sacred places, in which the All-Good One, having become incarnate, endured the most pure passions.

*Troparion:*
A saving weapon, an indestructible victory, the Christian hope, the precious Cross hidden by envy, thou didst reveal, O God-blessed one, inflamed with divine desire.

*Theotokion:*
Having fallen away from the sacred commonwealth, O most pure one, I was joined unto beasts, and was wholly condemned; do thou who didst bear the Judge deliver and save me from all condemnation!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*

*Then, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^322]
Having stretched thy senses toward heaven, and having learned the beauty of the stars, from them thou wast mystically taught of the Lord; and the sign of the Cross shone brightly in their midst, with the inscription thereon to conquer and be sovereign. Therefore having opened the eyes of thy soul, thou didst read the writing and wast taught the pattern, O all-honored Constantine: pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto them that celebrate thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
Having conceived Wisdom and the Word in thy womb without being consumed, O Mother of God, thou didst bear into the world Him that upholds the world, and in thine arms didst carry Him that carries all things, the Nourisher of all and Creator of creation. Therefore I pray thee, O all-holy Virgin, and in faith I glorify thee: that I may be delivered from transgressions, and on the Day of Judgment when I shall stand before the face of my Creator, O Lady, pure Virgin, grant me then thy help; for thou canst do all whatsoever thou desirest, O all-immaculate one!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
From heaven Christ the Lord of old caught thee, even as Paul, O Constantine, directing thee to honor this One King as Principle.

*Troparion:*
With an all-radiant sign formed of stars, O blessed one, Christ the Sun illumined thee, and showed thee forth as a beacon unto those in darkness.

*Troparion:*
God-loving in thy manner of life and marvelously worthy of honor by thy divine deeds wast thou, O blessed one; therefore in faith we glorify thee.

*Troparion:*
Having now revealed the divine trophy of the Cross that had been hidden for many years, by which we have been saved and delivered from the deceit of the demons.

*Theotokion:*
Illumine my soul, darkened by transgressions, O thou who didst bear the Sun of Righteousness, O Ever-Virgin!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early, we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us! For Thou art our God, and beside Thee we know no other.

*Troparion:*
Having risen early unto the unwaning Sun and Master, O God-wise emperor, thou wast filled with light.

*Troparion:*
Wearing love and perfect mercy as a purple robe, thou hast now settled in the heavenly kingdoms.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast joined the single chorus of the bodiless hosts, having well-pleased God by Thy virtuous deeds.

*Theotokion:*
Cleanse my soul, defiled by bodily pleasures through the serpent's malice!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and to Him will I proclaim my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and I pray like Jonah: From corruption, O God, raise me up!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst assemble the blessed choir of God-bearing Fathers most gloriously; and through them, O Constantine, thou didst confirm the wavering hearts of all, to doxologize the Word, of equal honor and co-enthroned with Him that begat Him.

*Troparion:*
Having believed in the living Lord Who grants being unto all, thou didst cast away the deadly services of abominable and defiled idols, and joyfully didst receive the Heavenly Kingdom, O Helena.

*Troparion:*
Guided by Thy hand, O Word, they that reigned through Thee cast away the deepest darkness of ignorance and the storm of cruel godlessness, and passed over with rejoicing unto the calm harbors of piety.

*Theotokion:*
Heal my heart, which hath been incurably diseased and sorely wounded by the wounding of the evil one, O Maiden, and vouchsafe me thy healing, and save me who place my hope in thee, through thine intercessions, O all-immaculate one!

*Kontakion, Tone 3. Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Today Constantine with his mother Helena reveals the Cross, the all-venerable Tree, which is the shame of all the Jews, and a weapon against adversaries for faithful sovereigns; for for our sake there hath appeared a great sign, terrible in battle.

*Ikos:*
Let us the faithful honor Constantine with his mother; for having also heard the words of David, they recognized the three-composite Cross upon cedar, pine, and cypress, upon which followed the saving Passion; and having summoned all the Jews, that they might prepare to show forth unto the people the great justification hidden because of their envy and hatred, they found and showed it forth. Therefore they appeared victorious unto all, bearing the invincible weapon, a great sign and terrible in battle.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Observing Thy commandments, Constantine submitted to Thy law; therefore he cast down the regiments of the lawless, crying aloud unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God!

*Troparion:*
The Tree that delivered all, O marvelously honorable one, which was buried through envy, thou dost uncover for us from the pit of destruction,[^323] burying the all-destructive demons unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
By divine deeds thou didst build thy heart as a temple unto God, O Helena, and didst erect sacred temples unto Him where in the flesh He submitted to most pure passions for our sake.

*Theotokion:*
Sinning of my own will and enslaved to improper habits, I now flee unto thy customary compassion; save me in my despair, O Most Holy Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tormentor furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Clothed in mercy as in purple, O glorious one, and in meekness as a chlamys, with a crown wast thou adorned through the perfect mind of the virtues, and thou didst pass from earth unto the heavenly kingdom, crying: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee rejoicing with thy God-wise son, O glorious Helena, in the Kingdom of God, we magnify Christ Who hath shown forth your precious feast unto us, which shines more brightly than the rays of the sun, illuminating us who faithfully sing: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
How marvelous was thy desire and divine thy character, O glorious Helena, praise of women! For having reached the places that received the honorable passions of the Master of all, thou didst adorn them with most beautiful temples, crying aloud: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Illumine the eyes of my soul, blinded by many transgressions, O Birthgiver of God; pacify my mind and my heart disturbed by manifold pleasures, through thy prayers, and save me as I cry aloud: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt the Pure One unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
At this heaven was filled with awe, and the ends of the earth were struck with wonder, that God appeared in the flesh unto mankind, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
The tomb wherein lies thy sacred and precious body, O Constantine, ever gushes forth the radiance of divine healings unto them that approach in purity, driving away the darkness of various passions, and illuminating with never-waning light them that praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Having holily ended thy life, thou hast now settled with the saints, having been filled with sanctification and illumination; therefore thou dost also ever pour forth streams of healings, and burnest away passions, O blessed Helena, and waterest our souls.

*Troparion:*
O pre-eternal, immortal King, Thou hast vouchsafed the heavenly kingdom unto them whom of old Thou didst piously justify to reign upon earth, O Lord, who purely loved Thee: Saint Helena and great Constantine; by whose prayers have compassion upon all!

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived the King and Creator of all, O Virgin, thou didst give birth unto Him, and lo, now as a queen thou standest at His right hand, O Pure One; therefore I pray thee: Deliver me from the portion on the left in the hour of judgment, and number me with the sheep on the right!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Жє́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Beacons that have illumined the whole universe with the faith of piety have ye truly appeared, O God-crowned Constantine and glorious Helena; we doxologize in hymns Christ Who hath magnified you, Who is marvelous in the saints.

*Glory... Another, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*

*Podoben: "With the Disciples" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ зрѧ́щымъ):*
Not from men did great Constantine with his mother receive imperial dominion, but from divine grace; illumined from heaven, he beheld the divine trophy of the Cross, and having thereby destroyed his enemies, he overthrew the idolatrous deceit, and confirmed the Orthodox Faith in the world.

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast on 4, and of the Saints on 3, Tone 8. Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Rejoice, O all-wise Constantine, fountain of Orthodoxy, ever watering the whole world under the sun with sweet streams! Rejoice, O root from which sprang forth the fruit that nourishes the Church of Christ! Rejoice, O glorious boast of the ends of the earth, first of Christian emperors! Rejoice, O joy of the faithful!

He that reigns over creation, foreseeing the well-disposed submission of thy heart, O all-wise one, through the Word caught thee who wast held by unreason; and having illumined thy understanding with the knowledge of piety, He showed thee forth unto the world radiant as the sun, emitting the rays of divine deeds, O most glorious one!

Having received the teachings of the Lord like choice ground, O all-praised one, thou didst sprout forth the good fruit of virtuous deeds, which nourishes our thoughts by the imitation of thy citizenship, O all-wise Helena; therefore we celebrate joyfully, triumphantly keeping thy memory today.

With the oil of gladness, O Christ, Thou didst most gloriously anoint Thy partakers, Constantine and Helena, who hated deceit and longed for Thy beauty; and of Thy heavenly kingdom Thou hast vouchsafed them, who first piously reigned upon earth by Thy nod, O Word.

*Glory... Tone 8. By Patriarch Methodius:*[^324]
The King of kings and God, Who adorns the worthy with rich gifts, Himself from heaven, as He did the glorious Paul, caught thee, O Constantine, by the sign of the Cross, saying: "By this conquer thine enemies!" Having sought Him with thy God-wise mother and found Him even as thou didst desire, thou didst mightily put them to flight. With her, then, pray unto the only Lover of mankind for Orthodox sovereigns, for the Christ-loving armed forces, and for all who faithfully celebrate thy memory, that we may be delivered from all wrath!

*Both now... of the Feast.*

*Great Doxology. Litanies and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode the appointed Ode, and of the Saints, Ode 6, on 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*
Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world [LXX Ps. 18:4].
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God [LXX Ps. 18:1].*

*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 200 [Gal. 1:11–19].*[^325]

*If it should fall within Pentecost, the Epistle in the Acts is read, Pericope 44 [Acts 18:22–28].*[^326]

*Alleluia, Tone 1:*
I have exalted one chosen out of My people [LXX Ps. 88:19b].
*Verse: O Lord, in Thy strength the king shall be glad [LXX Ps. 20:1].*

*Gospel according to John, Pericope 35 from the half [John 10:1–9].*[^327]

*Communion Hymn:*
Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth...

*Be it known: If the memory of the holy Emperor Constantine should happen to fall on Thursday of Ascension, or on the Seventh Sunday of the Holy Fathers, sing his service even as to John the Theologian, according to the same chapters.*[^328]

## May 22. Holy Martyr Basiliscus

*Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As One Valiant Among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
An unshakable kingdom didst thou receive, having reigned, O glorious Basiliscus, and thou standest before the King of hosts, rejoicing with all the angelic armies, with whom thou, O blessed one, unceasingly singest the divine hymn, brightly illuminated by divine radiances and communions.

Having bound thy feet, they pierced them with nails when thou didst walk with rejoicing upon the path of martyrdom, O most praised one! With them thou didst trample the head of the enemy and didst crush it utterly; and having swiftly traversed the heavenly ways,[^329] thou didst appear victorious before the Master, O glorious one!

Thy prayer caused a tree to grow that had not previously sprouted, and made a fountain of living water gush forth; and by the shedding of thy blood the earth was sanctified, and the air by the ascent of thy soul, O exceedingly rich one! Therefore in faith we celebrate thy holy and all-festal memory, O Basiliscus, in which thou didst lawfully contend.

*If it should fall during the Fast, seek the Stichera of the Birthgiver of God on the 20th day of this month (see fol. 80).[^330]*

*At Matins, the Canon, the acrostic whereof is: "The most great" The acrostic: "The most great Basiliscus I praise. Joseph." Tone 8.*[^331]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us send up a hymn, ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparion:*
For them that piously praise this bright festival of thine, O much-suffering martyr, pray unto Christ the King, to save us, and to make us partakers of the Heavenly Kingdom.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened wholly by the love of Christ, O exceedingly rich one, Thou didst disdain the flesh as subject to corruption, and didst endure torments with a courageous mind, exposing the idolatrous deceit.

*Troparion:*
Illumined by the enlightenment of the Most Holy Spirit, Thou didst pass through the night of ignorance, O Basiliscus; and by the brightness of thy sufferings Thou didst appear like the sun, brightly illuminating all creation.

*Theotokion:*
Gideon plainly foresaw thy most pure womb, O Virgin, which received the heavenly dew, drying up the sea of godlessness, O Maiden, and watering the minds that were parched.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit praises Thee!

*Troparion:*
By the blood of thy suffering thou didst extinguish the raging flame of idolatry, and rejoicing thou dost delight in the torrent of delight, O martyr.

*Troparion:*
Having left thee behind, O glorious one, the chosen pair of martyrs departed unto Christ to form a trinity of sufferers.

*Troparion:*
Having been loosed from the body, thou awaitest those who suffer with thee, confessing Christ the Lord, the King of all, O much-suffering Basiliscus.

*Theotokion:*
May I find thee, O all-immaculate one, ever guiding me unto virtues, and leading me into the way of repentance!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Thou standest now rejoicing before the only King, sacredly invested with a bright diadem and a garment dyed purple from thy blood, and deified by pure participation; therefore celebrating thy most holy memory today, we sing thy praise.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
O Hope of Christians, Most Holy Virgin, unceasingly pray with the heavenly hosts unto the God Whom thou didst bear beyond mind and word, to grant the remission of all our sins and amendment of life, unto them that with faith and love ever glorify thee with love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hanged dead upon the Tree, the immaculate Ewe-lamb weeping said, crying aloud maternally: How shall I endure Thy descent beyond word, O my Son, and Thy voluntary passions, O most good God?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Having tilled the earth of thy soul with the plow, O martyr, thou didst bring forth a hundredfold ear of corn of suffering, preserved in the divine granaries.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst hear a holy voice from above, O all-blessed one, certifying unto thee the end of thy suffering, which joined thee unto the choirs of martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Steadfast resistance didst thou show, O glorious one; bound with chains, thou didst endure, loosing every bond of deceit.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst choke Satan by the divine sinews of thy sufferings, O passion-bearer; therefore with the crown of victory wast thou adorned, O martyr Basiliscus!

*Theotokion:*
He that is consubstantial with the Father appeared in all things like unto men, having become incarnate even as He willed from thy blood, O all-immaculate one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early, we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us! For Thou art our God, beside Thee we know no other.

*Troparion:*
Led forth to suffer lawfully, by the power of the Spirit thou didst put the lawless to shame, O God-wise martyr.

*Troparion:*
Walking boldly along the path of martyrdom, thou didst crush the stings of the adversary, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
The piercings of thy driven nails, O God-wise one, utterly crushed the head of the enemy and adversary.

*Theotokion:*
In the likeness of flesh thou didst give birth, O Birthgiver of God, unto the incomprehensible and unsearchable God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and to Him will I proclaim my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and I pray like Jonah: From corruption, O God, raise me up!

*Troparion:*
Preaching Him Who was lifted up upon the Tree, thou wast bound upon a dry tree; and by the showers of thy prayers thou didst show it forth moist and adorned with mature foliage, unto the confirmation of the pious, O Basiliscus, and unto the glory of thy sufferings.

*Troparion:*
The lawless unjustly bound thee who hadst loosed thyself from fleshly passions, and nailed thee with iron nails, and led thee along a long road; and by thy blood thou didst sanctify the whole earth, O much-suffering martyr!

*Troparion:*
Standing bound by the hands, thou didst fix the eye of thy heart upon God, and didst beseech Him that a fountain of living water might gush forth, in thy all-glorious memory and for the healing of souls, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the contrite wounds of my wretched soul and its grievous passions, O Virgin, for thou didst ineffably give birth unto the Physician of souls and bodies; and save me who have placed my hope in thee, O all-immaculate one!

*Kontakion, Tone 8. Podoben: "To Thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной воево́дѣ):*
Strong and courageous didst Thou appear in suffering, and most marvelous in miracles; manifestly carrying forward the name of Christ, Thou didst put the tormentor to shame. Therefore we honor thee, O Basiliscus, ever crying aloud in piety: Rejoice, O radiant beauty of martyrs!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast been shown forth as the adornment of martyrs, dwelling in most fair mansions, O all-blessed one; and from us who honor thee with love, drive away worldly storm by thy prayers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show thyself to be a house of the Trinity, casting down carved images and idolatrous temples, O wise one, and singing unto the Master: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
The Master grants honor unto thee who didst suffer valiantly, and cast down the wiles of the enemy, and sangest harmoniously: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
A second firmament hast thou appeared, having acquired sufferings like the sun, and the sacred brightness and fullness of thy miracles like stars, O Basiliscus, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Lo, the Virgin of whom great Isaiah spoke by the Divine Spirit conceived God in her womb and gave birth; unto Whom we sing: O Lord God, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Having become victors over the tormentor and the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the children cried aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
A victor over tormentors and spirits of wickedness, O glorious one, hast thou appeared, singing unto Him that strengthened thee unceasingly: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst become dead unto the world, O glorious martyr Basiliscus, unswervingly following the Master Who breaths life into the dead, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Enduring torments in body with steadfastness of soul, O God-wise one, thou dancest with the divine bodiless angels, offering praise unto God and crying aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been shown to be more spacious than the heavens, having contained God in thy womb, Who delivers from the distress of the evil one all that sing, O most holy Maiden: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every hearing was awestruck at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High voluntarily descended even unto flesh from the virginal womb, having become man; therefore the faithful magnify the all-immaculate Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*
By the sail of the Cross having traversed the storm of martyrdom, O blessed one, thou didst reach the calm harbor of the kingdom; and like a good merchant, thou didst preserve the merchandise of piety for Christ, the King and our God!

*Troparion:*
Even as of old Elijah the Tishbite brought down fire from heaven, so, O blessed one, by thy prayer thou didst bring down a flame whereby the idolatrous temple was burned down and the demonic shrines were consumed, and God the Creator is magnified!

*Troparion:*
When thy body was loosed by the beheading of the sword, O wise one, and the wise hosts received thy divine spirit, thou didst conquer by grace the cruel spirits of malice, O exceedingly rich one; and now thou dwellest on high, a crown-bearer of the kingdom, O Basiliscus!

*Troparion:*
The earth indeed was sanctified by thy burial, O martyr, and heaven by thy soul, O God-inspired one; for clothed in thy blood as in a bright purple robe, thou ever reignest together with Christ our God, remembering those who faithfully remember thee!

*Theotokion:*
Being indeed God by nature, yet Thou didst endure to be clothed in man, having voluntarily settled in the womb of the immaculate one, O Lover of mankind; therefore, knowing Thy two-fold natural wills, we magnify Thee, faithfully blessing Thy Mother!

## May 23. Our Venerable Father and Confessor Michael, Bishop of Synnada[^332]

*Stichera of the Venerable Michael, Tone 8. Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O God-inspired Father Michael, fountain of good things! Thou wast worthily accounted worthy to behold the ultimate desire, the fulfillment of desires, and true blessedness, which every nature truly desires to see, O blessed one! O what fair adornment is thine, O glorious one! By this thou now standest rejoicing before Christ as a radiant hierarch!

O Father Michael, receiver of God, clothed in the vesture of the priesthood, thou didst hasten unto the judgment seat of martyrdom, and wast brought unto Christ, adorned with double crowns, O blessed one, beholding the ranks of the hierarchs! O what ineffable gladness is thine, O all-wise one, whereof thou hast truly been vouchsafed to partake, O God-blessed one!

O Father Michael, God-wise one, bedewed from above, thou didst extinguish the fire of temptations in a manner becoming God, and didst pass through unblemished; and rejoicing, O blessed one, thou didst join the former hierarchs, O radiant hieromartyr... O what an ineffable and radiant dwelling is thine, into which Christ hath now received thee as an honorable hierarch!

*Stichera of the Birthgiver of God, Tone 8. Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Rejoice, O illumination of souls, forgiveness of sinners, amendment of the negligent! Rejoice, O comfort and joy, helper of the afflicted! Rejoice, O good one, healing of infirm souls! Rejoice, O reconciliation with God and mediatress, who art more sacred than all the seraphim, O Pure One!

Having received flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth as protectress unto mankind, O Pure One! Therefore deliver thy servants from every calamity and adversity, and from the snares of the deceitful enemy; and vouchsafe all that glorify and worship thee to partake of the radiance of the elect!

Save me, O most pure Birthgiver of God, who didst ineffably bear Christ the Savior, for thee alone have I acquired[^333] as protectress,[^334] and an invincible wall, and a protection and joy, and the divine consolation of my soul! Deliver me therefore from the sleepless worm and from the eternal fire, O Mother of Christ God!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
With voices I magnify thee,[^335] with joyful lips, O Pure One, and I glorify the abyss of thy love for mankind; save me from afflictions, and deliver me from the soul-destroying arrows of the invisible and all-wicked enemy! For thee I set forth against him, O Bride of God, as a firm and invincible weapon, O Mother of Christ God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting the passions, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou endure unjust wounds, O Physician Who hast healed human infirmity, and by Thy mercy hast delivered all from corruption?

*Canon, Tone 2:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^336]
The overwhelming power of old cast down Pharaoh's whole army into the deep; and the Word made flesh destroyed all-evil sin; pre-glorified is the Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Shining forth with ineffable enlightenment, O father of temperance, thou wast a hierarch of God, O marvelous Michael, bearing the royal priesthood, and pasturing the Church of Christ, O most blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Having purified thy mouth with bright words, thou didst proclaim unto all in piety the God-revealed teachings, O exceedingly rich Michael, exposing the blasphemies of heresies, and thou wast a champion of piety, O most blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Having become a vessel of divine gifts, O blessed one, thou didst manifestly impart them unto all the faithful, O all-praised Michael, having acquired a God-like life, and being clad with righteousness as with a vesture.

*Troparion:*
To honorably venerate the sacred icon of Christ and of the Mother of God thou didst abide, O all-sacred Michael, and thou didst cast down the blasphemous babbling of the heretics, conquering them with words and sufferings.

*Theotokion:*
From thy womb, O all-immaculate and pure one, the Sun dawned upon us, and with God-worked splendors He made clear, having driven away all demonic dark mist, the pre-glorified Lord; for gloriously hath He been glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit rejoices for my spirit rejoices when singing:[^337] There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
By fleeting things thou didst acquire the things that pass not away and endure unto the ages, O God-proclaimer, for in diverse manners didst thou live, crying aloud: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Divine grace was poured out upon thy lips, enlarging thy mouth, and thou didst receive the gifts of wisdom, crying aloud: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst subdue the passions by temperance, thou didst enlighten thy mind by contemplation, thou wast active in deeds, crying aloud: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Let us hymn Mary, the most pure Birthgiver of God, who appeared as the mediatress of salvation, crying aloud in God-wisdom: There is none undefiled as thou, O all-immaculate one, and none most pure besides thee, O Lady!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*
Having sanctified thyself by a perfect life, thou didst appear as a priest of the Most High God, strengthened by Whom thou didst close the mouths of heretics that opened against the icon of Christ; and persecuted, O father, thou didst finish thy course.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
O unwedded and pure Virgin Birthgiver of God, the only protectress and protection of the faithful, deliver all from calamities and sorrows and cruel distresses, who place their hope in thee, O Maiden, and save our souls by thy divine prayers!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, outstretched dead upon the Cross, the all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Merciful One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^338]
Thou didst come forth from the Virgin, not an intercessor, nor an angel, but Thou Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and Thou didst save the whole of me, man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Desiring the crown of glory, thou didst abandon the indulgence of the flesh, O venerable sacred initiate; and thy whole life thou didst offer unto God as a sweet-smelling sacrifice.

*Troparion:*
Having grown lame from the right path, they that have now appeared as alienated fell into the pit of heresy, whom thou didst expose, O most blessed one, honoring the icon of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Richly hast thou received the rewards of thy sufferings, O all-honorable Michael; now rejoice with the heavenly hosts, and dance with the angels, O worthy of marvel!

*Troparion:*
Thy tongue showed itself to be the pen of the Spirit,[^339] O most glorious Michael, obeying to depict the bodily dispensation of the Word, the Ruler of all, O all-praised one!

*Troparion:*
Adorning thy body with the sacred and honorable vesture of thy priesthood by confession, thou becamest a spectacle unto angels and men,[^340] O all-honorable one!

*Theotokion:*
Direct my life, O Birthgiver of God, governing it by the divine commandments of the Word incarnate from thee; and lead me unto the light, O Virgin Mother Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Enlightenment of them that lie in darkness, salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior, unto Thee I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy radiance, for beside Thee I know no other God!

*Troparion:*
From youth having dedicated thyself unto the Word, O sacredly revealed one, thou didst appear as a divine offering; thou wast adorned with active contemplation, and having gathered the fruit of wisdom, thou wast a shepherd worthy of acceptance, O Michael.

*Troparion:*
The grace of God that richly dwelt in thee, O God-wise one, poured forth rivers of teachings, feeding on which we rejoice, O blessed one, having thee as a manifest intercessor, who makest Christ merciful unto us.

*Troparion:*
He Who adorned thee all with divine power hath now adorned thee, O God-revealed one, with the myrrh of secret priesthood; proclaim thy brightness, for thou hast appeared as a great-minded priest of God.

*Theotokion:*
He that deigned to become like unto us in all things dwelt in thee, O Mother of God, Who shone forth pre-eternally from the Father; and from thee He was born, O all-immaculate one, together man and God almighty.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^341]
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy mercy: from corruption, O God, raise me up!

*Troparion:*
Clothed in the vesture of salvation, O all-praised one, of divine joy, thou didst worthily receive from God the crown of confession.

*Troparion:*
Having endured bitter banishments, O wise one, into a spacious breadth, thou hast reached Paradise, dancing with the martyrs, O God-wise and exceedingly rich one!

*Troparion:*
Having become a disciple of Christ God, thou didst emulate His passions, O blessed one, valiantly enduring tribulations for His Church, O God-inspired one!

*Theotokion:*
Being spiritually instructed in thy ineffable childbearing, O all-immaculate one, all the prophets formed spiritual images and foretold unto all what was to come.

*Kontakion, Tone 8. Podoben: "As the First-fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
As an all-honorable hierarch and a sacred sufferer for piety, thou didst not fear the threats of the evil-named tyrant, but didst conquer his heretical opposition, crying aloud with a free voice in a loud voice: I venerate the icon of Christ and of His all-pure Mother! Therefore we honor thee, O Michael.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The god-opposing command of the lawless tormentor raised a high flame, but Christ stretched out unto the pious children a spiritual dew; He Who is blessed and supremely glorified!

*Troparion:*
The evil-named beast could not endure the divine utterance of thy tongue, O sacred Michael, but condemned thee unto distant exiles; yet he was put to shame, O sacred preacher, beholding thy steadfastness.

*Troparion:*
Wisely guided by thy teachings is the all-sacred Orthodox assembly of Christ; for thou wast an instrument, O sacred celebrant Michael, sounding by the Spirit, crying aloud the wonders of the Ruler of all.

*Troparion:*
Wisely didst thou expose the blasphemous impiety of the evil-named fosterers of Manes,[^342] clearly preaching the all-pure icon of Christ, O father, and of the Mother of God, and of the saints, O venerable Michael!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast a fair vessel of the incarnation of the Word of God, O all-immaculate Virgin; for taking from thee, by His great mercy He received the whole of me, man; He Who is blessed and supremely glorified!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew, sing, ye works, as the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Standing before the judgment seat, O God-preacher Michael, thou wast not dismayed by the threats of the lawless, but cried aloud with a free voice: I revere the all-pure icon of Christ the Savior, and of all the saints!

*Troparion:*
With a firm and steadfast character, wrestling wisely with the God-fighters, thou didst appear as a victor, having shown in deed and word, as was meet, that one should revere the all-pure icon of Christ the Savior, and of all the saints.

*Troparion:*
Knowing that the honor rendered unto an icon passes over to the prototype,[^343] O sacredly-revealed father, following the God-inspired scriptures, thou didst teach all to revere the icon of Christ and of all the saints.

*Theotokion:*
A stream of healings thou unceasingly pourest forth unto us the faithful, O Pure One; drawing whose abundant grace, we sing of thy most pure childbirth, and supremely exalt it unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin appeared unto us, to enlighten those in darkness, to gather those that were scattered; therefore the all-hymned Birthgiver of God we magnify!

*Troparion:*
Shining now with measureless illumination, O exceedingly rich one, standing as it were before the Great Light, thou wast accounted worthy, O father, to receive the end desired by thee; toward which thou didst run with zeal, having lived piously, O most blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast been accounted worthy to behold the brightness surpassing mind, which thou didst manifestly acquire upon earth, O father; but interceding to deliver thy disciples, pray unto Christ, for them that now praise thee and honor thee, O worthy of marvel!

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by divine grace, by the radiances of thy wisdom thou didst wisely extinguish the impudence of the God-fighters with rays from beyond, having acquired a fountain within thee, O marvelous Michael, sacred preacher of Christ!

*Theotokion:*
As the fleece, O all-immaculate one, having received the heavenly rain in thy womb,[^344] thou didst bear unto us Him that grants immortality unto them that piously hymn Him, and that proclaim thee as the all-hymned Birthgiver of God.

## May 24. Our Venerable Father Simeon of the Wonderful Mountain[^345]

*At "Lord, I Have Cried," Stichera of Simeon, on 6, Tone 8. Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Having an inscribed pillar of native virtues, by contemplations and deeds, O exceedingly rich Simeon, thou didst form thy soul, having conquered fleshly wantonness, and having clothed thyself in life-bearing mortification, O venerable one; and thou wast an ever-shining luminary, illuminating all the ends of the earth by grace!

Winged by divine desire, while still bearing flesh and clad with dust, thou didst manifestly become a fellow-citizen with the angels. Withdrawing indeed from earthly things, and being lifted up, O father, unto heavenly things by a high pillar, thou wast ever raised up unto God, and didst shine with ascents.

Having worn down the flesh by temperance, thou didst show thy soul to be lifted up on high and drawing near unto God, O Simeon, and a receptacle of the Spirit; and by His power thou wast accounted worthy to work miracles beyond nature; whence came all-effective action, which surpasses all mind and thoughts.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O father worthy of marvel, thou didst appear as a fair sprout of the wilderness and a protector unto us; for therein thou didst find the desire for the heavenly height, and wast accounted worthy of healing grace from the unstolen treasures of the Spirit. Therefore with both did Christ enrich thee, and showed thee forth as a worker of miracles, O venerable Simeon; pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Podoben: "On the Third Day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding our Life hanging upon the Tree, the all-immaculate Birthgiver of God, weeping maternally, cried aloud: O my Son and my God, save them that hymn thee with love!

*At the Aposticha, of the day. Glory... Tone 2, Idiomelon. By Germanus:*[^346]
Having ascetically ascended the high and wonderful mountain in figure, and having entered into the honorable ark as into the unapproachable sanctuary, by excellent action thou didst show forth the ascent of contemplations; having brightened thy life with iron bonds, adorned as with golden torcs;

...beholding God, and being beheld, conversing alone with the Alone; Whom do thou pray, O honorable Simeon, for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
The coal which glorious Isaiah foresaw of old,[^347] incarnate of the unwedded Mother, springing forth from the Begetter by nod, and being born is slaughtered voluntarily, taking away the sins of the world as an unblemished lamb; therefore the ewe-lamb and virgin, beholding Him upon the Cross, was consumed by the sword of sorrow.

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
A dweller of the wilderness, and an angel in the body, and a wonderworker wast thou shown to be, O our God-bearing father Simeon; by fasting, vigilance, and prayer having received heavenly gifts, thou healest the sick, and the souls of them that run unto thee with faith. Glory to Him that hath given thee strength, glory to Him that hath crowned thee, glory to Him that through thee works healings unto all!

*Canon of the Venerable One, on 6.*
*The acrostic whereof is: "I hymn thy grace, O marvelous Simeon." Tone 8.*[^348]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonderworking rod of Moses of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, submerged Pharaoh the chariot-driver, and saved Israel the fugitive, who traversed it on foot, singing a hymn unto God.

*Troparion:*
Truly a cause of theology is thy memory, O wonderworking father Simeon, and a radiance unto all who have assembled to hymn with pure hearts thy majesty and thy life worthy of marvel!

*Troparion:*
By the voice of the Forerunner and by a vision was good tidings announced unto thy glorious mother; and she conceived and bore thee, O all-blessed father, as a sweet-smelling and divine dwelling of the three-hypostatic Godhead!

*Troparion:*
Foreknowing that from thy mother's swaddling bands, O father, thou wouldst be a bearer of righteousness and of divine gifts beyond nature, the great Forerunner commanded thee to turn away from the left breast and to cleave unto the right.

*Troparion:*
Having received a pure life from the beginning, O exceedingly rich one, by the laver of regeneration a spiritual gift,[^349] from infancy thou didst divinely show the comeliness of thy soul to be brighter than the sun.

*Theotokion:*
Most glorious things were spoken of thee in generations of generations,[^350] who didst contain God the Word in thy womb, yet remained pure, O Birthgiver of God Mary; therefore we all honor thee as our protection after God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial circle and builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
From thy earliest youth thou didst grow up an excellent ascetic and an ardent lover of divine beauty. Therefore having foreknown thee from the womb, the Master sanctified thee, O most glorious father Simeon.

*Troparion:*
Having grown wise from tender age, O wonderworking venerable father, with true knowledge of the incomprehensible Spirit, thou wast accounted worthy to see with thine own eyes, being sprinkled with powers.

*Troparion:*
Having illuminated the senses of thy soul with bright contemplation, O venerable father, thou didst acquire a marvelous understanding of good things, and unto them that lived otherwise, O Simeon, thou didst show forth an unbeguiled life.

*Troparion:*
Mystically the sent angel stood before thee, O God-bearer, enjoining thee joyfully to embrace the angelic life; and thou didst touch the footsteps of him that appeared, O father.[^351]

*Theotokion:*
Having bodily dwelt in the Virgin, O Lord, Thou didst appear unto men even as it was meet to behold Thee; and through her Thou didst show her forth as true Birthgiver of God and helper of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Sanctified before conception, O father, thou wast given unto thy mother as a most fair sprout, about to be a vessel of the Divine Spirit, by Whose power from early age thou didst wound the multitude of wicked spirits with the rod of faith. Therefore having departed with glory, thou wast an unshakable pillar of the faithful and a bright luminary, O Simeon worthy of marvel! Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that honor thy holy memory with love. (Twice)

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast; or Theotokion:*
Having fallen into manifold temptations from visible and invisible enemies, held fast by the tempest of my numberless sins, as unto my fervent protection and defense, O Pure One, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness; therefore, O all-immaculate one, pray earnestly unto Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed, for all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O most pure Birthgiver of God, ever beseeching Him to grant remission of sins unto them that worthily hymn thy glory!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, she called out: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the mercy of compassion, O long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain, have compassion and grant remission of sins unto them that sing of Thy divine passions with faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^352]
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the bosom of the Father, and hast visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thou couldst by no means endure to remain within the world, O father, having the power of the divine Spirit guiding thee; but living in trackless wildernesses, and walking without water with wild beasts while a youth, thou didst rejoice in bodily afflictions.

*Troparion:*
Thirsting like a hart along the mountains, thou didst desire the God-flowing fountains, O father; and having found them, O Simeon, thou didst satisfy thy thirst for the love of God; fed whereon by theology, thou ever pourest forth teachings, beholding the face of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Clad in white, as upon a chariot, thou didst behold him borne, O venerable father, with the eyes of thy mind; and the youth being guided by a bright pillar, divine John stood upon the step before thy ascent unto him.

*Troparion:*
Being guided, O glorious one, by the grace given unto thee, as from strength unto divine strength, thou didst journey unto the dwelling of the God-wise John; with whom lovingly traversing the burning of the ascetic contest, thou didst appear as an angel.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the praise of the faithful, O Unwedded One; thou art the protectress, thou art the refuge of Christians, a wall and a haven; for thou bearest supplications unto thy Son, O all-immaculate one, and dost save from calamities them that with faith and love know thee to be the pure Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray!

*Troparion:*
Full of beauty, like a comely youth, O father, the Lord mystically stood upon the step where thou wast standing; and having shone with the glory of endurance, even as thou didst ask, showing Himself crucified Thy whole life shone with the radiance of revelations, whereby, being filled with the sweet fragrance of divine myrrh, as a sufferer, O favorite of Christ, Thou didst hew down the adverse demonic hosts.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst ascend by divine contemplations, O all-blessed one, through purity of mind; and having a heart that pours forth the streams of life-bearing wisdom, with teachings from God thou didst enlighten the souls of the sick.

*Theotokion:*
Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, despise not kindred care for us, we pray; for thee alone as a merciful cleansing do we Christians set forth unto the Master.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and I pray like Jonah: From corruption, O God, raise me up!

*Troparion:*
Great like Moses, having become marvelous in virtues, O father, having ascended upon a high pillar by the divine nod, thou wast taken up by the Spirit; and receiving enlightening power, even as he, thou wast glorified.

*Troparion:*
The enemy emptied out his quiver of soul-corrupting arrows, shooting furiously, O God-bearer, yet he shook not the pillar of thy soul; for upon the unshakable rock of Christ thou wast firmly established.

*Troparion:*
As thou didst readily endure the sweat of temperance, O marvelous father, the Master appeared unto thee; Christ entrusted unto thee a rod of power, and commanded thee thereby to heal the infirmities of the ailing.

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, dwelling place of the Master, with the bright rays of virginity shining luminously like a lily in the midst of thorns,[^353] I pray unto thee who art good: Grant me forgiveness of sins!

*Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Desiring the things on high and putting away things below, and having made thy pillar like heaven, therefore thou didst shine with the radiance of miracles, O venerable one; and thou unceasingly prayest unto Christ the God of all for all of us!

*Ikos:*
The all-radiant memory of the blessed one hath appeared as another day of salvation unto us, driving away the deep night of passions by the radiance of fasting, and enlightening with all-excellent deeds; wherein having appeared brightly like stars, all the faithful in gladness [offer hymns] unto Simeon[^354] unto Simeon let us offer, as unto a beacon; for beholding from on high, he enlightens us, and gives ineffable pacification, unceasingly praying unto Christ God for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Having quenched the all-destructive power of the Chaldean furnace through the image of the angel who descended, the youths cried out unto the Creator: Blessed art Thou and praised, O God of our fathers![^355]

*Troparion:*
Youthfully, O God-bearer, fleeing the worldly deception of worldly wisdom, and the downfall of vain glory, thou didst love to live in silence, and with the spirit crying out unceasingly: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Stretching out thy hands crosswise and performing prayer, visibly with thine eyes thou didst behold Christ in glory with the angels, O God-bearer, commanding thee to ascend this marvelous mountain.

*Troparion:*
Going by heavenly disposition toward the pillar, O father, thou didst ascend upon it; and before this, again obeying the divine command, thou didst reach this mountain, which thou thyself didst marvelously name "Marvelous."[^356]

*Troparion:*
Having found the high summit planted with all kinds of fragrant flowers, even as a heavenly paradise, thou didst make thy dwelling there; and this habitation thou didst miraculously establish by divine design.

*Theotokion:*
Walking in the light of the Lord, come, let us hymn the divine city of the King of kings, the all-pure Mother, the true Birthgiver of God, as the hope of our souls.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold the Chaldean tyrant madly kindled the furnace for the pious; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Redeemer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages![^357]

*Troparion:*
With God-fitting powers of thy soul, O venerable one, thou wast joined unto the thrice-radiant shining, being instructed by Him as from one Godhead; unto Him before thy pillar thou didst gloriously erect a most rich temple, and didst teach men to honor the uncreated Trinity in it unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
The all-powerful operation of the Holy Spirit, having divinely taken up abode in thee, O God-inspired one, moved those tormented by evil spirits from all ends of the earth to gather together; and healing them by thy powerful staff, thou teachest them to hymn the Creator unto all the ages.

*Troparion:*
Youthful carnal mindedness thou didst subdue, O father, by fasting and supplication in the spirit; having extinguished the fiery burning of the belly, thou didst consume it; and as one not eating beyond human nature thou didst appear, nourished only by heavenly food; and the Cause thereof thou hymnest unto the ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

*Troparion:*
Christ the Lord entrusts unto thee, O marvelous father, a divine choir of disciples; whom having received with love, thou didst endow with teachings to run the course unto temperance; and resting with them in the Kingdom, thou glorifiest the King of all unto all the ages.

*Theotokion:*
Pray unto thy Son and Lord for us who truly and purely cry aloud unto thee, and who confess thee to be the Mother of God, O pure one, asking for remission of sins, and for those who faithfully sing to obtain salvation: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear shuddered at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from the virginal womb. Therefore, O ye faithful, we magnify the all-pure Birthgiver of God.[^358]

*Troparion:*
With few loaves, O Simeon, having moved Christ by thy prayers, O thrice-blessed one, Who satisfied a great multitude of men: in thy monastery thou didst fill the empty granaries with wheat through thy blessings, and didst deliver thy flock from famine.[^359]

*Troparion:*
At the words of Martha, thy venerable and honorable mother, O blessed one, who entreated thee with supplications to beseech Christ, thou didst not refuse to fulfill her petitions, and thou didst deliver the city of God from the wrath of God.[^360]

*Troparion:*
Having known the end of thy ascetic labors, O Simeon, having been released from the body, thou didst enter the ineffable habitation of deification on high; unto which having taken flight from hence, thou rejoicest in the spirit, beholding because of it the tri-hypostatic radiance.

*Troparion:*
By thy beckoning, O father Simeon, and by thy providential cares, deliver from all passions of the enemy those who by their prayers have hastened unto thee from this turbulent life into thy haven; and brightly preserve those who feast thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O Mother of God, who didst give birth unto Christ my Savior, God and man, twofold in nature but not in hypostasis; the Only-begotten from the Father, and from thee the Firstborn of all creation.[^361] Therefore we all ceaselessly magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "O Light Immutable" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*

Having shone forth as a light, thou enlightenest the universe. The multitudes of monastics rejoice in thy memory, O Simeon, God-bearer; cease not praying for peace.

*On the Aposticha, of the day.*

*Glory, Tone 6:*
O venerable father Simeon, into all the earth went forth the proclamation of thy virtuous deeds; therefore in the heavens thou didst find the reward of thy labors, thou didst destroy the demonic hosts, and didst reach the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate. Having boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for our souls!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Standing once by the Tree at the time of the Crucifixion with the virgin disciple, the Virgin cried out weeping: Woe is me! How sufferest Thou, O impassible Son of all, O Christ?[^362]

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the feast, and of the saint, Ode 3.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord...*
*Verse: Hear, O God, my voice...*
*Epistle to the Colossians, Section 258 [Colossians 3:12–16].*
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord...*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Section 43 [Matthew 11:27–30].*
*Communion Verse: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous man be.*

## May 25. The Third Finding of the Precious Head of the Holy Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John[^363]

*At Vespers, we chant:* Blessed is the man.
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the feast, 3; and of the Forerunner, 5.*
*If there is no feast, Stichera on 6, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Miracle" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron:*
O blessed John the Forerunner, shining forth more brightly than rays of the sun, thy head gleamed from the earth and enlightened the faithful; having which as a divine treasure, we draw forth rich grace, sanctifying our souls and bodies and thoughts, and celebrating we call thee blessed.

*Sticheron:*
Pouring forth unfailing grace, thy sacred head, O blessed Forerunner, God-wise star and by grace, manifestly sanctifying the arrival of thy miracles with the God-wise people and the emperor, warmly confirming the Orthodox faith.[^364]

*Sticheron:*
We celebrate the third appearance of thy precious head, which the Trinity hath crowned, it having been beheaded, O glorious one, for the sake of divine zeal; and at its finding the angelic ranks rejoice, the company of martyrs, the divine apostles, and all the prophets; with whom ever remember us, O Forerunner of the Lord!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
A treasure of divine gifts, thy God-preserved head, O Forerunner, shone forth from the depths of the earth; receiving which faithfully and bowing down before it, O glorious one, we are enriched by thee, O Baptist of Christ, with most marvelous miracles and remission of transgressions.

*Now and ever, of the feast. If not: Now and ever, Theotokion in the same tone (Tone 6, First Tone of the Octoechos).*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 40 [Isaiah 40:1–3, 9; 41:17–18; 45:8; 48:20; 54:1]):*
Thus says the Lord: Comfort ye, comfort ye My people, says God. Ye priests, speak unto the heart of Jerusalem; entreat her, for her humiliation is multiplied, for her sin is pardoned, for she hath received from the hand of the Lord double for her sins. The voice of one crying in the wilderness: Prepare ye the way of the Lord, make straight the paths for our God! Get thee up into the high mountain, O thou that bringest glad tidings unto Zion; lift up thy voice with strength, O thou that bringest glad tidings unto Jerusalem; lift it up, be not afraid! I am the Lord God, I will hear the poor of Israel, and will not forsake them. But I will open rivers in the mountains, and fountains in the midst of the fields; I will make the wilderness into a pool, and the thirsty land into springs of water. Let heaven rejoice from above, and let the clouds drop down righteousness; let the earth shine forth, and let mercy blossom, and let righteousness spring up together. Proclaim with a voice of joy, and let it be heard; declare it even unto the ends of the earth; say ye: The Lord hath redeemed His servant Jacob. And if they thirst in the wilderness, He shall bring forth water for them out of the rock. Rejoice, thou barren that bearest not; break forth and cry, thou that travailest not; for more are the children of the desolate than of her that hath a husband.[^365]

*Reading from the Prophecy of Malachi (Chapter 3 [Malachi 3:1–3, 5–7, 12, 17–18; 4:4–6]):*
Thus says the Lord Almighty: Behold, I send My messenger before Thy face, who shall prepare Thy way before Thee; and the Lord Whom ye seek shall come unto His temple. And who shall abide the day of His coming? For He comes like a refiner's fire, and like fullers' soap. And He shall sit refining and purifying, as silver and gold; and He shall draw near unto you in judgment, and shall be a swift witness against the sorcerers, and against the adulterers, and against false swearers by My name, and against those who defraud the hireling of his wages, and against those who oppress the widow, and thrust aside the fatherless, and turn aside the judgment of the stranger, and fear not Him, says the Lord Almighty. For I am the Lord your God, and I change not; and ye sons of Jacob have turned aside from the law, and have not kept it. Therefore return unto Me, and I will return unto you, says the Lord Almighty; and all nations shall call you blessed. And understand that I am the Lord Who discerns between the righteous and between the lawless on that day wherein I will make provision for them that love Me. Know therefore and remember the law of Moses My servant, even as He commanded you in Horeb for all Israel, with the statutes and ordinances. And behold, I will send you Elijah the Tishbite before the coming of the great and illustrious day of the Lord; who shall turn the heart of the father unto the son, and the heart of a man unto his neighbor, lest I come and strike the earth suddenly, says the Lord Almighty, the Holy God of Israel.[^366]

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 4 [Wisdom 4:7–14; 5:1–5]):*
Though the righteous reach his end, he shall be in rest. And the righteous man that dies shall condemn the ungodly that are living. For they shall see the end of the righteous, and shall not understand what was counseled concerning him; for the Lord shall cast down the ungodly speechless on their face, and shall shake them from the foundations, and they shall be utterly laid waste in grief, and their memory shall perish. For they shall come with fear to the reckoning of their sins, and their lawlessness shall convict them to their face. Then shall the righteous stand in great boldness before the face of such as have afflicted him and made no account of his labors. When they see it, they shall be troubled with terrible fear, and shall be amazed at the marvelousness of his salvation; and repenting and groaning for anguish of spirit, they shall say within themselves: This was he whom we had sometimes in derision, and a proverb of reproach, fools that we were! We accounted his life madness, and his end dishonorable;

dishonorable. How is he numbered among the children of God, and his lot is among the saints? Therefore have we erred from the way of truth, and the light of righteousness shone not unto us, and the sun rose not upon us. We wearied ourselves in the way of wickedness and destruction, and we have gone through deserts where there was no way, but the way of the Lord we have not known.[^367]

*On the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron:*
Come, let us brightly accomplish a divine celebration! For the light of the Light, the voice of the Word, the friend of the Bridegroom, the great lampstand of truth hath appeared, the Baptist of the Lord, showing us his holy head.
*Verse: There will I make the horn of David to bud; I have prepared a lamp for Mine anointed.[^368]*
*Sticheron:*
Concerning the wanton lawlessness of Herod's deeds and the adulterous defilements, thou didst rebuke the lawless woman as a preacher of purity, and wast beheaded in the head, O Forerunner, which head through the grace of divine miracles thou hast now revealed unto the faithful.
*Verse: Lord, remember David, and all his meekness.[^369]*
*Sticheron:*
As a God-streaming fountain of grace from the earth's depths, gushing forth miracles, the head of the Forerunner, which was granted unto us from God as a manifest treasure, having joyfully hymned, let us praise the Giver of all good things.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
As a most venerable chest of divine thoughts, clearly foreseeing the mystery of the ineffable Essence, thy head, as from a mother's womb, hath shone forth today from earthly treasures, O all-praised John; and it hath perfumed the whole universe with fragrance, pouring out the myrrh of sanctification, spiritually proclaiming the way of repentance, and praying unto the Savior of all for our souls.

*Now and ever, of the day, or Theotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As a divine treasure hidden in the earth, Christ revealed thy head unto us, O prophet and Forerunner; having all assembled therefore at its finding, with God-inspired hymns let us sing unto the Savior Who saves us from corruption by thy prayers.

*Glory, now and ever:*
The mystery hidden from the ages... or of the feast.

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," if it is Pentecostarion season, Troparion of the feast, twice. Glory, of the Forerunner. Now and ever, of the feast. If not, Troparion of the Forerunner, twice. Glory, now and ever, Theotokion. After the first After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Go Thou Before" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Shining forth from the earth, the Forerunner's head emits rays of incorruption and healings unto the faithful; from on high it gathers the multitude of angels, and below it calls together the human race to send up one-voiced glory unto Christ God. (Twice)
*Glory, now and ever, of the feast.*
*If it be Lent: Glory, now and ever, Theotokion, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*
As thou art all-immaculate, who didst give birth to the transcendent God, together with the Forerunner unceasingly pray Him to grant us remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end, unto us who with faith and love hymn thee as is due, O only extolled one.

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Brought forth like gold from the ore, thy sacred head most gloriously from the unsetting depths of the earth hath enriched all the minds of the faithful today with divine understanding, crying aloud as is its wont: "Repent!" Bowing down before it, O Forerunner, with a loud voice in faith we glorify Christ. (Twice)
*Glory, now and ever, of the feast, or Theotokion:*
He that sits upon the cherubic throne, and makes His dwelling in the Father's bosom, sits upon His holy throne in thy bosom, O Sovereign Lady; for God, having truly become incarnate, hath reigned over all the nations, and wisely now we chant unto Him: Beseech Him that thy servants may be saved!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Herod, acting lawlessly, O Forerunner, wretchedly and accursedly severed thy precious head with the sword; which we, the faithful, kiss with love.
*Glory, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What Was Commanded" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*
The head of the Baptist was hidden like a pearl in the blessed earth, and adorned as a God-illumined candlestick, it shines forth working wonders for all the world; like the dawn hath the Forerunner of the Sun shone forth gloriously, and with unsetting light proclaiming again unto us, calling aloud: "[Behold,] the Lamb of God!"[^371]
*[Now and ever, Theot]okion:*
[As thou art all-immaculate, who didst bear the transcend]ent God: my [heart, which is ever] altered by sin [and sloth], and by the assaults of the [evil one, do thou es]tablish, O Good [One, by] thy [supreme prayers, O Birthgiver of God, and save] thy flock which thou hast acquired, O all-immaculate one.[^370]

*Gradual Hymns (Anavathmoi), the First Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: There will I make the horn of David to bud; I have prepared a lamp for Mine anointed.*
*Verse: Lord, remember David, and all his meekness.*
*Let every breath.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Section 31 [Luke 7:17–30].*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
She that aforetime upon a platter rebuked Herod's lawless impurity, and preached repentance unto all the faithful, the precious head of the Baptist of Christ, having appeared from earthly treasures, lies before those who faithfully hasten unto it, and touch it with fear, and bow down before it with heartfelt love: granting remission of transgressions, it provides needful things and great mercy unto their petitions.

*If it be Pentecostarion season, Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 6. If Lent, Canon of the Birthgiver of God with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Forerunner on 8, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
The wonder-working rod of Moses, having of old struck the sea crosswise and divided it, [drowned] the charioteer Pharaoh; but it saved Israel the fugitive, walking on foot, as they chanted a song unto God.[^372]

*Troparion:*
Having become the voice of the Word, loose my voicelessness, even as thou didst thy father's of old, that with a joyful voice I may praise thee at the present finding of thy head, O blessed Forerunner, heavenly man![^373]

*Troparion:*
Having laid thy hand upon the Head of the Church of God, O Forerunner, thou manifestest this third finding of thy precious head, which recognized the manifestation of the Trinity, preternaturally sanctifying the assemblies of the Orthodox.[^374]

*Troparion:*
