Not enduring to be hidden for long, O Forerunner of the Lord, thou disposest to appear again today, not shut up in a jar, but placed in a silver vessel in a sacred place, revealed unto the sacred minister.[^375]

*Troparion:*
The imperial city manifestly receives now the sacred gift, which before had been deprived of thy head, O Forerunner; adorning itself with its entry, it now rejoices, and as is meet calls thee blessed.[^376]

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst blossom from a barren womb, preparing by word the ways for the Creator, O all-praised one, Who shone forth ineffably from the all-holy Virgin; whom knowing to be the Birthgiver of God, all generations call blessed.

*Katavasia:*

*Katavasia of the feast.*
*If Lent:* I shall open my mouth...
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, firm foundation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.[^377]

*Troparion:*
By wanton dancing thy sacred head was given unto a vile woman, and was carried in her hands; but now chastely by the hands of the hieromonk, it is sacredly brought into the holy church.[^378]

*Troparion:*
Piously the assembly of the faithful meets thee, and with the hieromonk the emperor, O glorious Forerunner, chanting with joy; and he forestalls thee with love at thy common arrival.[^379]

*Troparion:*
From the unsetting sacred vaults, like the radiant sun, thy most sacred head having shone forth hath enlightened the whole universe, O prophet of Christ and Forerunner, with divine splendors.

*Theotokion:*
From barren loins thou didst shine forth as a luminous star of the Sun of glory, running before Him Who shone forth ineffably from the virginal cloud, O Baptist, and Who loosed the night of polytheism.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the feast.*
*Then Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Brought forth like gold from the ore, thy sacred head most gloriously from the unsetting depths of the earth, O Baptist of Christ, hath enriched all who hasten unto its finding, who with hymns magnify the Savior and Creator Who grants us great mercy for thy sake. (Twice)
*Glory, now and ever, of the feast, or Theotokion:*
Being a lover of sin, I pray thee who didst bear the sinless God Who takes away the sins of the world, O all-pure one: Have compassion upon my sinful soul, and cleanse my many sins; for thou art the cleansing of sinners, and the salvation and protection of the faithful.
*If Lent, only the Sessional Hymn twice; and Glory, now and ever, Theotokion.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have considered Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.[^380]

*Troparion:*
As the highest of the prophets, thou wast accounted worthy to behold Him Who was foretold, going before Him in the power of Elijah, O blessed Forerunner.[^381]

*Troparion:*
Thy most glorious head, gushing forth myrrh of healings thou hast now manifested a third time, driving away foul passions.

*Troparion:*
Not in a jar as aforetime, but buried in a silver vessel, thy head appears in a sacred place, O thou worthy of wonder!

*Troparion:*
Having assembled together, draw ye all inexhaustible healings gushing forth from the spring of the precious head of the Forerunner of Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Save me who am humbled, who have lived high-mindedly, O all-pure one who didst give birth unto Him Who exalted our humbled nature.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.[^382]

*Troparion:*
Having become the mediator between the Old and the New, O most sacred one, by divine mediation renew our oldness, as we celebrate renewal through the new appearance of thy head, O blessed Forerunner.

*Troparion:*
Bringing from Comana the grace of healings, thy head unto the imperial city, O Forerunner: thou summonest cities today unto its arrival; multitudes of the faithful assemble, being filled with incorruptible illumination.[^383]

*Troparion:*
The emperor rejoices more at the entry of thy head than in the purple; and the divine hierarch who shares the name of the God-bearer, O Forerunner, holding it in his palms, carefully guards it, praising the perfection of the faithful.[^384]

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the Word, thou hast appeared higher than the heavenly hosts, and hast deified the nature of earthborn men, O all-immaculate Maiden. Therefore as the all-true Birthgiver of God, in soul and tongue, we the faithful glorify thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation![^385]

*Troparion:*
Truth having shone forth from the earth, the head of Thy Baptist, O Christ: it hath driven away falsehood, destroyed the bitter winter of heresies, and enlightened the world.[^386]

*Troparion:*
Draw ye illumination, and God's grace and mercy! And sanctify your heads and souls, having touched the head of the Forerunner of grace, who touched the divine summit in the waters!

*Troparion:*
To crush the heads of enemies beneath the feet of our king, pray thou, O Forerunner, unto him who with love honors[^387] who with love honors thy head which rebuked Herod.

*Theotokion:*[^388]
Unto those in Hades thou didst proclaim the Light, Him Who comes from virginal blood, Who was clothed in flesh as man, for the beneficence of men, O blessed Forerunner.

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Fulfilling the Dispensation for Our Sake" (Є҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ):*
A light-manifest and divine pillar in the world, the lamp of the Sun, the Forerunner, having shown forth his light-bearing and divine head in the ends of the earth, sanctifies those who faithfully bow down and cry out: O most wise baptizer of Christ, save us all.

*Ikos:*
From the earth like a paradise spring, the God-beloved head of the Forerunner pours forth streams of grace and grants currents of miracles unto all who have need. Let us all therefore approach; let us fervently draw these streams of divine healings, for it waters souls with unceasing gifts, drowns bodily illnesses, and timely takes away every disease. This therefore let us hymn with love, and let us kiss it with faith, honorably greeting it, and with hymns like crowns let us, the faithful, crown it. Let us pre-cleanse our mental defilements, and let us adorn our souls with divine virtues, and let us firmly cry aloud: O most wise baptizer of Christ, save us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Judean youths having arrived in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trod down the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
A most radiant lamp of the countenance of glory wast thou, O Forerunner, driving away the darkness of deception and illuminating with faith those who call out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
This honorable Church brightly celebrates a suppliant feast, O Forerunner, honoring thy head and customarily crying out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst fore-announce the dread coming of the Word with divine leapings; thou dost announce the finding of thy head, O prophet, with unfalsified lips, unto the sacredly ministering shepherd who warmly desires thee.[^389]

*Theotokion:*
Virginity and childbearing above nature and word met together in thee, for thou didst bear God incarnate, ineffably, O Birthgiver of God, unto Whom we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried out to the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The Baptizer and Forerunner, the earthly angel, the certain rebuker of iniquity, having seen beforehand the manifestation of the Trinity, reveals his head a third time unto all who sing with love: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
The most meek emperor together with Christ-loving peoples fervently meets thee, O all-praised one; and the chief of shepherds stretches forth his hands unto thee, and pressing it to his chest, he leaps with gladness and cries out gratefully: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages.[^390]

*Troparion:*
Shining brighter than gold, O Forerunner, treasured in silver vessels like a precious wealth, thy head was given unto the imperial city; growing rich with its divine gifts, it fervently sings: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*
Unto Him Who was carried in the womb, Who bears all things, O blessed John, thou didst bow down rejoicing, though hidden in a barren womb, O all-praised one. Having proclaimed the Lamb of God Whom she bore from the Holy Virgin, thou didst piously cry aloud: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^391]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High of His own will came down even unto the flesh, from a virginal womb having become man. Therefore, the all-pure Birthgiver of God do we, the faithful, magnify.

*Troparion:*
Having sprouted as fruit from a barren one, higher than those born of women, warrior of Christ the King, the great Forerunner, who proclaimed the Lamb Who takes away the sins of the world, let him be praised with sacred hymns!

*Troparion:*
Having richly filled the chalice, with a lofty proclamation thou dost summon the Church of Christ by the finding of thy head, O Forerunner, crying out: Come, be filled with the good things which I have prepared, and magnify Him Who is, Who is granted unto men!

*Troparion:*
The angel-named emperor receives thee gladly into his kingdom, O angel of God, together with the most excellent chief shepherd who sacredly ministers with him, who is adorned with the calling and the purple, a truly God-bearing and God-wise priest.[^392]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst flash forth divine illumination by the entrance of thy head by the entrance of thy head, O glorious one, and thou didst make clear the hearts of the faithful who cry out unto thee: O unsetting lamp of the Sun, all-pure Forerunner, deliver us, the whole city, and the people from the gloom of passions!

*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Savior Who wast born, and Who didst keep her who bore Thee incorrupt after childbirth: when Thou shalt sit to judge my deeds, overlook my iniquities and my sins, as Thou art sinless, and merciful God and Lover of mankind.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Thou Hast Visited Us" (Посѣти́лъ ны̀):*
The earth endured not to hide thy head for long, as the voice of one crying out; in some manner thou didst announce its finding, O Forerunner John, for which we honor thee.

*Glory... Podoben: "With the Disciples" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ зрѧ́щымъ):*
At the finding of thy most honorable head, the Christ-loving emperor rejoiced far more than over his imperial purple; strengthened by the reward of victories from on high through it, do thou also preserve us and cover us with thy prayers, O all-praised baptizer John, who brightly celebrate and love thee.

*Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*
In thee with God we place our hope, O all-pure one; being co-crucified with Christ Who came forth from thee, by thy entreaties unto Him preserve us unharmed unto the end.

*At the Praises, Stichera, on 4. Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O Container of Ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, O sacred head, light-bearing and revered by angels, which was cut off by the sword of old, and by sharp rebukes cut down the shame of uncleanness! Wellspring of wonders, watering the faithful, and which proclaimed the Savior's saving advent, and which of old beheld the descent of the Spirit abiding upon Him! Mediatrix between the Old and the New Grace, implore Christ to grant our souls great mercy!

*Sticheron:*
Hidden of old in an earthen vessel, the Forerunner's head appeared, manifestly given forth from the earth's depths and gushing streams of wonders. For in the waters it washed the head of Him Who now covers the highest realms with waters and rains down divine forgiveness upon men. This head, therefore, let us truly bless as most glorious, and in its finding let us joyfully celebrate, imploring Christ to grant our souls great mercy!

*Sticheron:*
The head which proclaimed the Lamb of God Who appeared in the flesh, and made known unto all the saving paths of repentance by divine commandments which of old rebuked Herod's transgression of the law, and for this reason was severed from the body, and for a time endured concealment, has shone forth unto us like a light-manifest sun, crying out to repent and now to draw near in contrition of soul unto Christ, Who grants great mercy unto the world!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having received with a voice of praise in faith the most honorable head of Thy baptizer, O Lord, which has appeared today from the earth, bringing it forward in supplication, O Lover of mankind, we, Thy sinful servants, pray: through it, grant that we may obtain cleansing and great mercy from Thee on the day of judgment.

*Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*

*Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion: "The Mystery Hidden from All Ages" (Ezhe ot veka). Litanies and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Forerunner, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.*
*Verse: Hear my voice, O God, when I make supplication unto Thee.*
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 176 (2 Cor. 4:6–15).*[^393]
*Alleluia, Tone 5: Light has dawned for the righteous man.*
*Verse: Truth has sprung out of the earth, and righteousness has looked down from heaven.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 40 (Matt. 11:2–15).*[^394]
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous man be...*

*Mark: If the Finding of the Head of John the Baptist occurs on the 7th Sunday after Pascha, of the Holy Fathers:*[^395]
*The service of the Feast of Ascension is omitted, and the Sunday service is sung, together with that of the Forerunner and of the Holy Fathers. On Saturday at Small Vespers, we sing as indicated in the chapter of the Theologian.*[^396]
*At Great Vespers, after the customary Kathisma: At "Lord, I have cried," Sunday Stichera, 3; and of the Forerunner, 3; and of the Holy Fathers, 4. Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Sunday Theotokion of the tone. After the Entrance, Readings of the Fathers and of the Forerunner. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple, and the Praises Stichera of the Forerunner. Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion. At the Aposticha, Sunday Stichera. Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion, "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," twice; and of the Fathers, once; and a reading in the Acts of the Apostles. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Sunday Troparion and of the Fathers; Glory... of the Forerunner; Both now... Theotokion. After the Kathismata, Sunday Sessional Hymns, and reading in the Gospel. Then the Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymns of the Forerunner, both once, without Theotokia. Glory... Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Forerunner. The rest we sing at Matins and at the Praises similarly, and at the Hours and at the Liturgy, as indicated in the chapter of the Theologian.*

*It is fitting to know: If the commemoration of the Finding of the Precious Head occurs on the Saturday of Pentecost, or on the Sunday of Pentecost: we sing his service on Thursday of the 7th week.*

*If, however, the Finding of the Precious Head of John the Forerunner occurs on the Monday of Pentecost:*
*We sing it at Matins on Tuesday, together with the festal service.*

*If, moreover, the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner occurs on Friday of the 7th week after Pascha, on the Apodosis of the Ascension of Christ; or on the Saturday before the Sunday of All Saints, the Apodosis of the Feast of Pentecost: We sing his service in rank on Saturday, together with the Apodosis of the feast. On Friday evening, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st Stasis. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the feast, 6; and of the saint, 4. Glory... of the saint; Both now... of the feast. Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and 3 Readings of the saint. At the Litiya, Stichera of the feast, and the Aposticha Stichera of the saint; Glory... of the saint; Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the feast, with their refrains; Glory... of the saint; Both now... of the feast. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the saint; Glory... Both now... of the feast. And the Dismissal. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast, twice; Glory... of the saint; Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns, and reading of the feast. The Polyeleos, and all the Sessional Hymns of the saint; Glory... Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos of the saint; Both now... of the feast; and reading of the saint. The Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, and Gospel of the saint. Psalm 50, and Sticheron of the saint. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 6; and of the saint on 4; and the other of the feast on 4. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the saint, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the saint, twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast; and reading of the saint. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the feast; Glory... of the saint; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the feast, 3; and of the saint, 3; Glory... of the saint; Both now... of the feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the saint; Glory... Both now... of the feast; litanies, and Dismissal, and First Hour. At the Hours, Troparion of the feast; Glory... of the saint; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we alternate the Kontakia of the feast and of the saint. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the feast, Ode 4 on 4; and of the saint, Ode 3 on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the feast and of the saint; Glory... Kontakion of the saint; Both now... of the feast of the feast. Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn of the feast and of the saint. Epistle and Gospel of the day and of the saint.*

*Concerning the same feast, it is fitting to know: If the Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner occurs on the Sunday of All Saints, we sing his service on Friday. But if it be a great saint for whom the Typikon prescribes a Vigil, we sing the service of the great saint on this present Sunday of All Saints, just as that of John the Theologian on the 7th Sunday after Pascha, in rank with the Holy Fathers; so likewise is it sung here.*

## May 26. The Holy Apostle Carpus, One of the Seventy[^397]

*At Vespers, Stichera of the Apostle, Tone 4, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*

*Sticheron:*
By acceptable radiance making thy mind divine, thou hast been enlightened, O all-rich one; and thou didst go forth unto the preaching of Christ, and didst teach the peoples to honor Him as the Lover of mankind. And now pray thou, to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Having appeared as a most radiant star, by divine radiances thou dost ever enlighten the ends of the universe with thy teachings, O blessed one; and by grace thou dost drive away the gloom of infirmity from each one. And now pray thou, to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Offering unto God a bloodless, perfect sacrifice, for His sake, O martyr, thou didst sacrifice thyself and wast brought unto the spiritual table, a sweet-smelling sacrifice, O apostle Carpus, all-rich one! And now pray thou, to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*
All-hymned Birthgiver of God, hope and confirmation of the faithful, refuge and help: we pray thee, preserve thy servants from every distress, who faithfully bow down before thy childbearing; and implore Him to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the ewe-lamb and spotless Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Cross, having neither form nor comeliness, woe is me! she cried out weeping: Whither has Thy beauty set, O sweetest One? Whither Thy majesty? Whither the grace the grace flashing forth from Thy countenance, O my most loving Son?

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Holy Apostle Carpus, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Canon, the composition of Joseph.[^398] Whose acrostic is: "The glorious Carpus I praise with divine love." Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou didst lead Thy people out of Egyptian bondage, and didst cover Pharaoh's chariots and might.

*Troparion:*
Those who with love celebrate thy light-bearing, sacred, glorious, and all-honorable memory, fill with divine light through thy prayers, O apostle!

*Troparion:*
Having committed thyself wholly unto the Master, thou didst keep His honorable laws unharmed, and didst teach the lawless unto good lawfulness, O apostle!

*Troparion:*
Having loved the transcendent Mind, at the secret vision, being illuminated in mind, O all-blessed and sacred one, thou didst appear as a second light!

*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably given birth, O Virgin, thou didst renew the laws of nature; therefore we bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty has become weak, and the infirm have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart has been established in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
A psaltery sounding by the Spirit wast thou, O all-blessed one, thy God-moved tongue proclaiming the great things of our Savior and God!

*Troparion:*
Having the co-unoriginate Word guiding thee and manifestly strengthening thee, O Carpus, thou didst show saving paths unto the godless.

*Troparion:*
Having proclaimed the saving preaching unto creation, O Carpus, as a God-pleasing pillar, thou didst endure many persecutions and afflictions, O glorious one!

*Theotokion:*
As a new Child, thou didst bear Him Who before the ages shone forth from the Father, ineffably, O Birthgiver of God; therefore we glorify thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Having received the divine radiance of the Comforter, O most wise Carpus, thou didst enlighten those darkened by ignorance; and having passed over unto the kingdom on high, thou dost stand before the King of all, ever entreating Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*

He departed not from the divine nature, having become flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained such, Who after childbirth preserved thee as a virgin mother, as before childbirth, all-spotless, the one Lord: Him fervently implore, to grant us great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing suspended upon the Cross Him Who without pain sprang forth from her, fittingly weeping as a mother cried out: Woe is me, my Child! How art Thou suffering willingly, wishing to deliver man from shameful passions?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of love toward Thine image, O Bountiful One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise.

*Troparion:*
Having built the multitude of the faithful like stones upon the rock of piety, thou didst make them temples of the Holy Spirit, O God-wise apostle!

*Troparion:*
Having made men new through baptism, O God-blessed one, who had grown old in corrupt dispositions, thou didst save them as they glorify Christ our God!

*Troparion:*
Uttering the mystery of Him Who covered the heavens with virtue, O God-wise one, thou didst draw the nations unto knowledge, driving away the darkness.

*Troparion:*
Journeying often with Paul, the luminary of the universe, O Carpus, thou didst show unto the peoples the ways leading unto piety.[^399]

*Theotokion:*
In spirit the God-bearer, O Birthgiver of God, beheld thee as a great and overshadowing mountain, O maiden, from which our God came forth in the flesh.[^400]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thy light, O Lord, send down upon us, and from the gloom of transgressions release us, O Good One, granting Thy peace.

*Troparion:*
As an excellent husbandman, thou didst show unto the peoples the Tree of Life, of which whoever eats shall live henceforth unto the ages, O apostle, herald of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Having been an apostle, a preacher of the Gentiles, and an honorable teacher, a sacred initiate and sacred speaker, O Carpus, in gladness thou dost stand before the Master!

*Troparion:*
From the East thou didst come like an unsetting star, enlightening the universe with the beams of thy words and with sacred icons, O hierarch and apostle!

*Theotokion:*
Cleanse for me now the defilement of soul and body, O blessed one, by thy merciful regard, that I may hymn and magnify thee as is meet, O all-hymned one!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The Prophet Jonah cried out, foreshadowing the three-day burial, while praying in the whale: From corruption deliver me, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparion:*
The shrine of thy relics pours forth holy streams of healing unto those in need, through divine grace, and takes away passions, and drowns unclean spirits!

*Troparion:*
Thy word delivering men from irrationality, and the great demonstration of miracles, O glorious Carpus, guided the wandering unto piety.

*Troparion:*
Driven by divine power, having slain the serpent that spoke blasphemy against the height, O apostle, thou didst save those swallowed up by him!

*Theotokion:*
Without pain thou didst bear the Creator, O all-spotless one, Who takes away our sicknesses and toils, and accounts mankind worthy of painless food.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
As a most radiant star the Church has ever acquired thee, O apostle Carpus, enlightened by the abundant granting of thy miracles: save those who with faith honor thy memory.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamic youths of old in Babylon trod down the flame of the furnace, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Driving away diseases by thy speaking, healing passions by the sign alone, thou didst proclaim the saving preaching, O sacred initiate Carpus, enlightening those in darkness!

*Troparion:*
In no wise fearing the wrath of the princes, O wise one, valiantly thou didst strip thyself for the suffering of painful labors, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The faithful established thee as a divine visitor consecrated by the Lord, O Carpus, pre-eminently visiting them with divine visitations.

*Theotokion:*
As being more spacious than the heavens, O Virgin, thou didst receive into thy womb the uncontainable Word, Who delivers the human race from all narrowness.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who in the flesh was nailed upon the Cross, and showed unto us a weapon unto salvation, ye youths supremely exalt, Christ our God unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Serving wise Paul, the guide, in his divine epistles, O hierarch, thou didst dispense saving teachings unto all who are being saved!

*Troparion:*
A bright morning enlightening the universe with light, thou didst appear as a preacher, and the deep darkness of folly hast thou driven away hast thou driven away, O all-marvelous one!

*Troparion:*
Performing miracles by calling upon the Father and the Spirit, O wise one, and the incarnate Word, thou didst persuade the wise Greeks to revere the Tri-hypostatic Godhead!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst appear as heaven, O Birthgiver of God, having shone forth unto us the Sun of righteousness, illuminating all of us unto the knowledge of the knowledge of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thy childbirth appeared incorrupt: God came forth from thy womb, and appeared in the flesh upon the earth, and dwelt with men. Therefore, O Birthgiver of God, we all magnify thee!

*Troparion:*
Seeing God ascending with glory with His flesh into the highest realms, O Carpus, thou wast numbered among the seventy disciples, and didst go forth together with them, appearing as a divine preacher!

*Troparion:*
As a sheep in the midst of wolves wast thou sent, transforming them, O Carpus, unto understanding by the true word unto salvation, and converting their disposition unto piety, O God-proclaiming one!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a companion of angels, O apostle Carpus, God-proclaimer, and of the apostles and martyrs who marvelously finished their course in piety: rejoicing with them in gladness, remember us also!

*Troparion:*
Journeying upon the earth, O Carpus, thou didst sanctify it, and didst preach the holy Gospel in faith, and didst bring souls unto the Life-giving Word; therefore He grants thee the heavenly inheritance.

*Theotokion:*
We offer unto thee Gabriel's voice, O Virgin, crying aloud: Rejoice, O maiden, light cloud! Rejoice, untilled earth, bearing as fruit the Ear of grain that nourishes all creation!

## May 27. The Holy Hieromartyr Therapon[^401]

*At Vespers, Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "Thy Martyrs, O Lord" (Мч҃ницы твоѝ Гд҃и):*

*Sticheron:*
The all-praised Therapon, enlightened by divine deeds, was accounted worthy with the eyes of the heart to see the beauty of Christ, having pleased Him by suffering; therefore, by grace he ever heals the passions of souls and bodies of those who hasten unto him in faith.

*Sticheron:*
The all-wealthy Therapon, being a fountain of living water, pours forth streams of healings, and washes away unto the end the defilement of grievous diseases of all who hasten with faith: by his supplications, O Lord, grant unto all great mercy!

*Sticheron:*
The marvelous Therapon, sprinkled with crimson blood, having made his vestment most bright—the vestment truly of priesthood, in which he was divinely adorned—entered into the holy of holies: by his prayers, O Lord, grant unto all great mercy!

*Stichera of the Theotokos, same tone and podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Sticheron:*
Snatch me away, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand of the man-slaying serpent, who battles to swallow me up utterly through deceit: crush his jaws, I pray, and destroy his craftiness, that having been delivered from his claws, I may magnify thy power!

*Sticheron:*
I am a barren tree, O all-spotless one, bearing no fruit of salvation; therefore I tremble at the cutting down, lest I, the wretched one, be cast into the unquenchable fire: therefore I fall down before thee, deliver me from this, and through thy mediation show me fruitful unto thy Son, O pure one!

*Sticheron:*
A deadly arrow of desire has wounded my heart, and I am wounded through foolishness, and suffer incurably: O Mother of God, despise me not in my despair, but grant me healing, as having given birth unto the Lord and Savior, the only Compassionate One!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Unclean are my thoughts and deceitful my lips, and all my deeds are defiled, and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Sovereign Lady Virgin, pray unto thy Son, Creator, and Lord, that in repentance He may receive my spirit, as the only tender-hearted One!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
I cannot bear, O Child, to see Thee fallen asleep upon the Tree, Who grantest vigilance unto all, that unto those who of old by the fruit of transgression fell asleep in a destructive sleep, Thou mightest grant divine and saving vigilance! the Virgin said, weeping, whom we magnify.

*Canon, the composition of Joseph.[^402] Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion:*
Dancing ever with the sacred choirs in the heavens, deliver from passions and grievous afflictions those who on earth honor thy holy memory, O Therapon!

*Troparion:*
Extended throughout the world, thy sacred and honorable suffering rouses the faithful unto this: to glorify thee brightly, who didst act valiantly through grace, O wise Therapon!

*Troparion:*
Having shown thee as a hospitable star,[^403] the noetic Sun enlightens the faithful who sacredly praise thee, by the light of thy struggles and the radiance of thy miracles.

*Theotokion:*
The sacred prophetic choir foretold from afar that thou, O pure one, wouldst be the Birthgiver of God, higher than the Cherubim and all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a servant of Christ, truly and fittingly bearing thy name, O martyr,[^404] and thou dost ever heal the diseases of souls and bodies.

*Troparion:*
Blood mingled with ointment makes thy priesthood most radiant, O hierarch and shepherd, martyr and physician of the sick; therefore we praise thee!

*Troparion:*
Thou dost heal grievous passions of those who approach with faith, and layest low diseases, and cleansest the harm of evil spirits by the divine invocation of Christ, O all-blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Human mind cannot comprehend the ineffable depth of thy childbearing, O pure one; for God, having poured Himself out in His tender mercy into thy womb, renewed me wholly.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
O servant of Christ, having suffered lawfully, thou didst appear as a true hierarch and martyr; therefore we pray thee, O holy one, heal now the incurable passions of our souls, and entreat Christ, the Lover of mankind and Creator!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Guide us into the way of repentance, who have ever turned aside unto the pathless ways of evils and provoked the all-good Lord, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of despairing men, dwelling place of God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee dead, stretched upon the Cross, O Christ, the all-spotless Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, by which Thou hast saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands, O Bountiful One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying aloud, keeping festival from a pure mind in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having first cleansed thy soul from passionate turmoil, O celebrant of holy mysteries, thou didst receive the divine anointing, O holy one, and at thy departure thou wast adorned with the crown of suffering.

*Troparion:*
Thou art seen ever shining with signs and divine miracles, and thou dost illumine the whole world under the sun, having received grace from God, O right marvelous hierarch!

*Troparion:*
Having first shepherded the people with grace, having sacredly consecrated them with unbloody sacrifices, and having been slaughtered like a lamb, thou wast brought unto the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
As a gate leading unto the divine entrance, as a divine paradise, as a spiritual place of sanctification, as the beauty of Jacob, let us bless the Virgin!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who rise early unto Thee with love, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them forth from the gloom of sin.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with divine radiance, thou didst appear as a most radiant star, enlightening the world under the sun with divine signs, by the Spirit of God, O Therapon, God-blessed hierarch!

*Troparion:*
As a sacred whole-burnt offering, as an acceptable oblation, O father, and a perfect sacrifice, thou wast offered unto the Word Who suffered in the flesh, O Therapon, hierarch, having become a martyr!

*Troparion:*
Having poured out a most fair cup of confession in truth, by thy steadfast suffering thou dost give drink unto all, O venerable Therapon, calling them unto the high preachings!

*Theotokion:*
After thy fearful childbirth thou didst remain a virgin as before; for God was He Who was born, Who arranges all things by His will, O unwedded Mary, full of God's grace!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Divine was thy manner of life, divinely fitting thy life, and glorious thy death, shining with the beauties of martyrdom: by the brightness of miracles thou dost illumine the hearts of those who hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
We have known thee as a fruitful vine of the true spiritual vineyard, truly bearing the clusters of confession, pouring forth the excellent wine of endurance, O God-wise one!

*Troparion:*
Thy temple has been shown forth as a sacred infirmary, relieving by grace, relieving all sicknesses of the infirm, and untying diseases, O God-wise, right marvelous hierarch!

*Theotokion:*
For the Word in thy womb took on coarse flesh without seed, and appeared as perfect man, renewing nature in a God-befitting manner, as He alone knows, O door that contained God, all-spotless one!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
An angel made the furnace shed dew for the venerable youths, while the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having subdued the painful surging of passions by self-control, O venerable one, and having suffered, thou didst quench the burning of error by the flood of thy blood, O hieromartyr of Christ, venerable Therapon!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast perfected by the chrism of anointing, O venerable initiate of sacred mysteries; and having perfected the priests and the people as a shepherd, thou didst finish the good course, having been slain for Christ, O Therapon, wise martyr!

*Troparion:*
By the streams of thy blood thou didst drown the enemy's hosts, and by the radiance of miracles thou dost dispel the gloom of passions, O Therapon, having truly appeared as a wonderworker and physician of the infirm!

*Theotokion:*
He Who sits uncircumscribed in the bosom of the Begetter, O pure one, now sits circumscribed in thy bosom, clothed in thy form, in order to save Adam, having become the new Adam.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable youths, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou, O Christ, doest all things merely by willing them: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst offer unbloody sacrifices unto God, and wast sacrificed as a pure, spotless lamb; thou didst destroy demonic sacrifices, O Therapon, adornment of sufferers, hierarch!

*Troparion:*
Having become a divine inhabitant of paradise, thou wast shown to be a fellow-partaker with the holy angels, shining with the brightness of martyrdom, O passion-bearing martyr and servant of the Savior!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst humble the proud serpent by humility of heart, having resisted him valiantly; and having dyed thy sacred vestment with martyr's blood, O hierarch!

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered from the first curse through thy childbearing, O all-blessed Maiden, full of God's joy full of God's joy, we send up unto thee Gabriel's voice: Rejoice, the cause of the salvation of all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we bless thee!

*Troparion:*
Desiring to see the glory of the Almighty, thou didst endure an unjust death; and by thy blood, O glorious hierarch, thou didst ascend as upon a chariot, and having mounted unto the heavens, thou didst rest in joy.

*Troparion:*
Like light, like morning hast thou shone forth unto us, illumining the ends of the earth with bright beams, O God-inspired hierarch and shepherd, and thou dost dispel the gloom of passions; therefore we honor thee, and piously call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
Being with the choirs of the patriarchs and martyrs, apostles, prophets, and venerable ones, O God-blessed one, pray with them that remission of transgressions be granted unto all who piously keep thy divine memory!

*Troparion:*
The beauty of thy church, O martyr, ever enlightened by the flashings of miracles, dispels the gloom of passions and puts an end to diseases for those who approach with faith day by day, O martyr, hierarch, and right marvelous shepherd!

*Theotokion:*
With thy light, O Virgin full of God's grace among women, enlighten me, I pray, and deliver me from eternal fire, that I may magnify thee, that I may glorify thee, that I may worthily hymn thy great deeds!

## May 28. Our Venerable Father Nicetas, Bishop of Chalcedon[^405]

*At Vespers, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron:*
Emulating Abraham's love of strangers, and Isaac's love of God, and Jacob's guilelessness, thou didst portray the patience of Job, the chastity of Joseph,[^406] the meekness of David, and the gentleness of Moses; anointed with the holy chrism as Aaron of old, O hierarch, sacred wonderworker!

*Sticheron:*
Thy great gentleness drove away demonic malice by the grace of the divine Spirit; having gladdened Him by thy co-suffering, Whom thou didst desire, thy spirit was enlightened by diligent prayers; therefore thou didst receive the grace of miracles to heal diseases, O Nicetas, for those who piously hasten unto thee!

*Sticheron:*
Bound together by kinship, ye kept the laws of the Holy Spirit, O sacred ones; and following the footsteps of the God-wise shepherd who shone with the rays of most glorious healings, ye were adorned with like forms of healings, O most glorious Nicetas, together with God-wise Ignatius![^407]

*Stichera of the Theotokos, same tone and podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Sticheron:*
From condemnation deliver my humble soul, O all-pure Bride of God, and from grievous transgressions, and rescue me from death by thy prayers: and grant me to obtain justification in the day of trial, which the assemblies of the saints obtained: show me cleansed by repentance before the end, and by outpourings of tears!

*Sticheron:*
With the showers of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, bedew my mind, who didst bear Christ, the Drop that through compassions washes away innumerably the boundless iniquities of men: dry up the fountain of my passions, and vouchsafe me ever the torrent of living nourishment, by thy prayers!

*Sticheron:*
The excellent and saving medicine of repentance, O thou who didst bear God my Savior, and streams of tears, and the remembrance of that dread and fearful hour, and of the unbribable judgment, do thou grant me, O Virgin, that by thy prayers I may escape trembling torment, and obtain divine grace!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Ten thousand times, O all-pure one, have I promised to make repentance for my sins, yet the beloved habit of my evils does not leave me: therefore unto thee I cry, and falling down I pray: do thou, O Sovereign Lady, deliver me from such tyranny, guiding me unto better things that are close unto salvation!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thy Mother, the ewe-lamb, beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she marveled and cried out: What is this sight, O my beloved Son? Is this what the faithless assembly of the lawless has rendered unto Thee, who enjoyed Thy many miracles? But glory be to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and model of meekness, a teacher of temperance has the truth of things shown thee unto thy flock; therefore thou didst acquire high things by humility, and riches by poverty, O father Nicetas: entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-form hands of Moses, conquered Amalek's might in the wilderness.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with divine outpouring of light, O venerable one, thou wast radiant; enlighten those who faithfully keep thy light-bearing dormition, taking away the gloom of passions!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear with radiant deeds, full of light, enlightening those who are in the night of grievous things, and guiding them by the Spirit unto the never-setting light, O father Nicetas, sacred one!

*Troparion:*
Thou dost shine forth gifts of miracles unto those who ever approach thy sacred shrine, having received this from God, O all-blessed one, in return for thy labors and sicknesses.

*Theotokion:*
Without seed, by the Father's will, from the Divine Spirit thou didst conceive the Son of God, and didst bear Him in the flesh: from the Father without a mother, and for our sake from thee without a father.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge and establishment!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst ascend, having humbled thyself for Christ's sake, and the haughty brow of the noetic serpent thou didst cast down unto the earth by the Spirit, O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Vouchsafed great gifts from God, thou wast a great priest, O father, God-pleasing hierarch!

*Troparion:*
Accomplishing thy life in guilelessness like an infant, O venerable one, thou didst destroy all the malice of the enemies by grace!

*Theotokion:*
As the most divinely fair dwelling of God, let us all with joy glorify the Birthgiver of God, the only blessed one among women!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
From infancy taking up thy cross, thou didst piously follow Christ, withering the fleshly mind by self-control; therefore on the hierarch's cathedra, O Nicetas, thou didst magnify the Master, Who adorned thy shrine with diverse signs, O God-blessed one!

*Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*

O pure, all-spotless, and unwedded one, who alone didst bear in time the timeless Son and Word of God: entreat Him, together with the holy and honorable patriarchs, martyrs, prophets, and venerable ones, to grant us cleansing and great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, Thine all-pure Mother wept as a mother and said: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste death, wishing to give life unto the dead, as the Compassionate One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
By all-night vigils and all-day standings, O God-wise father, thou didst wither away all movements of the flesh; and rejoicing, thou didst cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst subject the insubordinate part of thy flesh to the powers of the soul, O God-wise one; thou didst receive grace, O all-blessed one, to drive away spirits and cleanse diseases.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by divine power, O hierarch, thou didst conquer all the demonic hosts: as one named after victory,[^408] thou didst inherit a crown of miracles from the Crown-giver!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast adorned with spiritual beauties, having conceived the Word co-unoriginate with the Father, and having born Him in the flesh, O all-spotless Virgin: pray unto Him for the world!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, O my Lord, hast come into the world as a light, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
Manifestly adorned with meekness of soul, O hierarch, thou didst dwell in the land of the meek, having become blessed.

*Troparion:*
Given to drink of the streams of the Spirit's gifts, O father, thou dost pour forth healings unto those who desire them, O God-wise, sacred Nicetas!

*Troparion:*
Thy life, shining with the light of virtues as with precious adornment, was beautified by the grace of miracles, O all-blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Thee do we set forth as an unconquerable weapon against enemies; thee have we acquired as establishment and the hope of our salvation, O Bride of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
A radiant light of the Trinity has shone forth: with Ignatius Nicetas, a sacred natural brotherhood; and they bestow healings upon the faithful, shining brightly.

*Troparion:*
Being kin to one another according to the flesh, O wise ones, ye have now been vouchsafed true kinship according to the Spirit, having lived of one mind and in a manner well-pleasing unto God.

*Troparion:*
Having fled the darkness of destructive heresy by the honorable veneration of Christ's icon, thou didst save that which is according to the image,[^409] O hierarch Nicetas, acceptable unto God!

*Theotokion:*
O door through which the Most High alone passed, open unto me the doors of repentance and the entrances of salvation, preserving me from every harm, O all-spotless one!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "To Thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной воево́дѣ):*
Thou didst shine forth by the brightness of thy deeds, O venerable one; thou wast an heir of the apostolic throne, O Nicetas; and like a sun thou didst shine upon thy flock, O father, being filled with all divine teachings: therefore we cry unto thee: Rejoice, adornment of Chalcedon!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths, consumed more by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou art truly dwelling with the hierarchs, O wise father, being filled with noetic radiance; illumine us also, who cry: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
As a chief shepherd, as a blameless priest of God, thou didst offer pure sacrifices unto Him, brightly chanting: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be a shepherd, O Nicetas, by the noetic flute calling the rational flock unto the pasture of true teachings, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having adorned thy life by confession, O father, thou didst receive the true grace of healings from the Spirit, crying: Blessed art Thou, O my God, in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
That He might deify humanity, the Redeemer appeared as man, O Maiden, having borrowed flesh of His own will from thy womb: Blessed art thou among women, O all-spotless Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Spreading out his hands, Daniel shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

...all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having manifestly received the rewards of Thy great labors—the ineffable light, the immortal life, O venerable one, and the indestructible bridal chamber—thou criest rejoicing unto Christ Thy Crown-giver: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Seeking mercy by mercy, thou didst satisfy the poor, thou didst feed the needy, thou didst clothe the naked, preparing for thyself a garment of glory: thou wast an example for monastics, O Nicetas, who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having become a fair temple of the Divine Spirit by the purity of thy mind, thou drivest away evil spirits and cleansest diseases for those who come unto thee and cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Able to do all things in Christ, and having now been united more clearly and purely unto God, stand before the flock which thou didst acquire, O venerable one, as it faithfully keeps thy divine dormition, and cries: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Christ, the Lord of all, dwelt in thy womb as He willed, and showed thee to be higher than the angels, O Virgin Mother: He granted sanctification and enlightenment unto us who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, an unhewn mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together separated natures: therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God!

*Troparion:*
Sacredly following thy divine namesake,[^410] thou wast vouchsafed this through his example, O father Nicetas, God-blessed one, and with gracious radiance of the Spirit thou wast enriched, O God-inspired one!

*Troparion:*
Ye appeared enriched with the grace of the Trinity, O God-bearers, having equally received from It the divine operation of healings, having lived honorably and pleasingly unto God, O right marvelous ones!

*Troparion:*
Today thy memory, O father, shines more than the sun with the radiance of virtues, illumining the universe with miracles and enlightening the faithful, O all-honorable initiate of sacred mysteries!

*Troparion:*
Having appeared like bright morning stars, O father Nicetas, together with sacredly-celebrating Ignatius, enlighten all who ever praise you, O pious and blessed ones!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be the dwelling of the Light, of the Word Who shone forth from the Father before the morning star, O Virgin Maiden, the only one full of God's joy, Who enlightens the faithful and crowns the venerable!

## May 29. The Holy Venerable Martyr Theodosia the Virgin[^411]

*Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Shining with the radiance of virginity, O venerable Theodosia, and brightly vested in the purple dye of the blood of martyrdom, O all-wise maiden; and having entered into the transcendent bridal chamber of Christ, O all-immaculate maiden, thou rejoicest with the angelic ranks, in truth holding an indestructible festival, O glorious one.

*Sticheron 2:* O virgin martyr, all-lauded Theodosia, having desired Christ with thy whole soul, thou didst endure the wounds of torture, gallantly struck for the sake of thy Lover, and scraped with iron claws along thy ribs. O thy struggles and steadfast resistance, by which thou didst truly cast down the proud one unto the earth!

*Sticheron 3:* Brightly adorned with the beauty of both soul and body, thou didst offer thyself unto Christ, desiring to be crowned by Him with a crown of glory, O all-glorious martyr Theodosia, and worthily to receive upon thy head the truly precious diadem of the Kingdom, having been shown forth as an incorrupt bride, O divinely wise one.

*Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone 8, Same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Theotokion 1:* Rejoice, enlightenment of souls, forgiveness of those who sin, amendment of the negligent; rejoice, consolation and joy, helper of the afflicted; rejoice, good one, healing of infirm souls; rejoice, reconciliation with God and mediatress, as thou art holier than all the Seraphim, O pure one.

*Theotokion 2:* Taking flesh from thy blood, the pre-eternal God showed thee forth as protectress unto mankind, O pure one. Therefore deliver thy servants from every calamity and distress, and from the snares of the crafty enemy; and vouchsafe all who glorify and bow down before thee to partake of the radiance of the elect.

*Theotokion 3:* Save me, O most pure Sovereign Lady, who didst ineffably bear the Savior Christ; for thee alone have I acquired as protectress, an invincible rampart, a shelter, joy, and divine consolation of my soul. Save me, deliver me who am wretched from the evil worm and from eternal fire, as thou art the Mother of Christ God:

That I may magnify thee with joyful voices, O pure one, and glorify the deep of thy love for mankind. Save me from calamities, and pluck me from the soul-corrupting arrows of the invisible and all-crafty enemy; for against him I set thee forth, O Bride of God, as a firm and invincible weapon, O Mother of Christ God.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

"What is this sight beheld by mine eyes, O Master, Thou Who holdest all creation, lifted up upon the Tree, and dying, Thou Who givest life unto all?" the Theotokos weeping said, when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and man, lifted up upon the Cross.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Acceptably hast thou attained unto virtue, O martyr, and becamest a pure receptacle of Christ and a beloved dwelling of the Holy Spirit; whence thou didst put to shame the enemy who wars against the human race, having suffered valiantly, and thou didst cut down Copronymus[^412] with the sword of faith, O Theodosia; and rejoicing thou hast passed over unto the heavenly bridal chamber. For those who honor thee with faith, entreat great mercy.

*Canon of the Saint, whose acrostic is: "To the martyr Theodosia I sing praise":*[^413]

*Ode 1, Heirmos:* Israel of old, traversing with dryshod feet the Red Sea deep, by Moses' hands stretched out crosswise conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion 1:* God became for thee the path of suffering, O Theodosia, He Who willingly ascended the Cross and cast down the tormentor, causing him to be mocked like a bird through thy torments.

*Troparion 2:* With mighty strength, O all-honored Theodosia, having suffered stoutly upon earth, O all-lauded one, thou didst dash to pieces the prince of delusion; and as a trophy-bearer, O martyr, thou didst receive the crown of glory.

*Troparion 3:* He who of old possessed great boastfulness is now cast down, confounded by thy patient endurance, O martyr; for having received the power of the Cross, thou didst put him to shame in his boundless raging.

*Theotokion:* The serpent craftily deceived me, took me captive, and cast into me the desire to become a god; but having been called back through thee, O most pure one, I am in truth immutably deified.

*Ode 3, Heirmos:* Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge, and my firm foundation!

*Troparion 1:* Divine desire showed thee forth as an honored bride, O Theodosia, a martyr of Christ, having loved His Cross.

*Troparion 2:* Having forsaken the corruptible provision of the flesh, O venerable one, thou livest by the life-creating Spirit, O all-glorious Theodosia.

*Troparion 3:* Heal my mind by thy grace that is full of divine joy, O honored one, and cure my soul which is paralyzed by passions.

*Theotokion:* Thou didst raise up my fallen form, O pure one, having given birth to the Prince of the resurrection of us all, O Sovereign Lady.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

By the streams of thy blood having truly submerged all the strength of the crafty devil, O glorious one, thou didst pass through the immense gulf of torments unharmed. And thou hast manifestly settled in the holy land, worthily receiving the recompense of thy labors, and wast vouchsafed the entrance of the heavenly bridal chamber, O all-lauded Theodosia. Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love celebrate thy holy memory.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Let us hymn the heavenly gate and the ark, the all-holy mountain, the bright cloud, the unburnt bush, the noetic paradise, the recall of Eve, the great treasure of the whole universe; for salvation was wrought in her for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions. Therefore we cry unto her: Pray unto thy Son to grant remission of sins unto those who piously bow down before thy most holy childbearing.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb wept and cried out, and bitterly lamenting exclaimed: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest in Thy tender mercy, O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible wellspring! Have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine Passion with faith!"

*Ode 4, Heirmos:* Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion 1:* The tormentor raged, wishing in his wretchedness to terrify the strength of thy patience with wounds; but thou, O all-lauded one, didst cry aloud: "Glory to my God!"

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast radiantly enriched with a God-given and divine name,[^414] O passion-bearing martyr; for thou becamest a divine gift rejoicing us who honor thee with faith and love.

*Troparion 3:* Adorned with a garment dipped in thy martyr's blood, O divinely wise Theodosia, thou didst cry aloud unto thy Bridegroom: "I run after Thee with love!"

*Theotokion:* Thou didst bear the incarnate Word in a manner befitting God, O pure Virgin, and remainest a virgin; therefore with unceasing voices, with unhesitating faith, we cry unto thee: "Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady!"

*Ode 5, Heirmos:* Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning those who hymn Thee with faith from the darkness of ignorance.

*Troparion 1:* Having loved the poured-out myrrh of Christ,[^415] O maiden, thou didst follow Him with divine wisdom, glorifying Him in hymns.

*Troparion 2:* For a little blood the martyr exchanged temporal life for eternal, and inherited the Heavenly Kingdom.

*Troparion 3:* Like wellsprings the martyr's relics pour forth healings unto those who are sick, and they cure infirmities of the soul with faith.

*Theotokion:* Who can tell thy mystery, O all-immaculate one? For in thy womb thou didst contain the Creator Whom none can contain.

*Ode 6, Heirmos:* "I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord," the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion 1:* With zeal the martyr Theodosia accepted death for Thy sake, not fearing the threats of the tormentors, conforming herself unto Thy voluntary Passion, O Christ.

*Troparion 2:* Readily strengthened by Thy Cross, women trample upon the heads of serpents, as Thou didst foretell, O Savior, being wounded by Thy love and by divine desire for Thee.

*Theotokion:* Finding thee from afar as a perfect and exceeding fair dove, a lily and flower of the valleys, O Mother of God, the noetic Bridegroom took up His abode in thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*

By labors hast thou attained unto the painless life, and with thy blood, O martyr, thou didst choke Copronymus, the vile enemy of the Church of Christ, O all-blessed Theodosia; and thou rejoicest with the heavenly hosts, sheltering those who honor thy memory.

*Ode 7, Heirmos:* In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burned rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Troparion 1:* Beholding thee adorned with God-given beauties, O martyr, with faith we brightly cry aloud unto thy Bridegroom in hymns: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Troparion 2:* With the lamp-bearing virgins, O martyr, thou hast now rejoicing ascended into the heavenly bridal chamber with Christ, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Theotokion:* He Who sits upon the throne exceeding high, having found thee, O Virgin, as a God-like throne upon earth, hath rested in thee. Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 8, Heirmos:* Daniel, stretching forth his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of the lions in the pit; and the children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 1:* Having dissolved the bond of the flesh, O venerable Theodosia, rejoicing thou didst fly unto the light-radiant bridal chamber, holding bridal choir, shining brightly in the crimson blood of martyrdom, and crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast vouchsafed to dwell brightly in the heavenly bridal chamber, O venerable Theodosia, having found everlasting blessing, O ever-memorable one, and being truly enriched by the fruit of thy courage, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion 3:* Manifestly wast thou adorned with thy namesake title, O pure Theodosia; for as an excellent gift of God wast thou given unto us, O all-wise one, pouring forth rivers of gifts beyond understanding unto those who sing in faith: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Theotokion:* Truly having given birth to God, truly wast thou named the most pure Mother of God, bearing truth, a dwelling-place of God according to every manner of childbirth. Therefore we the faithful glorify thee as the Theotokos with divine wisdom, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 9, Heirmos:* The cornerstone, Christ, was cut without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, uniting separated natures. Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion 1:* Loosen the bonds of transgressions of those who piously hymn thee, through thy supplications, O divinely wise Theodosia, all-lauded martyr, standing before God the Almighty, O all-honored one.

*Troparion 2:* Artfully receiving the desire of our foremother Eve, deified through beholding God, and having become and having become a god through divine communion. Therefore we magnify thee, O all-blessed Theodosia.

*Troparion 3:* Thy Bridegroom Christ is all sweetness, O passion-bearer, all consolation of soul, O martyr Theodosia; and now enjoying Him, remember us by thy prayers.

*Theotokion:* Desiring to come in the flesh, He Who framed all things by a word took up His abode in thee alone, having found thee as a rampart of all; and He showed thee forth as the true Mother of God, O Virgin Mother.

## May 30. Our Venerable Father Isaac, of the Dalmatian Monastery[^416]

*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Beholding the beauty of paradise, and richly enjoying its incorrupt flowers, thou didst blossom forth the divine understanding unto the world; and partaking thereof with spiritual love, O venerable father, we nurture salvation for our souls.

*Sticheron 2:* Having hedged about thy body with abstinence, O father, by prayers and vigils thou didst mortify the stirrings of passion. Therefore the spiritual power that overshadowed thee showed thee forth as a noetic luminary unto the whole world.

*Sticheron 3:* Thou didst illumine the monastic life, O father, shining forth like the sun from the wilderness upon the imperial city; therefore by thy teachings thou didst enlighten the faithful to bless the consubstantial Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.

*Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone 1, Same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Theotokion 1:* Beset by desperate falls, O pure one, having thee as our only protectress, O Virgin, we cry out gratefully: "Purify us, O all-holy Bride of God; for thou art the refuge of the world and the help of our race!"

*Theotokion 2:* By the staff of thy protection, O pure Mother of God, quickly drive away the beast-like passions of my wretched soul, peacefully guiding my life; and number me with the holy flock of thine elect sheep.

*Theotokion 3:* Overwhelmed by the deep of sins, fleeing unto the tranquil haven of thy most pure prayers, O Mother of God, I cry unto thee: "Save me, reaching forth thy sovereign right hand" unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having slipped into corrupt habits, I lie prostrate, and flee unto thy tranquility, O Virgin; from the adverse tempest and multiform temptations deliver me, that I may unceasingly hymn thy grace, O Theotokos, Mother and Ever-Virgin.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His long-suffering, the pure Mother wept and said: "Woe is me, my sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?"

*Canon of the Venerable Father Isaac, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1, Heirmos:* O Lord, Who didst deliver [Israel] from bondage by Thy sovereign right hand: as Thou didst save him, now save us!

*Troparion 1:* Coming forth from the desert regions, O venerable one, and scattering the assembly of heresy, O holy one, thou didst establish the faith of Christ.

*Troparion 2:* Standing before kings with feeble voice,[^417] thou wast never put to shame, desiring to confirm the Church of Christ.

*Troparion 3:* Cast out from the mire of delusion, O all-lauded one, thou didst shine forth like a pearl, illuminated by the radiance of the Spirit, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:* Having conceived the timeless Son in thy womb, born of the Father, O most pure and immaculate Virgin, thou gavest birth to Him as an infant.

*Ode 3, Heirmos:* Establish our minds and hearts, Thou Who by a word didst establish the heavens, that we may hymn and glorify Thee unto the salvation of our souls.

*Troparion 1:* Thou wast shown forth as a noetic trumpet of the Spirit, O father, summoning all, as if unto battle, to stand against the regiments of the enemy with the help of Christ the Son.

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast shown forth as a noetic ladder leading unto the Kingdom on high, O God-bearer, for those who ascend by faith, established in the Spirit by thy teachings.

*Troparion 3:* Truly wast thou shown forth as a mountain of teaching, O God-bearer, by love of the Master gathering all into the Church of Christ, bound unto Him by virtues.

*Theotokion:* Truly wast thou shown forth as Mother, O Virgin, having given birth to Emmanuel, God the Word; cease not to pray unto Him as an infant, O unwedded bride, blessed, pure Theotokos!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Thou wast shown forth as a rain-bearing spiritual cloud unto those upon earth, O all-blessed one, watering the hearts of the faithful like a furrow. Traversing the ends of the earth with thy divine words, thou didst pour forth a rain of teachings, O all-wise one. Therefore like streams thou didst divinely cultivate in the hearts of all the sweet fragrance of the Spirit, O God-bearer Isaac. Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As thou art the all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as thou art the unwedded Mother of the Redeemer, as thou art the receptacle of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: hasten to deliver me who am a vile dwelling of lawlessness and a plaything of demons in my mind, from their wickedness; and make me a bright dwelling of virtues, O light-bearing and incorrupt one; drive away the cloud of passions, and vouchsafe me by thy prayers to partake of the kingdom on high and of never-waning light.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb wept and cried out, and bitterly lamenting exclaimed: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest in Thy tender mercy, O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible wellspring! Have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine Passion with faith!"

*Ode 4, Heirmos:* I heard the report of Thy power, and my heart rejoiced in the Lord.

*Troparion 1:* Thou didst show forth an angel-like life upon earth, holding choir with the heavenly powers.

*Troparion 2:* Having first become a leader of monks,[^418] coming from the wilderness, O divinely wise one, thou wast shown forth as an example unto those in the city, O glorious one.

*Troparion 3:* Thy memory now in truth gladdens the hearts of those who hymn thee, O father Isaac.

*Theotokion:* O Sovereign Lady, now as ever, by thy prayers deliver thy servants from lawless enemies.

*Ode 5, Heirmos:* Thou Who from the night of ignorance didst enlighten the ends of the earth with divine knowledge, enlighten me with the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord!

*Troparion 1:* From the desert in truth thou didst shine forth like a bright star, and by thy teachings thou didst drive away the heretical night.

*Troparion 2:* Seizing the reins of the horse of Valens,[^419] O all-lauded one, thou didst rebuke his beast-like mind, manifestly putting him to shame.

*Troparion 3:* Thou didst yield thy head, O father, to be wounded with staves, noetically holding Christ God as the head of thy faith holding Christ God as the head of thy faith.

*Theotokion:* Having been shown forth as more honorable than the Cherubim, O all-hymned Virgin, pray unto thy Son to save the souls of those who hymn thee.

*Ode 6, Heirmos:* Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O plenteous in mercy, Christ our God!

*Troparion 1:* By the foreknowledge of the Spirit thou didst foretell an abominable death unto Valens,[^420] O wise one, and wast shown forth in truth as a glorious prophet.

*Troparion 2:* With the winnowing-fan of faith thou didst gather the faithful like wheat; and by thy prayers, O wise one, thou didst consume Valens like chaff in a barn.

*Troparion 3:* Thou didst smooth the tablets of the heart of those who listen unto thee with faith, O divinely wise Isaac, engraving upon them the image of Orthodoxy.

*Theotokion:* O thou who alone by a word didst bear the Word in the flesh, deliver our souls from the snares of the enemy, we pray.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As First-Fruits of Nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*

As a faithful servant of God, kindled with zeal for the Church of Christ, seizing the reins of Valens, thou didst prophetically foretell unto him a ruinous death for locking up the churches, O venerable one; therefore pray ever for us who honor thee, O venerable Isaac!

*Ode 7, Heirmos:* Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the earth, and by a word didst establish the heavens: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:* Thou Who gavest unto Thy venerable servant a word of rebuke against the malice of the tormentor: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:* Dwelling gloriously in the bright abode of the venerable, O father, thou criest unto the Creator: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:* O venerable father, cease not to pray unceasingly unto the Son for us, that thy flock may be delivered from every affliction and attain unto the life that cannot be taken away.

*Theotokion:* Thou Who didst take up Thine abode in the Virgin's womb, fashioning anew Adam therein: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Ode 8, Heirmos:* The unoriginate King of glory, before Whom the heavenly hosts tremble: praise Him, ye priests; and exalt Him supremely, ye people, unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:* Him Who showed forth Isaac as a champion of the Orthodox faith: all ye priests, praise Him; and ye people, exalt Him supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion 2:* Like a rational sheep thou didst come from the wilderness, driving the wild beasts away from the flock of Christ, expelling them to dwell in desolate places.

*Troparion 3:* With meekness of soul, O father, thou wast not afraid of the fierce and evil rage of Valens; for the Spirit of truth was active within thee.

*Theotokion:* The Stone of life was cut without human hands from thee, O all-holy pure one, as it is written: God, Who was well-pleased to take up His abode in thee.

*Ode 9, Heirmos:* Thee, the Theotokos and Mother of Light, do we magnify in hymns.

*Troparion 1:* With sovereign might, by the strength of the divine Spirit, thou didst crush the hosts of demons.

*Troparion 2:* By the bond of faith thou didst shatter the alliances and regiments of heresies. O divinely wise Isaac, cease not to remember us before God, that He may save us!

*Theotokion:* Thy childbearing, O Virgin, transformed the sorrowful childbirth of Eve.

## May 31. The Holy Apostle Hermes, and the Holy Martyr Hermias[^421]

*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Having quenched the fire of godlessness with drops of blood, O great martyr, thou didst richly water the Church of Christ, and didst make bright the hearts of the faithful who piously honor thy sacred and venerable memory.

*Sticheron 2:* Like an impregnable pillar, thou wast not shaken at all by the assaults of the enemy; rather, thou didst crush their hearts, O Hermias; and by the pain of thy valiant struggles thou didst cast down all their raging like a bowing wall.

*Sticheron 3:* Leading up those who had fallen into the abyss of unbelief by the word of God, O Hermias, thou didst endure the surging tempest of many torments, O all-lauded passion-bearer, until with them thou didst enter with joy into the eternal haven.

*Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone 1, Same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Theotokion 1:* Beset by desperate falls, O pure one, having thee as our only protectress, O Virgin, we cry out gratefully: "Purify us, O all-holy Bride of God" For thou art the refuge of the world, and the protection of our race.

*Theotokion 2:* With the rod of thy protection, O pure Mother of God, quickly drive away the bestial passions of my wretched soul, peacefully governing my life, and number me among the holy flock of thine elect sheep.

*Theotokion 3:* Drowning in the abyss of transgressions, having fled unto the calm haven of thy prayer, O most pure Theotokos, I cry out unto thee: Save me, extending thy mighty right hand unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one.

*Glory... Both now...*

*Theotokion 4:* Having slipped in the rotten gloom, I lie prostrate, and I flee unto thy calm, O Virgin; from the tempest of adversaries and multifaceted temptations deliver me, that I may unceasingly hymn thy grace, O Theotokos, Mother, Ever-Virgin.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When the immaculate Virgin beheld the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, weeping she cried aloud: "My sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How is it that Thou Who holdest all creation in Thy palm art nailed in the flesh unto the Tree?"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Thy martyr, O Lord, Hermias, in his suffering received an incorrupt crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers, and crushed the feeble insolence of the demons. By his prayers save our souls!

*Canon, the composition of Joseph.[^422] Tone 8.*

*Ode 1, Heirmos:* Having traversed the water as dry land, and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried out: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion 1:* A star shining with divine light, thou wast recognized by the radiance of martyrdom, brightly illuminating all who honor thee purely in faith, O Hermias.

*Troparion 2:* By looking continually unto God, having despised all things in life, O wise martyr, thou didst enter into the struggles of martyrdom, and didst mightily conquer the enemy.

*Troparion 3:* Being fair of countenance and of soul, O Hermias, thou didst enlist as a soldier unto the heavenly King, and didst utterly subdue His enemies, O martyr.

*Troparion 4:* Because thou didst not sacrifice unto idols, O blessed one, they tore out thy teeth;[^423] but thou didst crush the jaws of the spiritual lions, and didst clothe thyself with the unfading crown of victory.
